===Original===
**To**

===Chinese===
**献给**

===Original===
**My Mother and Father**

===Chinese===
**我的母亲和父亲**

## I Kona Weather / 一 科纳天气

===Original===
Miss Minerva Winterslip was a Bostonian in good standing, and long past the romantic age. Yet beauty thrilled her still, even the semi-barbaric beauty of a Pacific island. As she walked slowly along the beach she felt the little catch in her throat that sometimes she had known in Symphony Hall, Boston, when her favorite orchestra rose to some new and unexpected height of loveliness.

It was the hour at which she liked Waikiki best, the hour just preceding dinner and the quick tropic darkness. The shadows cast by the tall coconut palms lengthened and deepened, the light of the falling sun flamed on Diamond Head and tinted with gold the rollers sweeping in from the coral reef. A few late swimmers, reluctant to depart, dotted those waters whose touch is like the caress of a lover. On the springboard of the nearest float a slim brown girl poised for one delectable instant. What a figure! Miss Minerva, well over fifty herself, felt a mild twinge of envy﻿—youth, youth like an arrow, straight and sure and flying. Like an arrow the slender figure rose, then fell; the perfect dive, silent and clean.

Miss Minerva glanced at the face of the man who walked beside her. But Amos Winterslip was oblivious to beauty, he had made that the first rule of his life. Born in the Islands, he had never known the mainland beyond San Francisco. Yet there could be no doubt about it, he was the New England conscience personified﻿—the New England conscience in a white duck suit.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦·温特斯利普小姐（Miss Minerva Winterslip）是位体面的波士顿人，早已过了耽于浪漫的年纪。然而美依然令她心颤，哪怕是太平洋岛屿那种半带蛮荒的美。她沿着海滩缓缓漫步，喉咙里泛起一阵轻微的哽咽——这感觉她有时在波士顿的交响音乐厅（Symphony Hall）也有过，那时她最钟爱的乐团攀上了某种崭新而出人意料的妙境。

这是一天中她最爱怀基基（Waikiki）的时刻——晚餐之前、热带夜色倏然而降之前的那一刻。高高的椰子树投下的影子越拉越长、越来越浓，落日的光焰在钻石头山（Diamond Head）上燃烧，给从珊瑚礁外滚滚涌来的浪涛镀上一层金色。几个迟迟不肯离去的游泳者星星点点散布在那片水域上，海水的抚触宛如情人的爱抚。最近的浮台跳板上，一个棕色皮肤的苗条姑娘凝身伫立，姿态曼妙，只一瞬。多美的身段！年过五十好几的米纳瓦小姐微微感到一阵嫉妒的刺痛——青春啊，青春像一支箭，笔直、笃定、疾飞。那纤细的身影如箭般腾起，随即落下；完美的入水，悄然无声，干净利落。

米纳瓦小姐瞥了一眼走在身旁那个人的脸。可是阿莫斯·温特斯利普（Amos Winterslip）对美无动于衷——他早已把这立为人生第一戒律。他生在岛上，除了旧金山（San Francisco），从没见识过美国本土。然而毫无疑问，他就是新英格兰良知的化身——一位穿白帆布西装的新英格兰良知。

===Original===
“Better turn back, Amos,” suggested Miss Minerva. “Your dinner’s waiting. Thank you so much.”

“I’ll walk as far as the fence,” he said. “When you get tired of Dan and his carryings-on, come to us again. We’ll be glad to have you.”

“That’s kind of you,” she answered, in her sharp crisp way. “But I really must go home. Grace is worried about me. Of course, she can’t understand. And my conduct *is* scandalous, I admit. I came over to Honolulu for six weeks, and I’ve been wandering about these islands for ten months.”

===Chinese===
“还是回去吧，阿莫斯，”米纳瓦小姐提议道，“你的晚饭正等着呢。多谢你送我这一程。”

“我送到栅栏那儿，”他说，“哪天你受够了丹跟他那些名堂，就再回我们这边来住。我们都很乐意有你做伴。”

“多谢好意，”她以干脆利落的口吻答道，“可我实在该回家了。格蕾丝都在为我担心呢。当然，她是无法理解的。而且我的行径*确实*出格，这我承认。我本来只打算到檀香山（Honolulu）待六个星期，结果在这片群岛上流连了十个月。”

===Original===
“As long as that?”

She nodded. “I can’t explain it. Every day I make a solemn vow I’ll start packing my trunks﻿—tomorrow.”

“And tomorrow never comes,” said Amos. “You’ve been taken in by the tropics. Some people are.”

===Chinese===
“有那么久了？”

她点点头。“我自己也说不清。每天我都郑重发誓，明天就开始收拾行李——”

“可明天永远不会来，”阿莫斯说，“你叫热带给迷住了。有些人就是这样。”

===Original===
“Weak people, I presume you mean,” snapped Miss Minerva. “Well, I’ve never been weak. Ask anybody on Beacon Street.”

He smiled wanly. “It’s a strain in the Winterslips,” he said. “Supposed to be Puritans, but always sort of yearning toward the lazy latitudes.”

“I know,” answered Miss Minerva, her eyes on that exotic shore line. “It’s what sent so many of them adventuring out of Salem harbor. Those who stayed behind felt that the travelers were seeing things no Winterslip should look at. But they envied them just the same﻿—or maybe for that very reason.” She nodded. “A sort of gypsy strain. It’s what sent your father over here to set up as a whaler, and got you born so far from home. You know you don’t belong here, Amos. You should be living in Milton or Roxbury, carrying a little green bag and popping into a Boston office every morning.”

===Chinese===
“你是说意志薄弱的人吧，”米纳瓦小姐不客气地回敬，“哼，我从来不是意志薄弱的人。不信去问问灯塔街（Beacon Street）上随便哪一位。”

他淡淡地苦笑了一下。“这是温特斯利普家血脉里的一股怪劲，”他说，“照理说是清教徒，心里却总向往着那些懒洋洋的低纬度地方。”

“我知道，”米纳瓦小姐答道，眼睛望着那异域情调的海岸线。“正是这股劲头，当年把那么多温特斯利普家的人送出塞勒姆（Salem）港去闯荡。留下来的人觉得，那些远行者看到的东西是温特斯利普家的人不该看的。可他们照样眼红——说不定恰恰因为这一点才眼红。”她点点头。“某种吉普赛血统。当年就是它把你父亲支到这儿来干捕鲸的营生，也害你生在离家这么远的地方。你自己明白，你本不属于这里，阿莫斯。你本该住在米尔顿或罗克斯伯里，拎个小绿包，每天早晨钻进波士顿的哪间办公室。”

===Original===
“I’ve often thought it,” he admitted. “And who knows﻿—I might have made something of my life﻿—”

They had come to a barbed-wire fence, an unaccustomed barrier on that friendly shore. It extended well down on to the beach; a wave rushed up and lapped the final post, then receded.

Miss Minerva smiled. “Well, this is where Amos leaves off and Dan begins,” she said. “I’ll watch my chance and run around the end. Lucky you couldn’t build it so it moved with the tide.”

===Chinese===
“我常这么想，”他承认道，“谁知道呢——说不定我也能干出一番名堂来——”

两人来到一道带刺铁丝网栅栏前——在那片亲善无间的海岸上，这是个不同寻常的障碍。栅栏一直延伸到海滩低处；一道浪涌上来，舔过最末一根桩柱，又退了回去。

米纳瓦小姐笑了。“好了，这儿就是阿莫斯的地界到头、丹的地界开头的地方，”她说，“我瞧准浪头的空当，从栅栏尽头绕过去。幸好你们没法把它造成会随潮水移动的。”

===Original===
“You’ll find your luggage in your room at Dan’s, I guess,” Amos told her. “Remember what I said about﻿—” He broke off suddenly. A stocky, white-clad man had appeared in the garden beyond the barrier, and was moving rapidly toward them. Amos Winterslip stood rigid for a moment, an angry light flaming in his usually dull eyes. “Goodbye,” he said, and turned.

“Amos!” cried Miss Minerva sharply. He moved on, and she followed. “Amos, what nonsense! How long has it been since you spoke to Dan?”

He paused under an algaroba tree. “Thirty-one years,” he said. “Thirty-one years the tenth of last August.”

===Chinese===
“我想，你的行李已经放在丹家你那间屋里了，”阿莫斯对她说，“记住我说过的关于——”他猛地收住话头。栅栏那头的花园里出现了一个矮壮的白衣男人，正快步朝他们走来。阿莫斯·温特斯利普僵立了片刻，平日晦暗的眼睛里燃起怒火。“再见。”他说完，转身便走。

“阿莫斯！”米纳瓦小姐厉声喊道。他只顾往前走，她跟了上去。“阿莫斯，这多荒唐！你有多少年没跟丹说过话了？”

他在一棵牧豆树下停住脚步。“三十一年，”他说，“到上个八月十号，就满三十一年了。”

===Original===
“That’s long enough,” she told him. “Now, come around that foolish fence of yours, and hold out your hand to him.”

“Not me,” said Amos. “I guess you don’t know Dan, Minerva, and the sort of life he’s led. Time and again he’s dishonored us all﻿—”

“Why, Dan’s regarded as a big man,” she protested. “He’s respected﻿—”

“And rich,” added Amos bitterly. “And I’m poor. Yes, that’s the way it often goes in this world. But there’s a world to come, and over there I reckon Dan’s going to get his.”

===Chinese===
“够久了，”她对他说，“行了，绕过你那道愚蠢的栅栏，把手伸给他吧。”

“我才不呢，”阿莫斯说，“米纳瓦，我看你不了解丹，也不了解他过的是种什么日子。他一次又一次地让全家蒙羞——”

“咦，丹可被人们当作大人物呢，”她反驳道，“他很受敬重——”

“还有钱，”阿莫斯刻薄地补了一句，“而我穷。是啊，这世上往往就是这样。不过人还有来世，我算准了，到了那边，丹是要遭报应的。”

===Original===
Hardy soul though she was, Miss Minerva was somewhat frightened by the look of hate on his thin face. She saw the uselessness of further argument. “Goodbye, Amos,” she said. “I wish I might persuade you to come East some day﻿—” He gave no sign of hearing, but hurried along the white stretch of sand.

When Miss Minerva turned, Dan Winterslip was smiling at her from beyond the fence. “Hello, there,” he cried. “Come this side of the wire and enjoy life again. You’re mighty welcome.”

“How are you, Dan?” She watched her chance with the waves and joined him. He took both her hands in his.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐人虽硬朗，也不由得被他瘦脸上那副仇恨的神情吓了一跳。她明白再争下去也是徒劳。“再见，阿莫斯，”她说，“真希望哪天能劝你去东部看看——”他毫无听见的样子，只顾沿着那片白沙滩匆匆走了。

米纳瓦小姐转过身时，丹·温特斯利普（Dan Winterslip）正隔着栅栏朝她微笑。“嘿，你好啊，”他喊道，“到铁丝网这边来，重新享受生活吧。热烈欢迎。”

“你好吗，丹？”她瞅准浪头的空当，过去与他相会。他用双手握住了她的双手。

===Original===
“Glad to see you,” he said, and his eyes backed him up. Yes, he did have a way with women. “It’s a bit lonely at the old homestead these days. Need a young girl about to brighten things up.”

Miss Minerva sniffed. “I’ve tramped Boston in galoshes too many winters,” she reminded him, “to lose my head over talk like that.”

“Forget Boston,” he urged. “We’re all young in Hawaii. Look at me.”

===Chinese===
“见到你真高兴，”他说，他的眼神也这么作证。是啊，他对女人确实有一套。“这几天老宅子里有点冷清。正需要个年轻姑娘来给日子添点亮色。”

米纳瓦小姐嗤了一声。“我在波士顿穿了太多冬天的套鞋蹚雪，”她提醒他，“才不会为这种话晕头转向。”

“忘掉波士顿吧，”他劝道，“在夏威夷，我们人人都年轻。瞧瞧我。”

===Original===
She did look at him, wonderingly. He was sixty-three, she knew, but only the mass of wavy white hair overhanging his temples betrayed his age. His face, burned to the deepest bronze by long years of wandering under the Polynesian sun, was without a line or wrinkle. Deep-chested and muscular, he could have passed on the mainland for a man of forty.

“I see my precious brother brought you as far as the deadline,” he remarked as they moved on through the garden. “Sent me his love, I presume?”

“I tried to get him to come round and shake hands,” Miss Minerva said.

Dan Winterslip laughed. “Don’t deprive poor Amos of his hate for me,” he urged. “It’s about all he lives for now. Comes over every night and stands under that algaroba tree of his, smoking cigarettes and staring at my house. Know what he’s waiting for? He’s waiting for the Lord to strike me down for my sins. Well, he’s a patient waiter, I’ll say that for him.”

===Chinese===
她当真惊讶地打量起他来。她知道他六十三岁了，可只有垂覆在鬓角的那一大绺波浪状白发泄露了他的年纪。他的脸经过波利尼西亚骄阳下多年漂泊，晒成了最深的古铜色，没有一丝线条与皱纹。胸膛厚实，筋骨强健，在美国本土让人当作四十岁的人也绰绰有余。

“我瞧见我那位宝贝弟弟把你一直送到分界线，”两人穿过花园往前走时他说道，“想必他托你向我问好了？”

“我本想劝他过来跟你握手言和，”米纳瓦小姐说。

丹·温特斯利普哈哈大笑。“可别剥夺了可怜的阿莫斯对我的恨，”他劝道，“那差不多是他如今活下去的全部指望了。他每天晚上都过来，站在他那棵牧豆树下，抽着烟，瞪着我的房子。知道他在等什么吗？他在等上帝因我的罪孽把我劈倒。唉，论等人，他可真有耐性，这一点我得替他说句公道话。”

===Original===
Miss Minerva did not reply. Dan’s great rambling house of many rooms was set in beauty almost too poignant to be borne. She stood, drinking it all in again, the poinciana trees like big crimson umbrellas, the stately golden glow, the gigantic banyans casting purple shadows, her favorite hau tree, seemingly old as time itself, covered with a profusion of yellow blossoms. Loveliest of all were the flowering vines, the bougainvillea burying everything it touched in brick-red splendor. Miss Minerva wondered what her friends who every spring went into sedate ecstasies over the Boston Public Gardens would say if they could see what she saw now. They would be a bit shocked, perhaps, for this was too lurid to be quite respectable. A scarlet background﻿—and a fitting one, no doubt, for Cousin Dan.

They reached the door at the side of the house that led directly into the living-room. Glancing to her right, Miss Minerva caught through the lush foliage glimpses of the iron fence and tall gates that fronted on Kalia Road. Dan opened the door for her, and she stepped inside. Like most apartments of its sort in the Islands, the living-room was walled on but three sides, the fourth was a vast expanse of wire screening. They crossed the polished floor and entered the big hall beyond. Near the front door a Hawaiian woman of uncertain age rose slowly from her chair. She was a huge, high-breasted, dignified specimen of that vanishing race.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐没有作声。丹那座屋宇连绵、房间众多的宅邸，坐落在一派几乎浓艳得叫人承受不住的美景之中。她伫立着，再度将这一切尽收眼底：凤凰木像一把把绯红的大伞，仪态挺秀的金链花，投下紫色浓荫的巨榕，还有她心爱的那棵桐棉树——看上去古老得如同时光本身——缀满了繁盛的黄花。最美的是那些开花的藤蔓，叶子花（bougainvillea）把它触及的一切都埋进砖红色的华彩里。米纳瓦小姐寻思，她那些每年春天都对波士顿公共花园发出端庄惊叹的朋友们，若是见到她眼前的景象，会作何评论？只怕要吓一跳，因为这美艳得太扎眼，算不得体面。一片猩红的底色——对堂兄丹这样的人来说，无疑再合适不过。

两人走到房子侧面那扇直通起居室的门前。米纳瓦小姐朝右边一瞥，透过繁茂的枝叶，隐约望见临卡利亚路（Kalia Road）而立的铁栅栏和高大的门。丹为她开门，她走进屋去。和岛上多数同类屋宅一样，这间起居室只有三面是墙，第四面是一大片铁丝纱网。两人穿过光洁可鉴的地板，走进里面的大厅。前门附近，一个看不出年纪的夏威夷女人从椅子上缓缓起身。她身躯高大，胸脯丰满，气度端庄，是那个正在消逝的种族中的一个典范。

===Original===
“Well, Kamaikui, I’m back,” Miss Minerva smiled.

“I make you welcome,” the woman said. She was only a servant, but she spoke with the gracious manner of a hostess.

“Same room you had when you first came over, Minerva,” Dan Winterslip announced. “Your luggage is there﻿—and a bit of mail that came in on the boat this morning. I didn’t trouble to send it up to Amos’s. We dine when you’re ready.”

“I’ll not keep you long,” she answered, and hurried up the stairs.

===Chinese===
“嘿，卡迈库伊（Kamaikui），我回来了，”米纳瓦小姐笑道。

“欢迎你回来，”那女人说。她不过是个仆人，说话却带着女主人的雍容气度。

“还是你头一回来时住的那间屋，米纳瓦，”丹·温特斯利普说道，“你的行李在那儿——还有今早随船到的一点邮件。我没劳神送去阿莫斯家。你什么时候准备好，咱们什么时候开饭。”

“不会让你久等的，”她答道，匆匆上了楼。

===Original===
Dan Winterslip strolled back to his living-room. He sat down in a rattan chair that had been made especially for him in Hong-Kong, and glanced complacently about at the many evidences of his prosperity. His butler entered, bearing a tray with cocktails.

“Two, Haku?” smiled Winterslip. “The lady is from Boston.”

“Yes-s,” hissed Haku, and retired soundlessly.

===Chinese===
丹·温特斯利普踱回起居室。他在一把香港特意为他定做的藤椅里坐下，志得意满地环顾四周诸多兴旺的气象。他的管家端着一个盛着鸡尾酒的托盘走了进来。

“两杯，哈库（Haku）？”温特斯利普微笑道，“这位女士可是波士顿来的。”

“是——”哈库嘶声应道，悄没声儿地退了出去。

===Original===
In a moment Miss Minerva came again into the room. She carried a letter in her hand, and she was laughing.

“Dan, this is too absurd,” she said.

“What is?”

===Chinese===
不一会儿，米纳瓦小姐又回到房里。她手里拿着一封信，正笑着。

“丹，这事太可笑了，”她说。

“什么事？”

===Original===
“I may have told you that they were getting worried about me at home. Because I haven’t been able to tear myself away from Honolulu, I mean. Well, they’re sending a policeman for me.”

“A policeman?” He lifted his bushy eyebrows.

“Yes, it amounts to that. It’s not being done openly, of course. Grace writes that John Quincy has six weeks’ vacation from the banking house, and has decided to make the trip out here. ‘It will give you someone to come home with, my dear,’ says Grace. Isn’t she subtle?”

===Chinese===
“我也许跟你说过，家里人开始为我担心了。我是说，因为我一直没法从檀香山脱身。这不，他们要派个警察来押我了。”

“警察？”他扬起了浓眉。

“是啊，实质上就是这么回事。当然，不会做得那么露骨。格蕾丝来信说，约翰·昆西（John Quincy）从银行得了六个星期的假，决定到这边来一趟。『亲爱的，这样你回来就有人做伴了。』格蕾丝说。她可真够含蓄的。”

===Original===
“John Quincy Winterslip? That would be Grace’s son.”

Miss Minerva nodded. “You never met him, did you, Dan? Well, you will, shortly. And he certainly won’t approve of you.”

“Why not?” Dan Winterslip bristled.

“Because he’s proper. He’s a dear boy, but oh, so proper. This journey is going to be a great cross for him. He’ll start disapproving as he passes Albany, and think of the long weary miles of disapproval he’ll have to endure after that.”

===Chinese===
“约翰·昆西·温特斯利普（John Quincy Winterslip）？那就是格蕾丝的儿子了。”

米纳瓦小姐点点头。“你没见过他，对吧，丹？嗯，你很快就会见到了。而且他八成不会赞成你。”

“为什么不？”丹·温特斯利普登时恼了。

“因为他规行矩步。他是个可爱的孩子，可是哎呀，太规行矩步了。这趟旅行对他来说将是一副沉重的十字架。火车一过奥尔巴尼（Albany）他就要开始看不惯了，再想想此后千山万水的看不惯，他都得一一忍受。”

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t know. He’s a Winterslip, isn’t he?”

“He is. But the gypsy strain missed him completely. He’s all Puritan.”

“Poor boy.” Dan Winterslip moved toward the tray on which stood the amber-colored drinks. “I suppose he’ll stop with Roger in San Francisco. Write him there and tell him I want him to make this house his home while he’s in Honolulu.”

“That’s kind of you, Dan.”

“Not at all. I like youth around me﻿—even the Puritan brand. Now that you’re going to be apprehended and taken back to civilization, you’d better have one of these cocktails.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那可未必。他不也姓温特斯利普吗？”

“姓是姓温特斯利普。可吉普赛血统一点儿也没沾上他。他是十足的清教徒。”

“可怜的孩子。”丹·温特斯利普朝摆着琥珀色酒饮的托盘走去。“我想他会在旧金山罗杰（Roger）那儿停留。你写信寄到那儿，告诉他，他在檀香山期间，我要让这座宅子成为他自己的家。”

“你真客气，丹。”

“哪儿的话。我喜欢身边有年轻人——哪怕是清教徒牌的。既然你就要被缉拿归案、押回文明社会了，最好先来上一杯这种鸡尾酒。”

===Original===
“Well,” said his guest, “I’m about to exhibit what my brother used to call true Harvard indifference.”

“What do you mean?” asked Winterslip.

“I don’t mind if I do,” twinkled Miss Minerva, lifting a cocktail glass.

Dan Winterslip beamed upon her. “You’re a good sport, Minerva,” he remarked, as he escorted her across the hall.

===Chinese===
“好吧，”他的客人说，“我这就来展示一下我兄弟从前所谓的真正的哈佛式淡漠。”

“此话怎讲？”温特斯利普问。

“那我就却之不恭了，”米纳瓦小姐目光灼灼地打趣道，端起一杯鸡尾酒。

丹·温特斯利普对她笑逐颜开。“你真有雅量，米纳瓦，”他陪她穿过大厅时说道。

===Original===
“When in Rome,” she answered, “I make it a point not to do as the Bostonians do. I fear it would prove a rather thorny path to popularity.”

“Precisely.”

“Besides, I shall be back in Boston soon. Tramping about to art exhibits and Lowell Lectures, and gradually congealing into senility.”

===Chinese===
“入乡随俗嘛，”她答道，“我的守则是，绝不照波士顿人的那一套行事。我怕照那套行事，想讨人喜欢未免是条荆棘路。”

“正是如此。”

“再说了，我不久就要回波士顿了。四处奔走看美术展览、听洛厄尔讲座，然后渐渐凝固成老糊涂。”

===Original===
But she was not in Boston now, she reflected, as she sat down at the gleaming table in the dining-room. Before her, properly iced, was a generous slice of papaya, golden yellow and inviting. Somewhere beyond the foliage outside the screens, the ocean murmured restlessly. The dinner would be perfect, she knew, the Island beef dry and stringy, perhaps, but the fruits and the salad more than atoning.

“Do you expect Barbara soon?” she inquired presently.

Dan Winterslip’s face lighted like the beach at sunrise. “Yes, Barbara has graduated. She’ll be along any day now. Nice if she and your perfect nephew should hit on the same boat.”

===Chinese===
可是此刻她并不在波士顿，她一面在餐厅晶亮的餐桌旁落座一面想。她面前冰镇得恰到好处的，是一大块木瓜，金黄诱人。纱网外的葱茏草木之外的某处，海水在不安地低语。晚餐将是完美的，她知道——岛上的牛肉或许又干又柴，可水果和沙拉足以补偿有余。

“芭芭拉快回来了吧？”过了一会儿她问道。

丹·温特斯利普的脸豁然亮了，像日出时的海滩。“是啊，芭芭拉毕业了。这几天说不上哪天就回来。她要是跟你那位十全十美的侄子碰巧搭上同一条船，那才妙呢。”

===Original===
“Nice for John Quincy, at any rate,” Miss Minerva replied. “We thought Barbara a lively charming girl when she visited us in the East.”

“She’s all of that,” he agreed proudly. His daughter was his dearest possession. “I tell you, I’ve missed her. I’ve been mighty lonesome.”

Miss Minerva gave him a shrewd look. “Yes, I’ve heard rumors,” she remarked, “about how lonesome you’ve been.”

===Chinese===
“不管怎么说，对约翰·昆西是妙事，”米纳瓦小姐答道，“她到东部做客那会儿，我们都觉得芭芭拉是个活泼迷人的姑娘。”

“她何止如此，”他自豪地表示赞同。女儿是他的心头至宝。“跟你说吧，我想她想得厉害。我一直孤寂得很。”

米纳瓦小姐精明地看了他一眼。“是啊，我听到过些风言风语，”她说，“说的正是你孤寂得很的事。”

===Original===
He flushed under his tan. “Amos, I suppose?”

“Oh, not only Amos. A great deal of talk, Dan. Really, at your age﻿—”

“What do you mean, my age? I told you we’re all young out here.” He ate in silence for a moment. “You’re a good sport﻿—I said it and I meant it. You must understand that here in the Islands a man may behave a﻿—a bit differently than he would in the Back Bay.”

===Chinese===
他古铜色的脸底下泛起了红。“是阿莫斯说的吧，我猜？”

“哦，可不光是阿莫斯。闲话多了去了，丹。说真的，都你这把年纪了——”

“我这把年纪怎么了？我跟你说过了，我们这儿人人年轻。”他默默吃了一会儿。“你是有雅量的人——我说这话是当真的。你必须明白，在这群岛上，一个男人行事可以比在后湾区（Back Bay）稍微——稍微随便那么一点儿。”

===Original===
“At that,” she smiled, “all men in the Back Bay are not to be trusted. I’m not presuming to rebuke you, Dan. But﻿—for Barbara’s sake﻿—why not select as the object of your devotion a woman you could marry?”

“I could marry this one﻿—if we’re talking about the same woman.”

“The one I refer to,” Miss Minerva replied, “is known, rather widely, as the Widow of Waikiki.”

===Chinese===
“说到这个，”她微笑道，“后湾区的男人也并非个个靠得住。我并不是要教训你，丹。可是——为了芭芭拉——何不给你倾心的对象挑一个你娶得了的女人呢？”

“这一个我就娶得了——如果咱们说的是同一位的话。”

“我说的这一位，”米纳瓦小姐答道，“人家送了她个名号，叫怀基基寡妇，叫得还挺普遍。”

===Original===
“This place is a hotbed of gossip. Arlene Compton is perfectly respectable.”

“A former chorus girl, I believe.”

“Not precisely. An actress﻿—small parts﻿—before she married Lieutenant Compton.”

“And a self-made widow.”

===Chinese===
“这地方就是谣言的温床。阿琳·康普顿（Arlene Compton）完全是正派人家。”

“从前是个合唱队歌女，我没记错的话。”

“不很确切。是个演员——演些小角色——在嫁给康普顿中尉之前。”

“还是个自造的寡妇。”

===Original===
“Just what do you mean by that?” he flared. His gray eyes glittered.

“I understand that when her husband’s aeroplane crashed on Diamond Head, it was because he preferred it that way. She had driven him to it.”

“Lies, all lies!” Dan Winterslip cried. “Pardon me, Minerva, but you mustn’t believe all you hear on the beach.” He was silent for a moment. “What would you say if I told you I proposed to marry this woman?”

===Chinese===
“这话究竟什么意思？”他发作了。灰色的眼睛闪闪发亮。

“我了解到，她丈夫的飞机在钻石头山上坠毁，是因为他自己情愿那样。是她把他逼上那条路的。”

“谎话，全是谎话！”丹·温特斯利普嚷道，“恕我直言，米纳瓦，海滩上听来的话你可不能全信。”他沉默了片刻。“要是我告诉你，我打算娶这个女人，你会怎么说？”

===Original===
“I’m afraid I’d become rather bromidic,” she answered gently, “and remind you that there’s no fool like an old fool.” He did not speak. “Forgive me, Dan. I’m your first cousin, but a distant relative for all that. It’s really none of my business. I wouldn’t care﻿—but I like you. And I’m thinking of Barbara﻿—”

He bowed his head. “I know,” he said, “Barbara. Well, there’s no need to get excited. I haven’t said anything to Arlene about marriage. Not yet.”

Miss Minerva smiled. “You know, as I get on in years,” she remarked, “so many wise old saws begin to strike me as utter nonsense. Particularly that one I just quoted.” He looked at her, his eyes friendly again. “This is the best avocado I ever tasted,” she added. “But tell me, Dan, are you sure the mango is a food? Seems more like a spring tonic to me.”

===Chinese===
“我怕自己要落俗套了，”她温和地答道，“要提醒你一句：天下傻瓜，老糊涂为最。”他没有作声。“原谅我，丹。我是你的嫡堂妹，可说到底终究隔了一层。这本不关我的事。我原不会多管——可我喜欢你。而且我惦着芭芭拉——”

他低下了头。“我知道，”他说，“芭芭拉。唉，用不着激动。我还没对阿琳提过结婚的事。还没有。”

米纳瓦小姐笑了。“你知道，人上了年纪，”她说，“那么多睿智的老话就开始让我觉得全是胡扯了。尤其是我刚引用的那一句。”他望着她，眼色又友善起来。“这是我吃过的最好的鳄梨，”她补了一句，“不过告诉我，丹，你确定芒果算得上食物吗？在我看来，它更像一味春季的补药。”

===Original===
By the time they finished dinner the topic of Arlene Compton was forgotten and Dan had completely regained his good nature. They had coffee on his veranda﻿—or, in Island parlance, lanai﻿—which opened off one end of the living-room. This was of generous size, screened on three sides and stretching far down on to the white beach. Outside the brief tropic dusk dimmed the bright colors of Waikiki.

“No breeze stirring,” said Miss Minerva.

“The trades have died,” Dan answered. He referred to the beneficent winds which﻿—save at rare, uncomfortable intervals﻿—blow across the Islands out of the cool northeast. “I’m afraid we’re in for a stretch of Kona weather.”

===Chinese===
到晚餐结束时，阿琳·康普顿的话题已被抛诸脑后，丹也完全恢复了往日的好脾气。他们在他的游廊上喝咖啡——用岛上的话讲，露台（lanai）——露台从起居室一头开门出去，十分宽敞，三面装着纱网，一直伸展到白沙滩低处。外面，短暂的热带暮色正让怀基基的明艳色彩黯淡下去。

“一丝风也没有，”米纳瓦小姐说。

“信风停了，”丹答道。他指的是那种慈惠的风——除了偶有令人不适的间歇之外——从凉爽的东北方吹遍群岛。“恐怕我们要赶上一段科纳天气（Kona weather）了。”

===Original===
“I hope not,” Miss Minerva said.

“It saps the life right out of me nowadays,” he told her, and sank into a chair. “That about being young, Minerva﻿—it’s a little bluff I’m fond of.”

She smiled gently. “Even youth finds the Kona hard to endure,” she comforted. “I remember when I was here before﻿—in the ’eighties. I was only nineteen, but the memory of the sick wind lingers still.”

===Chinese===
“但愿不会，”米纳瓦小姐说。

“如今这天气能把我的精气神整个抽干，”他对她说，颓然坐进椅子里。“年轻什么的，米纳瓦——那不过是我爱说的一句唬人的场面话。”

她温和地笑了。“连年轻人也觉得科纳风难熬呢，”她宽慰道，“我记得上一回我来这儿——十九世纪八十年代。那时我才十九岁，可那病风的记忆至今萦绕不散。”

===Original===
“I missed you then, Minerva.”

“Yes. You were off somewhere in the South Seas.”

“But I heard about you when I came back. That you were tall and blonde and lovely, and nowhere near so prim as they feared you were going to be. A wonderful figure, they said﻿—but you’ve got that yet.”

===Chinese===
“那时候我可想你了，米纳瓦。”

“可不是。你那时在南太平洋的什么地方闯荡。”

“可我回来以后听说了你的事。说你个子高高的、金发、可爱，一点也不像他们原先担心的那么古板。还说身段好极了——这一点你如今还留着呢。”

===Original===
She flushed, but smiled still. “Hush, Dan. We don’t talk that way where I come from.”

“The ’eighties,” he sighed. “Hawaii was Hawaii then. Unspoiled, a land of opera bouffe, with old Kalakaua sitting on his golden throne.”

“I remember him,” Miss Minerva said. “Grand parties at the palace. And the afternoons when he sat with his disreputable friends on the royal lanai, and the Royal Hawaiian Band played at his feet, and he haughtily tossed them royal pennies. It was such a colorful, naive spot then, Dan.”

===Chinese===
她脸红了，但仍笑着。“少来，丹。在我来的地方，人们不这么说话。”

“八十年代啊，”他叹道，“那时的夏威夷才是夏威夷。未受糟蹋，一片轻歌剧般的乐土，老卡拉卡瓦（Kalakaua）坐在他的黄金御座上。”

“我记得他，”米纳瓦小姐说，“王宫里的盛大宴会。还有那些下午，他和他那班声名狼藉的朋友坐在王家的露台上，皇家夏威夷乐队在他脚下演奏，他傲然地向他们抛去一枚枚王家的便士。那时的这里，多么绚丽，多么天真啊，丹。”

===Original===
“It’s been ruined,” he complained sadly. “Too much aping of the mainland. Too much of your damned mechanical civilization﻿—automobiles, phonographs, radios﻿—bah! And yet﻿—and yet, Minerva﻿—away down underneath there are deep dark waters flowing still.”

She nodded, and they sat for a moment busy with their memories. Presently Dan Winterslip snapped on a small reading light at his side. “I’ll just glance at the evening paper, if you don’t mind.”

“Oh, do,” urged Miss Minerva.

===Chinese===
“全给毁了，”他哀伤地抱怨道，“太过模仿美国本土。太多你们那该死的机械文明——汽车、留声机、收音机——呸！然而——然而，米纳瓦——在那底下深处，幽暗的深水依然在流淌。”

她点点头，两人坐了片刻，各自沉浸在回忆里。过了一会儿，丹·温特斯利普啪地拧亮身边一盏小小的阅读灯。“不介意的话，我翻一眼晚报。”

“请便请便，”米纳瓦小姐催促道。

===Original===
She was glad of a moment without talk. For this, after all, was the time she loved Waikiki best. So brief, this tropic dusk, so quick the coming of the soft alluring night. The carpet of the waters, apple-green by day, crimson and gold at sunset, was a deep purple now. On top of that extinct volcano called Diamond Head a yellow eye was winking, as though to hint there might still be fire beneath. Three miles down, the harbor lights began to twinkle, and out toward the reef the lanterns of Japanese sampans glowed intermittently. Beyond, in the roadstead, loomed the battered hulk of an old brig slowly moving toward the channel entrance. Always, out there, a ship or two, in from the East with a cargo of spice or tea or ivory, or eastward bound with a load of tractor salesmen. Ships of all sorts, the spic and span liner and the rakish tramp, ships from Melbourne and Seattle, New York and Yokohama, Tahiti and Rio, any port on the seven seas. For this was Honolulu, the Crossroads of the Pacific﻿—the glamourous crossroads where, they said, in time all paths crossed again. Miss Minerva sighed.

===Chinese===
她乐得有片刻无须言语。因为这，说到底，才是她最爱怀基基的时刻。热带的暮色如此短促，柔媚诱人的夜来得如此迅疾。白日里苹果绿的海面，日落时绯红金黄，此刻已是一片深紫。在那座名叫钻石头山的死火山顶上，一只黄色的眼睛眨动着，仿佛在暗示底下也许仍有火。三英里外，港口的灯火开始闪烁，朝珊瑚礁那边，日本舢板的渔灯明明灭灭。再往外，锚地里隐现着一艘饱经风霜的老双桅船的残躯，正缓缓向航道口移动。那外面总有那么一两条船：从东方载着香料、茶叶或象牙而来，或是向东去，装了一船的拖拉机推销员。形形色色的船，崭新挺括的班轮与轻捷散漫的流浪货船，来自墨尔本和西雅图、纽约和横滨、塔希提和里约，来自七大洋上任何一个港口。因为这里是檀香山，太平洋的十字路口——那传说中所有道路迟早都会重新交会的、富有魅力的十字路口。米纳瓦小姐叹了口气。

===Original===
She was conscious of a quick movement on Dan’s part. She turned and looked at him. He had laid the paper on his knee, and was staring straight ahead. That bluff about being young﻿—no good now. For his face was old, old.

“Why, Dan﻿—” she said.

“I﻿—I’m wondering, Minerva,” he began slowly. “Tell me again about that nephew of yours.”

===Chinese===
她觉察到丹急促地动了一下。她转过脸看他。他已把报纸放在膝上，直勾勾地望着前方。什么年轻的场面话——眼下全不顶用了。因为他的脸苍老了，苍老了。

“怎么了，丹——”她说。

“我——我在想，米纳瓦，”他缓缓开口道，“再跟我说说你说起的那个侄子吧。”

===Original===
She was surprised, but hid it. “John Quincy?” she said. “He’s just the usual thing, for Boston. Conventional. His whole life has been planned for him, from the cradle to the grave. So far he’s walked the line. The inevitable preparatory school, Harvard, the proper clubs, the family banking house﻿—even gone and got himself engaged to the very girl his mother would have picked for him. There have been times when I hoped he might kick over﻿—the war﻿—but no, he came back and got meekly into the old rut.”

“Then he’s reliable﻿—steady?”

Miss Minerva smiled. “Dan, compared with that boy, Gibraltar wabbles occasionally.”

“Discreet, I take it?”

“He invented discretion. That’s what I’m telling you. I love him﻿—but a little bit of recklessness now and then﻿—However, I’m afraid it’s too late now. John Quincy is nearly thirty.”

===Chinese===
她吃了一惊，但掩饰住了。“约翰·昆西？”她说，“照波士顿的标准，他就是个再寻常不过的人。因循守旧。他这一生，从摇篮到坟墓都替他规划好了。到目前为止他一直严守规矩。该上的预科学校，哈佛，该进的俱乐部，家族银行——甚至连订婚，都自己去订了母亲会替他挑的那位姑娘。有好几次我盼着他能闹出点越轨的事来——大战那阵子——可是不，他回来了，乖乖地又落回旧辙里去了。”

“那么他可靠——稳当？”

米纳瓦小姐笑了。“丹，跟那孩子一比，直布罗陀岩都要偶尔晃上两晃。”

“口风紧，我猜是？”

“谨慎就是他发明的。我说的就是这个意思。我爱他——可偶尔来那么一点点不管不顾——唉，恐怕现在已经太迟了。约翰·昆西快三十了。”

===Original===
Dan Winterslip was on his feet, his manner that of a man who had made an important decision. Beyond the bamboo curtain that hung in the door leading to the living-room a light appeared. “Haku!” Winterslip called. The Jap came swiftly.

“Haku, tell the chauffeur﻿—quick﻿—the big car! I must get to the dock before the *President Tyler* sails for San Francisco. Wikiwiki!”

===Chinese===
丹·温特斯利普站起身来，那副神气，俨然一个做出了重大决定的人。通往起居室的门上悬着的竹帘后面亮起了一盏灯。“哈库！”温特斯利普喊道。那日本仆人快步走了过来。

“哈库，告诉司机——快——开那辆大车！我得在泰勒总统号（*President Tyler*）起航去旧金山之前赶到码头。快！快！”

===Original===
The servant disappeared into the living-room, and Winterslip followed. Somewhat puzzled, Miss Minerva sat for a moment, then rose and pushed aside the curtain. “Are you sailing, Dan?” she asked.

He was seated at his desk, writing hurriedly. “No, no﻿—just a note﻿—I must get it off on that boat﻿—”

There was an air of suppressed excitement about him. Miss Minerva stepped over the threshold into the living-room. In another moment Haku appeared with an announcement that was unnecessary, for the engine of an automobile was humming in the drive. Dan Winterslip took his hat from the Jap. “Make yourself at home, Minerva﻿—I’ll be back shortly,” he cried, and rushed out.

===Chinese===
仆人消失在起居室里，温特斯利普跟了进去。米纳瓦小姐有些纳闷，坐了片刻，随即起身掀开竹帘。“你是要坐船走吗，丹？”她问。

他已坐在书桌前，匆匆写着什么。“不是，不是——一张字条——我必须让它随那条船带走——”

他周身透着一股强自按捺的兴奋。米纳瓦小姐跨过门槛，走进起居室。不多时哈库出来禀报，其实多此一举，因为一辆汽车的引擎已在车道上嗡嗡作响。丹·温特斯利普从那日本仆人手里接过帽子。“就当在自己家一样，米纳瓦——我去去就回，”他喊了一声，冲了出去。

===Original===
Some business matter, no doubt. Miss Minerva strolled aimlessly about the big airy room, pausing finally before the portrait of Jedediah Winterslip, the father of Dan and Amos, and her uncle. Dan had had it painted from a photograph after the old man’s death; it was the work of an artist whose forte was reputed to be landscapes﻿—oh, it must assuredly have been landscapes, Miss Minerva thought. But even so there was no mistaking the power and personality of this New Englander who had set up in Honolulu as a whaler. The only time she had seen him, in the ’eighties, he had been broken and old, mourning his lost fortune, which had gone with his ships in an Arctic disaster a short time before.

Well, Dan had brought the family back, Miss Minerva reflected. Won again that lost fortune and much more. There were queer rumors about his methods﻿—but so there were about the methods of Bostonians who had never strayed from home. A charming fellow, whatever his past. Miss Minerva sat down at the grand piano and played a few old familiar bars﻿—“The Beautiful Blue Danube.” Her thoughts went back to the ’eighties.

===Chinese===
多半是生意上的事。米纳瓦小姐在这间宽敞透气的大屋子里信步走着，最后停在杰迪代亚·温特斯利普（Jedediah Winterslip）的画像前——他是丹和阿莫斯的父亲，也是她的伯父。老人去世后，丹照着一张照片请人画了这幅像；作画的据说是一位以风景画见长的画家——哦，想必必定是风景画，米纳瓦小姐心想。可即便如此，这位当年在檀香山落脚经营捕鲸业的新英格兰人的魄力与个性，依然错认不了。她唯一一次见到他本人是在十九世纪八十年代，那时他已颓唐衰老，正哀悼自己失去的家业——不久前，那笔家业随他的船队一起毁于一场北极灾难。

唉，是丹把家业重新挣回来的，米纳瓦小姐暗自思量。把那笔失去的家业又赢了回来，还远不止那些。关于他的手段有些古怪的传闻——可对于那些从未离开过家门的波士顿人，关于他们手段的传闻也同样古怪。不管过去如何，他是个有魅力的人。米纳瓦小姐在大钢琴前坐下，弹了几小节熟悉的老曲子——《美丽的蓝色多瑙河》。她的思绪回到了八十年代。

===Original===
Dan Winterslip was thinking of the ’eighties too as his car sped townward along Kalakaua Avenue. But it was the present that concerned him when they reached the dock and he ran, panting a little, through a dim pier shed toward the gangplank of the *President Tyler*. He had no time to spare, the ship was on the point of sailing. Since it was a through boat from the Orient it left without the ceremonies that attend the departure of a liner plying only between Honolulu and the mainland. Even so, there were cries of “Aloha,” some hearty and some tremulous, most of the travelers were bedecked with leis, and a confused little crowd milled about the foot of the plank.

===Chinese===
汽车沿着卡拉卡瓦大道（Kalakaua Avenue）朝城里疾驰，丹·温特斯利普想的也是八十年代。可等车到了码头，让他操心的就是眼下了：他微微喘着气，跑过一间昏暗的码头棚屋，直奔泰勒总统号的跳板。他一分钟也耽搁不得，船眼看就要起航。这是条从远东直航来的船，不在檀香山与美国本土之间专营，离港时便没有班轮启碇的那套仪程。饶是如此，码头上仍响着“阿罗哈”（Aloha）的呼喊，有的情真意切，有的颤颤巍巍，旅客们大多披挂着花环（leis），一小群人乱哄哄地挤在跳板口打转。

===Original===
Dan Winterslip pushed his way forward and ran up the sharp incline. As he reached the deck he encountered an old acquaintance, Hepworth, the second officer.

“You’re the man I’m looking for,” he cried.

“How are you, sir,” Hepworth said. “I didn’t see your name on the list.”

“No, I’m not sailing. I’m here to ask a favor.”

“Glad to oblige, Mr. Winterslip.”

===Chinese===
丹·温特斯利普挤上前去，跑上陡峭的跳板。他一上甲板，就碰见一位旧相识——二副赫普沃思（Hepworth）。

“我正要找你，”他喊道。

“您好，先生，”赫普沃思说，“乘客名单上没见着您的大名。”

“是啊，我不是坐船。我是来请你帮个忙的。”

“乐意效劳，温特斯利普先生。”

===Original===
Winterslip thrust a letter into his hand. “You know my cousin Roger in ’Frisco. Please give him that﻿—him and no one else﻿—as soon after you land as you possibly can. I’m too late for the mail﻿—and I prefer this way anyhow. I’ll be mighty grateful.”

“Don’t mention it﻿—you’ve been very kind to me and I’ll be only too happy﻿—I’m afraid you’ll have to go ashore, sir. Just a minute, there﻿—” He took Winterslip’s arm and gently urged him back on to the plank. The instant Dan’s feet touched the dock, the plank was drawn up behind him.

===Chinese===
温特斯利普把一封信塞进他手里。“你认识我在旧金山的堂弟罗杰。请把这个交给他——只能交给他，不能给任何别人——你一上岸就尽快送去。邮班我是赶不上了——再说，这条路子我本来就更为中意。我会非常感激的。”

“哪儿的话——您一直待我很好，我高兴还来不及——恐怕您得上岸了，先生。稍等，那边——”他挽住温特斯利普的手臂，轻轻把他劝回跳板。丹的双脚一沾码头，跳板便在他身后被抽了起来。

===Original===
For a moment he stood, held by the fascination an Islander always feels at sight of a ship outward bound. Then he turned and walked slowly through the pier-shed. Ahead of him he caught a glimpse of a slender lithe figure which he recognized at once as that of Dick Kaohla, the grandson of Kamaikui. He quickened his pace and joined the boy.

“Hello, Dick,” he said.

“Hello.” The brown face was sullen, unfriendly.

===Chinese===
他伫立片刻，被那种岛民望见出港船只时总会涌起的心驰神往攫住了。随后他转过身，慢慢穿过码头棚屋。前方，他瞥见一个苗条轻捷的身影，一眼便认出那是迪克·考赫拉（Dick Kaohla），卡迈库伊的孙子。他加快脚步，赶上了那少年。

“你好啊，迪克，”他说。

“你好。”那张棕色的脸阴沉着，不友好。

===Original===
“You haven’t been to see me for a long time,” Dan Winterslip said. “Everything all right?”

“Sure,” replied Kaohla. “Sure it’s all right.” They reached the street, and the boy turned quickly away, “Good night,” he muttered.

Dan Winterslip stood for a moment, thoughtfully looking after him. Then he got into the car. “No hurry now,” he remarked to the chauffeur.

===Chinese===
“你好久没来看我了，”丹·温特斯利普说，“一切都好吗？”

“好着呢，”考赫拉答道，“当然一切都好。”两人走到街上，少年倏地扭头就走。“晚安。”他咕哝了一句。

丹·温特斯利普站了一会儿，若有所思地望着他的背影。然后他上了车。“现在不用赶了，”他对司机说。

===Original===
When he reappeared in his living-room, Miss Minerva glanced up from the book she was reading. “Were you in time, Dan?” she asked.

“Just made it,” he told her.

“Good,” she said, rising. “I’ll take my book and go upstairs. Pleasant dreams.”

===Chinese===
他再回到起居室时，米纳瓦小姐从正读的书上抬起眼。“赶上了吗，丹？”她问。

“刚好赶上，”他告诉她。

“那就好，”她说着站起身，“我拿书上楼去了。祝你做好梦。”

===Original===
He waited until she reached the door before he spoke. “Ah﻿—Minerva﻿—don’t trouble to write your nephew about stopping here.”

“No, Dan?” she said, puzzled again.

“No. I’ve attended to the invitation myself. Good night.”

“Oh﻿—good night,” she answered, and left him.

===Chinese===
他等她走到门口才开口。“呃——米纳瓦——就不用劳神写信让你侄子住这儿了。”

“不写了？丹。”她说，又是一脸不解。

“不写了。邀请的事我自己办妥了。晚安。”

“哦——晚安，”她答道，离他而去。

===Original===
Alone in the great room, he paced restlessly back and forth over the polished floor. In a moment he went out on to the lanai, and found the newspaper he had been reading earlier in the evening. He brought it back to the living-room and tried to finish it, but something seemed to trouble him. His eyes kept straying﻿—straying﻿—with a sharp exclamation he tore one corner from the shipping page, savagely ripped the fragment to bits.

Again he got up and wandered about. He had intended paying a call down the beach, but that quiet presence in the room above﻿—Boston in its more tolerant guise but Boston still﻿—gave him pause.

===Chinese===
独自待在大屋子里，他在光洁的地板上不安地来回踱步。过了一会儿，他走到露台上，找到了傍晚早些时候读过的那张报纸。他把报纸拿回起居室，想把它读完，可似乎有什么搅得他心神不宁。他的目光不由自主地老是飘走——飘走——他猛地一声低喝，从航运版上撕下一角，狠狠地把那片纸撕了个粉碎。

他又站起来，四处转悠。他本打算沿海滩到下面去拜访一个人，可楼上房间那位安静的在场者——虽是比较宽容的面目、骨子里依然是波士顿——让他踌躇了。

===Original===
He returned to the lanai. There, under a mosquito netting, was the cot where he preferred to sleep; his dressing-room was near at hand. However, it was too early for bed. He stepped through the door on to the beach. Unmistakable, the soft treacherous breath of the Kona fanned his cheek﻿—the “sick wind” that would pile the breakers high along the coast and blight temporarily this Island paradise. There was no moon, the stars that usually seemed so friendly and so close were now obscured. The black water rolled in like a threat. He stood staring out into the dark﻿—out there to the crossroads where paths always crossed again. If you gave them time﻿—if you only gave them time﻿—

===Chinese===
他回到露台。那儿蚊帐底下摆着他更爱睡的帆布床；他的更衣室就在近旁。不过，此刻就寝还太早。他迈出门，走到海滩上。错不了的，科纳风那阴柔而不祥的气息扇着他的脸颊——那“病风”将在沿岸堆起高高的碎浪，一时摧残这座岛屿乐园。天上没有月亮，平日显得那么亲切、那么近人的星星，此刻都隐没了。黑沉沉的海水像威胁一般滚滚涌来。他伫立着，凝望黑暗深处——望向那座道路总会重新交会的十字路口。只要你给它们时间——只要你给它们时间——

===Original===
As he turned back, his eyes went to the algaroba tree beyond the wire, and he saw the yellow flare of a match. His brother Amos. He had a sudden friendly feeling for Amos, he wanted to go over and talk to him, talk of the far days when they played together on this beach. No use, he knew. He sighed, and the screen door of the lanai banged behind him﻿—the screen door without a lock in a land where locks are few.

Tired, he sat in the dark to think. His face was turned toward the curtain of bamboo between him and the living-room. On that curtain a shadow appeared, was motionless a second, then vanished. He caught his breath﻿—again the shadow. “Who’s there?” he called.

===Chinese===
转身往回走时，他的目光落向铁丝网外的那棵牧豆树，他看见一根火柴黄色的光焰。是他弟弟阿莫斯。他对阿莫斯忽生一股亲善之情，想过去同他说说话，聊聊当年他们一起在这片海滩上玩耍的遥远岁月。没有用的，他知道。他叹了口气，露台的纱门在他身后砰地撞上——在这个少有人用锁的地方，一扇不带锁的纱门。

累了，他坐在黑暗里想心事。他的脸朝着隔在他与起居室之间的那道竹帘。竹帘上映出一个影子，静止了一秒，随即消失。他倒吸一口气——影子又来了。“谁在那儿？”他喊道。

===Original===
A huge brown arm was thrust through the bamboo. A friendly brown face was framed there.

“Your fruit I put on the table,” said Kamaikui. “I go bed now.”

“Of course. Go ahead. Good night.”

===Chinese===
一条棕色的大手臂从竹帘间探了进来。一张和善的棕色脸庞嵌在帘子中间。

“你的水果，我放在桌上了，”卡迈库伊说，“我睡觉去了。”

“好。去吧。晚安。”

===Original===
The woman withdrew. Dan Winterslip was furious with himself. What was the matter with him, anyhow? He who had fought his way through unspeakable terrors in the early days﻿—nervous﻿—on edge﻿—

“Getting old,” he muttered. “No, by heaven﻿—it’s the Kona. That’s it. The Kona. I’ll be all right when the trades blow again.”

When the trades blew again! He wondered. Here at the crossroads one could not be sure.

===Chinese===
女人退了出去。丹·温特斯利普对自己恼火极了。他到底是怎么了？想当年，他闯过多少难以言说的凶险——如今竟神经紧张——风声鹤唳——

“老了，”他喃喃道，“不，老天在上——是科纳风。就是这么回事。科纳风。等信风再吹起来，我就没事了。”

等信风再吹起来！他心里犯疑。在这个十字路口，谁也说不准。

## II The High Hat / 二 高顶帽

===Original===
John Quincy Winterslip walked aboard the ferry at Oakland feeling rather limp and weary. For more than six days he had been marooned on sleepers—his pause at Chicago had been but a flitting from one train to another—and he was fed up. Seeing America first—that was what he had been doing. And what an appalling lot of it there was! He felt that for an eternity he had been staring at endless plains, dotted here and there by unesthetic houses the inmates of which had unquestionably never heard a symphony concert.

Ahead of him ambled a porter, bearing his two suitcases, his golf clubs and his hatbox. One of the man’s hands was gone—chewed off, no doubt, in some amiable frontier scuffle. In its place he wore a steel hook. Well, no one could question the value of a steel hook to a man in the porter’s profession. But how quaint—and western!

The boy indicated a spot by the rail on the forward deck, and the porter began to unload. Carefully selecting the man’s good hand, John Quincy dropped into it a tip so generous as to result in a touching of hook to cap in a weird salute. The object of this attention sank down amid his elaborate trappings, removed the straw hat from his perspiring head, and tried to figure out just what had happened to him.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西·温特斯利普（John Quincy Winterslip）在奥克兰登上渡轮时，只觉得浑身疲软乏力。一连六天多，他都被困在卧铺车厢里——在芝加哥那一停，也不过是从一趟车匆匆跳上另一趟车——他实在受够了。「先看美国」——他这些日子干的就是这个。可美国也实在多得吓人！他觉得自己仿佛已生生世世凝望着无边无际的大平原，原野上东一幢西一幢地点缀着毫不见美的房子，住在里头的人无疑从未听过一场交响音乐会。

前头一个搬运工慢悠悠地走着，替他扛着两口衣箱、高尔夫球杆和帽盒。那人的一只手没了——十有八九是在边疆某场和和气气的斗殴里被咬掉的。原处装着一只钢钩。唔，对干搬运工这一行的人来说，钢钩有多要紧，谁也不能不服气。可这是多么古雅——多么西部风啊！

小伙子指了指前甲板栏杆旁的一处地方，搬运工便动手卸行李。约翰·昆西小心挑了那人还完好的那只手，把一笔可观的小费放进掌心，结果那只钢钩以一种古怪的敬礼姿势碰了碰帽檐。受此厚遇的主角在他那一大堆繁复的行头中间坐了下来，从汗涔涔的脑袋上摘下草帽，努力想弄明白自己究竟遇上了什么事。

===Original===
Three thousand miles from Beacon Street, and two thousand miles still to go! Why, he inquired sourly of his usually pleasant self, had he ever agreed to make this absurd expedition into heathen country? Here it was late June, Boston was at its best. Tennis at Longwood, long mild evenings in a single shell on the Charles, weekends and golf with Agatha Parker at Magnolia. And if one must travel, there was Paris. He hadn’t seen Paris in two years and had been rather planning a quick run over, when his mother had put this preposterous notion into his head.

Preposterous—it was all of that. Traveling five thousand miles just as a gentle hint to Aunt Minerva to return to her calm, well-ordered life behind purple windowpanes on Beacon Street. And was there any chance that his strong-minded relative would take the hint? Not one in a thousand. Aunt Minerva was accustomed to do as she pleased—he had an uncomfortable, shocked recollection of one occasion when she had said she would do as she damn well pleased.

John Quincy wished he was back. He wished he was crossing Boston Common to his office on State Street, there to put out a new issue of bonds. He was not yet a member of the firm—that was an honor accorded only to Winterslips who were bald and a little stooped—but his heart was in his work. He put out a bond issue with loving apprehension, waiting for the verdict as a playwright waits behind the scenes on a first night. Would those First Mortgage Sixes go over big, or would they flop at his feet?

===Chinese===
离灯塔街（Beacon Street）已经三千英里，前头还有两千英里的路！他没好气地问他那个平日温和的自己：当初怎么会答应这趟闯进异教徒之地的荒唐远行？眼下是六月下旬，波士顿正是最好的时光。朗伍德（Longwood）的网球，查尔斯河上单人赛艇里一个又一个悠长温柔的黄昏，还有在玛格诺利亚（Magnolia）同阿加莎·帕克（Agatha Parker）共度的高尔夫周末。倘若人当真要旅行，还有巴黎呢。他两年没见巴黎了，本来正盘算着飞快地跑一趟，他母亲却把这么个荒唐念头塞进了他的脑子。

荒唐透顶——可不正是。足足跑上五千英里，只为委婉示意米纳瓦姑婆：请回到灯塔街紫色窗玻璃后面那种平静而有条理的生活里去吧。可这位意志坚定的亲戚领会这层暗示的机会有没有？千中无一。米纳瓦姑婆向来是想怎样就怎样——他有个至今叫他既震惊又不自在的记忆：有一回她竟说，她高兴怎么做就他妈的怎么做。

约翰·昆西恨不得已经回去了。他恨不得正穿过波士顿公园（Boston Common）走向州街（State Street）上的办公室，去发行新的一期债券。他还不是公司的合伙人——那份荣誉只留给秃了顶、有点驼背的温特斯利普家的人——但他把心放在工作上。他发行一期债券，怀着爱意也怀着忐忑，像剧作家首演之夜守在幕后等待评判。那批第一抵押六厘债券会一鸣惊人呢，还是栽倒在他脚边一败涂地？

===Original===
The hoarse boom of a ferry whistle recalled John Quincy to his present unbelievable location on the map. The boat began to move. He was dimly conscious of a young person of feminine gender who came and sat at his side. Away from the slip and out into the harbor the ferry carried John Quincy, and he suddenly sat up and took notice, for he was never blind to beauty, no matter where he encountered it.

And he was encountering beauty now. The morning air was keen and dry and bright. Spread out before him was that harbor which is like a tired navigator’s dream come true. They passed Goat Island, and he heard the faint echo of a bugle, he saw Tamalpais lifting its proud head toward the sparkling sky, he turned, and there was San Francisco scattered blithely over its many hills.

The ferry plowed on, and John Quincy sat very still. A forest of masts and steam funnels—here was the water front that had supplied the atmosphere for those romantic tales that held him spellbound when he was a boy at school—a quiet young Winterslip whom the gypsy strain had missed. Now he could distinguish a bark from Antwerp, a great liner from the Orient, a five-masted schooner that was reminiscent of those supposedly forgotten stories. Ships from the Treaty Ports, ships from coconut islands in southern seas. A picture as intriguing and colorful as a back drop in a theater—but far more real.

===Chinese===
渡轮汽笛沙哑的一声轰鸣，把约翰·昆西唤回到他此刻在地图上这处难以置信的位置。船开动了。他隐约觉察到一个年轻女子走来坐在他身边。渡轮载着约翰·昆西驶离泊位、进了港湾，他忽然坐直身子留起神来——因为无论在哪儿遇见美，他从不曾视而不见。

此刻他遇见的正是美。清晨的空气凛冽、干燥而明亮。在他眼前铺展开的，是那座像疲惫的航海者美梦成真的港湾。船过山羊岛（Goat Island），他听见军号隐约的回声；他望见塔马尔帕斯山（Tamalpais）向着闪闪发亮的天空昂起骄傲的头；他一转身，旧金山正欢欢喜喜地撒落在它重重叠叠的山丘上。

渡轮破浪前行，约翰·昆西端坐不动。桅杆和蒸汽烟囱的森林——这就是那道滨水风光，当年在学校里，正是那些以此为背景的浪漫故事让他听得神魂颠倒——他这个安静的温特斯利普家少年，偏偏没沾上家里那吉普赛血统的边。如今他分得出一艘来自安特卫普的三桅帆船、一艘来自东方的大邮轮，还有一艘五桅帆船，教人想起那些据说早已忘却的故事。来自通商口岸的船，来自南洋椰子岛的船。一幅像剧院画幕一样引人入胜、色彩斑斓的图景——只是远比画幕真实。

===Original===
Suddenly John Quincy stood up. A puzzled look had come into his calm gray eyes. “I—I don’t understand,” he murmured.

He was startled by the sound of his own voice. He hadn’t intended to speak aloud. In order not to appear too utterly silly, he looked around for someone to whom he might pretend he had addressed that remark. There was no one about—except the young person who was obviously feminine and therefore not to be informally accosted.

John Quincy looked down at her. Spanish or something like that, blue-black hair, dark eyes that were alight now with the amusement she was striving to hide, a delicate oval face tanned a deep brown. He looked again at the harbor—beauty all about the boat, and beauty on it. Much better than traveling on trains!

The girl looked up at John Quincy. She saw a big, broad-shouldered young man with a face as innocent as a child’s. A bit of friendliness, she decided instantly, would not be misunderstood.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西忽然站起来。他平静的灰眼睛里浮起困惑的神色。「我——我不明白，」他喃喃道。

他被自己的声音吓了一跳。他本没打算说出声来。为了不显得太傻，他四下里张望，想找个人好假装那句话是对他说的。周围一个人也没有——只有那位显然身为女性的年轻人，因此不便随随便便搭讪。

约翰·昆西低头看她。西班牙人，或者相仿的血统，蓝黑的头发，深色的眼睛此刻亮晶晶的，闪着她极力想藏住的笑意，一张纤秀的鹅蛋脸晒成深深的棕色。他又望了望港湾——船四面是美，船上也是美。比坐火车强多了！

姑娘抬头看约翰·昆西。她看到的是个高大宽肩的青年，一张脸天真得像孩子。她当即断定，表示一点友好不会被人误会。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon,” she said.

“Oh—I—I’m sorry,” he stammered. “I didn’t mean—I spoke without intending—I said I didn’t understand—”

“You didn’t understand what?”

“A most amazing thing has happened,” he continued. He sat down, and waved his hand toward the harbor. “I’ve been here before.”

===Chinese===
「打扰一下，」她说。

「哦——我——我很抱歉，」他结结巴巴地说。「我不是有意——我没想开口——我刚才说我不明白——」

「你不明白什么？」

「出了一件顶顶稀奇的事，」他接着说。他坐下来，朝港湾挥了挥手。「我以前来过这儿。」

===Original===
She looked perplexed. “Lots of people have,” she admitted.

“But—you see—I mean—I’ve never been here before.”

She moved away from him. “Lots of people haven’t.” She admitted that, too.

===Chinese===
她露出困惑。「来过的人多着呢，」她承认道。

「可是——您瞧——我是说——我从来没来过这儿。」

她从他身边挪开些。「没来过的人也多着呢。」这句她也认了。

===Original===
John Quincy took a deep breath. What was this discussion he had got into, anyhow? He had a quick impulse to lift his hat gallantly and walk away, letting the whole matter drop. But no, he came of a race that sees things through.

“I’m from Boston,” he said.

“Oh,” said the girl. That explained everything.

“And what I’m trying to make clear—although of course there’s no reason why I should have dragged you into it—”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西深深吸了口气。他这算陷进了一场什么谈话？他一时冲动，想潇洒地抬抬帽子走开去，把这一整桩事丢下不管。可是不，他出身于一个凡事必办到底的种族。

「我是波士顿人，」他说。

「哦，」姑娘说。这一句就把什么都说明白了。

「我想讲明白的是——当然，我原没有什么理由把您扯进来——」

===Original===
“None whatever,” she smiled. “But go on.”

“Until a few days ago I was never west of New York, never in my whole life, you understand. Been about New England a bit, and abroad a few times, but the West—”

“I know. It didn’t interest you.”

“I wouldn’t say that,” protested John Quincy with careful politeness. “But there was such a lot of it—exploring it seemed a hopeless undertaking. And then—the family thought I ought to go, you see—so I rode and rode on trains and was—you’ll pardon me—a bit bored. Now—I come into this harbor, I look around me, and I get the oddest feeling. I feel that I’ve been here before.”

===Chinese===
「一点儿也没有，」她微笑道。「不过请往下说。」

「几天以前，我还从来没到过纽约以西，一辈子都没有，您明白。新英格兰一带走走，国外也去过几回，可西部——」

「我知道。西部引不起您的兴趣。」

「那倒不见得，」约翰·昆西抗议道，口气客气而讲究。「只是它实在太多了——要把它看遍，似乎是一桩毫无指望的事。后来——家里人觉得我该来一趟，您知道——于是我就坐了又坐火车，坐得——请您原谅——有点烦了。这会儿——我一进了这港湾，朝四下一望，就生出一种顶顶古怪的感觉。我觉得我从前到过这儿。」

===Original===
The girl’s face was sympathetic. “Other people have had that experience,” she told him. “Choice souls, they are. You’ve been a long time coming, but you’re home at last.” She held out a slim brown hand. “Welcome to your city,” she said.

John Quincy solemnly shook hands. “Oh, no,” he corrected gently. “Boston’s my city. I belong there, naturally. But this—this is familiar.” He glanced northward at the low hills sheltering the Valley of the Moon, then back at San Francisco. “Yes, I seem to have known my way about here once. Astonishing, isn’t it?”

“Perhaps—some of your ancestors—”

“That’s true. My grandfather came out here when he was a young man. He went home again—but his brothers stayed. It’s the son of one of them I’m going to visit in Honolulu.”

“Oh—you’re going on to Honolulu?”

===Chinese===
姑娘的脸上满是同情。「别人也有过这样的体验，」她对他说。「都是挑出来的灵魂。您走了好久才来，可总算到家了。」她伸出一只纤细的棕色手。「欢迎来到您的城市，」她说。

约翰·昆西郑重地同她握手。「哦，不，」他温和地纠正道。「波士顿才是我的城市。我自然属于那边。可是这儿——这儿是熟稔的。」他朝北瞥了一眼环抱着月亮谷（Valley of the Moon）的低冈，又回头望旧金山。「是啊，我从前似乎认得这一带的路。令人吃惊，是不是？」

「也许——是您的哪位祖上——」

「这倒是真的。我祖父年轻时到过这边。后来他回老家去了——可他的几个兄弟留了下来。我要去檀香山拜访的，就是他们其中一个的儿子。」

「哦——您还要去檀香山？」

===Original===
“Tomorrow morning. Have you ever been there?”

“Ye—es.” Her dark eyes were serious. “See—there are the docks—that’s where the East begins. The real East. And Telegraph Hill—” she pointed; no one in Boston ever points, but she was so lovely John Quincy overlooked it—“and Russian Hill, and the Fairmont on Nob Hill.”

“Life must be full of ups and downs,” he ventured lightly. “Tell me about Honolulu. Sort of a wild place, I imagine?”

She laughed. “I’ll let you discover for yourself how wild it is,” she told him. “Practically all the leading families came originally from your beloved New England. ‘Puritans with a touch of sun,’ my father calls them. He’s clever, my father,” she added, in an odd childish tone that was wistful and at the same time challenging.

===Chinese===
「明天早晨。您去过那儿吗？」

「去——去过。」她深色的眼睛变得郑重。「瞧——那是船坞——东方从那儿开始。真正的东方。还有电报山（Telegraph Hill）——」她抬手指了指；波士顿人从来不拿手指点，可她那么可爱，约翰·昆西也就不计较了——「还有俄罗斯山（Russian Hill），还有诺布山（Nob Hill）上的费尔蒙旅馆（Fairmont）。」

「在这儿过日子，想必充满了上上下下，」他轻轻打趣道。「跟我讲讲檀香山吧。照我想，是个挺蛮荒的地方？」

她笑了。「它有多蛮荒，我让您自己去发现，」她对他说。「这儿上等人家几乎全都是从您心爱的新英格兰来的。『带一点阳光的清教徒』，我父亲这么叫他们。我父亲很聪明。」她补了一句，声调古怪而孩子气，既是渴望，又是挑衅。

===Original===
“I’m sure of it,” said John Quincy heartily. They were approaching the Ferry Building and other passengers crowded about them. “I’d help you with that suitcase of yours, but I’ve got all this truck. If we could find a porter—”

“Don’t bother,” she answered. “I can manage very well.” She was staring down at John Quincy’s hat box. “I—I suppose there’s a silk hat in there?” she inquired.

“Naturally,” replied John Quincy.

She laughed—a rich, deep-throated laugh. John Quincy stiffened slightly. “Oh, forgive me,” she cried. “But—a silk hat in Hawaii!”

===Chinese===
「这我信，」约翰·昆西热忱地说。船快靠渡轮大厦（Ferry Building）了，别的乘客往他们身边拥。「我倒想帮您提那只箱子，可我这一大堆劳什子。要是能找个搬运工——」

「不用麻烦，」她答道。「我自己照管得过来。」她正低头盯着约翰·昆西的帽盒。「我——我想这里面是一顶高顶礼帽吧？」她问道。

「那还用说，」约翰·昆西答道。

她笑了——一声低沉而饱满的笑。约翰·昆西微微僵住。「哦，原谅我，」她叫道。「可是——高顶礼帽，在夏威夷！」

===Original===
John Quincy stood erect. The girl had laughed at a Winterslip. He filled his lungs with the air sweeping in from the open spaces, the broad open spaces where men are men. A weird reckless feeling came over him. He stooped, picked up the hat box, and tossed it calmly over the rail. It bobbed indignantly away. The crowd closed in, not wishing to miss any further exhibition of madness.

“That’s that,” said John Quincy quietly.

“Oh,” gasped the girl, “you shouldn’t have done it.”

And indeed, he shouldn’t. The box was an expensive one, the gift of his admiring mother at Christmas. And the topper inside, worn in the gloaming along the water side of Beacon Street, had been known to add a touch of distinction even to that distinguished scene.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西站得笔直。这姑娘竟笑话起一个温特斯利普家的人。他深深吸满了一肺从开阔地扫荡而来的风，从男儿汉自是男儿汉的辽阔开阔地扫荡而来的风。一种古怪的、豁出去的感觉攫住了他。他弯下腰，拾起帽盒，不动声色地把它抛过了栏杆。帽盒气鼓鼓地一起一伏漂远了。人群围拢上来，谁也不愿错过这场疯癫表演的下一幕。

「这就结了，」约翰·昆西平静地说。

「哦，」姑娘倒抽一口气，「您不该这样的。」

的确不该。那帽盒价钱不便宜，是他那崇拜儿子的母亲圣诞节的礼物。盒里那顶高顶礼帽，黄昏时分戴着沿灯塔街水滨一带走走，据说即便在那等有气派的景致里也添得几分与众不同的风采。

===Original===
“Why not?” asked John Quincy. “The confounded thing’s been a nuisance ever since I left home. And besides—we do look ridiculous at times, don’t we? We easterners? A silk hat in the tropics! I might have been mistaken for a missionary.” He began to gather up his luggage. “Shan’t need a porter any more,” he announced gaily. “I say—it was awfully kind of you—letting me talk to you like that.”

“It was fun,” she told him. “I hope you’re going to like us out here. We’re so eager to be liked, you know. It’s almost pathetic.”

“Well,” smiled John Quincy, “I’ve met only one Californian to date. But—”

“Yes?”

===Chinese===
「为什么不？」约翰·昆西反问。「这该死的东西从我离家起就是个累赘。再说——我们确实有时候显得可笑，不是吗？我们东部人？高顶礼帽戴到热带来！人家没准儿把我错当成传教士了。」他动手收拾行李。「往后用不着搬运工了，」他欢欢喜喜地宣布。「我说——您真好——肯让我这样跟您说话。」

「挺有趣，」她对他说。「我希望您往后会喜欢我们这边的人。您不知道，我们多么巴望讨人喜欢。简直都有点可怜了。」

「这个嘛，」约翰·昆西微笑道，「到如今我只见过一个加利福尼亚人。不过——」

「不过怎么样？」

===Original===
“So far, so good!”

“Oh, thank you.” She moved away.

“Please—just a moment,” called John Quincy. “I hope—I mean, I wish—”

But the crowd surged between them. He saw her dark eyes smiling at him and then, irrevocably as the hat, she drifted from his sight.

===Chinese===
「到目前为止，好得很！」

「哦，谢谢您。」她走开了。

「请——稍等一下，」约翰·昆西喊道。「我希望——我是说，我但愿——」

可是人流涌来，隔开了他们俩。他看见她那双深色的眼睛还朝他微笑，随后，就像那顶礼帽一样无可挽回，她漂出了他的视线。

## III Midnight on Russian Hill / 三 俄罗斯山上的午夜

===Original===
A few moments later John Quincy stepped ashore in San Francisco. He had taken not more than three steps across the floor of the Ferry Building when a dapper Japanese chauffeur pushed through the crowd and singling out the easterner with what seemed uncanny perspicacity, took complete charge of him.

Roger Winterslip, the Jap announced, was too busy to meet ferries, but had sent word that the boy was to go up to the house and after establishing himself comfortably there, join his host for lunch downtown. Gratified to feel solid ground once more beneath his feet, John Quincy followed the chauffeur to the street. San Francisco glittered under the morning sun.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，约翰·昆西在旧金山登了岸。他穿过轮渡大厦还没迈出三步，一个精干利落的日本司机便从人群中挤上前来，凭着一种近乎神奇的眼力，一眼认定这位东部来客，随即将他全盘接管。

那日本人转告道，罗杰·温特斯利普（Roger Winterslip）公务繁忙，无暇到码头迎接，但已留下话：请少爷先上宅邸安顿妥当，再进城与主人共进午餐。脚下重新踩到坚实的地面，约翰·昆西心满意足，跟着司机走到街上。旧金山在晨光中熠熠生辉。

===Original===
“I always thought this was a foggy town,” John Quincy said.

The Jap grinned. “Maybe fog come, maybe it do not. Just now one time maybe it do not. Please.” He held open the car door.

===Chinese===
“我一直以为这座城老是雾腾腾的呢。”约翰·昆西说。

那日本人咧嘴一笑：“雾嘛，也许来，也许不来。眼下这一回，多半不来。请。”他拉开车门，一直扶着。

===Original===
Through bright streets where life appeared to flow with a pleasant rhythm, they bowled along. Beside the curbs stood the colorful carts of the flower venders, unnecessarily painting the lily of existence. Weary traveler though he was, John Quincy took in with every breath a fresh supply of energy. New ambitions stirred within him, bigger, better bond issues than ever before seemed ridiculously easy of attainment.

===Chinese===
汽车一路轻快地驶过明亮的街道，那里的生活仿佛踏着一种惬意的节拍流淌。路边停着卖花小贩五颜六色的推车，给本已绚烂的人生百合徒然添彩。约翰·昆西虽是旅途劳顿，却每呼吸一口，便吸入一股新的活力。新的雄心在他胸中萌动——比以往更大、更漂亮的债券生意，似乎都轻而易举得可笑。

===Original===
Roger Winterslip had not been among those lured to suburban life down the peninsula; he resided in bachelor solitude on Nob Hill. It was an ancient, battered house viewed from without, but within, John Quincy found, were all known comforts. A bent old Chinaman showed him his room and his heart leaped up when he beheld, at last, a veritable bath.

===Chinese===
罗杰·温特斯利普没有随大流搬到半岛上去过郊区生活，而是单身独居于诺布山。那宅子从外面看古老而破旧，但约翰·昆西发现，屋里样样舒适俱全。一个佝偻的华人老者领他到房间，当终于望见一只真正的浴缸时，他的心欢跳起来。

===Original===
At one o’clock he sought out the office where his relative carried on, with conspicuous success, his business as an engineer and builder. Roger proved a short florid man in his late fifties.

“Hello, son,” he cried cordially. “How’s Boston?”

“Everyone is quite well,” said John Quincy. “You’re being extremely kind﻿—”

“Nonsense. It’s a pleasure to see you. Come along.”

===Chinese===
一点钟，他去到亲戚的办事处——这位亲戚以工程师兼营造商的身份经营着一番成绩斐然的事业。罗杰本人原来五十七八岁年纪，身材矮小，满面红光。

“你好啊，孩子。”他热络地喊道，“波士顿怎么样？”

“大家都很好，”约翰·昆西说，“您实在客气极了——”

“哪里的话。见到你真高兴。走吧。”

===Original===
He took John Quincy to a famous club for lunch. In the grill he pointed out several well-known writers. The boy was not unduly impressed, for Longfellow, Whittier and Lowell were not among them. Nevertheless it was a pleasant place, the service perfect, the food of an excellence rare on the codfish coast.

“And what,” asked Roger presently, “do you think of San Francisco?”

“I like it,” John Quincy said simply.

===Chinese===
他带约翰·昆西到一家著名的俱乐部用午餐。在烧烤厅里，他指给他看几位名作家。小伙子并未受宠若惊，因为座中并没有朗费罗、惠蒂埃和洛威尔。尽管如此，那地方着实宜人，服务无可挑剔，菜肴之美，在鳕鱼海岸一带殊为罕见。

“那么，”罗杰过了一会儿问道，“你觉得旧金山怎么样？”

“我喜欢。”约翰·昆西老老实实地说。

===Original===
“No? Do you really mean that?” Roger beamed. “Well, it’s the sort of place that ought to appeal to a New Englander. It’s had a history, brief, but believe me, my boy, one crowded hour of glorious life. It’s sophisticated, knowing, subtle. Contrast it with other cities﻿—for instance, take Los Angeles﻿—”

He was off on a favorite topic and he talked well.

===Chinese===
“是吗？当真？”罗杰满面生辉。“唔，这地方正该合新英格兰人的口味。它有过一段历史，虽短暂，可是相信我，孩子，那是浓缩了辉煌人生的一个钟头。它世故、精明、微妙。拿它跟别的城市比比看——比如，拿洛杉矶来说——”

他谈起了最拿手的话题，而且谈得头头是道。

===Original===
“Writers,” he said at last, “are forever comparing cities to women. San Francisco is the woman you don’t tell the folks at home an awful lot about. Not that she wasn’t perfectly proper﻿—I don’t mean that﻿—but her stockings were just a little thinner and her laugh a little gayer﻿—people might misunderstand. Besides, the memory is too precious to talk about. Hello.”

A tall, lean, handsome Englishman was crossing the grill on his way out. “Cope! Cope, my dear fellow!” Roger sped after him and dragged him back. “I knew you at once,” he was saying, “though it must be more than forty years since I last saw you.”

===Chinese===
“作家们，”他终于说道，“总爱把城市比作女人。旧金山是这样的女人：关于她，你对家里人可不肯多讲。倒不是说她有哪点不正派——我不是那个意思——只是她的丝袜偏偏薄那么一点，笑声偏偏放浪那么一点——人家会误会的。再说，这段记忆太珍贵了，舍不得拿来讲。嘿。”

一个瘦高俊朗的英国人正穿过烧烤厅往外走。“科佩！科佩，我亲爱的伙计！”罗杰飞快追上去，把他拽了回来。“我一眼就认出你了，”他说，“尽管距我上回见到你，准有四十多年了。”

===Original===
The Britisher dropped into a chair. He smiled a wry smile. “My dear old chap,” he said. “Not so literal, if you don’t mind.”

“Rot!” protested Roger. “What do years matter? This is a young cousin of mine, John Quincy Winterslip, of Boston. Ah﻿—er﻿—just what is your title now?”

“Captain. I’m in the Admiralty.”

===Chinese===
那英国人落座在椅子里，苦笑了一下。“亲爱的老伙计，”他说，“别把年头报得那么精确，行不行。”

“胡说！”罗杰抗声道，“年头算得了什么？这是我的一位年轻堂侄，波士顿来的约翰·昆西·温特斯利普。呃——您如今的头衔是？”

“船长。我在海军部任职。”

===Original===
“Really? Captain Arthur Temple Cope, John Quincy.” Roger turned to the Englishman. “You were a midshipman, I believe, when we met in Honolulu. I was talking to Dan about you not a year ago﻿—”

An expression of intense dislike crossed the captain’s face. “Ah, yes, Dan. Alive and prospering, I presume?”

“Oh, yes,” answered Roger.

“Isn’t it damnable,” remarked Cope, “how the wicked thrive?”

An uncomfortable silence fell. John Quincy was familiar with the frankness of Englishmen, but he was none the less annoyed by this open display of hostility toward his prospective host. After all, Dan’s last name was Winterslip.

===Chinese===
“真的吗？约翰·昆西，这位是阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩（Arthur Temple Cope）船长。”罗杰转向那英国人。“咱们在檀香山相识那会儿，我记得你还是个海军见习生。就在不到一年前，我还跟丹谈起过你——”

一丝强烈的憎恶掠过船长的脸。“啊，是啊，丹。想必还活得兴旺吧？”

“哦，是啊。”罗杰答道。

“恶人偏偏发迹，”科佩说，“你说气人不气人？”

一阵尴尬的沉默。约翰·昆西领教过英国人的直率，但对这位客人在他未来的东道主面前如此公然表露敌意，仍不免着恼。毕竟，丹也姓温特斯利普。

===Original===
“Ah﻿—er﻿—have a cigarette,” suggested Roger.

“Thank you﻿—have one of mine,” said Cope, taking out a silver case. “Virginia tobacco, though they are put up in Piccadilly. No? And you, sir﻿—” He held the case before John Quincy, who refused a bit stiffly.

The captain nonchalantly lighted up. “I beg your pardon﻿—what I said about your cousin,” he began. “But really, you know﻿—”

===Chinese===
“呃——抽支烟吧。”罗杰提议。

“谢谢——来一支我的。”科佩说着掏出一只银烟盒。“弗吉尼亚烟草，虽然在皮卡迪利装的盒。不来了？您呢，先生——”他把烟盒递到约翰·昆西面前，对方有点拘谨地谢绝了。

船长若无其事地点上烟。“请原谅——我方才说你堂叔的那些话，”他开口道，“不过说真的，你知道——”

===Original===
“No matter,” said Roger cordially. “Tell me what you’re doing here.”

“On my way to Hawaii,” explained the captain. “Sailing at three today on the Australian boat. A bit of a job for the Admiralty. From Honolulu I drop down to the Fanning Group﻿—a little flock of islands that belongs to us,” he added with a fine paternal air.

“A possible coaling station,” smiled Roger.

“My dear fellow﻿—the precise nature of my mission is, of course, a secret.” Captain Cope looked suddenly at John Quincy. “By the way, I once knew a very charming girl from Boston. A relative of yours, no doubt.”

===Chinese===
“不碍事。”罗杰热络地说，“说说你在这儿做什么。”

“我要去夏威夷，”船长解释道，“今天三点搭乘澳大利亚轮船启程。替海军部办点差事。到了檀香山，我再南下范宁群岛——归我们所有的一小群岛屿。”他补了一句，一副得意洋洋的家长派头。

“没准是个备选的加煤站吧。”罗杰微笑道。

“亲爱的伙计——我此行任务的确切性质，自然是机密。”科佩船长忽然看向约翰·昆西。“对了，我从前认识一位波士顿来的非常迷人的姑娘。无疑是你的亲戚。”

===Original===
“A﻿—a girl,” repeated John Quincy, puzzled.

“Minerva Winterslip.”

“Why,” said John Quincy, amazed, “you mean my Aunt Minerva.”

The captain smiled. “She was no one’s aunt in those days,” he said. “Nothing auntish about her. But that was in Honolulu in the ’eighties﻿—we’d put in there on the old wooden *Reliance*﻿—the poor unlucky ship was limping home crippled from Samoa. Your aunt was visiting at that port﻿—there were dances at the palace, swimming parties﻿—ah, me, to be young again.”

===Chinese===
“一——一位姑娘？”约翰·昆西困惑地重复。

“米纳瓦·温特斯利普。”

“哎呀，”约翰·昆西惊道，“您说的是我的米纳瓦姑婆。”

船长笑了。“那个年月她可不是谁的姑婆，”他说，“她身上没有半点姑婆气。那是在十九世纪八十年代的檀香山——我们乘老式木船*Reliance*号停泊在那儿——那艘倒霉的可怜船从萨摩亚一路跛行着狼狈返航。你姑婆当时正在那座港口做客——王宫里有舞会，有游泳聚会——唉，真想再年轻一回啊。”

===Original===
“Minerva’s in Honolulu now,” Roger told him.

“No﻿—really?”

“Yes. She’s stopping with Dan.”

“With Dan.” The captain was silent for a moment “Her husband﻿—”

===Chinese===
“米纳瓦如今就在檀香山。”罗杰告诉他。

“不会吧——当真？”

“当真。她住在丹家里。”

“住在丹家。”船长沉默了片刻。“她丈夫——”

===Original===
“Minerva never married,” Roger explained.

“Amazing,” said the captain. He blew a ring of smoke toward the paneled ceiling. “The more shame to the men of Boston. My time is hardly my own, but I shall hope to look in on her.” He rose. “This was a bit of luck﻿—meeting you again, old chap. I’m due aboard the boat very shortly﻿—you understand, of course.” He bowed to them both, and departed.

“Fine fellow,” Roger said, staring after him. “Frank and British, but a splendid chap.”

===Chinese===
“米纳瓦从没结过婚。”罗杰解释道。

“真是咄咄怪事。”船长说。他朝镶板天花板吐出一个烟圈。“这就更显出波士顿男人们的不是了。我的时间难得归自己支配，但我盼着能去探望她。”他站起身。“真是个意外之喜——又见到了你，老伙计。我马上就得回船上去——你自然明白。”他向两人鞠了一躬，告辞而去。

“好人啊。”罗杰目送着他说，“直率得很英国派，不过是个好家伙。”

===Original===
“I wasn’t especially pleased,” John Quincy admitted, “by the way he spoke of Cousin Dan.”

Roger laughed. “Better get used to it,” he advised. “Dan is not passionately beloved. He’s climbed high, you know, and he’s trampled down a few on his way up. By the way, he wants you to do an errand for him here in San Francisco.”

===Chinese===
“他说起丹堂叔的那副口气，”约翰·昆西坦言，“我听着不太痛快。”

罗杰大笑。“你最好习惯才是，”他劝道，“丹可不太招人爱戴。他爬得很高，你知道，一路上也踩倒了几个。对了，他要你在这儿、在旧金山替他办一件差事。”

===Original===
“Me!” cried John Quincy. “An errand?”

“Yes. You ought to feel flattered. Dan doesn’t trust everybody. However, it’s something that must wait until dark.”

“Until dark,” repeated the puzzled young man from Boston.

“Precisely. In the meantime I propose to show you about town.”

===Chinese===
“我！”约翰·昆西叫道，“一件差事？”

“不错。你该觉得脸上有光才是。丹可不是谁都信的。不过，这件事得等到天黑才能办。”

“等到天黑。”这位波士顿来的年轻人茫然重复。

“正是。趁这工夫，我提议带你逛逛这座城。”

===Original===
“But﻿—you’re busy. I couldn’t think of taking you away﻿—”

Roger laid his hand on John Quincy’s shoulder. “My boy, no westerner is ever too busy to show a man from the East about his city. I’ve been looking forward to this chance for weeks. And since you insist on sailing tomorrow at ten, we must make the most of our time.”

===Chinese===
“可是——您那么忙。我怎么忍心占用您——”

罗杰把手搭在约翰·昆西肩上。“孩子，西部人再忙，也决计忙不到没工夫领东部来的客人逛逛自己的城市。这机会我盼了好几个星期了。你既然坚持明天十点上船，咱们就得把时间用到极致。”

===Original===
Roger proved an adept at making the most of one’s time in San Francisco. After an exhilarating afternoon of motoring over the town and the surrounding country, he brought John Quincy back to the house at six, urging him to dress quickly for a dinner of which he apparently had great hopes.

The boy’s trunk was in his room, and as he put on a dinner coat he looked forward with lively anticipation to a bit of San Francisco night life in Roger’s company. When he came downstairs his host was waiting, a distinguished figure in his dinner clothes, and they set out blithely through the gathering dusk.

===Chinese===
事实证明，罗杰深谙在旧金山把时间用到极致之道。驾着车逛遍全城和四郊，痛痛快快过了一下午之后，他六点钟把约翰·昆西送回宅子，催他赶紧更衣，去赴一顿他显然寄予厚望的晚餐。

小伙子的衣箱已在他房里；他一边套上无尾礼服，一边满心期待着由罗杰作伴，领略一番旧金山的夜生活。他下楼时，主人已候在那里，一身晚礼服，气度不凡，两人便趁着暮色四合，兴冲冲地出发了。

===Original===
“Little place I want you to try.” Roger explained as they sat down at a table in a restaurant that was outwardly of no special note. “Afterward we’ll look in on that musical show at the Columbia.”

The restaurant more than justified Roger’s hopes of it. John Quincy began to glow with a warm friendly feeling for all the world, particularly this city by the western gate. He did not think of himself as a stranger here. He wasn’t a stranger, anyhow. The sensation he had first experienced in the harbor returned to him. He had been here before, he was treading old familiar ground. In far, forgotten, happy times he had known the life of this city’s streets. Strange, but true. He spoke to Roger about it.

===Chinese===
“有家小馆子，我要你去尝尝。”落座后，罗杰解释道——那馆子外表毫不显眼。“吃完咱们再去哥伦比亚剧院看那出歌舞喜剧。”

这家馆子远不止没辜负罗杰的期望。约翰·昆西渐渐心头暖热，对全世界、尤其对这座西部门户之城生出一股暖融融的善意。他不觉得自己是此地的外人。反正他本来就不是外人。他初进港口时体验到的那种感觉又回来了：他从前来过这里，此刻正踏在旧日熟悉的土地上。在遥远得已被遗忘的幸福年代，他就熟识这座城市街头的生活。奇怪，然而千真万确。他把这感觉讲给罗杰听。

===Original===
Roger smiled. “A Winterslip, after all,” he said. “And they told me you were just a sort of﻿—of Puritan survival. My father used to know that sensation you speak of, only he felt it whenever he entered a new town. Might be something in reincarnation, after all.”

“Nonsense,” said John Quincy.

“Probably. Just the blood of the roaming Winterslips in your veins.” He leaned across the table. “How would you like to come to San Francisco to live?”

===Chinese===
罗杰微微一笑。“到底是温特斯利普家的人，”他说，“人家还告诉我，你不过是——不过是块清教徒活化石呢。我父亲从前也常有你说的那种感觉，只不过他每进一座新城，那感觉便会袭上来。看来轮回转世之说，也许真有几分道理。”

“胡说。”约翰·昆西道。

“多半是胡说。不过是你血管里流着流浪的温特斯利普家的血罢了。”他隔着桌子探讨身来。“搬到旧金山来住，你觉得怎么样？”

===Original===
“Wha-what?” asked John Quincy, startled.

“I’m getting along in years, and I’m all alone. Lots of financial details in my office﻿—take you in there and let you look after them. Make it worth your while.”

===Chinese===
“什——什么？”约翰·昆西愕然问道。

“我上年纪了，又孤身一人。我事务所里财务上的细务一大堆——把你带进去，交给你照管。报酬上亏待不了你。”

===Original===
“No, no, thank you,” said John Quincy firmly. “I belong back east. Besides, I could never persuade Agatha to come out here.”

“Agatha who?”

“Agatha Parker﻿—the girl I’m engaged to﻿—in a way. Been sort of understood between us for several years. No,” he added, “I guess I’d better stay where I belong.”

===Chinese===
“不，不，谢谢您。”约翰·昆西斩钉截铁地说，“我的根在东部。再说，我说什么也劝不动阿加莎到这边来。”

“哪个阿加莎？”

“阿加莎·帕克（Agatha Parker）——和我订了婚的姑娘——算是订了婚吧。好几年来，我们俩就算心照不宣了。不行，”他补了一句，“我看我还是待在我该待的地方好。”

===Original===
Roger Winterslip looked his disappointment. “Probably had,” he admitted. “I fancy no girl with that name would follow you here. Though a girl worth having will follow her man anywhere﻿—but no matter.” He studied John Quincy keenly for a moment. “I must have been wrong about you, anyhow.”

John Quincy felt a sudden resentment. “Just what do you mean by that?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
罗杰·温特斯利普一脸失望。“大概是得待在你该待的地方，”他承认道，“我看，叫这种名字的姑娘多半不会跟你上这儿来。虽说值得一娶的姑娘，天涯海角也肯跟着她的男人——罢了，不提了。”他锐利地打量了约翰·昆西片刻。“反正，我八成是看错你了。”

约翰·昆西忽然起了反感。“您这话究竟是什么意思？”他问。

===Original===
“In the old days,” Roger said, “Winterslips were the stuff of which pioneers are made. They didn’t cling to the apron-strings of civilization. They got up some fine morning and nonchalantly strolled off beyond the horizon. They lived﻿—but there, you’re of another generation. You can’t understand.”

“Why can’t I?” demanded John Quincy.

===Chinese===
“想当年，”罗杰说，“温特斯利普家的人个个是拓荒者的料。他们不攥着文明母亲的裙带不放。哪个晴好的早晨起了身，便潇潇洒洒地信步走到地平线之外。他们才叫活过——可是得，你们是另一代人了。你不会懂的。”

“我为什么不会懂？”约翰·昆西质问。

===Original===
“Because the same old rut has evidently been good enough for you. You’ve never known a thrill. Or have you? Have you ever forgot to go to bed because of some utterly silly reason﻿—because, for example, you were young and the moon was shining on a beach lapped by southern seas? Have you ever lied like a gentleman to protect a woman not worth the trouble? Ever made love to the wrong girl?”

“Of course not,” said John Quincy stiffly.

===Chinese===
“因为那条一成不变的老车辙，显然你就觉得挺好了。你从来不曾体验过刺激。或者说，有过吗？你可曾因为某个十足无聊的缘由忘了上床睡觉——比如，只因为你正年轻，南方的海洋轻舔着沙滩，月色正照在那沙滩上？你可曾为了保护一个不值当的女人，像个绅士那样撒谎？可曾爱错过姑娘？”

“当然没有。”约翰·昆西板着脸说。

===Original===
“Ever run for your life through crooked streets in the rowdy quarter of a strange town? Ever fought with a ship’s officer﻿—the old-fashioned kind with fists like flying hams? Ever gone out on a man hunt and when you got your quarry cornered, leaped upon him with no weapon but your bare hands? Have you ever﻿—”

“The type of person you describe,” John Quincy cut in, “is hardly admirable.”

“Probably not,” Roger agreed. “And yet﻿—those are incidents from my own past, my boy.” He regarded John Quincy sadly. “Yes, I must have been wrong about you. A Puritan survival, after all.”

John Quincy deigned no reply. There was an odd light in the older man’s eyes﻿—was Roger secretly laughing at him? He appeared to be, and the boy resented it.

===Chinese===
“可曾在陌生城市喧嚣的街区里，沿着七弯八拐的街巷没命奔逃？可曾跟船上的军官干过架——老式的那种，一对拳头抡起来像两只飞舞的火腿？可曾出马追捕凶徒，把猎物逼进死角后，赤手空拳、只身扑上去？你可曾——”

“您说的这种人，”约翰·昆西插嘴道，“实在谈不上可敬。”

“多半谈不上。”罗杰表示同意。“可是——那些都是我自己往日的事啊，孩子。”他忧伤地望着约翰·昆西。“是啊，我八成是看错你了。说到底，还是块清教徒活化石。”

约翰·昆西不屑作答。老人眼里有一道古怪的光——罗杰是在暗地里笑话他吗？看来正是如此，小伙子为此老大不痛快。

===Original===
But he forgot to be resentful at the revue, which proved to be witty and gay, and Roger and he emerged from the theater at eleven the best of friends again. As they stepped into Roger’s car, the older man gave the chauffeur an address on Russian Hill.

“Dan’s San Francisco house,” he explained, as he climbed in after John Quincy. “He comes over about two months each year, and keeps a place here. Got more money than I have.”

===Chinese===
不过一到歌舞喜剧场上，他就忘了不快——那出戏果然诙谐欢快，十一点钟罗杰和他走出剧院时，两人又成了最要好的朋友。跨进罗杰的汽车时，老人向司机报了个俄罗斯山上的地址。

“丹在旧金山的宅子，”他跟在约翰·昆西身后上车时解释道，“他每年过来住两个月光景，在这儿置了个窝。他的钱比我多。”

===Original===
Dan’s San Francisco house? “Oh,” said John Quincy, “the errand you mentioned?”

Roger nodded. “Yes.” He snapped on a light in the top of the limousine, and took an envelope from his pocket. “Read this letter. It was delivered to me two days ago by the Second Officer of the *President Tyler*.”

John Quincy removed a sheet of note paper from the envelope. The message appeared to be rather hastily scrawled.

===Chinese===
丹在旧金山的宅子？“哦，”约翰·昆西说，“就是您说的那件差事？”

罗杰点头。“正是。”他啪的一声打开豪华轿车顶上的灯，从口袋里取出一只信封。“读读这封信。两天前，泰勒总统号的二副亲手把它交到了我手里。”

约翰·昆西从信封里抽出一张信笺。信上的字迹显得是匆匆涂就。

===Original===
> “Dear Roger,” he read. “You can do me a great service﻿—you and that discreet lad from Boston who is to stop over with you on his way out here. First of all, give John Quincy my regards and tell him that he must make my house his home while he is in the Islands. I’ll be delighted to have him.
> “About the errand. You have a key to my house on Russian Hill. Go up there﻿—better go at night when the caretaker’s not likely to be around. The lights are off, but you’ll find candles in the pantry. In the store room on the top floor is an old brown trunk. Locked, probably﻿—smash the lock if it is. In the lower section you’ll find a battered strong box made of ohia wood and bound with copper. Initials on it﻿—T.M.B.
> “Wrap it up and take it away. It’s rather an armful, but you can manage it. Have John Quincy conceal it in his luggage and some dark night when the ship’s about halfway over, I want him to take it on deck and quietly drop it overboard. Tell him to be sure nobody sees him. That’s all. But send me a guarded cable when you get the box, and tell him to send me a radio when the Pacific has it at last. I’ll sleep better then.
> “Not a word, Roger. Not a word to anyone. You’ll understand. Sometimes the dead past needs a bit of help in burying its dead.
> “**Your Cousin Dan**.”

===Chinese===
> “亲爱的罗杰：”他读道。“你能帮我一个大忙——你，还有那位波士顿来的、要在你处小住、取道前来此地的稳重的小伙子。首先，代我向约翰·昆西问好，并告诉他，在岛上期间，务必把我的家当作他自己的家。我高兴还来不及呢。
> “至于那件差事。你手里有我俄罗斯山宅子的钥匙。上去一趟——最好夜里去，看房人多半不在。屋里灯是灭的，不过食品间里能找到蜡烛。顶楼的储藏室里有一只棕色的旧衣箱。多半上了锁——若上了锁，就把锁砸开。箱子下层，你会找到一只包着铜皮的破旧的奥希亚木盒。盒上有起首字母——T.M.B.
> “把它包好带走。抱着挺费胳膊，不过你们对付得来。让约翰·昆西把它藏进他的行李，等哪个漆黑的夜里，船行到差不多一半，我要他把它拿到甲板上，悄悄丢下海去。告诉他，务必别让任何人瞧见。就这些。不过拿到盒子后，给我发一封措辞谨慎的电报；等太平洋终于把它收下了，再让他给我来一封无线电报。到那时我就睡得安稳些了。
> “一个字也别说，罗杰。对谁都一个字也别说。你会明白的。有时候，死去的往事要把它的死者掩埋掉，也需要有人从旁帮上一把。
> “**你的堂兄丹**。”

===Original===
Solemnly John Quincy handed the letter back into Roger’s keeping. The older man thoughtfully tore it to bits and tossed them through the car window open beside him. “Well,” said John Quincy. “Well﻿—” A fitting comment eluded him.

“Simple enough,” smiled Roger. “If we can help poor old Dan to sleep better as easily as that, we must do it, eh?”

“I﻿—I suppose so,” John Quincy agreed.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西神色郑重地把信交还罗杰保管。老人若有所思地把它撕成碎片，从身旁敞开的车窗撒了出去。“这个，”约翰·昆西说，“这个——”他想不出一句贴切的评语。

“简单得很嘛。”罗杰含笑说，“要是这么轻而易举就能帮可怜的老丹睡个安稳觉，咱们何乐而不为，嗯？”

“我——我想是吧。”约翰·昆西表示同意。

===Original===
They had climbed Russian Hill, and were speeding along a deserted avenue lined by imposing mansions. Roger leaned forward. “Go on to the corner,” he said to the chauffeur. “We can walk back,” he explained to John Quincy. “Best not to leave the car before the house. Might excite suspicion.”

Still John Quincy had no comment to make. They alighted at the corner and walked slowly back along the avenue. In front of a big stone house, Roger paused. He looked carefully in all directions, then ran with surprising speed up the steps. “Come on,” he called softly.

===Chinese===
车子已爬上俄罗斯山，正沿着一条空无一人的大道飞驰，道旁华宅森然。罗杰欠身向前。“开到街角。”他对司机说。“咱们走回去，”他向约翰·昆西解释，“车最好别停在房子跟前，怕惹人起疑。”

约翰·昆西依旧无话可说。他们在街角下了车，沿着大道缓缓往回走。走到一座石头大宅跟前，罗杰停住脚。他仔细望了望四面八方，随即以出人意料的敏捷窜上台阶。“来。”他压低嗓子唤道。

===Original===
John Quincy came. Roger unlocked the door and they stepped into a dark vestibule. Beyond that, darker still, was a huge hall, the dim suggestion of a grand staircase. Here and there an article of furniture, shrouded in white, stood like a ghost, marooned but patient. Roger took out a box of matches.

“Meant to bring a flashlight,” he said, “but I clean forgot. Wait here﻿—I’ll hunt those candles in the pantry.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西跟了上去。罗杰开锁开门，两人跨进一间黑黢黢的门厅。门厅再往里，更是黑得深不可测，是一个巨大的厅堂，隐约透出一段气派楼梯的影子。四处零星立着蒙了白布罩的家具，像一尊尊幽灵，如遭弃置，却兀自耐心。罗杰掏出一盒火柴。

“本来想带支手电筒的，”他说，“可忘得干干净净。在这儿等等——我去食品间找那些蜡烛。”

===Original===
He went off into the dark. John Quincy took a few cautious steps. He was about to sit down on a chair﻿—but it was like sitting on the lap of a ghost. He changed his mind, stood in the middle of the floor, waited. Quiet, deathly quiet. The black had swallowed Roger, with not so much as a gurgle.

After what seemed an age, Roger returned, bearing two lighted candles. One each, he explained. John Quincy took his, held it high. The flickering yellow flame accentuated the shadows, was really of small help.

===Chinese===
他没入黑暗中去了。约翰·昆西小心翼翼地挪了几步。他正想在一把椅子上坐下——可那感觉像是坐到幽灵的膝头。他改变了主意，站到地板中央，等着。安静，死一般的安静。黑暗一口把罗杰吞了下去，连个咕噜声都没冒。

仿佛过了一世那样久，罗杰回来了，捧着两支点着的蜡烛。他说，一人一支。约翰·昆西接过自己那支，举高了。摇曳的黄焰反倒加深了四下的阴影，实在帮不上什么忙。

===Original===
Roger led the way up the grand staircase, then up a narrower flight. At the foot of still another flight, in a stuffy passage on the third floor, he halted.

“Here we are,” he said. “This leads to the storage room under the roof. By gad, I’m getting too old for this sort of thing. I meant to bring a chisel to use on that lock. I know where the tools are﻿—I’ll be gone only a minute. You go on up and locate the trunk.”

“All﻿—all right,” answered John Quincy.

===Chinese===
罗杰领头登上那座气派的大楼梯，接着又登上一段较窄的楼梯。在第三段楼梯脚下、三楼一条闷不透气的过道里，他停了下来。

“到了，”他说，“这楼梯通向屋顶底下的储藏室。老天作证，我这种年纪实在干不动这种事了。我本想带把凿子来对付那把锁。我知道工具搁在哪儿——我去去就回。你先上去，找到那只衣箱。”

“好——好吧。”约翰·昆西应道。

===Original===
Again Roger left him. John Quincy hesitated. Something about a deserted house at midnight to dismay the stoutest heart﻿—but nonsense! He was a grown man. He smiled, and started up the narrow stair. High above his head the yellow light of the candle flickered on the brown rafters of the unfinished store room.

He reached the top of the stairs, and paused. Gloom, gloom everywhere. Odd how floor boards will creak even when no one is moving over them. One was creaking back of him now.

===Chinese===
罗杰又一次撇下了他。约翰·昆西踌躇起来。午夜时分的一座空宅，总有某种东西能叫最硬的汉子心里发毛——可是去他的！他是成年人了。他笑了笑，迈上那段窄楼梯。烛光昏黄，在他头顶高处闪烁，映着储藏室尚未装修的褐色椽木。

他走到楼梯顶上，停住。阴森，四处阴森。真怪，明明没人走动，地板却会吱呀作响。此刻他身后就有一块正吱呀作响。

===Original===
He was about to turn when a hand reached from behind him and knocked the candle out of his grasp. It rolled on the floor, extinguished.

This was downright rude! “See here,” cried John Quincy, “wh﻿—who are you?”

A bit of moonlight struggled in through a far window, and suddenly between John Quincy and that distant light there loomed the determined figure of a man. Something told the boy he had better get ready, but where he came from one had a moment or two for preparation. He had none here. A fist shot out and found his face, and John Quincy Winterslip of Boston went down amid the rubbish of a San Francisco attic. He heard, for a second, the crash of planets in collision, and then the clatter of large feet on the stairs. After that, he was alone with the debris.

===Chinese===
他正要回身，一只手从他背后伸过来，把他手里的蜡烛打落在地。蜡烛在地板上滚了滚，熄灭了。

这可实在无礼！“喂，”约翰·昆西喊道，“你——你是谁？”

一缕月色从远处一扇窗里挣扎着透进来，蓦地，约翰·昆西和那点远光之间耸出一个男人下定决心的身影。冥冥中有什么告诉小伙子，他最好准备招架，可是在他来的地方，人总还有一两秒钟的工夫做准备。这儿却没有。一记拳头飞来，正中他的脸，波士顿的约翰·昆西·温特斯利普栽倒在旧金山一间阁楼的垃圾堆里。有那么一秒钟，他听见了行星相撞的轰响，随后是大脚板砸在楼梯上的哐哐声。再往后，便只剩他和满地狼藉了。

===Original===
He got up, thoroughly angry, and began brushing off the dinner coat that had been his tailor’s pride. Roger arrived. “Who was that?” he demanded breathlessly. “Somebody went down the back stairs to the kitchen. Who was it?”

“How should I know?” inquired John Quincy with pardonable peevishness. “He didn’t introduce himself to me.” His cheek was stinging; he put his handkerchief to it and noted in the light of Roger’s candle that it was red when it came away. “He wore a ring,” added John Quincy. “Damned bad taste!”

===Chinese===
他爬起来，怒不可遏，开始掸那件曾是他裁缝骄傲的无尾礼服。罗杰赶到了。“刚才那人是谁？”他气喘吁吁地问，“有人顺着后楼梯跑到厨房去了。是谁？”

“我怎么会知道？”约翰·昆西问道，这股火气倒也情有可原。“他又没跟我互通姓名。”他的脸颊火辣辣地疼；他把手帕按上去，就着罗杰的烛光一看，手帕拿下来时已经红了。“那人手上戴着戒指，”约翰·昆西补充道，“俗气得要命！”

===Original===
“Hit you, eh?” inquired Roger.

“I’ll say he did.”

“Look!” Roger cried. He pointed. “The trunk-lock smashed.” He went over to investigate. “And the box is gone. Poor old Dan!”

John Quincy continued to brush himself off. Poor old Dan’s plight gave him a vast pain, a pain which had nothing to do with his throbbing jaw. A fine nerve poor old Dan had to ask a complete stranger to offer his face for punishment in a dusty attic at midnight. What was it all about, anyhow?

===Chinese===
“打中你了，嗯？”罗杰问。

“可不是嘛。”

“瞧！”罗杰喊道。他指了过去。“衣箱的锁砸开了。”他走过去查看。“盒子也不见了。可怜的老丹！”

约翰·昆西继续掸着衣服。可怜的老丹这番处境，让他浑身泛起一阵无边无际的难受，这难受与他突突跳痛的下巴毫不相干。可怜的老丹脸皮可真厚，竟好意思请一个素昧平生的人，大半夜在一间积尘的阁楼里把脸凑上去挨打。这到底是怎么回事？

===Original===
Roger continued his search. “No use,” he announced. “The box is gone, that’s plain. Come on, we’ll go downstairs and look about. There’s your candle on the floor.”

John Quincy picked up the candle and relighted it from Roger’s flame. Silently they went below. The outer door of the kitchen stood open. “Left that way,” said Roger. “And, see”﻿—he pointed to a window with a broken pane﻿—“that’s where he came in.”

===Chinese===
罗杰继续搜寻。“没用了，”他宣布，“盒子不见了，明摆着的。走，咱们下楼四处看看。你的蜡烛在地上呢。”

约翰·昆西拾起蜡烛，就着罗杰的烛火重新点上。两人默然下楼。厨房的外门敞开着。“他是打那儿走的，”罗杰说，“还有，瞧——”他指着一扇破了一格玻璃的窗——“他是打这儿进来的。”

===Original===
“How about the police?” suggested John Quincy.

Roger stared at him. “The police? I should say not! Where’s your discretion, my boy? This is not a police matter. I’ll have a new glass put in that window tomorrow. Come on﻿—we might as well go home. We’ve failed.”

The note of reproof in his voice angered John Quincy anew. They left the extinguished candles on a table in the hall, and returned to the street.

===Chinese===
“报不报警？”约翰·昆西提议。

罗杰瞪着他。“报警？我看万万不可！你的稳重劲儿哪儿去了，孩子？这可不是警察管的事。明天我叫人给那扇窗换块新玻璃。走吧——咱们还是回家去。咱们白跑了一趟。”

话音里的责备意味让约翰·昆西又冒起火来。他们把熄灭的蜡烛搁在门厅的桌上，回到街上。

===Original===
“Well, I’ll have to cable Dan,” Roger said, as they walked toward the corner. “I’m afraid he’ll be terribly upset by this. It won’t tend to endear you to him, either.”
