“I can struggle along,” said John Quincy, “without his affection.”

“If you could only have held that fellow till I came﻿—”

===Chinese===
“唉，我只好给丹打电报了。”朝街角走去的路上，罗杰说，“只怕他会为此大为不安。这事也难让他对你增添好感。”

“没有他的疼爱，”约翰·昆西说，“我也凑合过得下去。”

“你要是能撑到我赶来时再放走那家伙——”

===Original===
“Look here,” said John Quincy, “I was taken unawares. How could I know that I was going up against the heavyweight champion in that attic? He came at me out of the dark﻿—and I’m not in condition﻿—”

“No offense, my boy,” Roger put in.

“I see my mistake,” went on John Quincy. “I should have trained for this trip out here. A stiff course in a gymnasium. But don’t worry. The next lad that makes a pass at me will find a different target. I’ll do a daily three dozen and I’ll take boxing lessons. From now on until I get home, I’ll be expecting the worst.”

===Chinese===
“您听我说，”约翰·昆西道，“我是猝不及防。我哪能想到，在那阁楼里等着跟我过招的是位重量级拳王？他从暗处朝我扑来——再说我状态不在——”

“别见怪，孩子。”罗杰插话。

“我明白我的失误了，”约翰·昆西接着说，“这趟出门前我本该训练训练的。去健身房狠狠练上一阵。不过您放心。下回再有哪位小伙子朝我动手，会发现自己面对的是另一副靶子。我要每天练三十六下，还要去学拳击。从今往后直到回家，我会随时防着最坏的事。”

===Original===
Roger laughed. “That’s a nasty cut on your cheek,” he remarked. “We’d better stop at this drug store and have it dressed.”

A solicitous drug clerk ministered to John Quincy with iodine, cotton and court plaster, and he reentered the limousine bearing honorably the scar of battle. The drive to Nob Hill was devoid of light chatter.

===Chinese===
罗杰大笑。“你脸颊上那道口子不轻，”他说，“咱们最好在这家药房停一停，包扎一下。”

一位殷勤周到的药房伙计拿碘酒、药棉和医用胶布给约翰·昆西敷治，他重新坐进豪华轿车，光荣地带着战斗的疤痕。回诺布山的一路，少了几分轻松的说笑。

===Original===
Just inside the door of Roger’s house, a whirlwind in a gay gown descended upon them. “Barbara!” Roger said. “Where did you come from?”

“Hello, old dear,” she cried, kissing him. “I motored up from Burlingame. Spending the night with you﻿—I’m sailing on the *President Tyler* in the morning. Is this John Quincy?”

===Chinese===
刚进罗杰宅子的门，一阵披着艳丽衣裙的小旋风便朝他们扑来。“芭芭拉！”罗杰说，“你从哪儿冒出来的？”

“你好啊，老宝贝。”她一边嚷一边亲吻他，“我开车从伯灵格姆上来的。今晚在你这儿过夜——明早我搭泰勒总统号走。这位就是约翰·昆西？”

===Original===
“Cousin John,” smiled Roger. “He deserves a kiss too. He’s had a bad evening.”

The girl moved swiftly toward the defenseless John Quincy. Again he was unprepared, and this time it was his other cheek that suffered, though not unpleasantly. “Just by way of welcome,” Barbara laughed. She was blonde and slender. John Quincy thought he had never seen so much energy imprisoned in so slight a form. “I hear you’re bound for the Islands?” she said.

===Chinese===
“堂侄约翰，”罗杰含笑说，“他也该得个吻才是。他今晚可够呛。”

姑娘轻捷地直扑毫无防备的约翰·昆西。他又一次措手不及，这一回遭殃的是他另一边脸颊——虽然遭得不无惬意。“权当是见面礼。”芭芭拉（Barbara）笑道。她金发纤细。约翰·昆西想，他从没见过这么多活力被囚禁在如此单薄的一副身躯里。“听说你要去岛上？”她说。

===Original===
“Tomorrow,” John Quincy answered. “On your boat.”

“Splendid!” she cried. “When did you get in?”

“John Quincy came this morning,” Roger told her.

“And he’s had a bad evening?” the girl said. “How lucky I came along. Where are you taking us, Roger?”

===Chinese===
“明天，”约翰·昆西答道，“搭你们的船。”

“太好了！”她嚷道，“你什么时候到的？”

“约翰·昆西今天早上到的。”罗杰告诉她。

“他就倒了今晚这个霉？”姑娘说，“幸亏我赶上了。罗杰，你要带我们去哪儿？”

===Original===
John Quincy stared. Taking them? At this hour?

“I’ll be getting along upstairs,” he ventured.

“Why, it’s just after twelve,” said Barbara. “Lots of places open. You dance, don’t you? Let me show you San Francisco. Roger’s a dear old thing﻿—we’ll let him pay the checks.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西目瞪口呆。带上我们？这个钟点？

“我看我还是上楼去吧。”他试探着说。

“嗐，才刚过十二点，”芭芭拉说，“还开着门的地方多着呢。你会跳舞，对吧？我来领你见识见识旧金山。罗杰是个可爱的老家伙——账单嘛，就让他来付。”

===Original===
“Well﻿—I﻿—I﻿—” stammered John Quincy. His cheek was throbbing and he thought longingly of that bed in the room upstairs. What a place, this West!

“Come along!” The girl was humming a gay little tune. All vivacity, all life. Rather pleasant sort at that. John Quincy took up his hat.

===Chinese===
“这个——我——我——”约翰·昆西结结巴巴。他的脸颊突突地跳痛，满怀渴望地想着楼上房间里的那张床。这西部，好一个地方！

“来吧！”姑娘正哼着一支轻快的小调。浑身是劲，浑身是活力。这样的人倒也讨人喜欢。约翰·昆西拿起了帽子。

===Original===
Roger’s chauffeur had lingered a moment before the house to inspect his engine. When he saw them coming down the steps, he looked as though he rather wished he hadn’t. But escape was impossible; he climbed to his place behind the wheel.

“Where to, Barbara?” Roger asked. “Tait’s?”

===Chinese===
罗杰的司机刚在宅子前耽搁了片刻，检查他的发动机。一见他们走下台阶，他的神情仿佛倒宁愿自己没耽搁这一刻。但逃跑是不可能了；他爬上方向盘后的座位。

“去哪儿，芭芭拉？”罗杰问，“泰特餐馆？”

===Original===
“Not Tait’s,” she answered. “I’ve just come from there.”

“What! I thought you motored in from Burlingame?”

“So I did﻿—at five. I’ve traveled a bit since then. How about some chop-suey for this Boston boy?”

===Chinese===
“不去泰特餐馆，”她答道，“我刚从那儿来。”

“什么！你不是开车从伯灵格姆来的吗？”

“是啊——五点来的。打那以后我又转悠了一阵。给这位波士顿小伙子来点炒杂碎怎么样？”

===Original===
Good lord, John Quincy thought. Was there anything in the world he wanted less? No matter. Barbara took him among the Chinese.

He didn’t give a hang about the Chinese. Nor the Mexicans, whose restaurants interested the girl next. At the moment, he was unsympathetic toward Italy. And even toward France. But he struggled on the international round, affronting his digestion with queer dishes, and dancing thousands of miles with the slim Barbara in his arms. After scrambled eggs at a place called Pete’s Fashion, she consented to call it an evening.

===Chinese===
天老爷，约翰·昆西想。这世上还有比这他更不想要的东西吗？罢了。芭芭拉领着他闯进了中国人堆里。

他压根不在乎什么中国人。也不在乎墨西哥人——那姑娘接着又对墨西哥馆子来了兴致。此刻他对意大利也提不起好感。甚至对法兰西也是。但他仍在这趟国际巡回中苦撑向前，拿稀奇古怪的菜肴糟蹋自己的肠胃，搂着纤细的芭芭拉，舞步加起来怕有几千英里。在一家名叫“皮特时尚”的馆子里吃过炒鸡蛋之后，她总算恩准今晚到此为止。

===Original===
As John Quincy staggered into Roger’s house, the great clock in the hall was striking three. The girl was still alert and sparkling. John Quincy hastily concealed a yawn.

“All wrong to come home so early,” she cried. “But we’ll have a dance or two on the boat. By the way, I’ve been wanting to ask. What does it mean? The injured cheek?”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西摇摇晃晃走进罗杰宅子时，门厅里的大钟正敲三点。姑娘依然精神抖擞、神采飞扬。约翰·昆西慌忙掩住一个哈欠。

“这么早回家，全然不对头，”她嚷道，“不过在船上咱们还可以跳上一两支舞。对了，我一直想问。这是怎么回事？你那受伤的脸颊？”

===Original===
“Why﻿—er﻿—I﻿—” John Quincy remarked. Over the girl’s shoulder he saw Roger violently shaking his head. “Oh, that,” said John Quincy, lightly touching the wound. “That’s where the West begins. Good night. I’ve had a bully time.” And at last he got upstairs.

He stood for a moment at his bedroom window, gazing down at the torchlight procession of the streets through this amazing city. He was a little dazed. That soft warm presence close by his side in the car﻿—pleasant, very pleasant. Remarkable girls out here. Different!

===Chinese===
“这个——呃——我——”约翰·昆西支吾道。越过姑娘的肩头，他看见罗杰在拼命摇头。“哦，那个啊，”约翰·昆西说着，轻轻碰了碰伤口，“那就是西部开始的地方。晚安。我玩得痛快极了。”他总算上了楼。

他在卧室窗前站了片刻，俯瞰这座奇妙城市里宛如火炬游行行列的条条街道。他有点恍惚。方才车里紧贴在他身侧的那团柔软温热——惬意，非常惬意。这儿的姑娘们真了不起。不一样！

===Original===
Beyond shone the harbor lights. That other girl﻿—wonderful eyes she had. Just because she had laughed at him his treasured hat box floated now forlorn on those dark waters. He yawned again. Better be careful. Mustn’t be so easily influenced. No telling where it would end.

===Chinese===
远处闪耀着港湾的灯火。还有那一位姑娘——她那双眼睛真美。只因她当时笑话了他，他那只心爱的帽盒此刻正孤零零漂在那片暗色的水面上。他又打了个哈欠。得当心点。不能这么容易受人影响。天晓得到头来会怎样。

## IV A Friend of Tim’s / 四 蒂姆的朋友

===Original===
It was another of those mornings on which the fog maybe did not come. Roger and his guests were in the limousine again; it seemed to John Quincy that they had left it only a few minutes before. So it must have seemed to the chauffeur too as, sleepy-eyed, he hurried them toward the waterfront.

“By the way, John Quincy,” Roger said, “you’ll want to change your money before you go aboard.”

John Quincy gathered his wandering thoughts. “Oh, yes, of course,” he answered.

===Chinese===
又是一个雾没准儿不来的早晨。罗杰和他的客人们再度坐进豪华轿车；在约翰·昆西看来，他们几分钟前才刚从这辆车里下来。司机想必也作如是想，只见他睡眼惺忪，匆匆载着他们往滨水区赶。

「对了，约翰·昆西，」罗杰说，「上船前你总得把钱换一换。」

约翰·昆西收拢起涣散的思绪。「哦，对，当然。」他答道。

===Original===
Roger smiled. “Just what sort of money would you like to change it for?” he inquired.

“Why—” began John Quincy. He stopped. “Why, I always thought—”

“Don’t pay any attention to Roger,” Barbara laughed. “He’s spoofing you.” She was fresh and blooming, a little matter like three a.m. made no difference to her. “Only about one person out of a thousand in this country knows that Hawaii is a part of the United States, and the fact annoys us deeply over in the Islands. Dear old Roger was trying to get you in wrong with me by enrolling you among the nine hundred and ninety-nine.”

“Almost did it, too,” chuckled Roger.

===Chinese===
罗杰微微一笑。「那么您想把您的钱换成哪一国的钱呢？」他问道。

「呃——」约翰·昆西开了口。他顿住了。「呃，我一向以为——」

「别理罗杰，」芭芭拉笑道。「他是逗你玩儿呢。」她神清气爽、容光焕发，凌晨三点那么点小事对她毫无影响。「这个国家里一千个人当中大约只有一个知道夏威夷是美国的一部分，这个事实让我们岛上的人憋气得很。亲爱的老罗杰方才就是想把你编进那九百九十九个里去，好叫你在我这儿失宠。」

「差一点儿就得手了，」罗杰咯咯直笑。

===Original===
“Nonsense,” said Barbara. “John Quincy is too intelligent. He’s not like that congressman who wrote a letter to ‘the American Consul at Honolulu.’ ”

“Did one of them do that?” smiled John Quincy.

“He certainly did. We almost gave up the struggle after that. Then there was the senator who came out on a junket, and began a speech with: ‘When I get home to my country—’ Someone in the audience shouted: ‘You’re there now, you big stiff!’ It wasn’t elegant, of course, but it expressed our feeling perfectly. Oh, we’re touchy, John Quincy.”

“Don’t blame you a bit,” he told her. “I’ll be very careful what I say.”

===Chinese===
「瞎说，」芭芭拉说。「约翰·昆西可聪明着呢。他不像那位给『檀香山的美国领事』写信的众议员。」

「真有议员干出这种事？」约翰·昆西含笑问。

「千真万确。打那以后，我们差点儿不想争这口气了。后来又有一位参议员出来游历，演说一开口便是：『等我回到了我的国家——』听众里有人喊：『你现在就在自个儿的国家，你这个大笨蛋！』话固然说得不雅，可把我们的心情表达得再透彻不过。唉，我们可敏感着呢，约翰·昆西。」

「一点儿也不怪你们，」他对她说。「往后我说话可要留神。」

===Original===
They had reached the Embarcadero, and the car halted before one of the piers. The chauffeur descended and began to gather up the baggage. Roger and John Quincy took a share of it, and they traversed the pier-shed to the gangplank.

“Get along to your office, Roger,” Barbara said.

“No hurry,” he answered. “I’ll go aboard with you, of course.”

===Chinese===
他们到了安巴卡德罗（Embarcadero）滨水码头区，汽车在其中一座码头前停住。司机下车动手搬行李，罗杰和约翰·昆西也各拎了一部分，三人穿过码头货棚，走上跳板。

「快去上你的班吧，罗杰。」芭芭拉说。

「不忙，」他答道。「我当然要送你们上船。」

===Original===
Amid the confusion of the deck, a party of girls swept down on Barbara, pretty lively girls of the California brand. John Quincy learned with some regret that they were there only to see Barbara off. A big broad-shouldered man in white pushed his way through the crowd.

“Hello there!” he called to Barbara.

“Hello, Harry,” she answered. “You, know Roger, don’t you? John Quincy, this is an old friend of mine, Harry Jennison.”

===Chinese===
甲板上一片忙乱，一群姑娘朝芭芭拉涌了过来，是加利福尼亚牌子的那种活泼漂亮姑娘。约翰·昆西略带遗憾地得知，她们只是来给芭芭拉送行的。一个穿白衣裳的宽肩膀大个子从人群中挤了过来。

「嘿，你好啊！」他朝芭芭拉喊道。

「你好，哈利，」她答道。「你认得罗杰的，对不对？约翰·昆西，这是我的一位老朋友，哈里·詹尼森（Harry Jennison）。」

===Original===
Mr. Jennison was extremely good-looking, his face was deeply tanned by the Island sun, his hair blond and wavy, his gray eyes amused and cynical. Altogether, he was the type of man women look at twice and never forget; John Quincy felt himself at once supplanted in the eyes of Barbara’s friends.

Jennison seized the boy’s hand in a firm grip. “Sailing too, Mr. Winterslip?” he inquired. “That’s good. Between us we ought to be able to keep this young woman entertained.”

===Chinese===
詹尼森先生生得极其英俊，脸被岛上的太阳晒成深棕色，一头金色的鬈发，灰眼睛里带着玩世不恭的笑意。总而言之，他是那种女人要看上第二眼、而且永远忘不掉的男人；约翰·昆西顿时感到，在芭芭拉这帮助友眼里，自己已经被人取而代之了。

詹尼森一把紧紧握住小伙子的手。「您也坐船，温特斯利普先生？」他问道。「那敢情好。咱们俩搭伙，保管陪得这位小姐开开心心。」

===Original===
The shore call sounded, and the confusion increased. Along the deck came a little old lady, followed by a Chinese woman servant. They walked briskly, and the crowd gave way before them.

“Hello—this is luck,” cried Roger. “Madame Maynard—just a moment. I want you to meet a cousin of mine from Boston.” He introduced John Quincy. “I give him into your charge. Couldn’t find a better guide, philosopher and friend for him if I combed the Islands.”

The old lady glanced at John Quincy. Her black eyes snapped. “Another Winterslip, eh?” she said. “Hawaii’s all cluttered up with ’em now. Well, the more the merrier. I know your aunt.”

===Chinese===
催客上岸的号声响了，人声愈发嘈杂。甲板上走来一位瘦小的老妇人，身后跟着一个中国女仆。两人步履轻快，人群在她们面前纷纷让路。

「嘿——真是巧了，」罗杰喊道。「梅纳德夫人（Madame Maynard）——请留步。我给您引见一位我波士顿来的堂兄弟。」他把约翰·昆西介绍给她。「我把他托付给您了。就算我把整个群岛翻个遍，也再找不出比您更好的导师、哲人和朋友。」

老妇人朝约翰·昆西瞥了一眼。她的黑眼睛亮光一闪。「又是温特斯利普家的人，嗯？」她说。「夏威夷如今满坑满谷都是你们姓温的。也罢，人越多越热闹。我认得你姑婆。」

===Original===
“Stick close to her, John Quincy,” Roger admonished.

She shook her head. “I’m a million years old,” she protested. “The boys don’t stick so close any more. They like ’em younger. However, I’ll keep my eye on him. My good eye. Well, Roger, run over some time.” And she moved away.

“A grand soul,” said Roger, smiling after her. “You’ll like her. Old missionary family, and her word’s law over there.”

“Who’s this Jennison?” asked John Quincy.

===Chinese===
「一步也别离开她，约翰·昆西。」罗杰叮嘱道。

她摇了摇头。「我都一百万岁了，」她抗议道。「小伙子们如今不肯紧贴着我喽。他们喜欢年轻的。不过，我会替你看着他点的。用我那只好眼睛。对了罗杰，得空过来串门。」说完她走开了。

「一个了不起的人，」罗杰望着她的背影微笑道。「你会喜欢她的。老传教士世家，在那边她一句话就是法律。」

「这个詹尼森是什么人？」约翰·昆西问。

===Original===
“Him?” Roger glanced over to where Mr. Jennison stood, the center of an admiring feminine group. “Oh, he’s Dan’s lawyer. One of the leading citizens of Honolulu, I believe. John J. Adonis himself, isn’t he?” An officer appeared, herding the reluctant throng toward the gangplank. “I’ll have to leave you, John Quincy. A pleasant journey. When you come through on your way home, give me a few more days to try to convince you on my San Francisco offer.”

John Quincy laughed. “You’ve been mighty kind.”

“Not at all.” Roger shook his hand warmly. “Take care of yourself over there. Hawaii’s a little too much like Heaven to be altogether safe. So long, my boy, so long.”

He moved away. John Quincy saw him kiss Barbara affectionately and with her friends join the slow procession ashore.

===Chinese===
「他呀？」罗杰朝詹尼森先生站的地方瞥了一眼——他正被一圈仰慕他的女客团团围住。「哦，他是丹的律师。檀香山的头面人物之一，我想。活脱脱又一位『约翰·杰·阿多尼斯』，对不对？」一名船上的官员出现，把恋恋不舍的人群往跳板那边赶。「我得告辞了，约翰·昆西。祝旅途愉快。回程路过时，再给我几天工夫，让我接着劝你接受我那个旧金山的差事。」

约翰·昆西笑了。「你待我实在太好了。」

「不值一提。」罗杰热乎乎地同他握手。「到那边多保重。夏威夷有点太像天堂，未免不完全保险。再会，孩子，再会。」

他走开了。约翰·昆西看见他深情地吻别芭芭拉，随后同她及她的朋友们一道，汇入缓缓登岸的行列。

===Original===
The young man from Boston stepped to the rail. Several hundred voices were calling admonitions, promises, farewells. With that holiday spirit so alien to John Quincy’s experience, those ashore were throwing confetti. The streamers grew in number, making a tangle of color, a last frail bond with the land. The gangplank was taken up, clumsily the *President Tyler* began to draw away from the pier. On the topmost deck a band was playing—“Aloha-oe,” the sweetest, most melancholy song of goodbye ever written. John Quincy was amazed to feel a lump rising in his throat.

===Chinese===
那个波士顿来的年轻人走到船栏边。岸上几百个嗓音在喊着叮嘱、许诺和道别。岸上的人们撒着彩纸屑，带着那种约翰·昆西全然陌生的节日兴致。彩带越抛越多，缠成一片乱纷纷的色彩，那是与陆地相连的最后一缕脆弱纽带。跳板收了起来，泰勒总统号笨手笨脚地开始驶离码头。最上层甲板上，乐队正奏着《阿罗哈·奥埃》（Aloha-oe）——世上谱写过的最甜美、最忧伤的一支告别曲。约翰·昆西惊讶地发觉，自己的喉咙里涌起一团哽咽。

===Original===
The frail, gay-colored bond was breaking now. A thin veined hand at John Quincy’s side waved a handkerchief. He turned to find Mrs. Maynard. There were tears on her cheeks.

“Silly old woman,” she said. “Sailed away from this town a hundred and twenty-eight times. Actual count—I keep a diary. Cried every time. What about? I don’t know.”

The ship was well out in the harbor now. Barbara came along, Jennison trailing her. The girl’s eyes were wet.

“An emotional lot, we Islanders,” said the old lady. She put her arm about the girl’s slim waist. “Here’s another one of ’em. Living way off the way we do, any goodbye at all—it saddens us.” She and Barbara moved on down the deck.

===Chinese===
这缕脆弱而艳丽的纽带此刻正在扯断。约翰·昆西身旁一只青筋虬结的瘦手挥着手帕。他回过头，看见了梅纳德夫人。她颊上有泪。

「傻老婆子，」她说。「打这城里坐船走了一百二十八回了。实打实数出来的——我记日记呢。回回都哭。哭什么呢？我自己也不知道。」

这会儿船已驶出港湾老远。芭芭拉走过来，詹尼森缀在后头。姑娘的眼睛湿汪汪的。

「我们岛民啊，就是一帮多愁善感的，」老妇人说。她伸臂搂住姑娘纤细的腰。「这不又是一个。像我们这样住得离一切老远老远的，随便哪一种告别——都叫人心里发酸。」她同芭芭拉往甲板那头去了。

===Original===
Jennison stopped. His eyes were quite dry. “First trip out?” he inquired.

“Oh, yes,” replied John Quincy.

“Hope you’ll like us,” Jennison said. “Not Massachusetts, of course, but we’ll do our best to make you feel at home. It’s a way we have with strangers.”

“I’m sure I shall have a bully time,” John Quincy remarked. But he felt somewhat depressed. Three thousand miles from Beacon Street—and moving on! He waved to someone he fancied might be Roger on the dock, and went to find his stateroom.

===Chinese===
詹尼森站住脚。他的眼睛干干的。「头一回坐船出来？」他问。

「哦，是的。」约翰·昆西答道。

「希望你会喜欢我们这儿，」詹尼森说。「当然，比不得马萨诸塞，可我们一定尽力让你觉得跟在家里一样。我们招呼生客，就有这么一手。」

「我保管痛痛快快玩一场。」约翰·昆西说。可他心里有些抑郁。离开灯塔街三千英里——而且还在往前赶！他朝码头上一个他琢磨着也许是罗杰的人影挥了挥手，便去找自己的舱房。

===Original===
He learned that he was to share his cabin with two missionaries. One was a tall, gloomy old man with a lemon-colored face—an honored veteran of the foreign field named Upton. The other was a ruddy-cheeked boy whose martyrdom was still before him. John Quincy suggested drawing lots for a choice of berths, but even this mild form of gambling appeared distasteful to these emissaries of the church.

“You boys take the berths,” said Upton. “Leave me the couch. I don’t sleep well anyhow.” His tone was that of one who prefers to suffer.

John Quincy politely objected. After further discussion it was settled that he was to have the upper berth, the old man the lower, and the boy the couch. The Reverend Mr. Upton seemed disappointed. He had played the role of martyr so long he resented seeing anyone else in the part.

===Chinese===
他这才知道，他的舱房要同两位传教士合住。一位是高个子、阴沉沉的老头，一张柠檬色的脸——海外传教区一位德高望重的老前辈，名叫厄普顿（Upton）。另一位是个红脸膛的小伙子，他的殉道之途还在前头。约翰·昆西提议抽签挑铺位，可就连这种顶温和的赌法，两位教会使者看来也觉得有伤大雅。

「你们两个孩子睡铺位，」厄普顿说。「睡榻留给我。反正我也睡不安稳。」他那口气，活脱是个甘愿受苦的人。

约翰·昆西客气地推让了一番。又商量了一阵，最后议定：他睡上铺，老人睡下铺，小伙子睡睡榻。厄普顿牧师似乎还挺失望。他扮殉道者扮了这么多年，眼看别人顶了这个角色，心里老大不痛快。

===Original===
The Pacific was behaving in a most unfriendly manner, tossing the great ship about as though it were a piece of driftwood. John Quincy decided to dispense with lunch, and spent the afternoon reading in his berth. By evening he felt better, and under the watchful and somewhat disapproving eyes of the missionaries, arrayed himself carefully for dinner.

His name being Winterslip, he had been invited to sit at the captain’s table. He found Madame Maynard, serene and twinkling, at the captain’s right, Barbara at his left, and Jennison at Barbara’s side. It appeared that oddly enough there was an aristocracy of the Islands, and John Quincy, while he thought it quaint there should be such distinctions in an outpost like Hawaii, took his proper place as a matter of course.

===Chinese===
太平洋表现得极不友善，把大轮船颠来簸去，好像它不过是一截浮木。约翰·昆西决定午饭免了，一下午躺在铺位上看书。傍晚时分他觉得好受了些，便在两位传教士留神而颇含非难的目光下，细细穿戴齐整，去进晚餐。

他既姓温特斯利普，便被邀在船长席用膳。他发现梅纳德夫人安详娴雅、眼波闪闪，坐在船长右首；芭芭拉坐左首，詹尼森挨着芭芭拉。说来也怪，岛上居然也有一个贵族阶层，而约翰·昆西一面觉得，在夏威夷这样的天涯前哨竟还讲这等门第，未免古雅，一面照例坐进了自己应得的位置。

===Original===
Mrs. Maynard chatted brightly of her many trips over this route. Suddenly she turned to Barbara. “How does it happen, my dear,” she asked, “that you’re not on the college boat?”

“All booked up,” Barbara explained.

“Nonsense,” said the frank old lady. “*You* could have got on. But then”—she looked meaningly toward Jennison—“I presume this ship was not without its attraction.”

The girl flushed slightly and made no reply.

“What,” John Quincy inquired, “is the college boat?”

===Chinese===
梅纳德夫人兴致勃勃地谈着她在这条航线上的多次旅行。忽然她转身向着芭芭拉。「亲爱的，这是怎么搞的，」她问，「你怎么没搭『大学船』？」

「都订满了，」芭芭拉解释道。

「胡说，」心直口快的老妇人说。「你要想上，总上得去的。不过嘛」——她意味深长地朝詹尼森瞟了一眼——「我估摸着，这条船也自有它的吸引力。」

姑娘微微红了脸，没有作声。

「什么叫『大学船』？」约翰·昆西问。

===Original===
“So many children from Hawaii at school on the mainland,” the old lady explained, “that every June around this time they practically fill a ship. We call it the college boat. This year it’s the *Matsonia*. She left San Francisco today at noon.”

“I’ve got a lot of friends aboard her,” Barbara said. “I do wish we could beat her in. Captain, what are the chances?”

“Well, that depends,” replied the captain cautiously.

“She isn’t due until Tuesday morning,” Barbara persisted. “Wouldn’t it be a lark if you could land us the night before? As a favor to me, Captain.”

===Chinese===
「夏威夷在大陆上念书的孩子那么多，」老妇人解释道，「每年六月赶这时候，他们差不多能坐满一整条船。我们管它叫『大学船』。今年是马特森尼亚号（*Matsonia*）。她今天正午驶离了旧金山。」

「我有一大帮朋友在那条船上，」芭芭拉说。「真盼着咱们能赶在她前头进港。船长，有多大把握？」

「唔，那得看情况，」船长谨慎地答道。

「她按班期礼拜二早上才到，」芭芭拉不肯罢休。「要是您能提前一夜让我们上岸，那该多带劲！就当帮我个忙，船长。」

===Original===
“When you look at me like that,” smiled the officer, “I can only say that I’ll make a supreme effort. I’m just as eager as you to make port on Monday—it would mean I could get off to the Orient that much sooner.”

“Then it’s settled,” Barbara beamed.

“It’s settled that we’ll try,” he said. “Of course, if I speed up there’s always the chance I may arrive off Honolulu after sundown, and be compelled to lay by until morning. That would be torture for you.”

“I’ll risk it,” Barbara smiled. “Wouldn’t dear old dad be pleased if I should burst upon his vision Monday evening?”

“My dear girl,” the captain said gallantly, “any man would be pleased to have you burst upon his vision any time.”

===Chinese===
「你这么望着我，」船长微笑道，「我就只能说：我一定尽最大的努力。我跟你们一样巴望礼拜一进港——那样我也能早些动身去东方。」

「那就说定了，」芭芭拉笑靥如花。

「说定的是我们尽力，」他说。「当然，我一加速，就总有这个可能：日落之后才抵达檀香山外海，只好停船等到天亮。那你们可要活受罪了。」

「这险我甘愿冒，」芭芭拉微笑道。「要是我礼拜一晚上突然显现在亲爱的老爸眼前，他该多高兴呀！」

「亲爱的姑娘，」船长殷勤地说，「任何一个男人，任什么时候让你突然显现在他眼前，都会高兴的。」

===Original===
There was, John Quincy reflected, much in what the captain said. Up to that moment there had been little of the romantic in his relations with girls; he was accustomed to look upon them merely as tennis or golf opponents or a fourth at bridge. Barbara would demand a different classification. There was an enticing gleam in her blue eyes, a hint of the eternal feminine in everything she did or said, and John Quincy was no wooden man. He was glad that when he left the dinner table, she accompanied him.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西心想，船长这话颇有道理。在此之前，他同姑娘们的交往里很少有罗曼蒂克的成分；他习惯把她们只当作网球或高尔夫球场上的对手，或是桥牌桌上凑一角的第四家。芭芭拉却须另归一类。她蓝眼睛里有一泓诱人的光彩，一言一行都透着永恒女性的意味，而约翰·昆西又不是木头人。他离席时她陪伴着他一同走出来，他为此感到高兴。

===Original===
They went on deck and stood by the rail. Night had fallen, there was no moon, and it seemed to John Quincy that the Pacific was the blackest, angriest ocean he had ever seen. He stood gazing at it gloomily.

“Homesick, John Quincy?” Barbara asked. One of his hands was resting on the rail. She laid her own upon it.

He nodded. “It’s a funny thing. I’ve been abroad a lot, but I never felt like this. When the ship left port this morning, I nearly wept.”

“It’s not so very funny,” she said gently. “This is an alien world you’re entering now. Not Boston, John Quincy, nor any other old, civilized place. Not the kind of place where the mind rules. Out here it’s the heart that charts our course. People you’re fond of do the wildest, most unreasonable things, simply because their minds are sleeping and their hearts are beating fast. Just—just remember, please, John Quincy.”

There was an odd note of wistfulness in her voice. Suddenly at their side appeared the white-clad figure of Harry Jennison.

===Chinese===
他们登上甲板，倚着栏杆站定。夜幕已落，没有月亮，在约翰·昆西看来，这太平洋是他生平见过的最黑、最愤怒的海洋。他郁郁地凝望着它。

「想家了，约翰·昆西？」芭芭拉问。他一只手正搭在栏杆上，她把自己的手轻轻覆了上去。

他点点头。「说来也怪。我出国不止一趟了，可从来没有过这种感觉。今天早上船离港的时候，我差一点儿哭出来。」

「这也并不怎么奇怪，」她温柔地说。「你如今走进的是一个全然陌生的世界。不是波士顿，约翰·昆西，也不是别的什么古老的、文明的地方。不是理性当家做主的地方。在这儿，替我们定航向的是心。你心爱的人会干出最狂乱、最没道理的事来，只因脑子睡熟了，心却跳得飞快。请——请你记住，约翰·昆西。」

她嗓音里有一缕说不出的怅惘。哈里·詹尼森一身白衣的身影忽然出现在两人身旁。

===Original===
“Coming for a stroll, Barbara?” he inquired.

For a moment she did not reply. Then she nodded. “Yes,” she said. And called over her shoulder as she went: “Cheer up, John Quincy.”

He watched her go, reluctantly. She might have stayed to assuage his loneliness. But there she walked along the dim deck, close to Jennison’s side.

===Chinese===
「去散散步吗，芭芭拉？」他问。

她一时没有答话。随后点了点头。「去，」她说。一边走一边回头喊道：「打起精神来，约翰·昆西。」

他不情愿地目送她走远。她本可以留下来，为他排遣这寂寞的。可她偏偏沿着昏暗的甲板走远了，紧挨着詹尼森的身边。

===Original===
After a time, he sought the smoking-room. It was deserted, but on one of the tables lay a copy of the *Boston Transcript*. Delighted, John Quincy pounced upon it, as Robinson Crusoe might have pounced on news from home.

The issue was ten days old, but no matter. He turned at once to the financial pages. There it was, like the face of a well-beloved friend, the record of one day’s trading on the Stock Exchange. And up in one corner, the advertisement of his own banking house, offering an issue of preferred stock in a Berkshire cotton mill. He read eagerly, but with an odd detached feeling. He was gone, gone from that world, away out here on a black ocean bound for picture-book islands. Islands where, not so long ago, brown tribes had battled, brown kings ruled. There seemed no link with that world back home, those gay-colored streamers of confetti breaking so readily had been a symbol. He was adrift. What sort of port would claim him in the end?

===Chinese===
过了一阵，他进了吸烟室。里面空无一人，只有一张桌上摊着一份《波士顿纪事报》（*Boston Transcript*）。约翰·昆西喜出望外，一把扑了上去，就像鲁滨逊·克鲁索扑向一纸家乡的消息似的。

那份报已是十天前的了，可不要紧。他立刻翻到财经版。它就在那儿，像一位亲爱朋友的眉眼——证券交易所一天的交易实录。角上还登着他自家银行的广告，正为伯克希尔（Berkshire）一家棉纺厂承销一批优先股。他读得津津有味，心里却有一种奇怪的隔膜。他已经走了，离开那个世界了，远远漂在这片黑色的大洋上，驶往画册里才有的岛屿。在那些岛上，就在不算太久以前，棕色皮肤的部族还在厮杀，棕肤的君王还在临朝。故乡那个世界似乎已同他断了牵连，那些一扯就断的艳丽彩带，便是一个象征。他成了漂萍无依的人。到头来，会是哪一处港口来收留他呢？

===Original===
He threw the paper down. The Reverend Mr. Upton entered the smoking-room.

“I left my newspaper here,” he explained. “Ah—did you care to look at it?”

“Thank you, I have,” John Quincy told him.

The old man picked it up in a great bony hand. “I always buy a *Transcript* when I get the chance,” he said. “It carries me back. You know, I was born in Salem, over seventy years ago.”

===Chinese===
他把报纸一撂。厄普顿牧师走进吸烟室。

「我把报纸落在这儿了，」他解释道。「啊——你赏光看过了？」

「多谢，看过了。」约翰·昆西告诉他。

老人用一只骨骼粗大的手把报纸拾起来。「我一有机会就要买份《纪事报》，」他说。「它带我回到从前。你可知道，七十多年前，我生在塞勒姆（Salem）。」

===Original===
John Quincy stared at him. “You’ve been a long time out here?” he asked.

“More than fifty years in the foreign field,” answered the old man. “I was one of the first to go to the South Seas. One of the first to carry the torch down there—and a dim torch it was, I’m afraid. Afterward I was transferred to China.” John Quincy regarded him with a new interest. “By the way, sir,” the missionary continued, “I once met another gentleman named Winterslip. Mr. Daniel Winterslip.”

“Really?” said John Quincy. “He’s a cousin of mine. I’m to visit him in Honolulu.”

“Yes? I heard he had returned to Hawaii, and prospered. I met him just once—in the ’eighties, it was, on a lonely island in the Gilbert group. It was—rather a turning point in his life, and I have never forgotten.” John Quincy waited to hear more, but the old missionary moved away. “I’ll go and enjoy my *Transcript*,” he smiled. “The church news is very competently handled.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西怔怔地望着他。「您来这边很久了吗？」他问。

「在海外传教区待了五十多年，」老人答道。「我是最早去南太平洋诸岛的人之一，最早把火炬送到那边去的人之一——只怕那是一支昏暗的火炬。后来我调去了中国。」约翰·昆西带着新的兴味打量他。「对了，先生，」传教士接着说，「我从前还遇到过另一位姓温特斯利普的先生。丹尼尔·温特斯利普先生。」

「当真？」约翰·昆西说。「他是我的堂叔。我要去檀香山看他。」

「是吗？听说他回了夏威夷，日子过得很兴旺。我同他只见过一面——那是十九世纪八十年代，在吉尔伯特群岛（Gilbert group）中一座荒凉的小岛上。那一次——可以说是他一生的转折点，我一直没有忘记。」约翰·昆西等着听下文，老传教士却走开了。「我去享受我的《纪事报》了，」他微笑道。「教会新闻办得很见功力。」

===Original===
John Quincy rose and went aimlessly outside. A dreary scene, the swish of turbulent waters, dim figures aimless as himself, an occasional ship’s officer hurrying by. His stateroom opened directly on the deck, and he sank into a steamer chair just outside the door.

In the distance he saw his room steward, weaving his way in and out of the cabins under his care. The man was busy with his last duties for the night, refilling water carafes, laying out towels, putting things generally to rights.

“Evening, sir,” he said as he entered John Quincy’s room. Presently he came and stood in the door, the cabin light at his back. He was a small man with gold-rimmed eyeglasses and a fierce gray pompadour.

“Everything OK, Mr. Winterslip?” he inquired.

“Yes, Bowker,” smiled John Quincy. “Everything’s fine.”

“That’s good,” said Bowker. He switched off the cabin light and stepped out on to the deck. “I aim to take particular care of you, sir. Saw your home town on the sailing list. I’m an old Boston man myself.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西站起身，漫无目的地走到外面。一派凄清光景：浊浪翻卷的哗哗声，同他一样没目的的朦胧人影，偶尔有个船上的官员匆匆走过。他的舱房门正开在甲板上，他便在门外一张船用躺椅上陷坐下去。

远远地他望见他舱房的侍者，正穿行于他照管的各舱之间。那人忙着做就寝前最后几桩事：给水瓶续水，铺毛巾，把各处归置齐整。

「晚上好，先生。」他走进约翰·昆西舱房时说。不多时他出来站在门口，舱灯在他背后。他是个小个子男人，戴金丝边眼镜，一头灰白的头发怒冲冲地高高耸起。

「一切都妥帖吗，温特斯利普先生？」他问。

「妥帖，鲍克（Bowker），」约翰·昆西微笑道。「一切都好。」

「那就好，」鲍克说。他熄了舱灯，跨步走上甲板。「我打算格外照应您，先生。在乘客名单上看见了您的家乡。我自己也是个波士顿老人儿。」

===Original===
“Is that so?” said John Quincy cordially. Evidently the Pacific was a Boston suburb.

“Not born there, I don’t mean,” the man went on. “But a newspaper man there for ten years. It was just after I left the University.”

John Quincy stared through the dark. “Harvard?” he asked.

“Dublin,” said the steward. “Yes, sir—” He laughed an embarrassed little laugh. “You might not think it now, but the University of Dublin, Class of 1901. And after that, for ten years, working in Boston on the *Gazette*—reporting, copy desk, managing editor for a time. Maybe I bumped into you there—at the Adams House bar, say, on a night before a football game.”

“Quite possible,” admitted John Quincy. “One bumped into so many people on such occasions.”

===Chinese===
「当真？」约翰·昆西热络地说。看来太平洋不过是波士顿的一个郊区。

「不是生在那儿，我不是那个意思，」那人接着说。「是在那儿做了十年报人，刚出大学校门不久的事。」

约翰·昆西在黑暗中端详他。「哈佛？」他问。

「都柏林，」侍者答。「是的，先生——」他尴尬地小小笑了一声。「如今您怕是看不出来：都柏林大学，1901 级。打那以后，在波士顿《公报》（*Gazette*）馆里做了十年——采访、改稿台，还当过一阵编辑主任。兴许在哪儿同您撞见过——比方说，橄榄球赛前一晚，在亚当斯楼（Adams House）的酒吧里。」

「大有可能，」约翰·昆西承认。「那种日子，这种场合撞见的人可多着呢。」

===Original===
“Don’t I know it?” Mr. Bowker leaned on the rail, in reminiscent mood. “Great times, sir. Those were the good old days when a newspaper man who wasn’t tanked up was a reproach to a grand profession. The *Gazette* was edited mostly from a place called the Arch Inn. We’d bring our copy to the city editor there—he had a regular table—a bit sloppy on top, but his desk. If we had a good story, maybe he’d stand us a cocktail.”

John Quincy laughed.

“Happy days,” continued the Dublin graduate, with a sigh. “I knew every bartender in Boston well enough to borrow money. Were you ever in that place in the alley back of the Tremont Theater—?”

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛！」鲍克先生倚着栏杆，一副追忆往昔的神情。「好时光啊，先生。那是黄金岁月：干报人的要是没灌得醉醺醺，简直是给这门堂皇的行当丢脸。《公报》多半是在一家叫『拱门客栈』（Arch Inn）的地方编出来的。我们把稿子送到那儿交给本市新闻主编——他有一张固定坐的桌子——桌面总有点汤汤水水，可那就算他的写字台。我们要是跑来了好新闻，兴许还能讨他赏一杯鸡尾酒。」

约翰·昆西笑了。

「快活的日子哟，」这位都柏林大学的毕业生接着说，叹了口气。「波士顿的每个酒保，我都熟到了能开口借钱的地步。特里蒙特剧院（Tremont Theater）后巷那家酒馆——您去过没有——？」

===Original===
“Tim’s place,” suggested John Quincy, recalling an incident of college days.

“Yeah, bo. Now you’re talking. I wonder what became of Tim. Say, and there was that place on Boylston—but they’re all gone now, of course. An old pal I met in ’Frisco was telling me it would break your heart to see the cobwebs on the mirrors back in Beantown. Gone to the devil, just like my profession. The newspapers go on consolidating, doubling up, combining the best features of both, and an army of good men go on the town. Good men and true, moaning about the vanished days and maybe landing in jobs like this one of mine.” He was silent for a moment. “Well, sir, anything I can do for you—as a mutual friend of Tim’s—”

===Chinese===
「蒂姆（Tim）的那家店？」约翰·昆西提示道，想起大学时代的一段旧事。

「是啊，老兄。这话说着了。也不知道蒂姆后来怎么样了。嗨，还有博伊尔斯顿街（Boylston）上那一家——如今当然全都没了。我在旧金山碰到一个老朋友，他说，回豆城（波士顿的昵称）去瞧瞧镜子上的蛛网，保管叫你心碎。全完啦，跟我这门行当一个样。报纸一家接一家兼并、搭伙，把两家的拿手好戏合到一处，一大群好汉子就此流落市井。都是好样儿的正派人，哀叹着逝去的好时光，末了兴许就落到干我这份差事。」他沉默了片刻。「好了，先生，有什么用得着我的——看在咱们都是蒂姆的朋友份上——」

===Original===
“As a friend of Tim’s,” smiled John Quincy, “I’ll not hesitate to mention it.”

Sadly Bowker went on down the deck. John Quincy sat lonely again. A couple passed, walking close, talking in low tones. He recognized Jennison and his cousin. “Between us we ought to be able to keep this young woman entertained,” Jennison had said. Well, John Quincy reflected, his portion of the entertainment promised to be small.

===Chinese===
「看在蒂姆的朋友份上，」约翰·昆西微笑道，「有事我绝不客气。」

鲍克怏怏地沿甲板走远了。约翰·昆西又孤零零一个人了。一对人影走过，挨得很近，低声交谈着。他认出是詹尼森和他的堂妹。「咱们俩搭伙，保管陪得这位小姐开开心心，」詹尼森当时说过这话。唉，约翰·昆西心想，分到他名下的那份开心，看来少得可怜。

## V The Blood of the Winterslips / 五 温特斯利普家的血脉

===Original===
The days that followed proved that he was right. He seldom had a moment alone with Barbara; when he did, Jennison seemed always to be hovering nearby, and he did not long delay making the group a threesome. At first John Quincy resented this, but gradually he began to feel that it didn’t matter.

===Chinese===
此后几天的情形，证明他想得没错。他难得有同芭芭拉单独相处的时刻；偶尔有了，詹尼森又总像在近旁盘旋，用不了多久便凑上来，把两个人变成三个人。起初约翰·昆西还为此恼火，渐渐地却觉得，这也没什么要紧了。

===Original===
Nothing appeared to matter any more. A great calm had settled over the waters and over John Quincy’s soul. The Pacific was one vast sheet of glass, growing a deeper blue with every passing hour. They seemed to be floating in space in a world where nothing ever happened, nothing could happen. Quiet restful days gave way to long brilliant nights. A little walk, a little talk, and that was life.

===Chinese===
仿佛再没有什么要紧事了。一片浩大的安宁笼罩着海面，也笼罩了约翰·昆西的心。太平洋成了一整幅巨大的玻璃，时辰每过一刻，那蓝色便更深一层。他们像漂浮在太空里，漂浮在一个万事不曾发生、也万事不会发生的世界。静谧安闲的白昼，让位给漫长明亮的夜晚。散一会儿步，聊一会儿天，这就是生活。

===Original===
Sometimes John Quincy chatted with Madame Maynard on the deck. She who had known the Islands so many years had fascinating tales to tell, tales of the monarchy and the missionaries. The boy liked her immensely, she was a New Englander at heart despite her glamourous lifetime in Hawaii.

===Chinese===
有时约翰·昆西在甲板上同梅纳德夫人（Madame Maynard）聊天。她在这些岛上住了这么多年，肚里有讲不完的迷人故事，王国的故事，传教士的故事。小伙子打心眼里喜欢她——尽管她在夏威夷过了一辈子光鲜日子，骨子里仍是个新英格兰人。

===Original===
Bowker, too, he found excellent company. The steward was that rarity even among college graduates, an educated man; there was no topic upon which he could not discourse at length and brilliantly. In John Quincy’s steamer trunk were a number of huge imposing volumes﻿—books he had been meaning to tackle long ago, but it was Bowker who read them, not John Quincy.

===Chinese===
鲍克也是极好的伴儿。这位乘务员是连大学毕业生堆里也少见的珍品——一个真正受过教育的人；无论什么题目，他都能滔滔不绝、头头是道地讲上一大篇。约翰·昆西的船用衣箱里塞着好几部气势逼人的大部头——那些他早就打算啃却一直没啃的书——不过啃它们的是鲍克，不是约翰·昆西。

===Original===
As the days slipped by, the blue of the water deepened to ultramarine, the air grew heavier and warmer. Underfoot throbbed the engines that were doing their best for Barbara and an early landing. The captain was optimistic, he predicted they would make port late Monday afternoon. But Sunday night a fierce sudden storm swept down upon them, and lashed the ship with a wet fury until dawn. When the captain appeared at luncheon Monday noon, worn by a night on the bridge, he shook his head.

===Chinese===
日子一天天溜过去，海水的蓝深成了群青色，空气越来越沉，越来越暖。脚底下的轮机突突震颤，为着芭芭拉、也为着早些登岸而竭尽全力。船长是乐观的，预言船将于周一下午晚些时候进港。不料星期天夜里，一场又急又猛的风暴兜头压了下来，带着湿漉漉的狂怒抽打轮船，一直抽到天明。星期一正午船长出现在午餐桌上时，在驾驶台上熬了一夜，一脸倦容，直摇头。

===Original===
“We’ve lost our bet, Miss Barbara,” he said. “I can’t possibly arrive off Hololulu before midnight.”

===Chinese===
「咱们那场赌打输了，芭芭拉小姐，」他说。「午夜之前，我说什么也到不了檀香山外海了。」

===Original===
Barbara frowned. “But ships sail at any hour,” she reminded him. “I don’t see why﻿—if we sent radios ahead﻿—”

===Chinese===
芭芭拉皱起眉。「可船几点钟都开呀，」她提醒他。「我不明白为什么——要是咱们提前发无线电报——」

===Original===
“No use,” he told her. “The Quarantine people keep early hours. No, I’ll have to lay by near the channel entrance until official sunrise﻿—about six. We’ll get in ahead of the *Matsonia* in the morning. That’s the best I can offer you.”

===Chinese===
「没用的，」他告诉她。「检疫所的人只赶早。唉，我只好在航道入口附近停船等着，一直等到官方的日出时刻——大约六点钟。明早咱们能抢在马特森尼亚号（*Matsonia*）前头进港。我能给您的，顶多就是这样了。」

===Original===
“You’re a dear, anyhow,” Barbara smiled. “That old storm wasn’t your fault. We’ll drown our sorrow tonight with one last glorious dance﻿—a costume party.” She turned to Jennison. “I’ve got the loveliest fancy dress﻿—Marie Antoinette﻿—I wore it at college. What do you say, Harry?”

===Chinese===
「不管怎样，您这人真好，」芭芭拉笑道。「那阵老风暴又不是您的错。今晚咱们借最后一场痛痛快快的舞会浇浇愁——开个化装舞会。」她转向詹尼森。「我有一件顶顶好看的化装裙子——玛丽·安托瓦内特——上大学时穿过的。你说呢，哈里？」

===Original===
“Fine!” Jennison answered. “We can all dig up some sort of costume. Let’s go.”

===Chinese===
「好啊！」詹尼森应道。「咱们谁都能翻出件把行头来。走吧。」

===Original===
Barbara hurried off to spread the news. After dinner that evening she appeared, a blonde vision straight from the French Court, avid for dancing. Jennison had rigged up an impromptu pirate dress, and was a striking figure. Most of the other passengers had donned weird outfits; on the Pacific boats a fancy dress party is warmly welcomed and amusingly carried out.

===Chinese===
芭芭拉匆匆跑去传消息。当晚晚饭之后她露面了，活脱脱一位从法兰西宫廷里走出来的金发幻影，一门心思只想跳舞。詹尼森凑了一身即兴的海盗装，模样着实惹眼。其余乘客大半也穿上了千奇百怪的行头；在太平洋航线的船上，化装舞会从来大受欢迎，人人办得妙趣横生。

===Original===
John Quincy took small part in the gaiety, for he still suffered from New England inhibitions. At a little past eleven he drifted into the main saloon and found Madame Maynard seated there alone.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西不大掺和这份热闹，他身上还压着新英格兰的清规戒律。十一点刚过，他晃进大客厅，见梅纳德夫人独自坐在那里。

===Original===
“Hello,” she said. “Come to keep me company? I’ve sworn not to go to bed until I see the light on Diamond Head.”

“I’m with you,” John Quincy smiled.

“But you ought to be dancing, boy. And you’re not in costume.”

===Chinese===
「你好，」她说。「来陪我坐坐？我发过誓了，不见着钻石头山上的灯，就不上床。」

「我奉陪，」约翰·昆西笑着说。

「可你该去跳舞呀，孩子。再说你也没穿化装衣裳。」

===Original===
“No,” admitted John Quincy. He paused, seeking an explanation. “A﻿—a fellow can’t make a fool of himself in front of a lot of strangers.”

“I understand,” nodded the old lady. “It’s a fine delicacy, too. But rather rare, particularly out this way.”

===Chinese===
「没穿，」约翰·昆西承认。他顿了顿，想找个说法。「有——有个人总不能当着一帮生人出洋相吧。」

「我懂，」老太太点点头。「这份细腻的顾忌也是桩好东西。只是相当难得，尤其是在这一带。」

===Original===
Barbara entered, flushed and vibrant. “Harry’s gone to get me a drink,” she panted. She sat down beside Mrs. Maynard. “I’ve been looking for you, my dear. You know, you haven’t read my palm since I was a child. She’s simply wonderful﻿—” this to John Quincy. “Can tell you the most amazing things.”

===Chinese===
芭芭拉进来了，红光满面，神采飞扬。「哈里给我取酒去了，」她喘着气说。她在梅纳德夫人身边坐下。「我正找您呢，亲爱的。您知道吗，打从我还是个娃娃起，您就没给我看过手相。她简直神极了——」这话是对约翰·昆西说的。「什么惊人的事儿都看得出来。」

===Original===
Mrs. Maynard vehemently shook her head. “I don’t read ’em any more,” she said. “Gave it up. As I’ve grown older, I’ve come to understand how foolish it is to peer into the future. Today﻿—that’s enough for me. That’s all I care to think about.”

===Chinese===
梅纳德夫人使劲摇头。「手相我再也不看了，」她说。「戒了。人上了年纪，我渐渐明白，去窥探将来是何等愚蠢。今天——这于我就够了。我只肯想今天的事。」

===Original===
“Oh, please,” the girl pouted.

The old woman took Barbara’s slim hand in hers, and studied the palm for a moment. John Quincy thought he saw a shadow cross her face. Again she shook her head.

===Chinese===
「哦，求您啦，」姑娘噘起了嘴。

老妇人把芭芭拉那只纤纤素手握在自己手里，端详掌心片刻。约翰·昆西仿佛看见一道阴影掠过她的脸。她又摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Carpe diem,” she said. “Which my nephew once translated as ‘grab the day.’ Dance and be happy tonight, and let’s not try to look behind the curtain. It doesn’t pay, my dear. Take an old woman’s word for that.”

===Chinese===
「及时行乐（Carpe diem），」她说。「这话我有个侄儿曾翻译成『抓住眼下这一天』。今晚只管跳、只管乐，咱们别去掀那道帘子偷看后头。那不划算，亲爱的。信我这老婆子一句话。」

===Original===
Harry Jennison appeared in the door. “Oh, here you are,” he said. “I’ve got your drink waiting in the smoking-room.”

“I’m coming,” the girl said, and went. The old woman stared after her.

===Chinese===
哈里·詹尼森（Harry Jennison）出现在门口。「哦，你们在这儿，」他说。「您的酒我在吸烟室里搁着呢。」

「来了，」姑娘说着走了。老妇人目送她的背影。

===Original===
“Poor Barbara,” she murmured. “Her mother’s life was none too happy, either﻿—”

“You saw something in her hand﻿—” John Quincy suggested.

===Chinese===
「可怜的芭芭拉，」她喃喃道。「她母亲的那一辈子，也算不上幸福——」

「您在她掌心里看出了什么——」约翰·昆西提了一句。

===Original===
“No matter,” the old lady snapped. “There’s trouble waiting for us all, if we look far enough ahead. Now, let’s go on deck. It’s getting on toward midnight.”

===Chinese===
「不碍事，」老太太厉声说。「只要往前看得够远，咱们人人头上都候着麻烦呢。走，上甲板去吧。快到半夜了。」

===Original===
She led him out to the starboard rail. A solitary light, like a star, gleamed in the distance. Land, land at last. “Diamond Head?” John Quincy asked.

===Chinese===
她领他出去，走到右舷栏杆边。远处一盏孤灯，像颗星，幽幽地亮着。陆地，终于见到陆地了。「那是钻石头山吗？」约翰·昆西问。

===Original===
“No,” she said. “That’s the beacon on Makapuu Point. We shall have to round Koko Head before we sight Honolulu.” She stood for a moment by the rail, one frail hand resting upon it. “But that’s Oahu,” she said gently. “That’s home. A sweet land, boy. Too sweet, I often think. I hope you’ll like it.”

===Chinese===
「不是，」她说。「那是玛卡普乌角（Makapuu Point）上的灯塔。还得绕过科科岬（Koko Head），咱们才望得见檀香山。」她在栏杆边伫立了片刻，一只瘦弱的手搭在栏杆上。「可那片就是瓦胡岛（Oahu）了，」她温柔地说。「那就是家了。一片甜美的土地，孩子。我常想，甜美得过了头。希望你会喜欢它。」

===Original===
“I’m sure I shall,” replied John Quincy gallantly.

===Chinese===
「我相信一定会的，」约翰·昆西殷勤地答道。

===Original===
“Let’s sit down here.” They found deck chairs. “Yes, a dear land,” she went on. “But we’re all sorts, in Hawaii﻿—just as it is the whole world over﻿—honest folks and rascals. From the four corners of the globe men come to us﻿—often because they were no longer welcome at home. We offer them a paradise, and some repay us by becoming good citizens, while others rot away. I often think it will take a lot of stamina to make good in Heaven﻿—and Hawaii is the same.”

===Chinese===
「咱们在这儿坐坐吧。」两人寻了甲板躺椅坐下。「是啊，一片可爱的土地，」她接着说。「可我们夏威夷什么人都有——同全世界一个样——有正派人，也有恶棍。天涯海角的人投奔我们而来——常常是因为在家乡已经不受欢迎了。我们给他们一个天堂，有的人报答我们，做了好公民；有的人却烂了下去。我常想，将来要在天堂里混出个名堂，也非得有副好筋骨不可——夏威夷就是这样。」

===Original===
The tall emaciated figure of the Reverend Mr. Upton appeared before them. He bowed. “Good evening, Madame. You’re nearly home.”

“Yes,” she said. “Glad of it, too.”

===Chinese===
厄普顿牧师（the Reverend Mr. Upton）那瘦高干瘪的身影出现在他们面前。他鞠了一躬。「晚上好，夫人。您快到家了。」

「是啊，」她说。「巴不得呢。」

===Original===
He turned to John Quincy. “You’ll be seeing Dan Winterslip in the morning, young man.”

“I expect I shall,” John Quincy replied.

===Chinese===
他转向约翰·昆西。「明天早上您就要见到丹·温特斯利普了，年轻人。」

「我料想会见到的，」约翰·昆西答道。

===Original===
“Just ask him if he recalls that day on Apiang Island in the ’eighties. The Reverend Frank Upton.”

===Chinese===
「只请您问问他，还记不记得八十年代阿皮昂岛（Apiang Island）上的那一天。就提弗兰克·厄普顿牧师。」

===Original===
“Of course,” replied John Quincy. “But you haven’t told me much about it, you know.”

“No, I haven’t.” The missionary dropped into a chair. “I don’t like to reveal any secrets about a man’s past,” he said. “However, I understand that the story of Dan Winterslip’s early life has always been known in Honolulu.” He glanced toward Madame Maynard.

===Chinese===
「当然，」约翰·昆西应道。「不过，那件事您还没怎么跟我讲呢。」

「是啊，没讲过。」传教士一屁股坐进椅子里。「我不爱揭人过去的老底，」他说。「不过，据我所知，丹·温特斯利普早年的经历，在檀香山本来就不是什么秘密。」他朝梅纳德夫人瞟了一眼。

===Original===
“Dan was no saint,” she remarked. “We all know that.”

He crossed his thin legs. “As a matter of fact, I’m very proud of my meeting with Dan Winterslip,” he went on. “I feel that in my humble way I persuaded him to change his course﻿—for the better.”

===Chinese===
「丹可不是圣徒，」她说。「这我们全知道。」

他跷起两条细腿。「说实话，同丹·温特斯利普的那次会面，我很引以为荣，」他接着说。「我觉得，我以自己微不足道的方式，劝得他改了航道——往好里改的。」

===Original===
“Humph,” said the old lady. She was dubious, evidently.

John Quincy was not altogether pleased at the turn the conversation had taken. He did not care to have the name of a Winterslip thus bandied about. But to his annoyance, the Reverend Mr. Upton was continuing.

===Chinese===
「哼，」老太太说。她显然不很信服。

话题转到这上面来，约翰·昆西心里并不痛快。他不愿温特斯利普家的姓氏就这样被人挂在嘴上嚼。可恼的是，厄普顿牧师偏偏还讲个没完。

===Original===
“It was in the ’eighties, as I told you,” said the missionary. “I had a lonely station on Apiang, in the Gilbert group. One morning a brig anchored just beyond the reef, and a boat came ashore. Of course, I joined the procession of natives down to the beach to meet it. I saw few enough men of my own race.

===Chinese===
「那是八十年代的事，我方才说了，」传教士说。「当年我在吉尔伯特群岛（Gilbert group）的阿皮昂岛上，独守着一处冷清的传教站。一天早晨，一艘双桅船在礁盘外抛了锚，一条小艇往岸上划来。我自然随着土人的行列下到滩头去迎接。能见到自己同种的人，在我可是稀罕事。

===Original===
“There was a ruffianly crew aboard, in charge of a dapper, rather handsome young white man. And I saw, even before they beached her, midway in the boat, a long pine box.

===Chinese===
「艇上是伙凶悍的水手，领头的是个衣着利落、相当英俊的白人青年。还没等小艇抢上滩，我就看见了艇当腰那口长长的松木箱。

===Original===
“The white man introduced himself. He said he was First Officer Winterslip, of the brig *Maid of Shiloh*. And when he mentioned the name of the ship, of course I knew at once. Knew her unsavory trade and history. He hurried on to say that their captain had died the day before, and they had brought him ashore to bury him on land. It had been the man’s last wish.

===Chinese===
「那白人自报了家门。他说自己是双桅船希洛少女号（*Maid of Shiloh*）的大副温特斯利普。他一提船名，我当下就明白了。明白她那桩不干净的名声和来历。他紧接着说，他们的船长前一天死了，他们把他抬上岸来，要葬在陆地上。那是死者最后的遗愿。

===Original===
“Well.” The Reverend Mr. Upton stared at the distant shore line of Oahu. “I looked over at that rough pine box﻿—four Malay sailors were carrying it ashore. ‘So Tom Brade’s in there,’ I said. Young Winterslip nodded. ‘He’s in there, right enough,’ he answered. And I knew I was looking on at the final scene in the career of a famous character of the South Seas, a callous brute who knew no law, a pirate and adventurer, the master of the notorious *Maid of Shiloh*. Tom Brade, the blackbirder.”

===Chinese===
「唉。」厄普顿牧师望着瓦胡岛远远的海岸线。「我朝那口粗陋的松木箱望去——四个马来水手正抬着它上岸。『这么说，汤姆·布雷德（Tom Brade）就在里头了，』我说。年轻的温特斯利普点点头。『在里头，错不了，』他答道。我心里明白，我正在目睹南海一位鼎鼎大名的人物一生事业的最后一幕——一个不知王法为何物的狠毒恶棍，海盗兼冒险家，那艘恶名昭彰的希洛少女号的船长。贩奴船员汤姆·布雷德。」

===Original===
“Blackbirder?” queried John Quincy.

===Chinese===
「贩奴船员？」约翰·昆西问。

===Original===
The missionary smiled. “Ah, yes﻿—you come from Boston. A blackbirder, my boy, is a shipping-master who furnishes contract labor to the plantations at so much a head. It’s pretty well wiped out now, but in the ’eighties! A horrible business﻿—the curse of God was on it. Sometimes the laborers came willingly. Sometimes. But mostly they came at the point of a knife or the muzzle of a gun. A bloody, brutal business.

===Chinese===
传教士微微一笑。「啊，是了——您是波士顿来的。所谓贩奴船员，我的孩子，就是按人头给种植园包送契约劳工的贩子。这行当如今差不多绝迹了，可在八十年代！可怕的营生——上帝的诅咒就压在它上头。有时候，劳工是自己甘愿来的。有时候是这样。可多半，是刀尖押着、枪口顶着押来的。血淋淋、狠巴巴的营生。

===Original===
“Winterslip and his men went up the beach and began to dig a grave under a coconut palm. I followed. I offered to say a prayer. Winterslip laughed﻿—not much use, he said. But there on that bright morning under the palm I consigned to God the soul of a man who had so much to answer for. Winterslip agreed to come to my house for lunch. He told me that save for a recruiting agent who had remained aboard the brig, he was now the only white man on the ship.

===Chinese===
「温特斯利普带着他的人走上滩头，在一棵椰子树下动手挖起墓穴。我跟了过去。我提出念一段祷文。温特斯利普笑了——他说，没多大用处。可就在那个天光明亮的早晨、那棵棕榈树下，我把一个罪债累累的灵魂，交托给了上帝。温特斯利普答应到我家来吃午饭。他告诉我，除了一个还留在双桅船上的招工贩子，此时船上就只剩他一个白人了。

===Original===
“During lunch, I talked to him. He was so young﻿—I discovered this was his first trip. ‘It’s no trade for you,’ I told him. And after a time, he agreed with me. He said he had two hundred blacks under the hatches that he must deliver to a plantation over in the Kings-mill group, and that after he’d done that, he was through. ‘I’ll take the *Maid* back to Sydney, Dominie,’ he promised, ‘and turn her over. Then I’m *pau*. I’m going home to Honolulu.’”

===Chinese===
「午饭桌上，我同他谈了谈。他那么年轻——我这才知道，这是他头一趟跑这种船。『这行当不是你干的，』我对他说。过了一阵，他也认了这话。他说舱盖底下还压着两百名黑人劳工，必须送到金斯米尔群岛（Kings-mill group）那边的一座种植园去，送完这趟，他就洗手不干了。『我要把希洛少女号开回悉尼，牧师先生，』他许诺道，『把船交掉。然后我就帕乌（pau，夏威夷语：完事）了。我要回檀香山的家去。』」

===Original===
The Reverend Mr. Upton rose slowly. “I learned later that he kept his word,” he finished. “Yes, Dan Winterslip went home, and the South Seas saw him no more. I’ve always been a little proud of my part in that decision. I’ve had few rewards. It’s not everywhere that the missionaries have prospered in a worldly way﻿—as they did in Hawaii.” He glanced at Madame Maynard. “But I’ve had satisfactions. And one of them arose from that meeting on the shore at Apiang. It’s long past my bed hour﻿—I must say good night.”

===Chinese===
厄普顿牧师缓缓站起身来。「后来我听说，他说话算话，」他收了尾。「是啊，丹·温特斯利普回了家，南海从此再没见过他。我在那桩抉择里尽的那份力，我一直有点引以为荣。我得的报偿不多。传教士们能在世俗意义上发迹起来——像他们在夏威夷那样——的地方，世上可并不多。」他朝梅纳德夫人瞥了一眼。「不过我也有我的安慰。其中之一，便来自阿皮昂那片海滩上的会面。早过了我该睡的时辰了——我得道声晚安了。」

===Original===
He moved away. John Quincy sat turning this horror over and over in his mind. A Winterslip in the blackbirding business! That was pretty. He wished he was back on Beacon Street.

===Chinese===
他走开了。约翰·昆西坐在那里，把这件骇人的旧事在心里翻来覆去。温特斯利普家的人，居然干过贩奴营生！真是漂亮得很。他真愿自己还待在灯塔街。

===Original===
“Sweet little dig for me,” the old lady was muttering indignantly. “That about the missionaries in Hawaii. And he needn’t be so cocky. If Dan Winterslip dropped blackbirding, it was only because he’d found something more profitable, I fancy.” She stood up suddenly. “At last,” she said.

===Chinese===
「这是存心刺我一下呢，」老太太愤愤地咕哝。「说什么夏威夷的传教士。他也犯不着这么神气活现。依我看，丹·温特斯利普就算撂下了贩奴营生，也不过是寻着了更赚钱的买卖罢了。」她霍地站起身。「总算来了，」她说。

===Original===
John Quincy rose and stood beside her. Far away a faint yellow eye was winking. For a moment the old lady did not speak.

“Well, that’s that,” she said finally, in a low voice. “I’ve seen Diamond Head again. Good night, my boy.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西起身站到她身旁。远处，一只昏黄的眼睛正一眨一眨。有一会儿，老太太没有说话。

「好了，就这样了，」她终于低声说道。「我又见到钻石头山了。晚安，孩子。」

===Original===
“Good night,” John Quincy answered.

He stood alone by the rail. The pace of the *President Tyler* was slowing perceptibly. The moon came from behind a cloud, crept back again. A sort of unholy calm was settling over the hot, airless, deep blue world. The boy felt a strange restlessness in his heart.

===Chinese===
「晚安，」约翰·昆西答道。

他独自站在栏杆边。泰勒总统号的航速明显缓了下来。月亮从云后探出，又悄悄缩了回去。一种邪异的平静，正渐渐罩住这个炎热、无风、深蓝的世界。少年心里生出一种莫名的烦躁。

===Original===
He ascended to the boat deck, seeking a breath of air. There, in a secluded spot, he came upon Barbara and Jennison﻿—and stopped, shocked. His cousin was in the man’s arms, and their bizarre costumes added a weird touch to the scene. They did not see John Quincy, for in their world at that moment there were only two. Their lips were crushed together, fiercely﻿—

===Chinese===
他登上艇甲板，想寻一口透气的风。就在一处僻静角落，他撞见了芭芭拉和詹尼森——猛地顿住脚，惊呆了。他的堂妹正偎在那男人怀里，两人一身古怪的化装行头，给这一幕平添了几分诡异。他们没有看见约翰·昆西，那一刻，他们的世界里只有两个人。两对嘴唇狠狠地压在一起，炽烈如火——

===Original===
John Quincy fled. Good lord! He had kissed a girl or two himself, but it had been nothing like that.

He went to the rail outside his stateroom. Well, what of it? Barbara was nothing to him, a cousin, yes, but one who seemed to belong to an alien race. He had sensed that she was in love with Jennison; this was no surprise. Why did he feel that frustrated pang deep in his heart? He was engaged to Agatha Parker.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西逃开了。天哪！他自己也吻过一两个姑娘，可从没有过那样的吻。

他走到自己舱房外的栏杆旁。得啦，这又算什么？芭芭拉于他本算不得什么，堂妹固然是堂妹，可那样子像是属于另一个人种似的。他早就觉出她爱着詹尼森；这并不出奇。可为什么他心底深处还泛起那阵懊丧的刺痛？他可是同阿加莎·帕克订了婚的人。

===Original===
He gripped the rail, and sought to see again Agatha’s aristocratic face. But it was blurred, indistinct. All Boston was blurred in his memory. The blood of the roaming Winterslips, the blood that led on to blackbirding and hot breathless kisses in the tropic night﻿—was it flowing in his veins too? Oh, lord﻿—he should have stayed at home where he belonged.

===Chinese===
他攥紧栏杆，想把阿加莎那张贵族气的脸重新唤到眼前。可它模糊了，不清楚了。记忆里，整个波士顿都模糊了。流浪的温特斯利普家的血脉——那引着人去贩奴、去热带之夜滚烫窒息的长吻的血脉——是不是也在他的血管里流淌？哦，老天——他本该老老实实待在老家才是。

===Original===
Bowker, the steward, came along. “Well, here we are,” he said. “We’ll anchor in twelve fathoms and wait for the pilot and the doctor in the morning. I heard they’d been having Kona weather out this way, but I imagine this is the tail end of it. There’ll be a moon shortly, and by dawn the old trades will be on the job again, God bless them.”

===Chinese===
乘务员鲍克走了过来。「哎，咱们到了，」他说。「咱们要在十二英寻深的水里抛锚，等明儿一早引水员和检疫医生上来。听说这一阵他们这边闹科纳天气，不过我估摸着，这是场尾巴了。一会儿月亮就出来，天一亮，老信风就又该上岗了，上帝保佑它们。」

===Original===
John Quincy did not speak. “I’ve returned all your books, sir,” the steward went on, “except that one by Adams on *Revolutionary New England*. It’s a mighty interesting work. I intend to finish it tonight, so I can give it to you before you go ashore.”

“Oh, that’s all right,” John Quincy said. He pointed to dim harbor lights in the distance. “Honolulu’s over there, I take it.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西没作声。「您的书我全还上了，先生，」乘务员接着说，「就差亚当斯那本《革命时期的新英格兰》。这书有意思得很。我打算今晚把它读完，好在您上岸之前交还给您。」

「哦，不碍事，」约翰·昆西说。他指向远处朦胧的港口灯火。「檀香山在那边，我猜。」

===Original===
“Yeah﻿—several miles away. A dead town, sir. They roll up the sidewalks at nine. And let me give you a tip. Keep away from the okolehau.”

===Chinese===
「是啊——隔着好几英里呢。一座死气沉沉的城，先生。到九点，人行道就卷起来收摊了。让我给您提个醒：离奥科勒豪（okolehau）远着点。」

===Original===
“The what?” asked John Quincy.

“The okolehau. A drink they sell out here.”

“What’s it made of?”

===Chinese===
「离什么远点？」约翰·昆西问。

「奥科勒豪。这边卖的一种酒。」

「它是拿什么做的？」

===Original===
“There,” said Bowker, “you have the plot for a big mystery story. What *is* it made of? Judging by the smell, of nothing very lovely. A few gulps, and you hit the ceiling of eternity. But oh, boy﻿—when you drop! Keep off it, sir. I’m speaking as one who knows.”

“I’ll keep off it,” John Quincy promised.

===Chinese===
「问到这上头，」鲍克说，「您可就攥住一部长篇侦探小说的情节了。它究竟拿什么做的？照那股气味判断，绝不是什么清雅之物。几口下肚，您就一路蹿上永恒的天花板。可是老兄——等您往下摔的时候！千万别沾它，先生。我这可是过来人的话。」

「我一定不沾它，」约翰·昆西应承道。

===Original===
Bowker disappeared. John Quincy remained by the rail, that restless feeling growing momentarily. The moon was hidden still, the ship crept along through the muggy darkness. He peered across the black waters toward the strange land that awaited him.

Somewhere over there, Dan Winterslip waited for him too. Dan Winterslip, blood relative of the Boston Winterslips, and ex-blackbirder. For the first time, the boy wished he had struck first in that dark attic in San Francisco, wished he had got that strong box and cast it overboard in the night. Who could say what new scandal, what fresh blot on the honored name of Winterslip, might have been averted had he been quicker with his fists?

===Chinese===
鲍克走远了。约翰·昆西还倚在栏杆边，那股躁动一阵紧似一阵。月亮仍藏着脸，轮船在湿闷的黑暗里悄悄爬行。他朝黑色的水面那头张望，望向那片正等着他的陌生土地。

在那片土地的某个地方，丹·温特斯利普也在等着他。丹·温特斯利普，波士顿温特斯利普家的血亲，前贩奴船员。生平头一回，小伙子懊悔自己当初在旧金山那间黑黢黢的阁楼里没有抢先动手，懊悔没把那只铁柜抢到手、趁黑夜里丢下海去。谁说得准呢——倘若他那时拳头再快上一步，温特斯利普这个素负重望的家名，或许就能免掉一场新的丑闻、再添一记新的污点了吧？

===Original===
As John Quincy turned and entered his cabin, he made a firm resolution. He would linger but briefly at this, his journey’s end. A few days to get his breath, perhaps, and then he would set out again for Boston. And Aunt Minerva would go with him, whether she wanted to or not.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西转身进了舱房，心里拿定了主意。在这趟旅程的终点，他只打算小作停留。缓口气，至多待上几天，便再动身回波士顿。米纳瓦姑婆也得跟他一道走——不管她愿不愿意。

## VI Beyond the Bamboo Curtain / 六 竹帘那边

===Original===
Had John Quincy been able to see his Aunt Minerva at that moment, he would not have been so sure that he could persuade her to fall in with his plans. He would, indeed, have been profoundly shocked at the picture presented by his supposedly staid and dignified relative.

For Miss Minerva was sitting on a grass mat in a fragrant garden in the Hawaiian quarter of Honolulu. Pale golden Chinese lanterns, inscribed with scarlet letters, hung above her head. Her neck was garlanded with ropes of buff ginger blossoms twined with maile. The sleepy, sensuous music of ukulele and steel guitar rose on the midnight air and before her, in a cleared space under the date palms, Hawaiian boys and girls were performing a dance she would not be able to describe in great detail when she got back to Beacon Street.

===Chinese===
倘若约翰·昆西此刻能亲眼见到他的米纳瓦姑婆，他就未必还那么有把握，认为自己能说动她依从自己的计划了。说实在的，这位向来端庄持重的亲戚眼下呈现的这副模样，准会叫他大为震惊。

因为此刻，米纳瓦小姐正坐在檀香山夏威夷人聚居区一座芬芳花园的草席上。她头顶悬着一盏盏淡金色的中国灯笼，灯笼上题着猩红的字样。她颈间挂着一串串花环，淡黄的姜花间缠着香藤迈莱（maile）。尤克里里和钢吉他那慵懒而撩人的乐声，升上午夜的空中；她面前，海枣树下清出的一块空地上，夏威夷的小伙子姑娘们正跳着一种舞——日后回到灯塔街，这舞她是没法与人细说的。

===Original===
Miss Minerva was, in her quiet way, very happy. One of the ambitions of her life had been realized, and she was present at a luau, or native Hawaiian feast. Few white people are privileged to attend this intimate ceremony, but Honolulu friends had been invited on this occasion, and had asked her to go with them. At first she had thought she must refuse, for Dan was expecting Barbara and John Quincy on Monday afternoon. When on Monday evening he had informed her that the *President Tyler* would not land its passengers until the next day, she had hastened to the telephone and asked to reconsider her refusal.

And she was glad she had. Before her, on another mat, lay the remnants of a dinner unique in her experience. Dan had called her a good sport, and she had this evening proved him to be correct. Without a qualm she had faced the queer food wrapped in brown bundles, she had tasted everything, poi served in individual calabashes, chicken stewed in coconut milk, squid and shrimps, limu, or seaweed, even raw fish. She would dream tonight!

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐以她静默的方式，快活极了。她平生的一桩夙愿得了却：她亲临了一场卢奥宴（luau），也就是夏威夷人的本土盛宴。鲜有白人得享殊荣，能参与这般亲近的典礼，但这一回主人家请了檀香山的朋友，朋友们又邀她同去。起初她想自己必须回绝，因为丹正等着芭芭拉和约翰·昆西周一下午抵达。待到周一傍晚，丹告知她泰勒总统号（*President Tyler*）要到次日才让乘客上岸，她便赶紧奔向电话，请求收回先前的谢绝。

而她庆幸自己去了。她面前的另一张席上，摊着一餐她平生仅见的残席。丹曾说她有雅量，这一晚她证明他没有说错。那些棕色叶包里裹着的古怪吃食，她毫不畏缩地一一迎了上去，样样都尝了：各人钵中盛的波伊（poi，芋泥），椰汁炖鸡，鱿鱼和虾，利穆（limu，即海藻），甚至连生鱼也不放过。今夜她怕是要做梦了！

===Original===
Now the feasting had given way to the dance. The moonlight was tracing lacy patterns on the lawn, the plaintive wail of the music rose ever louder, the Hawaiian young people, bashful at first in the presence of strangers, were bashful no longer. Miss Minerva closed her eyes and leaned back against the trunk of a tall palm. Even in Hawaiian love songs there is a note of hopeless melancholy, it touched her emotions as no symphony ever could. A curtain was lifted and she was looking into the past, the primitive, barbaric past of these Islands in the days before the white men came.

A long, heartbreaking crescendo, and the music stopped, the swaying bodies of the dancers were momentarily still. It seemed to Miss Minerva’s friends an opportune moment to depart. They entered the house and in the stuffy little parlor, took leave of their brown, smiling host and hostess. The baby whose arrival in the world was the inspiration for the luau awoke for a second and smiled at them too. Outside in the narrow street their car was waiting.

===Chinese===
此刻宴饮已让位于歌舞。月光在草坪上描出蕾丝般的纹样，乐声哀婉的呜咽一阵高过一阵，夏威夷的年轻人起初当着生人还面带腼腆，如今早已腼腆尽褪。米纳瓦小姐阖上双眼，向后倚在一株高耸棕榈的树干上。即便在夏威夷的情歌里，也总有一缕无望的哀愁，它拨动她的心绪，任何交响乐都不曾这样打动过她。一道帷幕掀起来了，她望进这些岛屿的往昔——白人到来之前，那原始而犷悍的往昔。

一段绵长而令人心碎的渐强音，乐声戛然而止，舞者们摇曳的身姿凝定了片刻。在米纳瓦小姐的朋友们看来，这正是告辞的好时机。他们走进屋去，在闷热的小客厅里，向棕肤含笑的男女主人道别。那个小婴孩——他的降生正是这场卢奥宴的缘起——也醒了一瞬，朝他们笑了笑。外面窄窄的街上，他们的汽车正候着。

===Original===
Through silent, deserted Honolulu they motored toward Waikiki. As they passed the Judiciary Building on King Street the clock in the tower struck the hour of one. She had not been out so late, Miss Minerva reflected, since that night when a visiting company sang “Parsifal” at the Boston Opera House.

The iron gates that guarded the drive at Dan’s house were closed. Leaving the car at the curb, Miss Minerva bade her friends good night and started up the walk toward the front door. The evening had thrilled her, and she moved with the long confident stride of youth. Dan’s scarlet garden was shrouded in darkness, for the moon, which had been playing an in-and-out game with the fast-moving clouds all evening, was again obscured. Exotic odors assailed her nostrils, she heard all about her the soft intriguing noises of the tropic night. She really should get to bed, she knew, but with a happy truant feeling she turned from the front walk and went to the side of the house for a last look at the breakers.

===Chinese===
汽车穿过沉寂无人的檀香山，朝怀基基驶去。经过国王街的司法大楼时，塔楼上的钟敲响了一点。米纳瓦小姐寻思，打从外来剧团在波士顿歌剧院献演《帕西法尔》（Parsifal）的那一晚起，她还不曾在外头待到这般晚。

丹宅车道口的铁门紧闭着。米纳瓦小姐在路边下了车，向朋友们道过晚安，便沿着步道朝正门走去。这个夜晚叫她心旌摇荡，她迈着年轻人那种悠长而笃定的步子。丹那猩红的花园已没入黑暗，整个晚上月亮都在同疾走的云彩玩着藏了又现的把戏，此刻又被遮住了。异香直扑她的鼻端，她听得见四面八方都是热带之夜轻柔而撩人的声息。她知道实在该上床了，却怀着逃学学童般的快活，拐下正前的步道，绕到房子侧面，想最后看一眼碎浪。

===Original===
She stood there under a poinciana tree near the door leading into Dan’s living-room. For nearly two weeks the Kona wind had prevailed, but now on her cheek, she thought she felt the first kindly breath of the trades. Very wide awake, she stared out at the dim foaming lines of surf between the shore and the coral reef. Her mind strayed back to the Honolulu she had known in Kalakaua’s day, to that era when the Islands were so naive, so colorful﻿—unspoiled. Ruined now, Dan had said, ruined by a damned mechanical civilization. “But away down underneath, Minerva, there are deep dark waters flowing still.”

The moon came out, touching with silver the waters at the crossroads, then was lost again under fleecy clouds. With a little sigh that was perhaps for her lost youth and the ’eighties, Miss Minerva pushed open the unlocked door leading into the great living-room, and closed it gently so as not to waken Dan.

===Chinese===
她在凤凰木下站定，树正挨着通往丹家起居室的那扇门。科纳风肆虐了将近两个星期，可此刻她觉得，颊上拂过的是信风最初的一缕慈息。她毫无睡意，凝望着海岸与珊瑚礁之间那一道道朦胧泛沫的浪线。她的心思飘回了她所熟识的、卡拉卡瓦王当政之日的檀香山，飘回那个群岛何等天真、何等绚丽——尚未被糟蹋的年代。如今全毁了，丹说过，毁于一种该死的机械文明。「可是——往下深处，米纳瓦，幽暗的深水依然在流淌呢。」

月亮出来了，给十字路口那片海面镀上一层银辉，随即又没入絮状的云后。米纳瓦小姐轻轻叹了口气——这叹息或许是为了她逝去的青春和八十年代——随后推开那扇没有上锁、通往大起居室的门，又轻轻掩上，免得吵醒丹。

===Original===
An intense darkness engulfed her. But she knew her way across the polished floor and set out confidently, walking on tiptoe. She had gone halfway to the hall door when she stopped, her heart in her mouth. For not five feet away she saw the luminous dial of a watch, and as she stared at it with frightened eyes, it moved.

Not for nothing had Miss Minerva studied restraint through more than fifty years. Many women would have screamed and fainted, Miss Minerva’s heart pounded madly, but that was all. Standing very still, she studied that phosphorescent dial. Its movement had been slight, it was now at rest again. A watch worn on someone’s wrist. Someone who had been on the point of action, but had now assumed an attitude of cautious waiting.

===Chinese===
一团浓黑将她吞没。但她认得穿过那打磨光亮的地板的路，便踮起脚尖，放心大胆地走了进去。走到离大厅门一半的地方，她停住了，心提到了嗓子眼。因为不出五英尺远，她看见一只表发出微光的表盘；她惊恐地瞪着它，它动了一下。

米纳瓦小姐修习克己功夫五十余年，绝非虚功。换了别的女人，早就尖叫着晕过去了；米纳瓦小姐的心狂跳不已，但也仅止于此。她一动不动地站着，端详那磷光闪闪的表盘。它方才动得轻微，此刻又静止了。一只戴在谁手腕上的表。那人本已将动未动，此刻却摆出了一副小心等候的架势。

===Original===
Well, Miss Minerva grimly asked herself, what was she going to do about it? Should she cry out a sharp: “Who’s there?” She was a brave woman, but the foolhardiness of such a course was apparent. She had a vision of that dial flashing nearer, a blow, perhaps strong hands at her throat.

She took a tentative step, and then another. Now, surely, the dial would stir again. But it remained motionless, steady, as though the arm that wore it were rigid at the intruder’s side.

===Chinese===
好吧，米纳瓦小姐冷峻地问自己，眼下她打算怎么办？要不要厉声喝一句：「谁在那儿？」她是个有胆量的女人，可这么办的鲁莽是明摆着的。她仿佛看见那表盘闪着光逼近来，一记重击，也许还有一双有力的大手掐上她的喉咙。

她试着迈了一步，又一步。这下那表盘总该再动一动了吧。它却纹丝不动、稳稳当当，仿佛戴表的那条手臂僵直地垂在这个不速之客的身侧。

===Original===
Suddenly Miss Minerva realized the situation. The wearer of the watch had forgotten the telltale numerals on his wrist, he thought himself hidden in the dark. He was waiting for her to go on through the room. If she made no sound, gave no sign of alarm, she might be safe. Once beyond that bamboo curtain leading into the hall, she could rouse the household.

She was a woman of great will power, but it took all she had to move serenely on her way. She shut her lips tightly and accomplished it, veering a bit from that circle of light that menaced her, looking back at it over her shoulder as she went. After what seemed an eternity the bamboo curtain received her, she was through it, she was on the stairs. But it seemed to her that never again would she be able to look at a watch or a clock and find that the hour was anything save twenty minutes past one!

===Chinese===
突然间，米纳瓦小姐明白了处境。戴表的人忘了腕上那出卖他的数字，自以为藏进了黑暗里。他在等她穿过这个房间。只要她不出声、不露惊慌，兴许就能平安无事。一旦穿过那道通往大厅的竹帘，她就能唤起全家。

她是意志坚强的女人，可要从容不迫地走完这段路，也用尽了她全部的力气。她紧闭双唇，做到了：稍稍绕开那圈威胁着她的光，一边走一边回头用眼角盯住它。挨过仿佛无穷无尽的时辰，竹帘终于接纳了她，她过去了，她踏上了楼梯。只是在她看来，往后无论看表还是看钟，指针除了指向一点二十分，再也不可能是别的时辰了！

===Original===
When she was halfway up the stairs, she recalled that it had been her intention to snap on the lights in the lower hall. She did not turn back, nor did she search for the switch at the head of the stairs. Instead, she went hastily on into her room, and just as though she had been an ordinary woman she closed her door and dropped down, trembling a little, on a chair.

But she was no ordinary woman, and in two seconds she was up and had reopened her door. Her sudden terror was evaporating; she felt her heart beat in a strong regular rhythm again. Action was what was required of her now, calm confident action; she was a Winterslip and she was ready.

===Chinese===
上到楼梯一半，她才想起自己原打算拧亮下面大厅的灯。她没有回头，也没有去摸楼梯顶上的开关，反而匆匆进了自己的房间，而且——活脱脱像个寻常女人似的——关上门，微微发着抖，跌坐进一把椅子里。

可她不是寻常女人，不出两秒钟，她已经站起身，重新打开了房门。骤然的恐惧正在消散；她感到自己的心跳又恢复了沉稳有力的节律。此刻需要她的是行动，沉着而自信的行动；她是温特斯利普家的人，她已准备停当。

===Original===
The servants’ quarters were in a wing over the kitchen, she went there at once and knocked on the first door she came to. She knocked once, then again, and finally the head of a very sleepy Jap appeared.

“Haku,” said Miss Minerva, “there is someone in the living-room. You must go down and investigate at once.”

He stared at her, seeming unable to comprehend.

“We must go down,” amended Miss Minerva.

“Wikiwiki!”

===Chinese===
仆役们的住处在厨房上头的一翼，她径直过去，在遇到的第一扇门上敲了敲。敲了一下，又敲一下，末了，一个睡得迷迷糊糊的日本仆人探出头来。

「哈库，」米纳瓦小姐说，「起居室里有个人。你必须马上下楼去查看。」

他瞪着她，像是听不明白。

「我们必须下去，」米纳瓦小姐改口道。

「快！快！」

===Original===
He disappeared, and Miss Minerva waited impatiently. Where was her nerve, she wondered, why hadn’t she seen this through alone? At home, no doubt, she could have managed it, but here there was something strange and terrifying in the very air. The moonlight poured in through a small window beside her, forming a bright square at her feet. Haku reappeared, wearing a gaudy kimono that he often sported on the beach.

Another door opened suddenly, and Miss Minerva started. Bah! What ailed her, anyhow, she wondered. It was only Kamaikui, standing there a massive figure in the dim doorway, a bronze statue clad in a holoku.

“Someone in the living-room,” Miss Minerva explained again. “I saw him as I came through.”

===Chinese===
他缩了回去，米纳瓦小姐不耐地等着。她的胆气哪儿去了，她纳闷，为何不独自把这事看到底？搁在老家，无疑她一个人就对付了，可在这儿，连空气里都有一种陌生而可怕的东西。月光从她身旁的小窗里倾泻进来，在她脚边铺出一方亮块。哈库再露面时，身上那件花哨的和服，正是他常穿着上海滩招摇的那件。

另一扇门忽然开了，米纳瓦小姐吓了一跳。呸！她到底是怎么了，她暗想。不过是卡迈库伊，站在昏暗的门口，一躯魁伟的身影，一尊裹着霍洛库长袍（holoku）的青铜雕像。

「起居室里有个人，」米纳瓦小姐又解释了一遍。「我穿过来的时候瞧见他的。」

===Original===
Kamaikui made no reply, but joined the odd little procession. In the upper hall Haku switched on the lights, both upstairs and down. At the head of the stairs there was a brief pause﻿—then Miss Minerva took her rightful place at the head of the line. She descended with a firm step, courageous and competent, Boston at its best. After her followed a stolid little Jap in a kimono gay with passionate poppies, and a Polynesian woman who wore the fearful Mother Hubbard of the missionaries as though it were a robe of state.

===Chinese===
卡迈库伊没有答话，只加入了这支古怪的小小队伍。在楼上大厅里，哈库拧亮了灯，楼上楼下一齐亮了。楼梯口停顿了片刻——随后米纳瓦小姐当仁不让地站到了队首。她迈着坚定的步子走下楼梯，沉着勇敢、干净利落，正是波士顿人最出彩的样子。她身后跟着一个神情木然的小个子日本仆人，和服上开满火热的大罂粟花；还跟着一位波利尼西亚女子，把传教士们那件令人生畏的宽松长袍（Mother Hubbard）穿得俨然一袭国礼服。

===Original===
In the lower hall Miss Minerva did not hesitate. She pushed on through the bamboo curtain and her hand﻿—it trembled ever so slightly﻿—found the electric switch and flooded the living-room with light. She heard the crackle of bamboo behind her as her strange companions followed where she led. She stood looking curiously about her.

There was no one in sight, no sign of any disturbance, and it suddenly occurred to Miss Minerva that perhaps she was behaving in a rather silly fashion. After all, she had neither seen nor heard a living thing. The illuminated dial of a watch that moved a little﻿—might it not have been a figment of her imagination? She had experienced a stirring evening. Then, too, she remembered, there had been that small glass of okolehau. A potent concoction!

===Chinese===
到了楼下大厅，米纳瓦小姐毫不迟疑。她径直穿过竹帘，她的手——只极轻微地抖了一抖——摸到了电灯开关，起居室里顿时一片通明。她听见身后竹帘窸窣作响，她那两位古怪的同伴跟着她走了进来。她站在那儿，好奇地四下打量。

一个人影也没有，毫无骚乱的迹象。米纳瓦小姐忽然觉得，自己这番举动兴许相当可笑。毕竟，她既没看见一个活物，也没听见半点声息。一块微微动了一下的发光表盘——会不会只是她凭空想出来的幻象？她刚度过一个激动人心的夜晚。再说，她还记得，还喝过那一小杯奥科勒豪（okolehau）呢。好烈的酒浆！

===Original===
Kamaikui and Haku were looking at her with the inquiring eyes of little children. Had she roused them for a fool’s errand? Her cheeks flushed slightly. Certainly in this big brilliant room, furnished with magnificent native woods and green with many potted ferns, everything seemed proper and in order.

“I﻿—I may have been mistaken,” she said in a low voice. “I was quite sure﻿—but there’s no sign of anything wrong. Mr. Winterslip has not been resting well of late. If he should be asleep we won’t waken him.”

===Chinese===
卡迈库伊和哈库正用小孩子般探问的眼睛望着她。她把两人唤起来，就为了一桩傻事么？她的面颊微微泛红。的确，在这间以华贵的本地木料为饰、摆满盆栽蕨草因而满目青翠的大房间里，一切都妥妥帖帖，井然有序。

「我——我也许弄错了，」她低声说。「我方才很笃定——可是看不出有什么不对头。温特斯利普先生近来一直睡不安稳。要是他睡着了，咱们就别吵醒他。」

===Original===
She went to the door leading on to the lanai and pushed aside the curtain. Bright moonlight outside revealed most of the veranda’s furnishings, and here, too, all seemed well. “Dan,” Miss Minerva called softly. “Dan. Are you awake?”

No answer. Miss Minerva was certain now that she was making a mountain out of a molehill. She was about to turn back into the living-room when her eyes, grown more accustomed to the semidarkness, noted a rather startling fact.

Day and night, over Dan’s cot in one corner of the lanai, hung a white mosquito netting. It was not there now.

===Chinese===
她走到通往露台的门边，撩开门帘。外面明亮的月光照出了廊下大部分的陈设，这里同样一切如常。「丹，」米纳瓦小姐轻声唤道。「丹。你醒着吗？」

没有应声。米纳瓦小姐此刻确信，自己是在小题大做了。她正要转身回起居室去，她的双眼已渐渐习惯了半暗的光线，忽然注意到一桩相当骇人的事实。

日日夜夜，丹睡在露台一角的那张帆布床上，总罩着一顶白蚊帐。此刻蚊帐不在了。

===Original===
“Come, Haku,” Miss Minerva said. “Turn on the light out here.”

Haku came, and the green-shaded lamp glowed under his touch. The little lamp by which Dan had been reading his evening paper that night when he had seemed suddenly so disturbed, and rushed off to send a letter to Roger in San Francisco. Miss Minerva stood recalling that incident, she recalled others, because she was very reluctant to turn toward that cot in the corner. She was conscious of Kamaikui brushing by her, and then she heard a low, half-savage moan of fear and sorrow.

===Chinese===
「来，哈库，」米纳瓦小姐说。「把外头的灯打开。」

哈库过来了，绿灯罩的灯在他手下一亮。正是那盏小灯：那天晚上，丹就在灯下读晚报，忽然显得心绪大乱，冲出去给旧金山的罗杰寄了一封信。米纳瓦小姐站在那儿回想那桩事，又想起了别的种种，因为她极不愿意把身子转向角落里那张帆布床。她觉出卡迈库伊从她身边擦过，接着，她听见一声低低的、半是野性的呜咽，含着惊惧与悲恸。

===Original===
Miss Minerva stepped to the cot. The mosquito netting had been torn down as though in some terrific struggle and there, entangled in the meshes of it, she saw Dan Winterslip. He was lying on his left side, and as she stared down at him, one of the harmless little Island lizards ran up his chest and over his shoulder﻿—and left a crimson trail on his white pajamas.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐走到床前。蚊帐已被扯了下来，仿佛经过了一场骇人的搏斗；就在那网眼里，她看见了丹·温特斯利普。他左侧卧着，她低头凝视他时，一只岛上无害的小蜥蜴爬上他的胸口，越过他的肩头——在他白色的睡衣上留下一道猩红的痕迹。

## VII Enter Charlie Chan / 七 陈查理登场

===Original===
Miss Minerva leaned far over, her keen eyes seeking Dan’s face. It was turned toward the wall, half buried in the pillow. “Dan,” she said brokenly. She put her hand on his cheek. The night air was warm and muggy, but she shivered a little as she drew the hand quickly away. Steady! She must be steady now.

She hurried through the living-room to the hall; the telephone was in a closet under the front stairs. Her fingers were trembling again as she fumbled with the numerals on the dial. She got her number, heard finally an answering voice.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐深深俯下身去，锐利的目光搜寻着丹的脸。那脸转向墙壁，半埋在枕头里。「丹，」她哽咽着唤道。她把手贴上他的面颊。夜里的空气湿热闷人，可她飞快缩回手时，还是微微打了个寒噤。稳住！此刻她必须稳住。

她匆匆穿过起居室奔向门厅；电话机在前楼梯下的一个小间里。她拨着号盘上的数字，手指又颤抖起来。电话通了，终于听见一个应答的声音。

===Original===
“Amos? Is that you, Amos? This is Minerva. Come over here to Dan’s as quickly as you can.”

The voice muttered in protest. Miss Minerva cut in on it sharply.

“For God’s sake, Amos, forget your silly feud. Your brother is dead.”

“Dead?” he repeated dully.

“Murdered, Amos. Will you come now?”

A long silence. What thoughts, Miss Minerva wondered, were passing through the mind of that stern unbending Puritan?

“I’ll come,” a strange voice said at last. And then, a voice more like that of the Amos she knew: “The police! I’ll notify them, and then I’ll come right over.”

===Chinese===
「阿莫斯吗？是你吗，阿莫斯？我是米纳瓦。尽快赶到丹的宅子里来。」

电话那头咕哝着推拒。米纳瓦小姐厉声打断了他。

「看在上帝份上，阿莫斯，忘掉你那场无聊的积怨吧。你哥哥死了。」

「死了？」他木然重复道。

「是被人谋害的，阿莫斯。这回你肯来了吗？」

电话里久久无声。米纳瓦小姐暗想，那位严峻而绝不肯低头的清教徒，此刻脑子里正转过些什么念头？

「我来。」终于，一个陌生的声音说道。接着，那声音又变回她所认识的那个阿莫斯：「警察！我先去通知他们，然后马上过来。」

===Original===
Returning to the hall, Miss Minerva saw that the big front door was closed. Amos would enter that way, she knew, so she went over and opened it. There was, she noted, an imposing lock, but the key had long since been lost and forgotten. Indeed, in all Dan’s great house, she could not recall ever having seen a key. In these friendly trusting islands, locked doors were obsolete.

She reentered the living-room. Should she summon a doctor? But no, it was too late, she knew that only too well. And the police﻿—didn’t they bring some sort of doctor with them? Suddenly she began to wonder about the police. During all her time in Honolulu she had never given them a thought before. Away off here at the end of the world﻿—did they have policemen? She couldn’t remember ever having seen one. Oh, yes﻿—there was that handsome, brown-skinned Hawaiian who stood on a box at the corner of Fort and King Streets, directing traffic with an air that would have become Kamehameha himself. She heard the scrape of a chair being moved on the lanai, and went to the door.

===Chinese===
回到门厅，米纳瓦小姐看见那扇高大的正门还关着。她知道阿莫斯要从那扇门进来，便走过去把它打开。她注意到，门上装着一把气派的锁，钥匙却早已遗失，被人忘了个干净。说实在的，在丹这座大宅子里，她想不起自己何曾见过一把钥匙。在这些友好而彼此信任的海岛上，锁门早已是过时的老皇历了。

她回到起居室。要不要请位医生来？不必了，太迟了，这一点她再清楚不过。再说警察——他们不是总会带个什么医生同来吗？忽然间，她对这里的警察起了好奇。在檀香山住了这么久，她还从没想起过他们。在这远在天涯海角的地方——也有警察吗？她不记得自己见过一个。哦，有的——福特街（Fort Street）和国王街（King Street）的路口，那个站在木箱上指挥交通的俊俏的棕皮肤夏威夷人，那副气派，连卡美哈梅哈（Kamehameha）王本尊也要自愧不如。她听见露台上有椅子挪动的声响，便朝门口走去。

===Original===
“Nothing is to be touched out here,” she said. “Leave it just as it was. You’d better go upstairs and dress, both of you.”

The two frightened servants came into the living-room, and stood there regarding her. They seemed to feel that this terrible affair called for discussion. But what was there to be said? Even in the event of murder, a Winterslip must maintain a certain well-bred aloofness in dealing with servants. Miss Minerva’s feeling for them was kindly. She sympathized with their evident grief, but there was, she felt, nothing to discuss.

“After you’ve dressed,” she ordered, “stay within reach. You’ll both be wanted.”

They went out, Haku in his absurd costume, Kamaikui moaning and muttering in a way that sent shivers up and down Miss Minerva’s spine. They left her there alone﻿—with Dan﻿—and she who had always thought herself equal to anything still hesitated about going out on the lanai.

===Chinese===
「外头的东西一件也不许动，」她说，「都保持原样。你们俩最好上楼把衣服穿好。」

两个吓坏了的仆人走进起居室，站在那里望着她。他们似乎觉得，这桩可怕的事总该商议商议。可有什么好说的呢？即便是出了人命，一个温特斯利普家的人，跟仆人打交道也须保持几分有教养的疏淡。米纳瓦小姐待他们素来和善。他们那份显而易见的悲伤，她也感同身受，只是照她想来，实在无事可议。

「穿好衣服，」她吩咐道，「待在附近别走远。你们俩都会被叫去问话。」

他们退出去了，哈库还穿着那身滑稽的行头，卡迈库伊一路哀叹低语，那声音顺着米纳瓦小姐的脊背上下游窜。他们把她一个人留在那里——陪着丹——而她这个一向自认什么场面都应付得来的人，一时竟踌躇着不肯走向露台。

===Original===
She sat down in a huge chair in the living-room and gazed about her at the trappings of wealth and position that Dan had left forever now. Poor Dan. Despite all the whispering against him, she had liked him immensely. It is said of many﻿—usually with small reason﻿—that their lives would make an interesting book. It had been said of Dan, and in his case it was true. What a book his life would have made﻿—and how promptly it would have been barred for all time from the shelves of the Boston Public Library! For Dan had lived life to the full, made his own laws, fought his battles without mercy, prospered and had his way. Dallied often along forbidden paths, they said, but his smile had been so friendly and his voice so full of cheer﻿—always until these last two weeks.

===Chinese===
她在起居室一把宽大的椅子里坐下，环顾四周那些如今已被丹永远撇下的富贵排场。可怜的丹。尽管针对他的闲言碎语从来不少，她却极其喜欢他。许多人——通常并没什么道理——都被人说成，他这一生写下来准是本有趣的书。这话也落到了丹头上，而在他身上倒千真万确。他这一生写下来该是怎样一本书啊——又该多快就被波士顿公共图书馆终身封禁！因为丹活得淋漓尽致，自立法度，争斗起来毫不留情，发达兴旺，事事随心。人们说他常在禁行的小径上流连，可他的笑容总是那么亲切，声音总是那么爽朗——直到这两周之前，一直如此。

===Original===
Ever since that night he sent the letter to Roger, he had seemed a different man. There were lines for the first time in his face, a weary apprehensive look in his gray eyes. And how furious he had been when, last Wednesday, he received a cable from Roger. What was in that message, Miss Minerva wondered; what were those few typewritten words that had caused him to fly into such a rage and set him to pacing the floor with tigerish step?

She thought of him as she had seen him last﻿—he had seemed rather pathetic to her then. When the news came that the *President Tyler* could not dock until morning, and that Barbara﻿—

Miss Minerva stopped. For the first time she thought of Barbara. She thought of a sprightly, vivacious girl as yet untouched by sorrow﻿—and of the morning’s homecoming. Tears came into her eyes, and it was through a mist she saw the bamboo curtain that led into the hall pushed aside, and the thin white face of Amos framed there.

===Chinese===
自打那晚给罗杰发信之后，他就像换了个人。脸上头一回添了皱纹，灰眼睛里带着一种疲惫而惶惶的神色。上星期三他收到罗杰的一封电报时，又是何等暴怒。那消息里写了什么，米纳瓦小姐暗想；那寥寥几个打出来的字，怎么就惹得他勃然大怒，迈着老虎般的步子在屋里团团打转？

她想起自己最后一次见到他的样子——那时他在她眼里已显得颇为可怜。消息传来，说泰勒总统号（*President Tyler*）要到早晨才能靠岸，说芭芭拉——

米纳瓦小姐收住了思绪。她头一回想到了芭芭拉。她想到那个活泼灵秀、还从未被悲伤碰过的姑娘——想到今早晨的归家。泪水涌上她的眼睛，隔着一层雾，她看见通往门厅的竹帘被掀开，阿莫斯那张苍白的瘦脸嵌在帘口。

===Original===
Amos entered, walking gingerly, for he was treading ground he had sworn his feet should never touch. He paused before Miss Minerva.

“What’s this?” he said. “What’s all this?”

She nodded toward the lanai, and he went out there. After what seemed a long time, he reappeared. His shoulders drooped wearily and his watery eyes were staring.

“Stabbed through the heart,” he muttered. He stood for a moment regarding his father’s picture on the wall. “The wages of sin is death,” he added, as though to old Jedediah Winterslip.

===Chinese===
阿莫斯走了进来，脚步小心翼翼，因为他正踩着一块他曾发誓永不踏足的地界。他在米纳瓦小姐面前站住。

「这是怎么回事？」他说，「这到底是怎么回事？」

她朝露台点了点头，他便走了出去。仿佛过了很久，他才重新现身，肩膀疲惫地耷拉着，一双水汪汪的眼睛直勾勾地瞪着。

「心脏上挨了一刀，」他喃喃道。他伫立片刻，望着墙上父亲的画像。「罪的工价乃是死，」他又补上一句，像是说给老杰迪代亚·温特斯利普听的。

===Original===
“Yes, Amos,” said Miss Minerva sharply. “I expected we should hear that from you. And there’s another one you may have heard﻿—judge not that ye be not judged. Further than that, we’ll waste no time moralizing. Dan is dead, and I for one am sorry.”

“Sorry!” repeated Amos drearily. “How about me? My brother﻿—my young brother﻿—I taught him to walk on this very beach﻿—”

“Yes.” Miss Minerva looked at him keenly. “I wonder. Well, Dan’s gone. Someone has killed him. He was one of us﻿—a Winterslip. What are we going to do about it?”

===Chinese===
「不错，阿莫斯，」米纳瓦小姐尖刻地说，「我早料到这话会从你嘴里说出来。还有一句你大概也听过——你们不要论断人，免得你们被论断。再往下，咱们就不必浪费光阴说教了。丹死了，至少我，是很难过的。」

「难过！」阿莫斯凄然重复道。「那我呢？我的兄弟——我的小兄弟——就在这片海滩上，是我手把手教他走路的——」

「是吗。」米纳瓦小姐锐利地打量着他。「那可难说。唉，丹去了。有人杀了他。他是我们中的一员——一个温特斯利普。我们打算怎么办？」

===Original===
“I’ve notified the police,” said Amos.

“Then why aren’t they here? In Boston by this time﻿—but then, I know this isn’t Boston. Stabbed, you say. Was there any sign of a weapon?”

“None whatever, that I could see.”

“How about that Malay kris on the table out there? The one Dan used as a paper cutter?”

“I didn’t notice,” Amos replied. “This is a strange house to me, Minerva.”

===Chinese===
「我已经报了警，」阿莫斯说。

「那他们怎么还没到？要是在波士顿，这时候早到了——不过也难怪，我知道这里不是波士顿。你说他是被刀捅的。凶器有下落吗？」

「依我看，一点儿影子也没有。」

「外头桌上那把马来克力士匕首（Malay kris）呢？丹拿来裁纸的那把？」

「我没留意，」阿莫斯答道，「这座宅子对我来说是陌生的，米纳瓦。」

===Original===
“So it is.” Miss Minerva rose and started for the lanai. She was her old competent self again. At that moment a loud knock sounded on the screen door at the front of the house. Presently there were voices in the hall, and Haku ushered three men into the living-room. Though evidently police, they were all in plain clothes. One of them, a tall, angular Yankee with the look of a sailing master about him, stepped forward.

“I’m Hallet,” he said. “Captain of Detectives. You’re Mr. Amos Winterslip, I believe?”

“I am,” Amos answered. He introduced Miss Minerva. Captain Hallet gave her a casual nod; this was man’s business and he disliked having a woman involved.
