「听着可真迷人。」约翰·昆西讥诮地说。

「妙就妙在这儿，」他姑婆答道，「它确实迷人。起初你会想的一件事，就是回家。等你不再想了，这事儿自然也就想不起来啦。」

===Original===
“We gathered that,” John Quincy told her.

“You’ll meet a man on the beach,” said Miss Minerva, “who stopped over between boats to have his laundry done. That was twenty years ago, and he’s still here.”

“Probably they haven’t finished his laundry,” suggested John Quincy, yawning openly. “Ho, hum. I’m going up to my room to change, and after that I believe I’ll write a few letters.” He rose with an effort and went to the door. “How’s Barbara?” he asked.

Miss Minerva shook her head. “Dan was all the poor child had,” she said. “She’s taken it rather hard. You won’t see her for some time, and when you do﻿—the least said about all this, the better.”

“Why, naturally,” agreed John Quincy, and went upstairs.

===Chinese===
「这我们领教过了。」约翰·昆西对她说。

「你会在海滩上碰到一个人，」米纳瓦小姐说，「他当年趁两班船之间的空档停留，为的是把换洗衣裳送出去洗。那是二十年前的事了，人到如今他还在这儿。」

「多半是那家洗衣店还没洗完他的衣裳。」约翰·昆西接口道，毫不遮掩地打着哈欠。「嗬，嗯。我上楼回房换衣裳去，回头我想写几封信。」他费了点劲站起来，走向门口。「芭芭拉怎么样？」他问。

米纳瓦小姐摇摇头。「可怜的孩子，就只有丹这么个亲人了。」她说，「这事她受得不轻。有一阵子你见不着她，就算见着了——这些事，还是越少提起越好。」

「这个自然。」约翰·昆西应道，上楼去了。

===Original===
After he had bathed and put on his whitest, thinnest clothes, he explored the desk that stood near his bed and found it well supplied with note paper. Languidly laying out a sheet, he began to write.

> “Dear Agatha: Here I am in Honolulu and outside my window I can hear the lazy swish of waters lapping the famous beach of﻿—”

===Chinese===
他沐浴完毕，换上最白最薄的一身衣裳，翻检了床边那张书桌，发现信纸备得足足的。他有气无力地铺开一张，动笔写起来。

> 「亲爱的阿加莎：我已身在檀香山，窗外听得见懒懒的水声，轻拍着那著名的——」

===Original===
Lazy, indeed. John Quincy had a feeling for words. He stopped and stared at an agile little cloud flitting swiftly through the sky﻿—got up from his chair to watch it disappear over Diamond Head. On his way back to the desk he had to pass the bed. What inviting beds they had out here! He lifted the mosquito netting and dropped down for a moment﻿—

===Chinese===
懒洋洋，真够可以。约翰·昆西对文字是有感觉的。他停了笔，凝望一朵轻捷的小云飞快掠过天空——干脆从椅子上起身，望着它消失在钻石头山后头。回书桌的路上，他必须经过那张床。这地方的床多么诱人啊！他掀起蚊帐，倒了下去，只消一小会儿——

===Original===
Haku hammered on the door at one o’clock, and that was how John Quincy happened to be present at lunch. His aunt was already at the table when he staggered in.

“Cheer up,” she smiled. “You’ll become acclimated soon. Of course, even then you’ll want your nap just after lunch every day.”

“I will not,” he answered, but there was no conviction in his tone.

“Barbara asked me to tell you how sorry she is not to be with you. She’s a sweet girl, John Quincy.”

“She’s all of that. Give her my love, won’t you?”

“Your love?” His aunt looked at him. “Do you mean that? Barbara’s only a second cousin﻿—”

He laughed. “Don’t waste your time matchmaking, Aunt Minerva. Someone has already spoken for Barbara.”

===Chinese===
一点钟，哈库在门上擂了一通，约翰·昆西这才赶上了午饭。他跌跌撞撞进去时，姑婆已经坐在桌前了。

「振作点儿，」她微笑道，「你很快就会服了这边的水土。当然喽，就算服了水土，每天午饭后你也照样要打你的盹。」

「我才不会。」他答道，可语气里没有一点底气。

「芭芭拉让我告诉你，不能来陪你，她心里很过意不去。她是个可人的姑娘，约翰·昆西。」

「她可不止可人。替我问她好，行吗？」

「你的爱？」他姑婆看着他，「这话当真？芭芭拉不过是隔房的堂妹——」

他笑了。「别浪费时间做媒了，米纳瓦姑婆。已经有人向芭芭拉求过亲了。」

===Original===
“Really? Who?”

“Jennison. He seems like a fine fellow, too.”

“Handsome, at any rate,” Miss Minerva admitted. They ate in silence for a time. “The coroner and his friends were here this morning,” said Miss Minerva presently.

“That so?” replied John Quincy. “Any verdict?”

“Not yet. I believe they’re to settle on that later. By the way, I’m going downtown immediately after lunch to do some shopping for Barbara. Care to come along?”

“No, thanks,” John Quincy said. “I must go upstairs and finish my letters.”

===Chinese===
「当真？是谁？」

「詹尼森。而且看着也是个正派人。」

「至少人是英俊的。」米纳瓦小姐承认。两人默默吃了一阵。「验尸官和他的同僚们今儿早上来过。」过了一会儿，米纳瓦小姐说。

「是吗？」约翰·昆西应道，「有裁定了吗？」

「还没有。我想他们晚些时候再定。对了，午饭后我马上进城，替芭芭拉买些东西。愿意一块儿去吗？」

「不了，谢谢，」约翰·昆西说，「我得回楼上去把信写完。」

===Original===
But when he left the luncheon table, he decided the letters could wait. He took a heavy volume with a South Sea title from Dan’s library, and went out on to the lanai. Presently Miss Minerva appeared, smartly dressed in white linen.

“I’ll return as soon as I’m *pau*,” she announced.

“What is this *pau*?” John Quincy inquired.

“*Pau* means finished﻿—through.”

“Good lord,” John Quincy said. “Aren’t there enough words in the English language for you?”

“Oh, I don’t know,” she answered, “a little Hawaiian sprinkled in makes a pleasant change. And when one reaches my age, John Quincy, one is eager for a change. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
可一离了午饭桌，他就决定信不妨缓一缓。他从丹的书房里取了一部南太平洋题材的大部头，走到露台上去。不多时，米纳瓦小姐来了，一身白色亚麻衣裙，打扮得清清爽爽。

「我一『帕乌』完就回来。」她宣布道。

「这个『帕乌』是什么？」约翰·昆西问道。

「帕乌就是完了——收工了的意思。」

「天哪，」约翰·昆西说，「英语里的词还不够你使的吗？」

「哦，那倒难说，」她答道，「掺上几句夏威夷话，换换口味挺不错。人到了我这把年纪，约翰·昆西，就巴望着换换花样了。再见。」

===Original===
She left him to his book and the somnolent atmosphere of Dan’s lanai. Sometimes he read, colorful tales of other islands farther south. Sometimes he sat and thought. Sometimes he just sat. The blazing afternoon wore on; presently the beach beyond Dan’s garden was gay with bathers, sunburned men and girls, pretty girls in brief and alluring costumes. Their cries as they dared the surf were exultant, happy. John Quincy was keen to try these notable waters, but it didn’t seem quite the thing﻿—not just yet, with Dan Winterslip lying in that room upstairs.

===Chinese===
她把他留给了他的书，和丹家露台上那催眠的空气。有时他读书，读的是更南边那些岛屿的绚丽故事。有时他坐着想事情。有时他只是坐着。炎炎的午后缓缓过去；不多时，丹家花园外的海滩上热闹起来，满是游泳的人，晒得黝黑的男人和姑娘们，漂亮姑娘穿着简短诱人的泳衣。她们冲着浪头嬉闹，喊声欢腾雀跃。约翰·昆西很想试试这片有名的海水，可总觉得有点不像话——至少眼下不行，丹·温特斯利普还停放在楼上那个房间里呢。

===Original===
Miss Minerva reappeared about five, flushed and﻿—though she well knew it was not the thing for one of her standing in the Back Bay﻿—perspiring. She carried an evening paper in her hand.

“Any news?” inquired John Quincy.

She sat down. “Nothing but the coroner’s verdict. The usual thing﻿—person or persons unknown. But as I was reading the paper in the car, I had a sudden inspiration.”

“Good for you. What was it?”

===Chinese===
五点钟光景，米纳瓦小姐回来了，脸红扑扑的，还出着汗——她心里很明白，这在她这样一位后湾身份的女士，实在有失体统。她手里拿着一份晚报。

「有什么新闻吗？」约翰·昆西问。

她坐下来。「没什么，就一条验尸官的裁定。老一套——凶手为身份不明之一人或数人。不过方才在汽车里读报时，我突然来了灵感。」

「好极了。什么灵感？」

===Original===
Haku appeared at the door leading to the living-room. “You ring, miss?” he said.

“I did. Haku, what becomes of the old newspapers in this house?”

“Take and put in a closet beside kitchen,” the man told her.

“See if you can find me﻿—no, never mind. I’ll look myself.”

She followed Haku into the living-room. In a few minutes she returned alone, a newspaper in her hand.

===Chinese===
哈库出现在通起居室的门口。「小姐，您按铃了？」他说。

「按了。哈库，这宅子里的旧报纸都怎么处理？」

「拿去，放到厨房旁边的小间里。」那男仆告诉她。

「你去看看能不能给我找——不，不用了。我自己去找。」

她跟着哈库进了起居室。几分钟后她独自回来，手里拿着一份报纸。

===Original===
“I have it,” she announced triumphantly. “The evening paper of Monday, June sixteenth﻿—the one Dan was reading the night he wrote that letter to Roger. And look, John Quincy﻿—one corner has been torn from the shipping page!”

“Might have been accidental,” suggested John Quincy languidly.

“Nonsense!” she said sharply. “It’s a clue, that’s what it is. The item that disturbed Dan was on that missing corner of the page.”

“Might have been, at that,” he admitted. “What are you going to do﻿—”

“You’re the one that’s going to do it,” she cut in. “Pull yourself together and go into town. It’s two hours until dinner. Give this paper to Captain Hallet﻿—or better still, to Charlie Chan. I am impressed by Mr. Chan’s intelligence.”

===Chinese===
「找着了。」她得意洋洋地宣布。「六月十六日星期一的晚报——正是丹给罗杰写那封信的当晚读的那份。你瞧，约翰·昆西——船期版上有一角被人撕掉了！」

「兴许是碰巧撕的呢。」约翰·昆西懒懒地提示道。

「胡扯！」她尖声道，「这是条线索，错不了。搅扰了丹的那条消息，就在缺掉的那个角上。」

「这么说，倒也兴许是。」他承认道，「那你打算怎么办——」

「要去的是你。」她打断道，「打起精神进城去。离晚饭还有两个钟头。把这份报纸交给哈利特警长——或者更好，交给陈查理。陈先生的智识给我留下了深刻印象。」

===Original===
John Quincy laughed. “Damned clever, these Chinese!” he quoted. “You don’t mean to say you’ve fallen for that bunk. They seem clever because they’re so different.”

“We’ll see about that. The chauffeur’s gone on an errand for Barbara, but there’s a roadster in the garage﻿—”

“Trolley’s good enough for me,” said John Quincy. “Here, give me the paper.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西笑了。「『这些中国人，精明得邪门！』」他引了一句旧话。「你该不会是叫那套鬼话迷住了吧。他们显得精，无非因为跟我们太不一样。」

「走着瞧吧。司机替芭芭拉跑差事去了，不过车房里还有一辆双座跑车——」

「我坐电车就挺好。」约翰·昆西说，「来，把报纸给我。」

===Original===
She explained to him how he was to reach the city, and he got his hat and went. Presently he was on a trolley-car surrounded by representatives of a dozen different races. The melting pot of the Pacific, Carlota Egan had called Honolulu, and the appellation seemed to be correct. John Quincy began to feel a fresh energy, a new interest in life.

===Chinese===
她向他交代了进城的路，他拿了帽子便出门。不多时，他已坐在一辆有轨电车上，身边围着十来个不同种族的代表。卡洛塔·伊根管檀香山叫太平洋的熔炉，这名号看来名副其实。约翰·昆西开始觉出一股新的活力，一种对生活的新兴致。

===Original===
The trolley swept over the low swampy land between Waikiki and Honolulu, past rice fields where quaint figures toiled patiently in water to their knees, past taro patches, and finally turned on to King Street. Every few moments it paused to take aboard new immigration problems, Japs, Chinamen, Hawaiians, Portuguese, Philippinos, Koreans, all colors and all creeds. On it went. John Quincy saw great houses set in blooming groves, a Japanese theater flaunting weird posters not far from a Ford service station, then a huge building he recognized as the palace of the monarchy. Finally it entered a district of modern office buildings.

===Chinese===
电车飞驰过怀基基与檀香山之间低洼的沼泽地，经过一片片稻田——古怪的身影在齐膝深的水里耐着性子劳作——经过芋头田，最后转上国王街。隔不了多久，它便停一停，收载新的移民问题：日本人、中国人、夏威夷人、葡萄牙人、菲律宾人、朝鲜人，各色皮肤，各种信仰。车一路行去。约翰·昆西看见掩映在花木葱茏里的大宅，看见一家日本戏院张挂着怪异的海报，离一家福特汽车服务站不远；又看见一座宏大建筑，他认出那是王朝的宫殿。末了，电车驶进了一片现代化写字楼的地界。

===Original===
Mr. Kipling was wrong, the boy reflected, East and West could meet. They had.

===Chinese===
吉卜林先生错了，这少年心想，东方与西方是可以相遇的。它们已经相遇了。

===Original===
This impression was confirmed when he left the car at Fort Street and for a moment walked about, a stranger in a strange land. A dusky policeman was directing traffic on the corner, officers of the United States army and navy in spotless duck strolled by, and on the shady side of the street Chinese girls, slim and immaculate in freshly laundered trousers and jackets, were window shopping in the cool of the evening.

===Chinese===
他在福特街下了车，以异乡异客之身走了片刻，这印象愈发深了。街角上，一名深肤色的警察在指挥交通，美国陆海军的军官穿着一尘不染的白帆布军服信步走过；街的阴凉一面，几个中国姑娘穿着浆洗一新的裤褂，纤细娟洁，趁着傍晚的凉意在逛橱窗。

===Original===
“I’m looking for the police station,” John Quincy informed a big American with a friendly face.

“Get back on to King Street,” the man said. “Go to your right until you come to Bethel, then turn makai﻿—”

“Turn what?”

The man smiled. “A malihini, I take it. Makai means toward the sea. The other direction is mauka﻿—toward the mountains. The police station is at the foot of Bethel, in Kalakaua Hale.”

John Quincy thanked him and went on his way. He passed the post-office and was amazed to see that all the lock boxes opened on the street. After a time, he reached the station. A sergeant lounging behind the desk told him that Charlie Chan was at dinner. He suggested the Alexander Young Hotel or possibly the All American Restaurant on King Street.

===Chinese===
「我在找警察局。」约翰·昆西向一个面色和善的大个子美国人打听。

「回到国王街上去。」那人说，「往右走，走到贝瑟尔街，然后朝玛凯方向拐——」

「朝哪儿拐？」

那人笑了。「我看你是个玛利希尼——初来乍到的。玛凯是朝着海的方向。另一个方向叫毛卡——朝着山。警察局就在贝瑟尔街尽头，卡拉卡瓦楼里。」

约翰·昆西道了谢，继续赶路。路过邮局，他惊讶地看到所有私人信箱都朝着大街开口。走了一阵，他到了警察局。桌后闲坐着一名警佐，告诉他陈查理吃晚饭去了。警佐建议他去亚历山大·扬旅馆，或者国王街上的全美餐馆看看。

===Original===
The hotel sounded easiest, so John Quincy went there first. In the dim lobby a Chinese house boy wandered aimlessly about with broom and dust pan, a few guests were writing the inevitable postcards, a Chinese clerk was on duty at the desk. But there was no sign of Chan, either in the lobby or in the dining-room at the left. As John Quincy turned from an inspection of the latter, the elevator door opened and a Britisher in mufti came hurriedly forth. He was followed by a Cockney servant carrying luggage.

“Captain Cope,” called John Quincy.

===Chinese===
旅馆听着最好找，约翰·昆西便先去了那里。昏暗的大堂里，一个华人侍童拿着扫帚簸箕漫无目的地转悠，几位住客在写那种少不得的明信片，柜台当值的是一名中国职员。可大堂里没有陈查理的影子，左边餐厅里也没有。约翰·昆西正从后者的探望中转过身来，电梯门开了，一个穿便装的英国人匆匆走出，身后跟着一个提行李的伦敦东城口音的仆人。

「科佩船长。」约翰·昆西招呼道。

===Original===
The captain paused. “Hello,” he said. “Oh﻿—Mr. Winterslip﻿—how are you?” He turned to the servant. “Buy me an evening paper and an armful of the less offensive-looking magazines.” The man hurried off, and Cope again addressed John Quincy. “Delighted to see you, but I’m in a frightful rush. Off to the Fanning Islands in twenty minutes.”

“When did you get in?” inquired John Quincy. Not that he really cared.

“Yesterday at noon,” said Captain Cope. “Been on the wing ever since. I trust you are enjoying your stop here﻿—but I was forgetting. Fearful news about Dan Winterslip.”

“Yes,” said John Quincy coolly. Judging by the conversation in that San Francisco club, the blow had not been a severe one for Captain Cope. The servant returned.

“Sorry to run,” continued the captain. “But I must be off. The service is a stern taskmaster. My regards to your aunt. Best of luck, my boy.”

He disappeared through the wide door, followed by his man. John Quincy reached the street in time to see him rolling off in a big car toward the docks.

===Chinese===
船长顿住。「你好，」他说，「哦——温特斯利普先生——你好吗？」他转向仆人。「去给我买份晚报，再抱一摞看着不那么扎眼的杂志。」那人急急去了，科佩重新对约翰·昆西说话。「见到你很高兴，可我忙得要命。二十分钟后就要动身去范宁群岛。」

「你什么时候到的？」约翰·昆西问。倒不是他真想知道。

「昨天中午。」科佩船长说，「打那以后一路奔波。我相信你在此地停留得尽兴——瞧我，差点忘了。丹·温特斯利普的事，可怕的消息。」

「是啊。」约翰·昆西冷冷地说。听那晚旧金山俱乐部里的谈话，这打击于科佩船长并不算如何沉重。仆人回来了。

「失陪了，」船长接着说，「我必须走了。海军这份差事，是个严厉的监工。替我问候你姑婆。祝你好运，小伙子。」

他由仆人跟着，从宽阔的大门里消失了。约翰·昆西赶到街上，正好望见他坐着一辆大汽车朝码头驶去。

===Original===
Noting the cable office nearby, the boy entered and sent two messages, one to his mother and the other to Agatha Parker. He addressed them to Boston, Mass. U. S. A., and was accorded a withering look by the young woman in charge as she crossed out the last three letters. There were only two words in each message, but he returned to the street with the comfortable feeling that his correspondence was now attended to for some time to come.

===Chinese===
他注意到近旁的电报局，进去发了两封电报，一封给他母亲，一封给阿加莎·帕克。收报地址他写的是「马萨诸塞州波士顿，美利坚合众国」，当值的年轻小姐划去末尾「美利坚合众国」几个字时，赏了他一记足以杀人的白眼。每封电报里不过两个词，可他回到街上时心怀舒畅，觉得今后好一阵子的通信事务都已料理停当。

===Original===
A few moments later he encountered the All American Restaurant and going inside, found himself the only American in the place. Charlie Chan was seated alone at a table, and as John Quincy approached, he rose and bowed.

“A very great honor,” said the Chinaman. “Is it possible that I can prevail upon you to accept some of this terrible provision?”

“No, thanks,” answered John Quincy. “I’m to dine later at the house. I’ll sit down for a moment, if I may.”

“Quite overwhelmed,” bobbed Charlie. He resumed his seat and scowled at something on the plate before him. “Waiter,” he said. “Be kind enough to summon the proprietor of this establishment.”

The proprietor, a suave little Jap, came gliding. He bowed from the waist.

“Is it that you serve here insanitary food?” inquired Chan.

“Please deign to state your complaint,” said the Jap.

“This piece of pie are covered with fingermarks,” rebuked Chan. “The sight are most disgusting. Kindly remove it and bring me a more hygienic sector.”

The Jap picked up the offending pastry and carried it away.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，他撞见了全美餐馆，进门一看，满堂之中唯有他一个美国人。陈查理独自坐在一张桌旁，约翰·昆西一走近，他便起身鞠躬。

「无上荣幸。」那中国人说。「可否容我劝说于您，赏光接受些许此地的可怕食物？」

「不了，谢谢。」约翰·昆西答道，「我晚些还要回宅子里用晚餐。不见怪的话，我坐一小会儿。」

「实在令我不胜惶恐。」陈查理点头哈腰地说。他重新落座，对面前盘中之某物皱起了眉。「伙计，」他说，「劳驾，请唤这家铺子的东家前来。」

东家是个圆滑的日本小个子，滑步而来，躬身行礼。

「贵店所供应的，莫非是不洁之食物？」陈查理质问道。

「敬请明示您的不满。」那日本人说。

「这一块馅饼，布满指印。」陈查理申斥道，「此一景象，令人作呕至极。劳驾撤去，另换一块较为卫生的部分来。」

那日本人拾起那件惹祸的点心，端走了。

===Original===
“Japanese,” remarked Chan, spreading his hands in an eloquent gesture. “Is it proper for me to infer that you come on business connected with the homicide?”

John Quincy smiled. “I do,” he said. He took the newspaper from his pocket, pointed out the date and the missing corner. “My aunt felt it might be important,” he explained.

“The woman has a brain,” said Chan. “I will procure an unmutilated specimen of this issue and compare. The import may be vast.”

===Chinese===
「日本人哪。」陈查理评了一句，摊开双手，做了个意味深长的手势。「我可否合理推断，您此来是为那桩凶案之事？」

约翰·昆西笑了。「正是。」他说。他从口袋里掏出那份报纸，指明日期和缺掉的一角。「我姑婆觉得它也许要紧。」他解释道。

「这位女士生有头脑。」陈查理说，「我将设法取得同日未经毁损的一份，加以比对。其内涵或甚为宏大。」

===Original===
“You know,” remarked John Quincy, “I’d like to work with you on this case, if you’ll let me.”

“I have only delight,” Chan answered. “You arrive from Boston, a city most cultivated, where much more English words are put to employment than are accustomed here. I thrill when you speak. Greatest privilege for me, I would say.”

“Have you formed any theory about the crime?” John Quincy asked.

Chan shook his head. “Too early now.”

“You have no fingerprints to go on, you said.”

Chan shrugged his shoulders. “Does not matter. Fingerprints and other mechanics good in books, in real life not so much so. My experience tell me to think deep about human people. Human passions. Back of murder what, always? Hate, revenge, need to make silent the slain one. Greed for money, maybe. Study human people at all times.”

“Sounds reasonable,” admitted John Quincy.

===Chinese===
「我说，」约翰·昆西说，「这案子，要是你肯，我倒想跟你一起办。」

「我只有欢喜。」陈查理答道，「您从波士顿而来，那是一座极富教养的城市，在那里，被投入使用的英文词，远多于此地所惯用。您开口说话时，我便激动不已。这是我至大的荣幸，我愿如此说。」

「关于这桩罪案，你形成什么理论了吗？」约翰·昆西问。

陈查理摇头。「如今尚早。」

「你说过，眼下没有指纹可以凭借。」

陈查理耸耸肩。「并无干系。指纹以及其他机械之术，在书本里甚好，在现实生活中便不如此。我的经验告诉我，要深深思考人类之人。人类的激情。谋杀的背后所藏为何，从来都是？仇恨，报复，必须使被杀者归于沉默。对金钱之贪欲，也许。研究人类之人，在任何时候。」

「听着挺在理。」约翰·昆西承认道。

===Original===
“Mostly so,” Chan averred. “Enumerate with me the clues we must consider. A guest book devoid of one page. A glove button. A message on the cable. Story of Egan, partly told. Fragment of Corsican cigarette. This newspaper ripped maybe in anger. Watch on living wrist, numeral 2 undistinct.”

“Quite a little collection,” commented John Quincy.

“Most interesting,” admitted the Chinaman. “One by one, we explore. Some cause us to arrive at nowhere. One, maybe two, will not be so unkind. I am believer in Scotland Yard method﻿—follow only essential clue. But it are not the method here. I must follow all, entire.”

“The essential clue,” repeated John Quincy.

“Sure.” Chan scowled at the waiter, for his more hygienic sector had not appeared. “Too early to say here. But I have fondness for the guest book with page omitted. Watch also claims my attention. Odd enough, when we enumerate clues this morning, we pass over watch. Foolish. Very good-looking clue. One large fault, we do not possess it. However, my eyes are sharp to apprehend it.”

“I understand,” John Quincy said, “that you’ve been rather successful as a detective.”

Chan grinned broadly. “You are educated, maybe you know,” he said. “Chinese most psychic people in the world. Sensitives, like film in camera. A look, a laugh, a gesture perhaps. Something go click.”

===Chinese===
「大体如此。」陈查理断言道，「同我一起数一数必须考虑的线索。一本缺失一页的旅客登记簿。一枚手套纽扣。电报上的一个信息。伊根的陈述，只讲了一半。科西嘉烟蒂一截。这张报纸，也许是怒中所撕。一只戴于活人手腕的表，数字2模糊难辨。」

「小小一批收藏呢。」约翰·昆西评论道。

「最有趣味。」那中国人承认道，「一条一条，我们去探查。有几条会领我们抵达空无之地。有一条，也许两条，不会如此无情。我是苏格兰场方法的信徒——只跟踪最紧要的线索。但那不是此间的方法。我必须跟踪全部，无一遗漏。」

「最紧要的线索。」约翰·昆西重复道。

「是啊。」陈查理朝伙计皱眉，因为他那块较为卫生的部分迟迟未见踪影。「此处下断语，为时尚早。但我对那本缺页的登记簿心怀偏爱。手表也攫住了我的注意。怪哉，今晨我们列举线索时，竟将手表略了过去。愚蠢。非常好看的一条线索。只欠一大缺——它不在我们手中。然而，我的双眼锐利，随时预备将其捉拿。」

「我听说，」约翰·昆西说，「你当侦探是相当有成就的。」

陈查理咧嘴而笑。「您受过教育，或许知道，」他说，「中国人是世上最具通灵之能的民族。感觉灵敏，如相机中之底片。一个眼神，一声笑，或者一个手势。咔哒——有什么东西便响了。」

===Original===
John Quincy was aware of a sudden disturbance at the door of the All American Restaurant. Bowker, the steward, gloriously drunk, was making a noisy entrance. He plunged into the room, followed by a dark, anxious-looking youth.

Embarrassed, John Quincy turned away his face, but to no avail. Bowker was bearing down upon him, waving his arms.

“Well, well, well, well!” he bellowed. “My o’ college chum. See you through the window.” He leaned heavily on the table. “How you been, o’ fellow?”

“I’m all right, thanks,” John Quincy said.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西察觉全美餐馆门口起了一阵骚动。乘务员鲍克，酩酊大醉，正大张旗鼓地进门。他一头扎进店堂，身后跟着一个皮肤黝黑、满面焦急的青年。

约翰·昆西窘迫地别过脸去，却是徒劳。鲍克挥舞着双臂，直朝他压过来。

「嗬，嗬，嗬，嗬！」他大吼道，「我的老——大学老伙计。隔着窗户就瞅见你了。」他沉甸甸地撑在桌沿上。「近来咋样啊，老——老伙计？」

「我挺好的，谢谢。」约翰·昆西说。

===Original===
The dark young man came up. He was, from his dress, a shore acquaintance of Bowker’s. “Look here, Ted,” he said. “You’ve got to be getting along﻿—”

“Jush a minute,” cried Bowker. “I want y’ to meet Mr. Quincy from Boston. One best fellows God ever made. Mushual friend o’ Tim’s﻿—you’ve heard me speak of Tim﻿—”

“Yes﻿—come along,” urged the dark young man.

“Not yet. Gotta buy thish boy a lil’ drink. What you having, Quincy, o’ man?”

“Not a thing,” smiled John Quincy. “You warned me against these Island drinks yourself.”

“Who﻿—me?” Bowker was hurt. “You’re wrong that time, o’ man. Don’ like to conter﻿—conterdict, but it mush have been somebody else. Not me. Never said a word﻿—”

===Chinese===
那黝黑的青年走上前来。看穿着，他是鲍克在岸上结识的朋友。「我说，泰德，」他说，「你该动身了——」

「就……一小会儿。」鲍克嚷道，「我要让你见见波士顿来的昆西先生。上帝造过的顶好的人之一。蒂姆的共……共同朋友——听我说起过蒂姆吧——」

「听过——走吧。」那黝黑青年催促道。

「还不成。得请这小哥喝……喝一小盅。你喝啥，昆西，老伙计？」

「什么也不喝。」约翰·昆西微笑道，「当初还是你自己告诫我，防着这些岛上的酒水呢。」

「谁——我？」鲍克受了委屈，「这回你可弄……弄错了，老伙计。不爱跟人唱反……反调，可那准是别人说的。不是我。一个字也没说过——」

===Original===
The young man took his arm. “Come on﻿—you’re due on the ship﻿—”

Bowker wrenched away. “Don’ paw me,” he cried. “Keep your hands off. I’m my own mashter, ain’t I? I can speak to an o’ friend, can’t I? Now, Quincy, o’ man﻿—what’s yours?”

“I’m sorry,” said John Quincy. “Some other time.”

Bowker’s companion took his arm in a firmer grasp. “You can’t buy anything here,” he said. “This is a restaurant. You come with me﻿—I know a place﻿—”

“Awright,” agreed Bowker. “Now you’re talking. Quincy, o’ man, you come along﻿—”

“Some other time,” John Quincy repeated.

Bowker assumed a look of offended dignity. “Jush as you say,” he replied. “Some other time. In Boston, hey? At Tim’s place. Only Tim’s place is gone.” A great grief assailed him. “Tim’s gone﻿—dropped out﻿—as though the earth swallowed him up﻿—”

“Yes, yes,” said the young man soothingly. “That’s too bad. But you come with me.”

===Chinese===
那青年挽住他的胳膊。「走吧——你船上还有差事呢——」

鲍克猛地挣开。「别扒……扒拉我。」他喊道，「把手拿开。我是我自己的主……主人，不是吗？跟老朋友说说话也不成吗？来嘛，昆西，老伙计——你喝啥？」

「对不住，」约翰·昆西说，「改天吧。」

鲍克的同伴更紧地扣住他的胳膊。「这儿什么也买不着。」他说，「这是饭馆。你跟我来——我知道一处地方——」

「好……好嘞。」鲍克同意了，「这才像话。昆西，老伙计，一块儿去——」

「改天吧。」约翰·昆西又说了一遍。

鲍克摆出一副受辱的尊严面孔。「随……随你的便。」他答道，「改天。在波士顿，嘿？在蒂姆的店里。只是蒂姆的店没……没了。」巨大的悲恸袭上他心头。「蒂姆没了——人间蒸发了——就像大地把他吞了下去——」

「是，是，」那青年好声好气地哄着，「太不幸了。可你得跟我来。」

===Original===
Submitting at last, Bowker permitted his companion to pilot him to the street. John Quincy looked across at Chan.

“My steward on the *President Tyler*,” he explained. “The worse for wear, isn’t he?”

The waiter set a fresh piece of pie before the Chinaman.

“Ah,” remarked Chan, “this has a more perfect appearance.” He tasted it. “Appearance,” he added with a grimace, “are a hellish liar. If you are quite ready to depart﻿—”

In the street Chan halted. “Excuse abrupt departure,” he said. “Most honored to work with you. The results will be fascinating, I am sure. For now, good evening.”

===Chinese===
鲍克终于屈服，由同伴把他领到街上去了。约翰·昆西望向对面的陈查理。

「泰勒总统号上伺候我的乘务员。」他解释道，「瞧他那副狼狈相，是不？」

伙计在那位中国人面前摆上一块新馅饼。

「啊，」陈查理说，「这一块，外观周正多了。」他尝了一口。「外观，」他做了个苦脸，又补上一句，「是地狱一般的说谎者。您若已备妥动身——」

到了街上，陈查理停住脚。「恕我骤然告退。」他说，「与您共事，是无上光荣。结果必将引人入胜，我确信之。眼下——晚安。」

===Original===
John Quincy was alone again in that strange town. A sudden homesickness engulfed him. Walking along, he came to a news-cart that was as well supplied with literature as his club reading room. A brisk young man in a cap was in charge.

“Have you the latest *Atlantic*?” inquired John Quincy.

The young man put a dark brown periodical into his hand. “No,” said John Quincy. “This is the June issue. I’ve seen it.”

“July ain’t in. I’ll save you one, if you say so.”

“I wish you would,” John Quincy replied. “The name is Winterslip.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西又独自一人待在这座陌生的城里。一阵突来的乡愁将他吞没。走着走着，他来到一辆售报车前，车上书报之丰盛，不亚于他会所的阅览室。看车的是个戴便帽的利落青年。

「最新的《大西洋月刊》有吗？」约翰·昆西问。

青年把一本深棕色的杂志放进他手里。「不要了，」约翰·昆西说，「这是六月号。我看过。」

「七月号还没到呢。你要是肯要，我给你留一本。」

「那敢情好，」约翰·昆西答道，「我姓温特斯利普。」

===Original===
He went on to the corner, regretting that July wasn’t in. A copy of the *Atlantic* would have been a sort of link with home, a reminder that Boston still stood. And he felt the need of a link, a reminder.

A trolley-car marked “Waikiki” was approaching. John Quincy hailed it and hopped aboard. Three giggling Japanese girls in bright kimonos drew in their tiny sandaled feet and he slipped past them to a seat.

===Chinese===
他走到街角，心里惋惜七月号还没到。一本《大西洋月刊》，本可以算是一条连着家的纽带，一个提醒：波士顿还立在那里。而他正需要一条纽带，一个提醒。

一辆标着「怀基基」的有轨电车驶来。约翰·昆西招手拦住，跳了上去。三个穿鲜艳和服的日本姑娘咯咯笑着，收拢穿木屐的小脚，他从她们身边侧身挤过，找了个座位坐下。

## XI The Tree of Jewels / 十一 珠宝树

===Original===
Two hours later, John Quincy rose from the table where he and his aunt had dined together.

“Just to show you how quick I am to learn a new language,” he remarked, “I’m quite *pau*. Now I’m going makai to sit on the lanai, there to forget the pilikia of the day.”

===Chinese===
两个钟头后，约翰·昆西从餐桌旁起身，方才他和姑婆在这张桌上共进了晚餐。

「正好向你显摆一下，我学新语言学得多快，」他说，「我已经相当*pau*（完事了）。现在我要*makai*（往海边去），坐到露台上去，把这一天的*pilikia*（烦恼）统统忘掉。」

===Original===
Miss Minerva smiled and rose too. “I expect Amos shortly,” she said as they crossed the hall. “A family conference seemed advisable, so I’ve asked him to come over.”

“Strange you had to send for him,” said John Quincy, lighting a cigarette.

“Not at all,” she answered. She explained about the long feud between the brothers.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐莞尔一笑，也站起身来。「我等阿莫斯一会儿就来，」两人穿过门厅时她说。「看来有必要开个家庭会议，所以我请他过来一趟。」

「说也奇怪，竟要你派人去请他。」约翰·昆西点上一支烟说。

「一点也不奇怪，」她答道。她把兄弟俩多年失和的宿怨讲了一遍。

===Original===
“Didn’t think old Amos had that much fire in him,” commented John Quincy, as they found chairs on the lanai. “A rather anemic specimen, judging by the look I had at him this morning. But then, the Winterslips always were good haters.”

For a moment they sat in silence. Outside the darkness was deepening rapidly, the tropic darkness that had brought tragedy the night before. John Quincy pointed to a small lizard on the screen.

===Chinese===
「没想到老阿莫斯肚子里还有这么大的火气，」约翰·昆西评论道，这时两人在露台上寻了椅子坐下。「今早我看他那模样，不过是个贫血无力的人物。不过话说回来，温特斯利普家的人向来都是记仇的行家。」

有一会儿，两人默默坐着。外头夜色急速加深，正是前一夜酿出惨剧的那种热带夜色。约翰·昆西指着纱门上一只小蜥蜴。

===Original===
“Pleasant little beast,” he said.

“Oh, they’re quite harmless,” Miss Minerva told him. “And they eat the mosquitos.”

“They do, eh?” The boy slapped his ankle savagely. “Well, there’s no accounting for tastes.”

===Chinese===
「讨人喜欢的小东西。」他说。

「哦，它们全然无害，」米纳瓦小姐告诉他，「还吃蚊子呢。」

「是吗？」年轻人狠狠拍了一下脚踝。「唉，口味这东西真是没道理可讲。」

===Original===
Amos arrived presently, looking unusually pale in the half-light. “You asked me to come over, Minerva,” he said, as he sat down gingerly on one of Dan Winterslip’s Hong-Kong chairs.

“I did. Smoke if you like.” Amos lighted a cigarette, which seemed oddly out of place between his thin lips. “I’m sure,” Miss Minerva continued, “that we are all determined to bring to justice the person who did this ghastly thing.”

===Chinese===
不多时阿莫斯到了，半明半暗的光线里，他的脸色白得异样。「你叫我过来，米纳瓦。」他说着，小心翼翼地在丹·温特斯利普的一把港式座椅上落了座。

「不错。想抽烟请便。」阿莫斯点了支烟，那支烟夹在他薄薄的嘴唇间，显得说不出的别扭。「我确信，」米纳瓦小姐接着说，「我们全都铁了心，要把干出这桩可怕事情的凶手绳之以法。」

===Original===
“Naturally,” said Amos.

“The only drawback,” she went on, “is that in the course of the investigation some rather unpleasant facts about Dan’s past are likely to be revealed.”

“They’re bound to be,” remarked Amos coldly.

“For Barbara’s sake,” Miss Minerva said. “I’m intent on seeing that nothing is revealed that is not absolutely essential to the discovery of the murderer. For that reason, I haven’t taken the police completely into my confidence.”

===Chinese===
「那是自然。」阿莫斯说。

「唯一的难处，」她接着说，「在于调查的过程中，丹过去的一些相当不体面的事实，多半会被翻出来。」

「那是免不了的。」阿莫斯冷冷地说。

「为了芭芭拉，」米纳瓦小姐说，「我一心要确保：凡是与查出凶手无绝对必要的事，一概不得披露。为此，我没有对警察和盘托出。」

===Original===
“What!” cried Amos.

John Quincy stood up. “Now look here, Aunt Minerva—”

“Sit down,” snapped his aunt. “Amos, to go back to a talk we had at your house when I was there, Dan was somewhat involved with this woman down the beach. Arlene Compton, I believe she calls herself.”

===Chinese===
「什么！」阿莫斯叫了起来。

约翰·昆西站起身。「我说，米纳瓦姑婆——」

「坐下。」姑婆厉声喝道。「阿莫斯，说回我上次在你家时我们谈过的那件事——丹跟海滩那头那个女人有些牵扯。她自称阿琳·康普顿，我记得没错的话。」

===Original===
Amos nodded. “Yes, and a worthless lot she is. But Dan wouldn’t see it, though I understand his friends pointed it out to him. He talked of marrying her.”

“You knew a good deal about Dan, even if you never spoke to him,” Miss Minerva went on. “Just what was his status with this woman at the time of his murder—only last night, but it seems ages ago.”

===Chinese===
阿莫斯点头。「是啊，那女人一无可取。可丹偏看不透，虽说据我所知，他的朋友们也向他点破过。他还谈起过要娶她。」

「你虽从不同丹说话，对他的事倒知道不少，」米纳瓦小姐接着说，「他被杀的时候，他和这女人究竟是什么光景——不过是昨天夜里的事，却恍如隔世了。」

===Original===
“I can’t quite tell you that,” Amos replied. “I do know that for the past month a malihini named Leatherbee—the black sheep of a good family in Philadelphia, they tell me—has been hanging around the Compton woman, and that Dan resented his presence.”

“Humph.” Miss Minerva handed to Amos an odd old brooch, a tree of jewels against an onyx background. “Ever see that before, Amos?”

He took it, and nodded. “It’s part of a little collection of jewelry Dan brought back from the South Seas in the ’eighties. There were earrings and a bracelet, too. He acted rather queerly about those trinkets—never let Barbara’s mother or anyone else wear them. But he must have got over that idea recently. For I saw this only a few weeks ago.”

===Chinese===
「这个我说不太准，」阿莫斯答道，「我只知道近一个月来，有个名叫莱瑟比的外来客（malihini）——听说原是费城好人家的败家子——一直在那个康普顿女人身边转悠，而丹对他的存在很是恼火。」

「哼。」米纳瓦小姐递给阿莫斯一枚古怪的老式胸针，缟玛瑙的底衬上立着一棵珠宝缀成的树。「见过这个吗，阿莫斯？」

他接过来，点了点头。「这是丹十九世纪八十年代从南洋带回来的一小批首饰里的一件。原本还有一副耳环和一只手镯。他对那些小玩意儿的行为颇为古怪——从不让芭芭拉的母亲或任何别的人佩戴。不过他最近想必想通了，因为几个星期前我就见过这枚胸针。」

===Original===
“Where?” asked Miss Minerva.

“Our office has the renting of the cottage down the beach occupied at present by the Compton woman. She came in not long ago to pay her rent, and she was wearing this brooch.” He looked suddenly at Miss Minerva. “Where did you get it?” he demanded.

“Kamaikui gave it to me early this morning,” Miss Minerva explained. “She picked it up from the floor of the lanai before the police came.”

===Chinese===
「在哪儿？」米纳瓦小姐问。

「海滩那头那座小屋眼下由康普顿那个女人住着，正是我们事务所经手出租的。不久前她来交房租，身上就别着这枚胸针。」他蓦地看向米纳瓦小姐。「你这是从哪儿弄来的？」他追问。

「卡迈库伊今天一早给我的，」米纳瓦小姐解释道，「警察来之前，她从露台的地板上拾起了它。」

===Original===
John Quincy leaped to his feet. “You’re all wrong, Aunt Minerva,” he cried. “You can’t do this sort of thing. You ask the help of the police, and you aren’t on the level with them. I’m ashamed of you—”

“Please wait a moment,” said his aunt.

“Wait nothing!” he answered. “Give me that brooch. I’m going to turn it over to Chan at once. I couldn’t look him in the eye if I didn’t.”

“We’ll turn it over to Chan,” said Miss Minerva calmly, “if it seems important. But there is no reason in the world why we should not investigate a bit ourselves before we do so. The woman may have a perfectly logical explanation—”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西一跃而起。「你全错了，米纳瓦姑婆，」他嚷道，「这种事你做不得。一边请警察帮忙，一边又对他们不吐真情。我替你害臊——」

「请稍等片刻。」姑婆说。

「一刻也不等！」他答道，「把胸针给我。我要立刻把它交给陈查理。不然的话，我没脸直视他的眼睛。」

「我们会交给陈查理的，」米纳瓦小姐不慌不忙地说，「如果它确实要紧的话。可在那之前，我们先自己稍稍查证一下，这于情于理没有任何说不过去的。那女人说不定有个合情合理、无懈可击的解释——」

===Original===
“Rot!” interrupted John Quincy. “The trouble with you is, you think you’re Sherlock Holmes.”

“What is your opinion, Amos?” inquired Miss Minerva.

“I’m inclined to agree with John Quincy,” Amos said. “You are hardly fair to Captain Hallet. And as for keeping anything dark on account of Barbara—or on anybody’s account—that won’t be possible, I’m afraid. No getting round it, Minerva, Dan’s indiscretions are going to be dragged into the open at last.”

===Chinese===
「胡扯！」约翰·昆西打断她。「你的毛病在于，你自以为是歇洛克·福尔摩斯。」

「你的意见呢，阿莫斯？」米纳瓦小姐问。

「我倾向于站在约翰·昆西一边，」阿莫斯说，「你对哈利特警长未免不公道。至于为了芭芭拉——或者为了任何人——隐瞒什么，恐怕都是办不到的。绕不过去的，米纳瓦，丹那些不检点的旧账，终究要被拖到光天化日之下了。」

===Original===
She caught the note of satisfaction in his tone, and was nettled by it. “Perhaps. At the same time, it isn’t going to do any harm for some member of the family to have a talk with this woman before we consult the police. If she should have a perfectly sincere and genuine explanation—”

“Oh, yes,” cut in John Quincy. “She wouldn’t have any other kind.”

“It won’t be so much what she says,” persisted Miss Minerva. “It will be the manner in which she says it. Any intelligent person can see through deceit and falsehood. The only question is, which of us is the intelligent person best fitted to examine her.”

===Chinese===
她听出他话音里那丝幸灾乐祸的得意，心头火起。「也许是吧。不过，在报警之前，由家里哪一位先去找这女人谈一谈，总不至于是什么坏事。倘若她当真有个诚意十足、货真价实的解释——」

「哦，是啊，」约翰·昆西插嘴道，「她还能拿出别的哪一种来。」

「要紧的倒不在她说了什么，」米纳瓦小姐固执地说，「而在她怎么说。凡是聪明人，都能看穿欺骗与谎言。唯一的问题是，咱们当中哪一位是最适合去盘问她的那个聪明人。」

===Original===
“Count me out,” said Amos promptly.

“John Quincy?”

The boy considered. He had asked for the privilege of working with Chan, and here, perhaps, was an opportunity to win the Chinaman’s respect. But this sounded rather like a woman who would be too much for him.

“No, thanks,” he said.

===Chinese===
「别把我算上。」阿莫斯当即说。

「约翰·昆西呢？」

年轻人踌躇了。他先前主动讨过与陈查理共事的差事，而这，或许正是个赢得那华人敬重的机会。只是眼前这位，听着颇像是那种他招架不住的女人。

「不去了，谢谢。」他说。

===Original===
“Very good,” replied Miss Minerva, rising. “I’ll go myself.”

“Oh, no,” cried John Quincy, shocked.

“Why not? If none of the men in the family are up to it. As a matter of fact, I welcome the opportunity—”

Amos shook his head. “She’ll twist you round her little finger,” he predicted.

===Chinese===
「很好，」米纳瓦小姐答道，站起身来。「那我自己去。」

「哦，那可使不得。」约翰·昆西叫道，大为骇然。

「为什么使不得？家里的男人们既然一个个都不顶事。说实话，我还巴不得有这个机会呢——」

阿莫斯摇摇头。「她会把你耍得团团转。」他预言道。

===Original===
Miss Minerva smiled grimly. “I should like to see her do it. Will you wait here?”

John Quincy went over and took the brooch from Amos’s hand. “Sit down, Aunt Minerva,” he said. “I’ll see this woman. But I warn you that immediately afterward I shall send for Chan.”

“That,” his aunt told him, “will be decided at another conference. I’m not so sure, John Quincy, that you are the proper person to go. After all, what experience have you had with women of this type?”

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐冷冷一笑。「我倒要看看她怎么个转法。你们在这儿等着？」

约翰·昆西走过去，从阿莫斯手里拿过胸针。「坐下，米纳瓦姑婆，」他说，「我去见这女人。但我警告你，一谈完我立刻就去请陈查理。」

「那个嘛，」姑婆告诉他，「留待下次会议再议。我可不怎么放心让你去，约翰·昆西。说到底，你跟这种类型的女人打过多少交道？」

===Original===
John Quincy was offended. He was a man, and he felt that he could meet and outwit a woman of any type. He said as much.

Amos described the woman’s house as a small cottage several hundred yards down the beach, and directed the boy how to get there. John Quincy set out.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西觉得受了冒犯。他是堂堂男子汉，自忖无论哪种类型的女人，他都应付得了、也斗得过。他把这层意思明明白白说了出来。

阿莫斯把那女人的住处描述了一番：海滩下游几百码外的一座小屋，又给年轻人指点了路径。约翰·昆西动身了。

===Original===
Night had fallen over the island when he reached Kalia Road, a bright silvery night, for the Kona weather was over and the moon traveled a cloudless sky. The scent of plumeria and ginger stole out to him through hedges of flaming hibiscus; the trade winds, blowing across a thousand miles of warm water, still managed a cool touch on his cheek. As he approached what he judged must be the neighborhood of the woman’s house, a flock of Indian myna birds in a spreading algaroba screamed loudly, their harsh voices the only note of discord in that peaceful scene.

===Chinese===
他走到卡利亚路时，夜幕已笼罩全岛。这是一个明亮银白的夜，科纳天气已经过去，月亮在无云的天空中行进。鸡蛋花与姜花的香气，从烈焰般盛开的朱槿花篱间悄悄袭来；信风跋涉过一千英里的暖水，拂到他脸颊上时仍带着几分清凉。行近他估计该是那女人住处的一片地方时，一棵枝繁叶茂的牧豆树里，一群印度八哥高声尖叫着，那粗厉的嗓音是这片安宁景致中唯一的不谐之音。

===Original===
He had some difficulty locating the cottage, which was almost completely hidden under masses of flowering alamander, its blossoms pale yellow in the moonlight. Before the door, a dark fragrant spot under a heavily laden trellis, he paused uncertainly. A rather delicate errand, this was. But he summoned his courage and knocked.

Only the myna birds replied. John Quincy stood there, growing momentarily more hostile to the Widow of Waikiki. Some huge coarse creature, no doubt, a man’s woman, a good fellow at a party—that kind. Then the door opened and the boy got a shock. For the figure outlined against the light was young and slender, and the face, dimly seen, suggested fragile loveliness.

===Chinese===
他费了些周折才找到那座小屋——它几乎整个儿掩没在成丛盛放的黄蝉花下，花朵在月光里泛着淡黄。屋门前，一架开得沉甸甸的花棚底下，有块幽暗芬芳的所在，他在那里迟疑地驻足。这可是一桩颇为微妙的差事。但他鼓起勇气，叩响了门。

应门的只有八哥。约翰·昆西站在那儿，对这位怀基基寡妇的敌意分分钟在增。准是个高大粗俗的货色，没跑——男人的女人，宴会上称兄道弟的好伙伴——就那一类。谁知门一开，年轻人吃了一惊。灯光衬出的身影年轻而苗条，那张朦胧可见的脸，透着一种易碎的秀美。

===Original===
“Is this Mrs. Compton?” he inquired.

“Yeah—I’m Mrs. Compton. What do you want?” John Quincy was sorry she had spoken. For she was, obviously, one of those beauties so prevalent nowadays, the sort whom speech betrays. Her voice recalled the myna birds.

“My name is John Quincy Winterslip.” He saw her start. “May I speak with you for a moment?”

===Chinese===
「请问是康普顿夫人吗？」他问道。

「是啊——我就是康普顿夫人。你要干什么？」她一开口，约翰·昆西便觉遗憾。因为她显然属于如今遍地都是的那一类美人：一开口便露馅的那种。她的嗓音让人想起八哥。

「我叫约翰·昆西·温特斯利普。」他看见她一怔。「可以同您说几句话吗？」

===Original===
“Sure you can. Come in.” She led the way along a low narrow passage into a tiny living-room. A pasty-faced young man with stooped shoulders stood by a table, fondling a cocktail shaker.

“Steve,” said the woman, “this is Mr. Winterslip. Mr. Leatherbee.”

Mr. Leatherbee grunted. “Just in time for a little snifter,” he remarked.

===Chinese===
「当然可以。进来吧。」她引路走过一条低矮狭窄的过道，进了一间小小的起居室。一个面色苍白的驼背青年站在桌旁，手里摩挲着一只鸡尾酒摇壶。

「史蒂夫，」女人说，「这位是温特斯利普先生。这位是莱瑟比先生。」

莱瑟比先生哼了一声。「来得正好，赶上一小盅。」他说。

===Original===
“No, thanks,” John Quincy said. He saw Mrs. Compton take a smoking cigarette from an ash tray, start to convey it to her lips, then, evidently thinking better of it, crush it on the tray.

“Well,” said Mr. Leatherbee, “your poison’s ready, Arlene.” He proffered a glass.

She shook her head, slightly annoyed. “No.”

“No?” Mr. Leatherbee grinned. “The more for little Stevie.” He lifted a glass. “Here’s looking at you, Mr. Winterslip.”

===Chinese===
「不了，谢谢。」约翰·昆西说。他看见康普顿夫人从烟灰盘里拿起一支点燃的烟，正要送到唇边，又显然回心转意，把它在盘里捻灭了。

「哎，」莱瑟比先生说，「你那杯『毒药』得了，阿琳。」他递过一只酒杯。

她摇摇头，微露愠色。「不喝。」

「不喝？」莱瑟比先生咧嘴一笑。「那小史蒂维就多沾光喽。」他举起一杯。「这杯敬你，温特斯利普先生。」

===Original===
“Say, I guess you’re Dan’s cousin from Boston,” Mrs. Compton remarked. “He was telling me about you.” She lowered her voice. “I’ve been meaning to get over to your place all day. But it was such a shock—it knocked me flat.”

“I understand,” John Quincy replied. He glanced at Mr. Leatherbee, who seemed not to have heard of prohibition. “My business with you, Mrs. Compton, is private.”

Leatherbee stiffened belligerently. But the woman said: “That’s all right. Steve was just going.”

===Chinese===
「我说，你准是丹家那位波士顿来的堂亲吧，」康普顿夫人搭话道，「他跟我说起过你。」她压低了嗓音。「我一整天都想着过府上去看看。可这事太叫人震惊了——把我整个人都击垮了。」

「我理解。」约翰·昆西答道。他瞥了一眼莱瑟比先生——此人似乎压根没听说过禁酒令。「我找您的事，夫人，是私事。」

莱瑟比挑衅地绷起了身子。那女人却说：「没事儿。史蒂夫正要走呢。」

===Original===
Steve hesitated a moment, then went. His hostess accompanied him. John Quincy heard the low monotone of their voices in the distance. There was a combined odor of gin and cheap perfume in the air; the boy wondered what his mother would say if she could see him now. A door slammed, and the woman returned.

“Well?” she said. John Quincy perceived that her eyes were hard and knowing, like her voice. He waited for her to sit down, then took a chair facing her.

“You knew my Cousin Dan rather intimately,” he suggested.

===Chinese===
史蒂夫迟疑了一下，还是走了。女主人送他出去。约翰·昆西听见远处两人低低的、平板的说话声。屋里浮着一股杜松子酒混着廉价香水的气味；年轻人暗想，倘若母亲此刻见到他这副光景，不知会说什么。一扇门砰地关上，女人回来了。

「怎么样？」她说。约翰·昆西察觉她的目光同她的嗓音一样，又硬又世故。他等她坐下，才在她对面落了座。

「您跟我堂亲丹的交情，可不浅吧。」他试探道。

===Original===
“I was engaged to him,” she answered. John Quincy glanced at her left hand. “He hadn’t come across—I mean, he hadn’t given me a ring, but it was—you know—understood between us.”

“Then his death is a good deal of a blow to you?”

She managed a baby stare, full of pathos. “I’ll say it is. Mr. Winterslip was kind to me—he believed in me and trusted me. A lone woman way out here don’t get any too much char—kindness.”

===Chinese===
「我跟他订过婚来着。」她答道。约翰·昆西瞟了一眼她的左手。「他还没兑现——我是说，他还没给我戒指，可咱们俩——你懂的——彼此心里有数。」

「那么他的死，对您打击不小吧？」

她挤出一副婴孩般的眼神，楚楚可怜。「可不是嘛。温特斯利普先生待我好——他信我这个人，也信得过我。孤身一个女人在这天边上，得不到多少善心——好意的。」

===Original===
“When did you see Mr. Winterslip last?”

“Three or four days ago—last Friday evening, I guess it was.”

John Quincy frowned. “Wasn’t that rather a long stretch?”

She nodded. “I’ll tell you the truth. We had a little—misunderstanding. Just a lover’s quarrel, you know. Dan sort of objected to Steve hanging around. Not that he’d any reason to—Steve’s nothing to me—just a weak kid I used to know when I was trouping. I was on the stage—maybe you heard that.”

===Chinese===
「您最后一次见温特斯利普先生，是什么时候？」

「三四天前吧——大概是上星期五晚上。」

约翰·昆西皱起眉头。「隔得未免久了些吧？」

她点点头。「我跟您说实话吧。我们闹了点小——小别扭。不过是情人拌嘴罢了，您知道的。丹嘛，有点看不惯史蒂夫老在旁边转。倒不是说他有什么理由——史蒂夫对我什么也不是——不过是我当年跑码头演戏时认识的一个软骨头小子。我从前是吃舞台饭的——兴许您听说过。」

===Original===
“Yes,” said John Quincy. “You hadn’t seen Mr. Winterslip since last Friday. You didn’t go to his house last evening?”

“I should say not. I got my reputation to think of—you’ve no idea how people talk in a place like this—”

John Quincy laid the brooch down upon the table. It sparkled in the lamplight—a reading lamp, though the atmosphere was not in the least literary. The baby stare was startled now. “You recognize that, don’t you?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「嗯。」约翰·昆西说，「就是说，从上星期五起您没再见过温特斯利普先生。昨天晚上，您也没去过他家？」

「当然没有。我还得顾着自己的名声呢——您想象不到这种地方的人嘴有多碎——」

约翰·昆西把胸针搁在桌上。它在灯光下熠熠生辉——那是一盏读书用的台灯，虽说这屋里的气氛丝毫谈不上文学。那副婴孩神情顿时惊慌起来。「您认得它，对吧？」他问。

===Original===
“Why—yes—it’s—I—”

“Just stick to the truth,” said John Quincy, not unkindly. “It’s an old piece of jewelry that Mr. Winterslip gave you, I believe.”

“Well—”

“You’ve been seen wearing it, you know.”

“Yes, he did give it to me,” she admitted. “The only present I ever got from him. I guess from the look of it Mrs. Noah wore it on the Ark. Kinda pretty, though.”

===Chinese===
「这个——是的——这是——我——」

「实话实说就好。」约翰·昆西说，语气并不刻薄。「我想，这是温特斯利普先生送您的一件老首饰。」

「唔——」

「有人见过您戴着它呢。」

「好吧，他确实给过我。」她认了。「这是他给过我唯一的一件礼物。瞧它这副老相，只怕挪亚太太当年在方舟上都戴过。不过倒还挺俏的。」

===Original===
“You didn’t visit Mr. Winterslip last night,” persisted John Quincy. “Yet, strangely enough, this brooch was found on the floor not far from his dead body.”

She drew in her breath sharply. “Say—what are you? A cop?” she asked.

“Hardly,” John Quincy smiled. “I am here simply to save you, if possible, from the hands of the—er—the cops. If you have any real explanation of this matter, it may not be necessary to call it to the attention of the police.”

===Chinese===
「您昨晚没有拜访过温特斯利普先生，」约翰·昆西紧追不舍，「可是说来奇怪，这枚胸针在他尸首近旁的地板上被人发现了。」

她猛地倒吸一口气。「嘿——你是什么人？条子？」她问。

「那倒不是，」约翰·昆西含笑说，「我来这里，只是为了——如果办得到的话——把您从呃——从条子们手里搭救出来。倘若您对这件事当真有什么解释，或许就不必把它捅到警察那儿去了。」

===Original===
“Oh!” She smiled. “Say, that’s decent of you. Now I will tell you the truth. That about not seeing Dan Winterslip since Friday was bunk. I saw him last night.”

“Ah—you did? Where?”

“Right here. Mr. Winterslip gave me that thing about a month ago. Two weeks ago he came to me in a sort of excited way and said he must have it back. It was the only thing he ever give me and I liked it and those emeralds are valuable—so—well, I stalled a while. I said I was having a new clasp put on it. He kept asking for it, and last night he showed up here and said he just had to have it. Said he’d buy me anything in the stores in place of it. I must say he was pretty het up. So I finally turned it over to him and he took it and went away.”

“What time was that?”

===Chinese===
「哦！」她笑了。「嘿，你可真够意思。这回我跟你说实话。什么从星期五起没见过丹·温特斯利普，那是瞎编的。昨天夜里我见着他了。」

「哦——是吗？在哪儿？」

「就在这儿。温特斯利普先生一个月前把这玩意儿给了我。两星期前他来找我，一副火急火燎的样子，说他必须把它收回去。这是他给过我唯一的东西，我喜欢它，再说那些祖母绿也值钱——所以——唔，我就拖了一阵子。我说我正给它配个新搭扣。他一个劲儿地讨，昨天夜里他跑到这儿来，说他非拿到手不可。还说店里随便我挑什么，他都买给我。我得说，他那会儿火气真不小。最后我只好交还给他，他拿了就走。」

「那是几点的事？」

===Original===
“About nine-thirty. He was happy and pleasant and he said I could go to a jewelry store this morning and take my pick of the stock.” She looked pleadingly at John Quincy. “That’s the last I ever saw of him. It’s the truth, so help me.”

“I wonder,” mused John Quincy.

She moved nearer. “Say, you’re a nice kid,” she said. “The kind I used to meet in Boston when we played there. The kind that’s got some consideration for a woman. You ain’t going to drag me into this. Think what it would mean—to me.”

John Quincy did not speak. He saw there were tears in her eyes. “You’ve probably heard things about me,” she went on, “but they ain’t true. You don’t know what I been up against out here. An unprotected woman don’t have much chance anywhere, but on this beach, where men come drifting in from all over the world—I been friendly, that’s my only trouble. I was homesick—oh, God, wasn’t I homesick! I was having a good time back there, and then I fell for Bill Compton and came out here with him, and sometimes in the night I’d wake up and remember Broadway was five thousand miles away, and I’d cry so hard I’d wake him. And that made him sore—”

===Chinese===
「九点半的样子。他高高兴兴、和和气气的，说我今天上午尽可以去哪家珠宝店，店里随便挑。」她一副恳求相望着约翰·昆西。「那是我最后一回见到他。句句是实话，天知道。」

「难说啊。」约翰·昆西沉吟道。

她挪近了些。「我说，你是个好孩子，」她说，「是我们从前在波士顿演出时常碰到的那种人。懂得体恤女人的那一种。你不会把我拽进这摊事里去的。想想那意味着什么——对我。」

约翰·昆西没有作声。他看见她眼里噙着泪。「你们八成听说过我的种种闲话，」她接着说，「可那些不是真的。你不知道我在这儿是何等处境。一个没遮没靠的女人，在哪儿都没多少活路，何况在这片海滩上，天南地北的男人漂流而至——我不过是待人热络了些，这是我唯一的毛病。我想家——哦，天哪，我何尝不想家！我在那边本来过得快活，后来迷上了比尔·康普顿，跟着他到了这儿。有时半夜里我醒来，想起百老汇远在五千英里之外，就哭得死去活来，把他都哭醒了。这一来他就恼了——」

===Original===
She paused. John Quincy was impressed by the note of true nostalgia in her voice. He was, suddenly, rather sorry for her.

“Then Bill’s plane crashed on Diamond Head,” she continued, “and I was all alone. And these black sheep along the beach, they knew I was alone—and broke. And I was homesick for Forty-second Street, for the old boardinghouse and the old gang and the Automat and the chewing-gum sign, and tryouts at New Haven. So I gave a few parties just to forget, and people began to talk.”

“You might have gone back,” John Quincy suggested.

===Chinese===
她顿住了。约翰·昆西被她话音里那缕真切的乡愁打动了。一时间，他竟有些怜惜她。

「后来，比尔的飞机在钻石头山上摔了下来，」她继续说，「我就彻底孤身一人了。海滩边这些不务正业的家伙，他们知道我孤身——还穷。我想念第四十二街，想念老寄宿公寓和老伙伴们，想念自动餐馆和那块口香糖大招牌，想念纽黑文的试演。于是我办了几场聚会，只为把这一切忘掉，于是人们就开始嚼舌根了。」

「你本可以回去的。」约翰·昆西提了一句。

===Original===
“I know—why didn’t I? I been intending to, right along, but every day out here is just like any other day, and somehow you don’t get round to picking one out—I been drifting—but honest to God if you keep me out of this I’ll go home on the first boat. I’ll get me a job, and—and—If you’ll only keep me out of it. You got a chance now to wreck my life—it’s all up to you—but I know you ain’t going to—”

She seized John Quincy’s hand in both of hers, and gazed at him pleadingly through her tears. It was the most uncomfortable moment of his life. He looked wildly about the little room, so different from any in the house on Beacon Street. He pulled his hand away.

“I’ll—I’ll see,” he said, rising hastily. “I’ll think it over.”

“But I can’t sleep tonight if I don’t know,” she told him.

===Chinese===
「我知道——我怎么就没回去呢？我一直打算回去来着，可这地方的日子天天一个模样，不知怎么的就是挑不出个动身的日子——我一路漂着——可老天在上，只要你不把我扯进去，我头一班船就回家。我会找份活儿干，还有——还有——只要你肯不把我扯进去。你如今有的是机会毁掉我一辈子——一切全在你手里——可我知道你不会的——」

她双手一齐抓住约翰·昆西的手，噙着泪恳求地凝望他。这是他平生最狼狈的一刻。他慌乱地环顾这间小屋，它同灯塔街宅子里的任何一间都毫无相似之处。他把手抽了回来。

「我——我考虑考虑，」他赶忙起身说，「我会想想的。」

「可我要是不知道个准信儿，今晚觉都睡不成了。」她对他说。

===Original===
“I’ll have to think it over,” he repeated. He turned toward the table in time to see the woman’s slim hand reach out and seize the bit of jewelry. “I’ll take the brooch,” he added.

She looked up at him. Suddenly John Quincy knew that she had been acting, that his emotions had been falsely played upon, and he felt again that hot rush of blood to the head, that quick surge of anger, he had experienced in Dan Winterslip’s hall. Aunt Minerva had predicted he couldn’t handle a woman of this type. Well, he’d show her—he’d show the world. “Give me that brooch,” he said coldly.

“It’s mine,” answered the woman stubbornly.

John Quincy wasted no words; he seized the woman’s wrist. She screamed. A door opened behind them.

===Chinese===
「我得考虑考虑。」他重复道。他转向桌子，恰好看见那女人纤瘦的手探出去，一把攫住那件首饰。「胸针我带走了。」他补了一句。

她抬眼瞪着他。约翰·昆西霎时明白了：她方才全是在做戏，他的怜悯之情被人耍弄了。他再度感到那股直冲头顶的热血、那阵急涌上来的怒火，同他在丹·温特斯利普家门厅里体味过的一模一样。米纳瓦姑婆料定他应付不了这种类型的女人。好啊，他要做给她看——做给全世界看。「把胸针给我。」他冷冷地说。

「那是我的。」女人硬邦邦地答道。

约翰·昆西不再费话，一把攥住她的手腕。她尖叫起来。他们身后有一扇门开了。

===Original===
“What’s going on here?” inquired Mr. Leatherbee.

“Oh, I thought you’d left us,” said John Quincy.

“Steve! Don’t let him have it,” cried the woman. Steve moved militantly nearer, but there was a trace of caution in his attitude.

===Chinese===
「这儿唱的是哪一出啊？」莱瑟比先生问道。

「哦，我还当你已经走了呢。」约翰·昆西说。

「史蒂夫！别让他拿走。」女人喊道。史蒂夫气势汹汹地逼近一步，姿态里却带着三分小心。

===Original===
John Quincy laughed. “You stay where you are, Steve,” he advised. “Or I’ll smash that sallow face of yours.” Strange talk for a Winterslip. “Your friend here is trying to hang on to an important bit of evidence in the murder up the beach, and with the utmost reluctance I am forced to use strong-arm methods.” The brooch dropped to the floor, he stooped and picked it up. “Well, I guess that’s about all,” he added. “I’m sorry if you’ve been homesick, Mrs. Compton, but speaking as a Bostonian, I don’t believe Broadway is as glamourous as you picture it. Distance has lent enchantment. Good night.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西笑了。「你待在原地别动，史蒂夫，」他劝道，「不然我就砸扁你那张菜色脸。」这话出自温特斯利普家的人之口，可算新鲜。「你这位朋友一心想霸住海滩上游那桩凶案里的一件重要物证，万般无奈之下，我只得动用武力手段了。」胸针落到了地板上，他俯身拾起。「好了，该办的差不多都办完了，」他补了一句，「你要是想家，我深表遗憾，康普顿夫人。不过，以一个波士顿人的身份讲句话，我不信百老汇有你描绘的那么令人神往。距离增添魅力罢了。晚安。」

===Original===
He let himself out, and found his way to Kalakaua Avenue. He had settled one thing to his own satisfaction; Chan must know about the brooch, and at once. Mrs. Compton’s story might be true or not, it certainly needed further investigation by some responsible person.

John Quincy had approached the cottage by way of Kalia Road, he was planning to return to Dan’s house along the better lighted avenue. Having reached that broad expanse of asphalt, however, he realized that the Reef and Palm Hotel was near at hand. There was his promise to Carlota Egan—he had said he would look in on her again today. As for Chan, he could telephone the Chinaman from the hotel. He turned in the direction of the Reef and Palm.

===Chinese===
他自己开门出去，一路寻到卡拉卡瓦大道。有一件事他心中已然拿定：胸针的事必须让陈查理知道，而且刻不容缓。康普顿夫人的说辞是真是假且不论，总之得有个负责任的人再细细查上一查。

约翰·昆西来时走的是卡利亚路，本打算顺着这条灯火较亮的大道回丹宅。然而一踏上那片开阔的沥青路面，他才想起「礁石棕榈」旅馆近在咫尺。他还欠着卡洛塔·伊根一个承诺——他说过今天要再去看她。至于陈查理，可以从旅馆里给那华人打个电话。他转身朝「礁石棕榈」的方向去了。

===Original===
Stumbling through the dark garden, he saw finally the gaunt old hulk of the hotel. Lights of low candle power burned at infrequent intervals on the double-decked veranda. In the huge lobby a few rather shabby-looking guests took their ease. Behind the desk stood—nobody but the Japanese clerk.

John Quincy was directed to a telephone booth, and his keen Bostonian mind required Nipponese aid in mastering the dial system favored by the Honolulu telephone company. At length he got the police station. Chan was out, but the answering voice promised that he would be told to get in touch with Mr. Winterslip immediately on his return.

===Chinese===
深一脚浅一脚地穿过黑黢黢的花园，他终于望见旅馆那具嶙峋的老骨架。双层游廊上，隔着老远才亮着一盏盏昏弱的光。宽敞的大堂里，几位模样颇为寒酸的客人在悠然歇息。柜台后站着的——除了那个日本职员，别无他人。

有人把约翰·昆西领到电话亭，而要降服檀香山电话公司所青睐的那套拨号系统，他这颗精明的波士顿头脑还须借助日本人的援手。好半晌他才接通警察局。陈查理外出了，不过接电话的那位声音答应，他一回来就立刻让他联系温特斯利普先生。

===Original===
“How much do I owe you?” inquired John Quincy of the clerk.

“Not a penny,” said a voice, and he turned to find Carlota Egan at his elbow. He smiled. This was more like it.

“But I say—you know—I’ve used your telephone—”

“It’s free,” she said. “Too many things are free out here. That’s why we don’t get rich. It was so kind of you to come again.”

===Chinese===
「我该付多少钱？」约翰·昆西问那职员。

「一分钱也不用付。」一个声音说，他转过身，正见卡洛塔·伊根在自己肘边。他笑了。这才像话嘛。

「可是我说——你知道——我用了你们的电话——」

「不要钱的，」她说，「这地方白送的东西太多了。所以我们才发不了财。你肯再来，真是太好心了。」

===Original===
“Not at all,” he protested. He looked about the room. “Your father—”

She glanced at the clerk, and led the way out to the lanai at the side. They went to the far end of it, where they could see the light on Diamond Head, and the silvery waters of the Pacific sweeping in to disappear at last beneath the old Reef and Palm.

“I’m afraid poor dad’s having a bad time of it,” she said, and her voice broke slightly. “I haven’t been able to see him. They’re holding him down there—as a witness, I believe. There was some talk of bail, but I didn’t listen. We haven’t any money—at least, I didn’t think we had.”

===Chinese===
「哪里的话。」他客气着。他环顾屋子。「令尊——」

她朝那职员瞥了一眼，领着他出门，来到侧面的露台上。两人走到露台尽头，从那里望得见钻石头山上的灯光，望得见太平洋银亮的海水一阵阵涌来，最终消逝在古老的礁石棕榈之下。

「恐怕可怜的爸爸眼下日子很不好过，」她说，嗓音微微发颤。「我一直没能见到他。他们把他扣押在那边——我想是当作证人。有人提过保释，我没往下听。我们一点钱也没有——至少，我原以为没有。」

===Original===
“You didn’t think—” he began, puzzled.

She produced a small bit of paper, and put it in his hand. “I want to ask your advice. I’ve been cleaning up dad’s office, and just before you came I ran across that in his desk.”

John Quincy stared down at the little pink slip she had given him. By the light of one of the small lamps he saw that it was a check for five thousand dollars, made out to “Bearer” and signed by Dan Winterslip. The date was that of the day before.

“I say, that looks important, doesn’t it?” John Quincy said. He handed it back to her, and thought a moment. “By gad—it is important. It seems to me it’s pretty conclusive evidence of your father’s innocence. If he had that, his business with Cousin Dan must have come to a successful end, and it isn’t likely he would—er—do away with the man who signed it and complicate the cashing of it.”

===Chinese===
「你原以为没有——」他困惑地开了口。

她掏出一小张纸片，放进他手里。「我想请你参谋参谋。我一直在收拾爸爸的办公室，就在你来之前，我在他书桌抽屉里翻出了这个。」

约翰·昆西低头盯着她递来的那张粉色小纸片。借着一盏小灯的光，他看出这是一张五千元的支票，抬头写着「持票人」，签名是丹·温特斯利普。日期就是前一天。

「我说，这东西瞧着要紧，是不是？」约翰·昆西说。他把它递还给她，思忖片刻。「天哪——是要紧。依我看，这几乎是令尊清白的确凿证据。既然他拿到了这张支票，他同我堂亲丹的那桩交易必定已圆满了结，他总不至于——呃——把签支票的人结果掉，再叫兑起支票来节外生枝吧。」

===Original===
The girl’s eyes shone. “Just the way I reasoned. But I don’t know what to do with it.”

“Your father has engaged a lawyer, of course.”

“Yes, but a rather poor one. The only kind we can afford. Should I turn this over to him?”

“No—wait a minute. Any chance of seeing your father soon?”

===Chinese===
姑娘的眼睛亮了。「我也是这么推断的。可我不知道拿它怎么办才好。」

「令尊当然请了律师吧。」

「请了，可请的是个不怎么样的。我们只请得起那一档的。我该把这个交给他吗？」

「不——等等。最近有机会见到令尊吗？」

===Original===
“Yes. It’s been arranged I’m to visit him in the morning.”

John Quincy nodded. “Better talk with him before you do anything,” he advised. He had a sudden recollection of Egan’s face when he refused to explain his business with Dan Winterslip. “Take this check with you and ask your father what he wants done with it. Point out to him that it’s vital evidence in his favor.”

“Yes, I guess that’s the best plan,” the girl agreed. “Will—will you sit down a moment?”

“Well.” John Quincy recalled Miss Minerva waiting impatiently for news. “Just a moment. I want to know how you’re getting on. Any big arithmetical problems come up yet?”

===Chinese===
「有。已经安排好了，明天上午我去探望他。」

约翰·昆西点点头。「采取行动之前，最好先跟他本人谈谈。」他建议道。他蓦地记起伊根拒不肯说明他与丹·温特斯利普有何干系时的那张脸。「带上这张支票，问问令尊想怎么处置。向他点明，这是对他有利的关键证据。」

「好的，我想这是最好的办法。」姑娘表示赞同。「你——你愿不愿意坐一会儿？」

「好吧。」约翰·昆西想起米纳瓦小姐还正不耐烦地等着消息。「就坐一会儿。我想知道你近来干得怎么样。有没有碰上什么大算术难题？」

===Original===
She shook her head. “Not yet. It really isn’t so bad, the work. We haven’t many guests, you know. I could be quite happy—if it weren’t for poor dad.” She sighed. “Ever since I can remember,” she added, “my happiness has had an if in it.”

He led her on to speak about herself, there in the calm night by that romantic beach. Through her talk flashed little pictures of her motherless childhood on this exotic shore, of a wearing fight against poverty and her father’s bitter struggle to send her to school on the mainland, to give her what he considered her proper place in the world. Here was a girl far different from any he had met on Beacon Street, and John Quincy found pleasure in her talk.

===Chinese===
她摇摇头。「还没有。这活儿其实不算坏。你知道，我们的客人不多。我本可以过得相当快活——要不是为了可怜的爸爸。」她叹了口气。「打从我记事的时候起，」她补了一句，「我的快活里就总带着个『要是』。」

在那个安宁的夜晚，在那片浪漫的海滩旁，他引着她讲起了她自己。她的话语间闪过一幅幅小小的图景：她在这片异域海岸上失去母亲的童年，一场与贫穷的持久鏖战，她父亲含辛茹苦供她去本土上学，好让她得到他心目中她在这世上应有的位置。这是一位同他在灯塔街结识的任何姑娘都迥然不同的姑娘，约翰·昆西听她倾谈，只觉其乐陶陶。

===Original===
Finally he forced himself to leave. As they walked along the balcony they encountered one of the guests, a meek little man with stooped shoulders. Even at that late hour he wore a bathing suit.

“Any luck, Mr. Saladine?” the girl inquired.

“Luck ith againth me,” he lisped, and passed hastily on.

Carlota Egan laughed softly. “Oh, I really shouldn’t,” she repented at once. “The poor man.”

===Chinese===
最后，他逼着自己起身告辞。两人沿着阳台走的时候，迎面撞见一位房客——一个缩肩驼背、低眉顺眼的小个子男人。都这么晚了的时辰，他身上居然还穿着泳装。

「有收获吗，萨拉迪尼先生？」姑娘问道。

「运气又跟我作对。」他口齿漏风地说着，匆匆走了过去。

卡洛塔·伊根轻轻笑出声来。「哦，我真不该笑，」她随即悔道，「可怜的人。」

===Original===
“What’s his trouble?” asked John Quincy.

“He’s a tourist—a business man,” she said. “Des Moines, or some place like that. And he’s had the most appalling accident. He’s lost his teeth.”

“His teeth!” repeated John Quincy.

“Yes. Like so many things in this world, they were false. He got into a battle with a roller out by the second raft, and they disappeared. Since then he spends all his time out there, peering down into the water by day, and diving down and feeling about by night. One of the tragic figures of history,” she added.

John Quincy laughed.

===Chinese===
「他怎么了？」约翰·昆西问。

「他是位游客——位生意人，」她说，「得梅因，还是那一类地方来的。他遭了一场骇人听闻的意外。他把牙齿丢了。」

「牙齿！」约翰·昆西重复道。

「是啊。跟这世上的许多东西一样，那是假牙。他在第二座浮台外同一个翻滚的巨浪搏斗了一场，牙就没了踪影。打那以后，他把全部时间都泡在那儿：白天眯着眼往水里张望，夜里潜下去四处摸索。史上悲剧人物之一。」她补了一句。

约翰·昆西大笑起来。

===Original===
“That’s the most tragic part of it,” the girl continued. “He’s the joke of the beach. But he goes on hunting, so serious. Of course, it is serious for him.”

They passed through the public room to the front door. Mr. Saladine’s tragedy slipped at once from John Quincy’s mind.

“Good night,” he said. “Don’t forget about the check, when you see your father tomorrow. I’ll look in on you during the day.”

“It was so good of you to come,” she said. Her hand was in his. “It has helped me along—tremendously.”

===Chinese===
「最惨的还在这一节呢，」姑娘接着说，「他成了全海滩的笑柄。可他照找不误，一副郑重其事的架势。当然啦，对他本人来说，这确是正经大事。」

两人穿过公共休息室，来到前门。萨拉迪尼先生的悲剧，立刻从约翰·昆西的脑海里溜了出去。

「晚安，」他说，「明天见着你父亲，别忘了那张支票。白天我会再来看你。」

「你能来，真是太好了。」她说。她的手正握在他手中。「这帮我撑住了——撑了不知多少。」

===Original===
“Don’t you worry. Happy days are not far off. Happy days without an if. Hold the thought!”

“I’ll hold it,” she promised.

“We’ll both hold it.” It came to him that he was also holding her hand. He dropped it hastily. “Good night,” he repeated, and fled through the garden.

===Chinese===
「别担心。好日子不远了。没有『要是』的好日子。守住这个念头！」

「我守住它。」她允诺道。

「咱们俩都守住。」他忽然意识到，自己也在握着她的手。他慌忙撒开。「晚安。」他又说了一遍，逃也似的穿过花园去了。

===Original===
In the living-room of Dan’s house he was surprised to find Miss Minerva and Charlie Chan sitting together, solemnly staring at each other. Chan rose hurriedly at his entrance.

“Hello,” said John Quincy. “I see you have a caller.”

“Where in the world have you been?” snapped Miss Minerva. Evidently entertaining a Chinaman had got a bit on her nerves.

===Chinese===
回到丹宅的起居室，他惊讶地发现米纳瓦小姐和陈查理正坐在一起，两人一本正经地相视无言。陈查理见他进来，慌忙起身。

「你好啊，」约翰·昆西说，「我瞧着家里来了位客人。」

「你究竟是上哪儿去了？」米纳瓦小姐厉声问道。显然，招待一位华人客人，让她有几分心浮气躁。

===Original===
“Well—I—I—” John Quincy hesitated.

“Speak out,” said Miss Minerva. “Mr. Chan knows everything.”

“Most flattering,” grinned Chan. “Some things are not entirely well known to me. But about your call on Widow of Waikiki I learn soon after door receives you.”

“The devil you did,” said John Quincy.

===Chinese===
「呃——我——我——」约翰·昆西支吾起来。

「有话直说。」米纳瓦小姐说，「陈先生可是什么都知道了。」

「过誉之至。」陈咧嘴一笑，「有些事情，在下尚未能尽数通晓。但关于阁下对怀基基寡妇的造访，房门接待阁下之后不久，在下便已获悉。」

「这可真是活见鬼了。」约翰·昆西说。

===Original===
“Simple enough,” Chan went on. “Study human people, as I relate to you. Compton lady was friend to Mr. Dan Winterslip. Mr. Leatherbee rival friend. Enter jealous feelings. Since morning both of these people are under watchful regard of Honolulu police. Into the scene, you walk. I am notified and fly to beach.”

“Ah—does he also know—” began John Quincy.

“About the brooch?” finished Miss Minerva. “Yes—I’ve confessed everything. And he’s been kind enough to forgive me.”

“But not nice thing to do,” added Chan. “Humbly begging pardon to mention it. All cards should repose on table when police are called upon.”

===Chinese===
「简单之至。」陈接下去说，「研究人类之人，正如在下向你讲述过的那样。康普顿女士，乃是丹·温特斯利普先生之友人。莱瑟比先生，则是竞争对手之友人。嫉妒之情，随之登场。自清晨起，此二人即处于檀香山警察警惕的注视之下。而阁下，信步走入场景之中。在下接获通报，即刻飞赴海滩。」

「啊——那他是不是也知道——」约翰·昆西开口道。

「胸针的事？」米纳瓦小姐替他说完，「是的——我一古脑儿全招了。他也好心肠，原谅了我。」

「然而此举并非美事。」陈补充道，「斗胆提及，伏乞恕罪。警察既蒙延请，所有纸牌均应安卧于桌面之上。」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Miss Minerva, “he forgave me, but I have been gently chided. I have been made to feel, as he puts it, most naughty.”

“So sorry,” bowed Chan.

“Well, as a matter of fact,” said John Quincy, “I was going to tell Mr. Chan the whole story at once.” He turned to the Chinaman. “I’ve already tried to reach you by telephone at the station. When I left the woman’s cottage—”

“Police affairs forbid utmost courtesy,” interrupted Chan. “I cut in to remark from the beginning, if you will please do so.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，」米纳瓦小姐说，「他原谅了我，可我挨了一顿温存的数落。他弄得我自觉——按他的说法——淘气到了十成十。」

「甚为抱歉。」陈躬身一礼。

「呃，其实呢，」约翰·昆西说，「我本来就打算立刻把事情原原本本奉告陈先生的。」他转向那华人。「我先前已往警局打电话找过你。我离开那女人的小屋时——」

「警务在身，恕难全礼。」陈打断道，「在下插话，是想有劳阁下从头讲起，若蒙俯允。」

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” smiled John Quincy. “Well, the woman herself let me in, and showed me into her little living-room. When I got there this fellow Leatherbee was mixing cocktails by the table—”

Haku appeared at the door. “Mr. Charlie Chan wanted by telephone,” he announced.

Chan apologized and hastened out.

“I intend to tell everything,” John Quincy warned his aunt.

===Chinese===
「哦，好的。」约翰·昆西微笑道，「是这样，那女人亲自放我进门，领我进了她那间小起居室。我到的时候，莱瑟比这家伙正伏在桌边调鸡尾酒——」

哈库出现在门口。「陈查理先生有电话找。」他通报道。

陈致了歉，匆匆出去了。

「我可是打算一五一十全说出去的。」约翰·昆西向姑婆先打了招呼。

===Original===
“I shan’t interfere,” she answered. “That slant-eyed Chinaman has been sitting here looking at me more in sorrow than in anger for the better part of an hour, and I’ve made up my mind to one thing. I shall have no more secrets from the police.”

Chan reentered the room. “As I was saying,” John Quincy began, “this fellow Leatherbee was standing by the table, and—”

“Most sorry,” said Chan, “but the remainder of that interesting recital is to be told at the station-house.”

“At the station-house!” cried John Quincy.

“Precisely the fact. I am presuming you do me the great honor to come with me to that spot. The man Leatherbee is apprehended aboard boat *Niagara* on verge of sailing to Australia. Woman are also apprehended in act of tearful farewell. Both now relax at police station.”

“I thought so,” said John Quincy.

“One more amazing fact comes into light,” added Chan. “In pocket of Leatherbee are the page ruthlessly extracted from guest book. Kindly procure your hat. Outside I have waiting for me one Ford automobile.”

===Chinese===
「我不拦你，」她答道，「那个斜眼中国佬在这儿坐了大半个钟头，望着我，那眼神哀矜多于恼怒。我也已拿定了一个主意：往后对警察，我再不藏任何秘密。」

陈查理回到屋里。「我刚才说到，」约翰·昆西重新开口，「莱瑟比这家伙站在桌边，还有——」

「万分抱歉，」陈说，「不过那段有趣陈述的余下部分，须移驾警察局方能聆听。」

「移驾警察局！」约翰·昆西叫道。

「正是此一事实。在下揣想，阁下定肯赏光，偕同在下前往该地。莱瑟比其人，已在行将启航驶往澳大利亚的尼亚加拉号（*Niagara*）船上被拘获。女人亦于挥泪话别之当场被拘获。二人此刻正在警察局中安闲休憩。」

「我猜着就是。」约翰·昆西说。

「另有一桩惊人事实，业已显露于世。」陈补充道，「莱瑟比的口袋之中，有从留名簿上残忍撕下的一页。劳驾取来阁下的帽子。外间有一辆福特汽车，正为在下守候。」

## XII Tom Brade the Blackbirder / 十二 贩奴船员汤姆·布雷德

===Original===
In Hallet’s room at headquarters they found the Captain of Detectives seated grimly behind his desk staring at two reluctant visitors. One of the visitors, Mr. Stephen Leatherbee, stared back with a look of sullen defiance. Mrs. Arlene Compton, late of Broadway and the Automat, was dabbing at her eyes with a tiny handkerchief. John Quincy perceived that she had carelessly allowed tears to play havoc with her makeup.
===Chinese===
在警察总部的哈利特房间里，他们看见刑警队长板着面孔坐在办公桌后，盯着两个一百个不情愿的来访者。其中一位，史蒂芬·莱瑟比先生（Stephen Leatherbee），回瞪过去，神情阴郁而不服。另一位，阿琳·康普顿夫人——昔日混迹于百老汇和自助食堂的人物——正用一方小巧的手帕轻拭眼角。约翰·昆西看出，她漫不经心，任由泪水把脸上的脂粉冲得一片狼藉。

===Original===
“Hello, Charlie,” said Hallet. “Mr. Winterslip, I’m glad you came along. As you may have heard, we’ve just pulled this young man off the *Niagara*. He seemed inclined to leave us. We found this in his pocket.”

He put into the Chinaman’s hand a time-yellowed page obviously from Dan Winterslip’s guest book. John Quincy and Chan bent over it together. The inscription was written in an old-fashioned hand, and the ink was fading fast. It ran:

“In Hawaii all things are perfect, none more so than the hospitality I have enjoyed in this house.﻿—Joseph E. Gleason, 124 Little Bourke Street, Melbourne, Victoria.”
===Chinese===
「你好啊，陈查理。」哈利特说。「温特斯利普先生，很高兴你也来了。你大概听说了，我们刚把这位年轻人从尼亚加拉号（*Niagara*）上揪了下来。他似乎有心要撇下我们远走高飞。我们在他口袋里搜出了这个。」

他把一页年代久远、泛黄的纸放进那中国人手里，那显然是从丹·温特斯利普的留名簿上撕下来的。约翰·昆西和陈查理一齐俯身细看。题词是一手老派字迹，墨水正迅速褪色。上面写着：

「在夏威夷，万事皆臻完美，而尤甚者，莫过于我在这宅中享有的殷勤款待。——约瑟夫·E·格利森（Joseph E. Gleason），维多利亚州墨尔本小伯克街 124 号」

===Original===
John Quincy turned away, shocked. No wonder that page had been ripped out! Evidently Mr. Gleason had not enjoyed the privilege of studying A. S. Hill’s book on the principles of rhetoric. How could one thing be more perfect than another?

“Before I take a statement from these people,” Hallet was saying, “what’s all this about a brooch?”
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西转过身去，心头震骇。难怪那一页被撕掉了！显然，格利森先生未曾有幸研读 A. S. 希尔那部讲修辞原则的著作。一物怎能比另一物「更完美」呢？

「在我给这几位录口供之前，」哈利特正说着，「胸针到底是怎么回事？」

===Original===
John Quincy laid the piece of jewelry on the captain’s desk. He explained that it had been given Mrs. Compton by Dan Winterslip, and told of its being discovered on the floor of the lanai.

“When was it found?” demanded the captain, glaring his disapproval.
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西把那件首饰放到警长的办公桌上。他解释说，这是丹·温特斯利普送给康普顿夫人的，又讲了它如何在露台地板上被发现的经过。

「什么时候发现的？」警长厉声质问，怒目之中满是不以为然。

===Original===
“Most regrettable misunderstanding,” put in Chan hastily. “Now completely wiped out. The littlest said, sooner repairs are made. Mr. Winterslip has already tonight examined this woman﻿—”

“Oh, he has, has he!” Hallet turned angrily on John Quincy. “Just who is conducting this case?”
===Chinese===
「莫大的遗憾，一场误会，」陈查理急忙插言，「现已全然冰释。小小格言有云：补救之工，愈早愈善。温特斯利普先生今夜已经盘问过这位女士——」

「哦，是吗，他倒盘问上了！」哈利特愤然转向约翰·昆西。「这案子到底是谁在办？」

===Original===
“Well,” began John Quincy uncomfortably, “it seemed best to the family﻿—”

“Damn the family!” Hallet exploded. “This affair is in my hands﻿—”

“Please,” broke in Chan soothingly. “Waste of time to winnow that out. Already I have boldness to offer suitable rebukes.”
===Chinese===
「这个，」约翰·昆西局促地开口，「家里人觉得这样最好——」

「家里人见鬼去吧！」哈利特勃然大怒。「这桩案子由我掌管——」

「息怒，」陈查理打着圆场插进来。「费时去扬糠秕厘清此事，大可不必。我已斗胆对他施以恰当的训责。」

===Original===
“Well, you talked with the woman, then,” said Hallet. “What did you get out of her?”

“Say, listen,” put in Mrs. Compton. “I want to take back anything I told this bright-eyed boy.”

“Lied to him, eh?” said Hallet.
===Chinese===
「这么说，你跟那女人谈过了，」哈利特说。「从她嘴里问出什么了？」

「哎，听着，」康普顿夫人插嘴道。「我对这位眼睛发亮的小伙子说过的话，一律要求收回。」

「对他撒了谎，嗯？」哈利特说。

===Original===
“Why not? What right did he have to question me?” Her voice became wheedling. “I wouldn’t lie to a cop,” she added.

“You bet your life you wouldn’t,” Hallet remarked. “Not if you know what’s good for you. However, I want to hear what you told this amateur detective. Sometimes lies are significant. Go on, Winterslip.”
===Chinese===
「干吗不？他有什么权利盘问我？」她的声音变得撒娇耍赖起来。「对一个警察，我可是不会撒谎的。」她补了一句。

「你最好把命押上别撒谎，」哈利特说。「要是你知道什么对你有好处，就绝不敢撒。不过，我倒要听听你跟这位业余侦探都说了些什么。谎言有时候意味深长。说吧，温特斯利普。」

===Original===
John Quincy was deeply annoyed. What was this mix-up he had let himself in for, anyhow? He had a notion to rise, and with a cold bow, leave the room. Something told him, however, that he couldn’t get away with it.

Very much on his dignity, he repeated the woman’s story to him. Winterslip had come to her cottage the night before to make a final appeal for the brooch. On his promise to replace it with something else, she had given it up. He had taken it and left her at nine-thirty.
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西恼火极了。他到底是给自己揽下了怎样一摊子乱事？他真想站起身来，冷冷一躬，扬长而去。然而冥冥中有个声音告诉他，这一手行不通。

他端足架子，把那女人的说法向警长复述了一遍。头天夜里，温特斯利普到她的小屋去，为那枚胸针作最后一次恳求。他许诺另换一件给她，她便把胸针交了出来。他收下胸针，九点半离开了她。

===Original===
“That was the last she saw of him,” finished John Quincy.

Hallet smiled grimly. “So she told you, at any rate. But she admits she was lying. If you’d had the sense to leave this sort of thing to the proper people﻿—” He wheeled on the woman. “You were lying, weren’t you?”
===Chinese===
「那是她最后一回见到他。」约翰·昆西说完。

哈利特冷冷一笑。「至少她是这么跟你说的。可她自己承认撒了谎。你要是有点脑子，把这种事留给专司其职的人——」他猛地转向那女人。「你是在撒谎，对不对？」

===Original===
She nodded nonchalantly. “In a way. Dan did leave my cottage at nine-thirty﻿—or a little later. But I went with him﻿—to his house. Oh, it was perfectly proper. Steve went along.”

“Oh, yes﻿—Steve.” Hallet glanced at Mr. Leatherbee, who did not appear quite the ideal chaperon. “Now, young woman, go back to the beginning. Nothing but the truth.”
===Chinese===
她满不在乎地点点头。「算是吧。丹的确是九点半离开我的小屋——或者稍晚一点。可我跟他一道走的——去了他家。哦，完全是正大光明的。史蒂夫也一起去了。」

「哦，是吗——史蒂夫。」哈利特瞥了莱瑟比先生一眼，此人实在算不上理想的监护人。「好了，姑娘，从头说起。句句实话，别无其他。」

===Original===
“So help me,” said Mrs. Compton. She attempted a devastating smile. “I wouldn’t lie to you, Captain﻿—you know I wouldn’t. I realize you’re a big man out here, and﻿—”

“Give me your story,” cut in Hallet coldly.
===Chinese===
「苍天在上。」康普顿夫人说。她努力做出一个倾倒众生的微笑。「我不会对你撒谎的，警长——你知道我不会。我明白你在这地方是个大人物，而且——」

「说你的事。」哈利特冷冷打断。

===Original===
“Sure. Dan dropped into my place for a chat last night about nine, and he found Mr. Leatherbee there. He was jealous as sin, Dan was﻿—honest to God, I don’t know why. Me and Steve are just pals﻿—eh, Steve?”

“Pals, that’s all,” said Steve.
===Chinese===
「行。昨晚九点来钟，丹顺路到我那儿闲聊，正撞上莱瑟比先生在场。他醋劲大得吓人，丹就是那样——老天爷作证，我也不知道为什么。我跟史蒂夫不过是普通朋友——呃，史蒂夫？」

「朋友，仅此而已。」史蒂夫说。

===Original===
“But anyhow, Dan flew off the handle, and we had one grand blowup. I tried to explain Steve was just stopping over on his way to Australia, and Dan wants to know what’s detaining him. So Steve tells about how he lost all his money at bridge on the boat coming out here. ‘Will you move on,’ says Dan, ‘if I pay your passage?’ And Steve answers he will, like a shot. Am I getting this straight, Steve?”
===Chinese===
「可不管怎么说，丹大发雷霆，我们大吵了一场。我解释说史蒂夫不过是去澳大利亚途中在此地稍作停留，丹偏要知道是什么把他绊住了。于是史蒂夫讲起他坐船来这儿的路上打桥牌把钱输了个精光。『要是我替你出路费，』丹说，『你肯不肯挪窝？』史蒂夫回答说他肯，立马就走。我讲得没错吧，史蒂夫？」

===Original===
“Absolutely,” approved Mr. Leatherbee. “It’s just as she says, Captain. Winterslip offered to give﻿—loan me passage money. It was only a loan. And I agreed to sail on the *Niagara* tonight. He said he had a little cash in his safe at the house, and invited Arlene and me to go back with him﻿—”
===Chinese===
「千真万确。」莱瑟比先生表示认可。「警长，事情就跟她说的一模一样。温特斯利普提出给——借给我路费。只是借用而已。我便应承今晚搭尼亚加拉号走。他说他家的保险柜里有点现钱，就邀请阿琳和我跟他一同回去——」

===Original===
“Which we did,” said Arlene. “Dan opened the safe and took out a roll of bills. He peeled off three hundred dollars. You didn’t often see him in that frame of mind﻿—but as I was saying, he give the money to Steve. Then Steve begins to beef a little﻿—yes, you did, Steve﻿—and wants to know what he’s going to do in Australia. Says he don’t know a soul down there and he’ll just plain starve. Dan was sore at first, then he laughs a nasty little laugh and goes over and tears that there page out of the guest book and gives it to Steve. ‘Look him up and tell him you’re a friend of mine,’ he says. ‘Maybe he’ll give you a job. The name is Gleason. I’ve disliked him for twenty years, though he don’t know that!’ ”
===Chinese===
「我们就这样去了。」阿琳说。「丹打开保险柜，取出一沓钞票，数出三百块。他那种心境可不常见——不过我刚说了，他把钱给了史蒂夫。接着史蒂夫开始嘟嘟囔囔发点小牢骚——是啊，你就是嘟囔了，史蒂夫——想知道自己去澳大利亚能干什么。他说他在那边一个熟人也没有，简直只有饿死的份儿。丹起先还冒火，后来发出一声阴恻恻的轻笑，走过去把留名簿上那一页撕下来，递给史蒂夫。『去找他，就说你是我的朋友，』他说，『说不定他会给你份差事。那人姓格利森。我看他不顺眼已经二十年了，他自己可不知道！』」

===Original===
“A dirty dig at me,” Leatherbee explained. “I took the loan and this Gleason’s address and we started to go. Winterslip said he wanted to talk to Arlene, so I came away alone. That was about ten o’clock.”
===Chinese===
「这是拿我开涮的一记阴招。」莱瑟比解释道。「我收下那笔借款和这位格利森的地址，我们便动身要走。温特斯利普说想跟阿琳谈谈，我就独自先走了。那是在十点钟左右。」

===Original===
“Where did you go?” Hallet asked.

“I went back to my hotel downtown. I had to pack.”

“Back to your hotel, eh? Can you prove it?”
===Chinese===
「你去哪儿了？」哈利特问。

「回市中心我住的旅馆了。总得收拾行李。」

「回旅馆了，嗯？你能证明吗？」

===Original===
Leatherbee considered. “I don’t know. The boy at the desk may remember when I came in, though I didn’t stop there for my key﻿—I had it with me. Anyhow, I didn’t see Winterslip after that. I just went ahead with my preparations to sail on the *Niagara*, and I must say you’ve got your nerve﻿—”

“Never mind that!” Hallet turned to the woman. “And after Leatherbee left﻿—what happened then?”
===Chinese===
莱瑟比想了想。「不知道。柜台上那个伙计兴许记得我什么时候进的门，不过我没在柜台停下领钥匙——钥匙就在我身上。反正，打那以后我再没见过温特斯利普。我只管接着收拾，准备搭尼亚加拉号动身，而且我不得不说，你们的脸皮也真够厚的——」

「那个不用你说！」哈利特转向那女人。「莱瑟比走了之后——接着又怎么样？」

===Original===
“Well, Dan started in on that brooch again,” she said. “It made me sore, too﻿—I never did like a tightwad. Besides, my nerves was all on edge, I’m funny that way, rows get me all upset. I like everybody pleasant around me. He went on arguing, so finally I ripped off the brooch and threw it at him, and it rolled away under the table somewhere. Then he said he was sorry, and that was when he offered to replace it with something more up-to-date. The best money could buy﻿—that was what he promised. Pretty soon we was friends again﻿—just as good friends as ever when I came away, about ten-fifteen. His last words was that we’d look round the jewelry stores this morning. I ask you, Captain, is it reasonable to think I’d have anything to do with murdering a man who was in a buying mood like that?”
===Chinese===
「唉，丹又提起那枚胸针。」她说。「这也叫我上火——我一向看不上小气鬼。再说，我的神经本来就绷得紧紧的，我这人就这样，一吵架就心烦意乱。我喜欢身边人人客客气气的。他一个劲儿地争，最后我一把扯下胸针朝他扔过去，胸针骨碌碌滚到桌子底下什么角落去了。跟着他就赔了不是，还就是那时候，他许诺给我换件更时新的。钱能买到的最好的货色——他是这么许诺的。没多久我们又和好了——十点一刻左右我走的时候，交情跟往常一样好。他最后的话是今早我们一道去逛珠宝店。警长，我问你，一个人正起劲要掏钱买东西，你们倒觉得我跟谋害这么个人能有什么牵连？这说得通吗？」

===Original===
Hallet laughed. “So you left him at ten-fifteen﻿—and went home alone?”

“I did. And when I saw him last he was alive and well﻿—I’ll swear to that on a stack of Bibles as high as the Times Building. Gee, don’t I wish I was safe on Broadway tonight!”
===Chinese===
哈利特笑了。「这么说，十点一刻你离开了他——然后独自回家？」

「正是。我最后一回见到他时，他活得好好的——这话我可以对着一摞跟《时报》大厦一样高的《圣经》起誓。天哪，我多希望这会儿已经安安稳稳回到百老汇！」

===Original===
Hallet thought for a moment. “Well, we’ll look into all this. You can both go﻿—I’m not going to hold you at present. But I expect you both to remain in Honolulu until this affair is cleared up, and I advise you not to try any funny business. You’ve seen tonight what chance you’ve got to get away.”
===Chinese===
哈利特沉吟片刻。「好吧，这些我们都会查证。你们两个可以走了——眼下我还不打算扣押你们。但这案子水落石出之前，你们两个都给我留在檀香山，我劝你们别耍什么花招。今晚你们也亲眼看见了，想跑，机会有多大。」

===Original===
“Oh, that’s all right.” The woman stood, looking her relief. “We’ve got no reason to beat it, have we, Steve?”

“None in the world,” agreed Steve. His facetious manner returned. “Speaking for myself,” he added, “innocent is my middle name.”

“Good night, all,” said Mrs. Compton, and they went out.
===Chinese===
「哦，那没问题。」女人站起身，一脸如释重负。「我们没理由跑路，对吧，史蒂夫？」

「天底下一点理由都没有。」史蒂夫附和道。他那副嬉皮笑脸的做派又回来了。「替我自己说一句，」他补充道，「清白就是我的中名。」

「各位晚安。」康普顿夫人说，两人便出门去了。

===Original===
Hallet sat staring at the brooch. “A pretty straight story,” he remarked, looking at Chan.

“Nice and neat,” grinned the Chinaman.

“If true.” Hallet shrugged his shoulders. “Well, for the present, I’m willing to believe it.” He turned to John Quincy. “Now, Mr. Winterslip,” he said severely, “I want it understood that any other evidence your family digs up﻿—”
===Chinese===
哈利特坐在那儿，盯着那枚胸针。「一套挺干净利落的说法。」他望着陈查理说道。

「又漂亮又齐整。」那中国人咧嘴一笑。

「但愿是真的。」哈利特耸耸肩。「好吧，眼下我愿意相信它。」他转向约翰·昆西。「好了，温特斯利普先生，」他语气严厉，「我要你明白，你们家再挖出什么证据来——」

===Original===
“Oh, that’s all right,” interrupted the boy. “We’ll turn it over at once. I’ve already given to Chan the newspaper my cousin was reading that night he wrote the letter to Roger Winterslip.”

Chan took the paper from his pocket. “Such a busy evening,” he explained, “the journal was obscure in my mind. Thanks for the recollection.” He called to his chief’s attention the mutilated corner.

“Look into that,” said Hallet.
===Chinese===
「哦，没问题。」小伙子打断道。「我们立刻移交。我已经把我那位堂亲当晚给罗杰·温特斯利普写信时所读的那份报纸，交给陈查理了。」

陈查理从口袋里取出那份报纸。「这样一个繁忙的夜晚，」他解释道，「此报在我头脑中已然湮没不明。多谢您重新唤起。」他请上司注意那个残缺的边角。

「去查一查这个。」哈利特说。

===Original===
“Before sleeping,” promised Chan. “Mr. Winterslip, we pursue similar paths. The honor of your company in my humble vehicle would pleasure me deeply.” Once in the car on the deserted street, the Chinaman spoke again. “The page ripped from guest book, the brooch lying silent on floor. Both are now followed into presence of immovable stone wall. We sway about, looking for other path.”
===Chinese===
「就寝之前必办。」陈查理允诺道。「温特斯利普先生，我们二人所行乃同一条路。您若肯屈尊乘坐鄙人寒陋的车辆同行，将令我深感欣悦。」上了车，行在空寂的街上，那中国人又开口了。「留名簿上撕去的一页，静卧于地板的胸针。这两条线索如今皆被追至一堵岿然不动的高墙之前。我们左右逡巡，另觅他路。」

===Original===
“Then you think those two were telling the truth?” John Quincy asked.

“As to that, I do not venture to remark,” Chan replied.

“How about those psychic powers?” inquired John Quincy.

Chan smiled. “Psychic powers somewhat drowsy just now,” he admitted. “Need prodding into wakefulness.”
===Chinese===
「这么说，你认为那两个说的是实话？」约翰·昆西问。

「关于此节，我不敢妄言。」陈查理答道。

「那您那通灵之能怎么说？」约翰·昆西又问。

陈查理微微一笑。「通灵之能此刻颇感困倦。」他承认道。「需加捅弄，方可醒转。」

===Original===
“Look here,” said John Quincy, “there’s no need for you to take me out to Waikiki. Just drop me on King Street, and I’ll get a trolley.”

“Making humble suggestion,” Chan replied, “is it not possible you will accompany me to newspaper rooms, where we set out on different path?”

John Quincy looked at his watch; it was ten minutes past eleven. “I’ll be glad to, Charlie,” he said.
===Chinese===
「听我说，」约翰·昆西道，「你不必把我送到怀基基去。让我在国王街下车就行，我自己搭电车。」

「容我卑微进一言，」陈查理答道，「您可否陪我同往报馆一行？在那里，我们将踏上另一条路径启程。」

约翰·昆西看看表，十一点十分。「我很乐意去，查理。」他说。

===Original===
Chan beamed with pleasure. “Greatly honored by your friendly manner,” he remarked. He turned into a side street. “Newspaper of this nature burst out at evening, very quiet now. Somebody may loiter in rooms, if we have happy luck.”

They had just that, for the building of the evening journal was open, and in the city room an elderly man with a green shade over his eyes hammered on a typewriter.

“Hello, Charlie,” he said cordially.
===Chinese===
陈查理喜形于色。「您友好之态度，令我深感荣宠。」他说。他拐入一条小街。「此类报纸，傍晚喧腾印出，此刻已归于沉静。若有幸缘，或有人流连于房中。」

他们恰恰交上了这份好运——晚报的办公楼门户洞开，编辑室里，一位戴绿色遮光眼罩的老人正在打字机上噼啪敲击。

「你好啊，查理。」他热络地说。

===Original===
“Hello, Pete. Mr. Winterslip of Boston, I have all the honor to present this Pete Mayberry. For many years he explore waterfront ferreting for whatever news are hiding there.”

The elderly man rose and removed his eyeshade, revealing a pleasant twinkle. He was evidently interested to meet a Winterslip.
===Chinese===
「你好，皮特。波士顿来的温特斯利普先生，请容我怀全部荣幸，向您引荐这位皮特·梅伯里（Pete Mayberry）。多年以来，他在滨水区四处探查，嗅出藏匿于彼处的种种新闻。」

老人站起身，摘下眼罩，露出一双讨人喜欢的炯炯眸子。能见到一位温特斯利普家的人，他显然饶有兴致。

===Original===
“We pursue,” continued Chan, “one copy of paper marked June sixteen, present year. If you have no inclination for objecting.”

Mayberry laughed. “Go to it, Charlie. You know where the files are.”
===Chinese===
「我们追寻一份标着今年六月十六日的报纸。」陈查理接着说。「倘若您并无反对之意。」

梅伯里笑了。「尽管找去，查理。合订档在哪儿你是熟的。」

===Original===
Chan bowed and disappeared. “Your first appearance out here, Mr. Winterslip?” inquired the newspaper man.

John Quincy nodded. “I’ve only just got here,” he said, “but I can see it’s a rather intriguing place.”
===Chinese===
陈查理一躬身，便不见了人影。「您头一回到这地方来，温特斯利普先生？」报人问道。

约翰·昆西点点头。「我才刚到，」他说，「不过我看得出来，这是个相当引人入胜的地方。」

===Original===
“You’ve said it,” smiled Mayberry. “Forty-six years ago I came out from Portsmouth, New Hampshire, to visit relatives. I’ve been in the newspaper game here ever since﻿—most of the time on the waterfront. There’s a lifework for you!”

“You must have seen some changes,” remarked John Quincy inanely.
===Chinese===
「这话可叫你说着了。」梅伯里含笑说。「四十六年前，我从新罕布什尔州的朴次茅斯出来，走亲戚。打那以后，我就一直在这儿干报馆这一行——大半时间泡在滨水区。这可是够干一辈子的营生！」

「那您一定见了不少变化。」约翰·昆西没话找话地说。

===Original===
Mayberry nodded. “For the worse. I knew Honolulu in the glamourous days of its isolation, and I’ve watched it fade into an eighth carbon copy of Babbittville, U.S.A. The waterfront’s just a waterfront now﻿—but once, my boy! Once it oozed romance at every pore.”
===Chinese===
梅伯里点点头。「越变越糟了。我认识的是与世隔绝、光华璀璨年月里的檀香山，后来眼睁睁看着它褪色成美国巴比特维尔的第八张复写纸。如今滨水区不过是片寻常滨水区——可是想当年，孩子！想当年，它浑身上下每个毛孔都往外渗着罗曼史。」

===Original===
