Chan returned, carrying a paper. “Much to be thankful for,” he said to Mayberry. “Your kindness are quite overwhelming﻿—”

“Anything doing?” asked Mayberry eagerly.

Chan shook his head. “Presently speaking, no. Our motions just now must be blackly clouded in secrecy.”
===Chinese===
陈查理拿着一份报纸回来了。「可感之事甚多。」他对梅伯里说。「您的好意实在浩荡——」

「有动静？」梅伯里急切地问。

陈查理摇摇头。「就眼下而言，没有。我们的行动此刻必须裹在漆黑的秘密之中。」

===Original===
“Well,” said the reporter, “when it comes time to roll them clouds away, don’t forget me.”

“Impossibility,” protested Chan. “Good night.”

They left Mayberry bending over his typewriter, and at Chan’s suggestion went to the All American Restaurant, where the Chinaman ordered two cups of “your inspeakable coffee.” While they waited to be served, he spread out on the table his complete copy of the newspaper, and laying the torn page on its counterpart, carefully removed the upper right-hand corner.
===Chinese===
「好吧，」报人说，「等到了该把那些云团拨开的时候，可别忘了我。」

「绝无可能。」陈查理正色道。「晚安。」

他们丢下俯身打字的梅伯里走了。依陈查理的主意，两人进了全美餐馆，那中国人要了两杯「贵店那难以言说的咖啡」。等餐的当口，他把那份完整无缺的报纸摊在桌上，将撕下的那页覆在它原本的位置上，小心裁下右上角。

===Original===
“The missing fragment,” he explained. For a time he studied it thoughtfully, and finally shook his head. “I apprehend nothing to startle,” he admitted. He handed it across the table. “If you will condescend greatly﻿—”

John Quincy took the bit of newspaper. On one side was the advertisement of a Japanese dealer in shirtings who wrote his own publicity. Anyone might carry off, he said, six yards for the price of five. And in case the buyer cried loudly in amaze, how can do, it was a matter he was happy to explain. John Quincy laughed aloud.
===Chinese===
「缺失的那一角。」他解释道。他对着它沉思良久，末了摇了摇头。「我未见任何骇人之处。」他承认道。他把它隔着桌子递过去。「倘若您肯大加屈尊——」

约翰·昆西接过那片报纸。一面是个日本布商的广告，广告词是他亲自撰写的。他说，任何人都可以花五码的价钱拿走六码。倘有买主惊得大声嚷嚷，怎么会这样（how can do），这个嘛，他很乐意解释。约翰·昆西放声大笑。

===Original===
“Ah,” said Chan, “you are by rights mirthful. Kiku-chi, purveyor of shirting cloth, seize on grand English language and make it into idiotic jumble. On that side are nothing to detain us. But humbly hinting you reverse the fragment﻿—”

John Quincy reversed it. The other side was a part of the shipping page. He read it carefully, news of sailings and arrivals, there would be places for five passengers to the Orient on the *Shinyo Maru*, leaving Wednesday, the *Wilhelmina* was six hundred and forty miles east of Makupuu Point, the brig *Mary Jane* from the Treaty Ports﻿—

John Quincy started, and caught his breath. A small item in tiny print had met his eye.
===Chinese===
「啊，」陈查理说，「您发笑是有道理的。菊池（Kiku-chi），衬衫布料的供应商，将堂堂英语一把攥住，揉成一团痴愚的乱麻。这一面没有什么值得我们逗留。但请容我卑微地提点一句——您把碎片翻个面——」

约翰·昆西把它翻了过来。另一面是航运版的一部分。他仔细读着——船期与到港的消息：星期三启航开往东方的真洋丸（*Shinyo Maru*）上尚余五个客位；威廉明娜号（*Wilhelmina*）位于马库普乌角以东六百四十英里；来自条约口岸的双桅船玛丽·简号（*Mary Jane*）——

约翰·昆西猛然一惊，屏住了呼吸。一行小字排印的简短消息跃入了他的眼帘。

===Original===
> “Among the passengers who will arrive here on the *Sonoma* from Australia a week from Saturday are: Mr. and Mrs. Thomas Macan Brade, of Calcutta﻿—”
===Chinese===
> 「乘坐索诺玛号（*Sonoma*）自澳大利亚前来、将于下周六抵港的旅客中有：托马斯·马坎·布雷德先生及夫人，来自加尔各答——」

===Original===
John Quincy sat staring at the unwashed window of the All American Restaurant. His mind went back to the deck of the *President Tyler*, to a lean old missionary telling a tale of a bright morning on Apiang, a grave under a palm tree. “Mr. and Mrs. Thomas Macan Brade, of Calcutta.” He heard again the missionary’s high-pitched voice. “A callous brute, a pirate and adventurer. Tom Brade, the blackbirder.”

But Brade had been buried in a long pine box on Apiang. Even at the Crossroads of the Pacific, his path and that of Dan Winterslip could hardly have crossed again.
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西坐在那里，怔怔望着全美餐馆那扇久未擦洗的窗子。他的思绪回到泰勒总统号的甲板上，回到一位清瘦老传教士讲述的故事里——阿皮昂一个晴朗的清晨，棕榈树下一座坟茔。「托马斯·马坎·布雷德先生及夫人，来自加尔各答。」他又听见了那传教士尖细的嗓音。「一个狠心的畜生，海盗兼冒险家。汤姆·布雷德，贩奴船员。」

可是布雷德早已躺在阿皮昂一口长长的松木箱里入了土。即便在这太平洋的十字路口，他同丹·温特斯利普的路，也不该再有交会的一天了。

===Original===
The waiter brought the coffee. Chan said nothing, watching John Quincy closely. Finally the Chinaman spoke: “You have much to tell me.”

John Quincy looked around quickly, he had forgotten Chan’s presence.
===Chinese===
侍者端来咖啡。陈查理一言不发，紧盯着约翰·昆西。终于，那中国人开口了：「您有许多事要告诉我。」

约翰·昆西慌忙环顾左右，他竟把陈查理就在身边这回事忘在了脑后。

===Original===
His dilemma was acute. Must he here in this soiled restaurant in a far town reveal to a Chinaman that ancient blot on the Winterslip name? What would Aunt Minerva say? Well, only a short time ago she had remarked that she was resolved to have no more secrets from the police. However, there was family pride﻿—

John Quincy’s eye fell on the Japanese waiter. What were those lines from *The Mikado*? “But family pride must be denied and mortified and set aside.”
===Chinese===
他左右为难，好不煎熬。难道要他在这远方城市一间油腻腻的餐馆里，向一个中国人揭开温特斯利普姓氏上那块古老的污渍？米纳瓦姑婆会怎么说？好吧，就在不久之前，她才说过，自己已下定决心，不再对警方隐瞒任何秘密。可是，家族的体面——

约翰·昆西的目光落到那个日本侍者身上。《日本天皇》（*The Mikado*）里那几句唱词是怎么说的来着？「然而家族的骄傲，终究须被否认、被折辱、被搁置一旁。」

===Original===
The boy smiled. “Yes, Charlie,” he admitted, “I have much to tell you.” And over the inspeakable coffee of the All American Restaurant he repeated to the detective the story the Reverend Frank Upton had told on the *President Tyler*.

Chan beamed. “Now,” he cried, “we arrive in the neighborhood of something! Brade the blackbirder, master *Maid of Shiloh* boat, on which Mr. Dan Winterslip are first officer﻿—”
===Chinese===
小伙子笑了。「好吧，查理，」他承认道，「我确实有许多事要告诉你。」于是，就着全美餐馆那难以言说的咖啡，他把弗兰克·厄普顿牧师在泰勒总统号上讲的故事，向这位侦探原原本本复述了一遍。

陈查理满面生辉。「这下，」他扬声道，「我们总算抵达某桩事情的左近了！贩奴船员布雷德，希洛少女号船长，而丹·温特斯利普先生曾任该船大副——」

===Original===
“But Brade was buried on Apiang,” protested John Quincy.

“Yes, indeed. And who saw him, pardon me? Was it then an unsealed box? Oh, no!” Chan’s eyes were dancing. “Please recollect something more. The strong box of ohia wood. Initials on it are T.M.B. Mysteries yet, but we move, we advance!”

“I guess we do,” admitted John Quincy.
===Chinese===
「可布雷德已经葬在阿皮昂了。」约翰·昆西提出异议。

「正是，确实。然而请问，又有谁亲眼见他入土了？恕我多问。那口箱子当时可曾开过封？哦，没有！请再回想一事。那只奥希亚木的结实箱子。箱上首字母是 T.M.B.。谜团犹在，但我们在行动，我们在推进！」

「我们确实在推进。」约翰·昆西承认道。

===Original===
“This much we grasp,” Chan continued. “Dan Winterslip repose for quiet hour on lanai, in peaceful reading. This news assault his eye. He now leaps up, paces about, flees to dock to send letter requesting, please the ohia wood box must be buried deep in Pacific. Why?” Fumbling in his pocket, Chan took out a sheaf of papers, evidently lists of steamer arrivals. “On Saturday just gone by, the *Sonoma* make this port. Among passengers﻿—yes﻿—yes﻿—Thomas Macan Brade and honorable wife, Calcutta. It is here inscribed they arrive to stay, not being present when *Sonoma* persist on journey. On the night of Monday, Mr. Dan Winterslip are foully slain.”
===Chinese===
「以下几端，我们已牢牢把握。」陈查理接着说。「丹·温特斯利普先生安卧于露台之上，静享一小时闲读。这条新闻猝然扑入他的眼帘。他随即跃起，来回踱步，奔往码头发出信函，恳请务必将那奥希亚木盒深葬于太平洋底。这是为何？」陈查理在口袋里摸索一阵，掏出一叠纸，显然是轮船到港旅客名单。「刚过去的星期六，索诺玛号驶抵本港。旅客之中——正是——正是——托马斯·马坎·布雷德及其可敬的夫人，来自加尔各答。名单上载明，他们是到来久居的；索诺玛号继续航程时，二人并未随船在场。星期一夜里，丹·温特斯利普先生惨遭毒手。」

===Original===
“Which makes Mr. Brade an important person to locate,” said John Quincy.

“How very true. But the hurry are not intense. No boats sailing now. Before sleeping, I will investigate downtown hotels, Waikiki tomorrow. Where are you, Mr. Brade?” Chan seized the check. “No﻿—pardon me﻿—the honor of paying for this poison-tasting beverage must be mine.”
===Chinese===
「这么一来，找到布雷德先生便成了要紧大事。」约翰·昆西说。

「千真万确。然事不宜急，急亦无益。眼下并无船只出港。就寝之前，我将查访市中心各旅馆，明日再赴怀基基。布雷德先生，您究竟人在何方？」陈查理一把抓过账单。「不——恕我失礼——为这杯毒药滋味的饮料付账的荣耀，必须归于我。」

===Original===
Out in the street, he indicated an approaching trolley. “It bears imprint of your destination,” he pointed out. “You will require sleep. We meet tomorrow. Congratulations on most fruitful evening.”
===Chinese===
到了街上，他指了指一辆驶来的电车。「车身带有您目的地的印记。」他指出。「您需要睡眠了。我们明日再会。恭喜您度过一个果实丰硕的夜晚。」

===Original===
Once more John Quincy was on a Waikiki car. Weary but thrilled, he took out his pipe and filling it, lighted up. What a day! He seemed to have lived a lifetime since he landed that very morning. He perceived that his smoke was blowing in the face of a tired little Japanese woman beside him. “Pardon me,” he remarked, and knocking the pipe against the side rail, put it in his pocket. The woman stared at him in meek startled wonder; no one had ever asked her pardon before.
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西又坐上了开往怀基基的电车。他疲惫不堪，却又兴奋难抑，取出烟斗，装上烟丝，点着了。多么漫长的一天！从今儿早上登岸那一刻算起，他仿佛已经活过了整整一世。他发觉自己的烟正喷在身旁一位倦容满面的小个子日本妇人脸上。「对不住。」他说着，把烟斗在车帮栏杆上磕了磕，收进口袋。那妇人怯生生、怔忡忡地望着他——从来没有人向她道过歉。

===Original===
On the seat behind John Quincy a group of Hawaiian boys with yellow leis about their necks twanged on steel guitars and sang a plaintive love song. The trolley rattled on through the fragrant night; above the clatter of the wheels the music rose with a sweet intensity. John Quincy leaned back and closed his eyes.

A clock struck the hour of midnight. Another day﻿—Wednesday﻿—it flashed through his mind that today his firm in Boston would offer that preferred stock for the shoe people in Lynn. Would the issue be oversubscribed? No matter.
===Chinese===
约翰·昆西身后的座位上，一群夏威夷少年脖挂黄色花环，拨响钢棒吉他，唱着一支哀婉的情歌。电车哐当哐当，驶过芬芳香馥的夜；乐声越过车轮的喧响，甜浓地升腾而起。约翰·昆西仰身靠下，闭上了眼睛。

时钟敲响了午夜。又是新的一天——星期三了——他脑中倏地闪过，今天他波士顿的商行要为林恩那边的制鞋商发行那批优先股。认购会超额吗？无所谓了。

===Original===
Here he was, out in the middle of the Pacific on a trolley-car. Behind him brown-skinned boys were singing a melancholy love song of long ago, and the moon was shining on crimson poinciana trees. And somewhere on this tiny island a man named Thomas Macan Brade slept under a mosquito netting. Or lay awake, perhaps, thinking of Dan Winterslip.
===Chinese===
他人在这里，在太平洋正中的一辆电车上。身后，棕色皮肤的少年们唱着一支久远年代的忧郁情歌，月光正照在猩红的凤凰木上。而在这座小岛的某个地方，一个名叫托马斯·马坎·布雷德的人正睡在蚊帐底下。也许还醒着，正想着丹·温特斯利普。

## XIII The Luggage in Room Nineteen / 十三 十九号房间的行李

===Original===
John Quincy emerged from sleep the next morning with a great effort, and dragged his watch from under the pillow. Eight-thirty! Good lord, he was due at the office at nine! A quick bath and shave, a brief pause at the breakfast table, a run past the Public Gardens and the Common and down to School Street﻿—

He sat up in bed. Why was he imprisoned under mosquito netting? What was the meaning of the little lizard that sported idly outside the cloth? Oh, yes﻿—Honolulu. He was in Hawaii, and he’d never reach his office by nine. It was five thousand miles away.

The low murmur of breakers on the beach confirmed him in this discovery and stepping to his window, he gazed out at the calm sparkling morning. Yes, he was in Honolulu entangled in a murder mystery, consorting with Chinese detectives and Waikiki Widows, following clues. The new day held interesting promise. He must hurry to find what it would bring forth.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，约翰·昆西费了九牛二虎之力才从睡梦中挣脱出来，从枕头底下拖出怀表。八点半了！老天爷，九点他就该到写字楼了！赶紧洗个澡、刮个脸，在早餐桌上略停一停，一路跑过公共花园和波士顿公园，直下学校街——

他在床上坐起身来。自己怎么会被囚在蚊帐底下？帐外那只闲逛嬉戏的小蜥蜴又作何解？哦，对了——檀香山。他在夏威夷，九点钟是断断到不了写字楼了。那地方远在五千英里之外。

海滩上碎浪的低吟证实了这一发现。他步至窗前，凝望外面平静闪亮的清晨。是的，他在檀香山，缠在一桩凶杀谜案里，同华人侦探和怀基基寡妇们厮混，一路追索线索。新的一天藏着有意思的盼头。他得赶紧去看看，它会生出些什么来。

===Original===
Haku informed him that his aunt and Barbara had already breakfasted, and set before him a reddish sort of cantaloupe which was, he explained in answer to the boy’s question, a papaya. When he had eaten, John Quincy went out on the lanai. Barbara stood there, staring at the beach. A new Barbara, with the old vivacity, the old joy of living, submerged; a pale girl with sorrow in her eyes.

John Quincy put his arm about her shoulder; she was a Winterslip and the family was the family. Again he felt in his heart that flare of anger against the “person or persons unknown” who had brought this grief upon her. The guilty must pay﻿—Egan or whoever, Brade or Leatherbee or the chorus girl. Pay and pay dearly﻿—he was resolved on that.

===Chinese===
哈库告诉他，姑婆和芭芭拉已经用过早餐，随即在他面前端上一只红彤彤、甜瓜模样的小东西——他问起来，哈库解释说，这叫木瓜。吃完之后，约翰·昆西走到露台上去。芭芭拉正站在那儿望着海滩出神。一个面目一新的芭芭拉：往日的那股活泼、那股生之欢愉都沉没了；一个脸色苍白的姑娘，眼里盛着悲伤。

约翰·昆西伸手搂住她的肩头；她是温特斯利普家的人，而家族终究是家族。他心里又一次腾起怒火，冲着那给她们带来这场伤痛的「身份不明之一人或数人」。有罪的必须付出代价——不管是伊根还是别的什么人，是布雷德、莱瑟比，还是那个歌舞队女郎。要付代价，而且要付得惨重——这一点他已拿定主意。

===Original===
“My dear girl,” he began. “What can I say to you﻿—”

“You’ve said it all, without speaking,” she answered. “See, John Quincy, this is my beach. When I was only five I swam alone to that first float. He﻿—he was so proud of me.”

“It’s a lovely spot, Barbara,” he told her.

“I knew you’d think so. One of these days we’ll swim together out to the reef, and I’ll teach you to ride a surfboard. I want your visit to be a happy one.”

He shook his head. “It can’t be that,” he said, “because of you. But because of you, I’m mighty glad I came.”

She pressed his hand. “I’m going out to sit by the water. Will you come?”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的姑娘，」他开口道，「我能对你说些什么呢——」

「你什么都不说，也已经全说了。」她答道。「瞧，约翰·昆西，这是我的海滩。我才五岁的时候，就能一个人游到头一个浮台。他——他可为我骄傲呢。」

「这地方真美，芭芭拉。」他对她说。

「我就知道你会这么想。过些日子咱们一块儿游到礁石那儿去，我教你骑冲浪板。我盼着你这趟做客过得快快活活。」

他摇了摇头。「那是办不到的，」他说，「因为有你在。可也因为有你在，我才万分庆幸自己来了。」

她握了握他的手。「我要到水边去坐坐。你来吗？」

===Original===
The bamboo curtain parted, and Miss Minerva joined them. “Well, John Quincy,” she said sharply, “this is a pretty hour for you to appear. If you’re going to rescue me from lotus land, you’ll have to be immune yourself.”

He smiled. “Just getting acclimated,” he explained. “I’ll follow you in a moment, Barbara,” he added, and held open the door for her.

===Chinese===
竹帘一挑，米纳瓦小姐加入进来。「哎呀，约翰·昆西，」她语气尖利地说，「你挑的这个露面时辰可真够瞧的。你若要把我从这忘忧乡里搭救出去，你自己先得百毒不侵才行。」

他微微一笑。「不过是在服水土。」他解释道。「我随后就来，芭芭拉。」他又添了一句，替她拉住了门。

===Original===
“I waited up,” Miss Minerva began, when the girl had gone, “until eleven-thirty. But I’d had very little sleep the night before, and that was my limit. I make no secret of it﻿—I’m very curious to know what happened at the police station.”

He repeated to her the story told by Mrs. Compton and Leatherbee. “I wish I’d been present,” she said. “A pretty woman can fool all the men in Christendom. Lies, probably.”

“Maybe,” admitted John Quincy. “But wait a minute. Later on, Chan and I followed up your newspaper clue. And it led us to a startling discovery.”

“Of course it did,” she beamed. “What was it?”

===Chinese===
「我熬夜等来着，」姑娘走后，米纳瓦小姐开了腔，「一直等到十一点半。可前一晚我几乎没合眼，那就是我的极限了。我不瞒你——警察局里到底出了什么事，我好奇得很。」

他把康普顿夫人和莱瑟比讲的那套说辞对她复述了一遍。「真希望我当时在场。」她说。「一个漂亮女人，能把全基督教世界的男人都骗过去。多半是谎话。」

「也许吧。」约翰·昆西承认道。「不过且慢。后来我和陈查理顺着你那条报纸线索追了下去。它把我们引向了一个惊人的发现。」

「当然会引向。」她眉开眼笑。「是什么发现？」

===Original===
“Well,” he said, “first of all, I met a missionary on the boat.” He told her the Reverend Frank Upton’s tale of that morning on Apiang, and added the news that a man named Thomas Macan Brade was now in Honolulu.

She was silent for a time. “So Dan was a blackbirder,” she remarked at last. “How charming! Such a pleasant man, too. But then, I learned that lesson early in life﻿—the brighter the smile, the darker the past. All this will make delightful reading in the Boston papers, John Quincy.”

“Oh, they’ll never get it,” her nephew said.

“Don’t deceive yourself. Newspapers will go to the ends of the earth for a good murder. I once wrote letters to all the editors in Boston urging them to print no more details about homicides. It hadn’t the slightest effect﻿—though I did get an acknowledgment of my favor from the *Herald*.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，」他说，「头一件，我在船上结识了一位传教士。」他把弗兰克·厄普顿牧师讲的那个阿皮昂岛清晨的故事说给她听，又添上一条消息：一个名叫托马斯·梅肯·布雷德的人如今就在檀香山。

她沉默了片刻。「这么说，丹竟是个贩奴船员。」她终于开口道。「多么动人啊！还是这么一位讨人喜欢的男人。不过话说回来，这门功课我人生里学得早——笑容越亮，往昔越黑。这一切到了波士顿的报纸上，读起来才叫津津有味呢，约翰·昆西。」

「噢，他们绝对到不了手。」她的侄子说。

「别自己骗自己了。为了一桩像样的凶案，报馆肯追到天涯海角。有一回我给波士顿各家主编统统去了信，力劝他们别再刊登凶案的种种细节。毫厘效果也没有——不过《先驱报》倒是惠赐回函，谢领了我这一番好意。」

===Original===
John Quincy glanced at his watch. “Perhaps I should go down to the station. Anything in the morning paper?”

“A very hazy interview with Captain Hallet. The police have unearthed important clues, and promise early results. You know﻿—the sort of thing they always give out just after a murder.”

The boy looked at her keenly. “Ah,” he said, “then you read newspaper accounts of the kind you tried to suppress?”

“Certainly I do,” snapped his aunt. “There’s little enough excitement in my life. But I gladly gave up my port wine because I felt intoxicants were bad for the lower classes, and﻿—”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西看了看表。「也许我该到警察局去一趟。晨报上有什么吗？」

「一篇同哈利特警长的访谈，云山雾罩。警方已掘出重要线索，并许诺早日见分晓。你知道的——凶案刚出，他们总要发放这么一套说辞。」

那青年锐利地看了她一眼。「啊，」他说，「这么说，您自己也读起您当年想禁绝的那类凶案报道来了？」

「当然读。」他的姑婆没好气地说。「我生活里的刺激本来就少得可怜。不过当年我心甘情愿戒了波尔图葡萄酒，是因为我觉得酒类于下层阶级有害，而且——」

===Original===
Haku interrupted with the news that John Quincy was wanted on the telephone. When the boy returned to the lanai, there was a brisk air of business about him.

“That was Charlie,” he announced. “The day’s work is about to get under way. They’ve located Mr. and Mrs. Brade at the Reef and Palm Hotel, and I’m to meet Charlie there in fifteen minutes.”

“The Reef and Palm,” repeated Miss Minerva. “You see, it keeps coming back to Egan. I’d wager a set of Browning against a modern novel that he’s the man who did it.”

“You’d lose your Browning, and then where would you be when the lecture season started?” laughed John Quincy. “I never knew you to be so stupid before.” His face became serious. “By the way, will you explain to Barbara that I can’t join her, after all?”

Miss Minerva nodded. “Go along,” she said. “I envy you all this. First time in my life I ever wished I were a man.”

===Chinese===
哈库进来打岔，报说有电话找约翰·昆西。待那青年回到露台上，周身已裹上一层干脆利落的公务气息。

「是陈查理打来的。」他宣布道。「今天的工作这就开场了。他们查明布雷德先生和太太落脚在『礁石棕榈』旅馆，我十五分钟后要到那儿同陈查理会合。」

「『礁石棕榈』。」米纳瓦小姐重复道。「你瞧，兜来兜去总绕回伊根身上。我敢拿一套勃朗宁全集对一本现代小说打赌，下手的就是他。」

「那您就要输掉勃朗宁了，等演讲季开场，您可怎么办？」约翰·昆西笑道。「我从前可不知道您这么糊涂。」他的脸色正经起来。「对了，请您跟芭芭拉解释一句，我到底还是不能陪她了。」

米纳瓦小姐点了点头。「去吧。」她说。「这一切我真眼红你。这辈子头一回，我恨不得自己是个男人。」

===Original===
John Quincy approached the Reef and Palm by way of the beach. The scene was one of bright serenity. A few languid tourists lolled upon the sand; others, more ambitious, were making picture postcard history out where the surf began. A great white steamer puffed blackly into port. Standing in water up to their necks, a group of Hawaiian women paused in their search for luncheon delicacies to enjoy a moment’s gossip.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西取道海滩走近「礁石棕榈」。眼前是一片明亮的安谧。几个倦怠的游客歪在沙滩上；另一些更有进取心的，正在浪头初起处营造明信片画境般的历史。一艘白色巨轮喷着黑烟进港。一群夏威夷女人立在齐颈深的水里，暂停搜寻午餐的珍馐，享受片刻的闲话。

===Original===
John Quincy passed Arlene Compton’s cottage and entered the grounds of the Reef and Palm. On the beach not far from the hotel, an elderly Englishwoman sat on a camp stool with an easel and canvas before her. She was seeking to capture something of that exotic scene﻿—vainly seeking, for John Quincy, glancing over her shoulder, perceived that her work was terrible. She turned and looked at him, a weary look of protest against his intrusion, and he was sorry she had caught him in the act of smiling at her inept canvas.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西走过阿琳·康普顿的小屋，进了「礁石棕榈」的地界。离旅馆不远的沙滩上，一位上了年纪的英国妇人坐在折凳上，面前支着画架和画布。她正试图把那异域景致捕捉几分到画上——白费心思，因为约翰·昆西从她肩后一瞥，便看出她画得一塌糊涂。她回过头来望他，一脸倦怠的神色，抗议他闯到跟前；偏偏她撞见了他正对着她那不济事的画布微笑，这叫他好生过意不去。

===Original===
Chan had not yet arrived at the hotel, and the clerk informed John Quincy that Miss Carlota had gone to the city. For that interview with her father, no doubt. He hoped that the evidence of the check would bring about Egan’s release. It seemed to him that the man was being held on a rather flimsy pretext, anyhow.

===Chinese===
陈查理还未到旅馆，职员告诉约翰·昆西，卡洛塔小姐进城去了。不用说，是为着同她父亲的那场会面。他指望那张支票的证据能促成伊根获释。再说，在他看来，警方扣着这个人的由头，本来就近于站不住脚。

===Original===
He sat down on the lanai at the side, where he could see both the path that led in from the street and the restless waters of the Pacific. On the beach nearby a man in a purple bathing suit reclined dejectedly, and John Quincy smiled in recollection. Mr. Saladine, alone with his tragedy, peering out at the waters that had robbed him﻿—waiting, no doubt, for the tide to yield up its loot.

===Chinese===
他在侧面露台上拣了个座，从那儿既能望见由街上进来的小径，又能望见太平洋躁动不息的水面。近旁沙滩上，一个穿紫色泳衣的男人颓然仰卧着，约翰·昆西忆起前情，不禁微笑。萨拉迪尼先生，独自伴着他的悲剧，凝望那掠夺了他的海水——不用问，正等着潮水吐出它的赃物呢。

===Original===
Some fifteen or twenty minutes passed, and then John Quincy heard voices in the garden. He saw that Hallet and Chan were coming up the walk and went to meet them at the front door.

“Splendid morning,” said Chan. “Nice day to set out on new path leading unevitably to important discovery.”

John Quincy accompanied them to the desk. The Japanese clerk regarded them with sullen unfriendliness; he had not forgotten the events of the day before. Information had to be dragged from him bit by bit. Yes, there was a Mr. and Mrs. Brade stopping there. They arrived last Saturday, on the steamship *Sonoma*. Mr. Brade was not about at the moment. Mrs. Brade was on the beach painting pretty pictures.

===Chinese===
约莫过了一刻钟到二十分钟，约翰·昆西听见花园里起了人声。他看见哈利特和陈查理正沿小径走来，便到正门口迎他们。

「早晨壮丽之极。」陈查理说。「好日子，宜于启程踏上新路——新路不可避免地，引向重大发现。」

约翰·昆西陪他们走到账台前。那日本职员阴沉沉地板着脸，爱答不理；前一天的事他还没忘。情报得从他嘴里一点一点往外抠。是，店里住着一位布雷德先生和太太。他们是上星期六到的，搭乘索诺马号（*Sonoma*）轮船。布雷德先生眼下不在。布雷德太太在沙滩上画好看的画儿呢。

===Original===
“Good,” said Hallet, “I’ll have a look around their room before I question them. Take us there.”

The Jap hesitated. “Boy!” he called. It was only a bluff; the Reef and Palm had no bellboys. Finally, with an air of injured dignity, he led the way down a long corridor on the same floor as the office and unlocked the door of nineteen, the last room on the right. Hallet strode in and went to the window.

“Here﻿—wait a minute,” he called to the clerk. He pointed to the elderly woman painting on the beach. “That Mrs. Brade?”

“Yes-s,” hissed the Jap.

“All right﻿—go along.” The clerk went out. “Mr. Winterslip, I’ll ask you to sit here in the window and keep an eye on the lady. If she starts to come in, let me know.” He stared eagerly about the poorly furnished bedroom. “Now, Mr. Brade, I wonder what you’ve got?”

===Chinese===
「好。」哈利特说，「盘问之前，我要先到他们房里查看一番。带我们去。」

那日本人犹豫了。「茶房！」他喊了一声。不过是虚张声势；「礁石棕榈」压根儿没雇茶房。末了，他摆出一副尊严受了伤的神气，领他们沿账台同一层的一条长走廊走去，打开了右手最末一间——十九号房——的门锁。哈利特大步跨进去，直奔窗口。

「喂——且慢。」他朝职员喊道。他指着沙滩上作画的老妇人。「那是布雷德太太？」

「是——」日本人嘶声答道。

「行了——去吧。」职员出去了。「温特斯利普先生，请你坐在这窗口，替我盯住那位太太。她要是起身往里走，就知会我一声。」他急切地环视这间陈设寒素的卧室。「好了，布雷德先生，我倒要看看，你藏着什么玩意儿？」

===Original===
John Quincy took the post assigned him, feeling decidedly uncomfortable. This didn’t seem quite honorable to him. However, he probably wouldn’t be called upon to do any searching himself, and if policemen were forced to do disagreeable things﻿—well, they should have thought of that before they became policemen. Not that either Hallet or Chan appeared to be embarrassed by the task before them.

There was a great deal of luggage in the room﻿—English luggage, which is usually large and impressive. John Quincy noted a trunk, two enormous bags, and a smaller case. All were plastered with labels of the *Sonoma*, and beneath were the worn fragments of earlier labels, telling a broken story of other ships and far hotels.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西在分派给他的岗位上落了座，心里老大不自在。这在他看来未免有欠光明正大。不过，多半轮不到他亲自动手搜查；再说，警察若是不得不做些倒胃口的事——唔，谁叫他们当警察之前不先想到这一层呢。倒是哈利特也好，陈查理也罢，面对眼前的差事，谁也不见有半点难为情。

房里行李甚多——英式行李，照例又大又气派。约翰·昆西数出一只大衣箱、两只硕大的提包，外加一只小些的箱子。上面统统贴满了索诺马号的标签，标签底下还压着先前各张标签磨残的碎片，断断续续，诉说着别的轮船和远方旅馆的沧桑旧事。

===Original===
Hallet and Chan were old hands at this game; they went through Brade’s trunk rapidly and thoroughly, but without finding anything of note. The captain turned his attention to the small traveling case. With every evidence of delight he drew forth a packet of letters, and sat down with them at a table. John Quincy was shocked. Reading other people’s mail was, in his eyes, something that simply wasn’t done.

===Chinese===
这种活计，哈利特和陈查理都是此道老手；他们把布雷德的衣箱飞快而彻底地过了一遍，却没翻出什么值得注意的东西。警长把注意力转到那只小旅行箱上。他明摆着乐不可支，抽出一扎信件，携着它们在桌边坐定。约翰·昆西大吃一惊。在他眼里，私拆旁人的信件，是断乎干不得的事。

===Original===
It was done by Hallet, however. In a moment the captain spoke. “Seems to have been in the British civil service in Calcutta, but he’s resigned,” he announced to Chan. “Here’s a letter from his superior in London referring to Brade’s thirty-six years on the job, and saying he’s sorry to lose him.” Hallet took up another letter, his face brightened as he read. “Say﻿—this is more like it!” He handed the typewritten page to Chan. The Chinaman looked at it, and his eyes sparkled. “Most interesting,” he cried, and turned it over to John Quincy.

===Chinese===
哈利特却干得出来。不多时，警长发话了。「此人从前似在加尔各答的英国文职衙门当差，如今辞了职。」他对陈查理宣布道。「这儿有封他伦敦上司的来信，提到布雷德当差三十六年，说痛失此人深表惋惜。」哈利特又拿起一封，读着读着，脸上亮了起来。「嘿——这才像话！」他把那页打字信递给陈查理。那华人看了看，双目生辉。「大为有趣。」他喊道，又把它转递给约翰·昆西。

===Original===
The boy hesitated. The standards of a lifetime are not easily abandoned. But the others had read it first, so he put aside his scruples. The letter was several months old, and was addressed to Brade in Calcutta.

> “Dear Sir: In reply to your inquiry of the sixth instant, would say that Mr. Daniel Winterslip is alive and is a resident of this city. His address is 3947 Kalia Road, Waikiki, Honolulu, T.H.”

The signature was that of the British consul at Honolulu. John Quincy returned the epistle to Hallet, who put it in his pocket. At that instant Chan, who had been exploring one of the larger bags, emitted a little grunt of satisfaction.

===Chinese===
那青年犹豫了。一辈子的操守，不是轻易抛得开的。可旁人已经先读过了，他也就把顾忌撇在一边。这封信是几个月前的，收信人是加尔各答的布雷德。

> 「敬启者：兹覆贵方本月六日惠函所询事项，谨告以丹尼尔·温特斯利普先生健在，且为本城居民。其住址为：檀香山怀基基卡利亚路 3947 号，夏威夷领地。」

落款是英国驻檀香山领事。约翰·昆西把信函交还哈利特，哈利特把它揣进口袋。恰在此时，一直在搜检一只大提包的陈查理，发出一声满意的轻哼。

===Original===
“What is it, Charlie?” Hallet asked.

The Chinaman set out on the table before his chief a small tin box, and removed the lid. It was filled with cigarettes. “Corsican brand,” he announced cheerfully.

“Good,” said Hallet. “It begins to look as though Mr. Thomas Macan Brade would have a lot to explain.”

===Chinese===
「是什么，查理？」哈利特问。

那华人在上司面前的桌上摆出一只小铁盒，揭开盖子。盒里装满了香烟。「科西嘉牌。」他欢欢喜喜地宣布道。

「好。」哈利特说。「看来这位托马斯·梅肯·布雷德先生，要有一大篇话好解释了。」

===Original===
They continued their researches, while John Quincy sat silent by the window. Presently Carlota Egan appeared outside. She walked slowly to a chair on the lanai, and sat down. For a moment she stared at the breakers, then she began to weep.

John Quincy turned uncomfortably away. It came to him that here in this so-called paradise sorrow was altogether too rampant. The only girls he knew were given to frequent tears, and not without reason.

“If you’ll excuse me﻿—” he said. Hallet and Chan, searching avidly, made no reply, and climbing over the sill, he stepped on to the lanai. The girl looked up as he approached.

===Chinese===
他们继续搜检，约翰·昆西默坐在窗边。不多时，卡洛塔·伊根在外面现身了。她慢慢走到露台的一把椅子跟前，坐下。有一会儿她盯着碎浪，随即哭了起来。

约翰·昆西不自在地扭过头去。他忽然省悟，在这座所谓的乐园里，愁苦未免泛滥得太厉害了。他认识的这几位姑娘，动辄落泪，倒也并非全无来由。

「失陪一下——」他说。哈利特和陈查理正搜得起劲，没有应声，他便跨过窗台，踏上了露台。他走近时，姑娘抬起眼来。

===Original===
“Oh,” she said, “I thought I was alone.”

“You’d like to be, perhaps,” he answered. “But it might help if you told me what has happened. Did you speak to your father about that check?”

She nodded. “Yes, I showed it to him. And what do you think he did? He snatched it out of my hand and tore it into a hundred pieces. He gave me the pieces to﻿—to throw away. And he said I was never to mention it to a soul.”

===Chinese===
「哦，」她说，「我还当只有我一个人呢。」

「也许你巴不得如此。」他答道。「不过，把出了什么事讲给我听，兴许有点用处。那张支票的事，你跟你父亲说了吗？」

她点点头。「说了，我拿给他看了。你猜他怎么着？他一把从我手里抢过去，撕成了一百片。他把碎片交给我，让我——让我扔掉。还说我永远不许对任何人提起。」

===Original===
“I don’t understand that,” frowned John Quincy.

“Neither do I. He was simply furious﻿—not like himself at all. And when I told him you knew about it, he lost his temper again.”

“But you can rely on me. I shan’t tell anyone.”

“I know that. But of course father wasn’t so sure of you as﻿—as I am. Poor dad﻿—he’s having a horrible time of it. They don’t give him a moment’s rest﻿—keep after him constantly﻿—trying to make him tell. But all the policemen in the world couldn’t﻿—Oh, poor old dad!”

===Chinese===
「这我就不懂了。」约翰·昆西皱眉道。

「我也不懂。他简直是气疯了——全然不像他平日的为人。我告诉他你也知道这件事，他又发作了一通。」

「可你尽管信得过我。我谁也不告诉。」

「这我知道。可父亲对你自然不像——不像我这样有把握。可怜的爸爸——他眼下正受着大罪呢。他们不给他片刻安宁——时时刻刻逼着他——想逼他招认。可天底下所有的警察加在一处，也休想——哦，可怜的爸爸！」

===Original===
She was weeping again, and John Quincy felt toward her as he had felt toward Barbara. He wanted to put his arm about her, just by way of comfort and cheer. But alas, Carlota Maria Egan was not a Winterslip.

“Now, now,” he said, “that won’t do a bit of good.”

She looked at him through her tears. “Won’t it? I﻿—I don’t know. It seems to help a little. But”﻿—she dried her eyes﻿—“I really haven’t time for it now. I must go in and see about lunch.”

===Chinese===
她又哭了起来，约翰·昆西对她的心肠，一如方才对芭芭拉。他想搂住她的肩，权作一点宽慰和打气。可惜啊，卡洛塔·玛丽亚·伊根不是温特斯利普家的人。

「好了，好了，」他说，「这样一点儿用处也没有。」

她噙着泪望他。「没有吗？我——我不知道。好像确实好受一点。不过」——她拭干了眼睛——「我眼下实在没工夫哭。我得进去料理午餐了。」

===Original===
She rose, and John Quincy walked with her along the balcony. “I wouldn’t worry if I were you,” he said. “The police are on an entirely new trail this morning.”

“Really?” she answered eagerly.

“Yes. There’s a man named Brade stopping at your hotel. You know him, I suppose?”

She shook her head. “No, I don’t.”

===Chinese===
她站起身，约翰·昆西陪她沿阳台走去。「我要是你，就不操这份心。」他说。「今天早晨，警方追的是一条全新的线索。」

「真的？」她急切地应道。

「真的。有个姓布雷德的人住在你们旅馆。我猜你认得他吧？」

她摇摇头。「不认得。」

===Original===
“What! Why, he’s a guest here.”

“He was. But he isn’t here now.”

“Wait a minute!” John Quincy laid his hand on her arm, and they stopped. “This is interesting. Brade’s gone, you say?”

“Yes. I understand from the clerk that Mr. and Mrs. Brade arrived here last Saturday. But early Tuesday morning, before my boat got in, Mr. Brade disappeared and he hasn’t been seen since.”

“Mr. Brade gets better all the time,” John Quincy said. “Hallet and Chan are in his room now, and they’ve unearthed some rather intriguing facts. You’d better go in and tell Hallet what you’ve just told me.”

===Chinese===
「什么！嗐，他可是这儿的房客啊。」

「曾经是。可他如今不在这儿了。」

「且慢！」约翰·昆西把手搭上她的手臂，两人停住脚。「这就有意思了。你是说，布雷德不见了？」

「是。我从职员那儿得知，布雷德先生和太太是上星期六到的店里。可是星期二一大早，在我搭的船进港之前，布雷德先生就不见了踪影，打那以后再没人见过他。」

「这位布雷德先生，真是越来越有看头了。」约翰·昆西说。「哈利特和陈查理这会儿就在他房里，已经掘出几桩颇为蹊跷的事实。你最好进去，把方才跟我说的话告诉哈利特。」

===Original===
They entered the lobby by a side door. As they did so, a slim young Hawaiian boy was coming in through the big door at the front. Something in his manner caught the attention of John Quincy, and he stopped. At that instant a purple bathing suit slipped by him, and Mr. Saladine also approached the desk. Carlota Egan went on down the corridor toward room nineteen, but John Quincy remained in the lobby.

===Chinese===
他们从一扇边门进了大堂。正这时，一个瘦长的夏威夷青年从前头的大门走进来。他举止间有什么东西勾住了约翰·昆西的注意，他停了下来。恰在此刻，一件紫色泳衣从他身边滑过去，萨拉迪尼先生也朝账台走来。卡洛塔·伊根径自沿走廊往十九号房去了，约翰·昆西却留在大堂里。

===Original===
The Hawaiian boy moved rather diffidently toward the clerk. “Excuse me, please,” he said. “I come to see Mr. Brade. Mr. Thomas Brade.”

“Mr. Brade not here,” replied the Jap.

“Then I will wait till he comes.”

The clerk frowned. “No good. Mr. Brade not in Honolulu now.”

===Chinese===
那夏威夷青年怯生生地朝职员挪过去。「劳驾，」他说，「我来看看布雷德先生。托马斯·布雷德先生。」

「布雷德先生不在这儿。」日本人答道。

「那我就等他回来。」

职员皱起眉头。「没用的。布雷德先生现在不在檀香山。」

===Original===
“Not in Honolulu!” The Hawaiian seemed startled by the news.

“Mrs. Brade outside on the beach,” continued the Jap.

“Oh, then Mr. Brade returns,” said the boy with evident relief. “I call again.”

===Chinese===
「不在檀香山！」那夏威夷人听了这消息，似乎吃了一惊。

「布雷德太太在外面沙滩上。」日本人接着说。

「哦，那布雷德先生总要回来的。」那青年显然松了口气，说道，「我回头再来。」

===Original===
He turned away, moving rapidly now. The clerk addressed Mr. Saladine, who was hovering near the cigar case. “Yes, sir, please?”

“Thigarettes,” said the bereft Mr. Saladine.

The Jap evidently knew the brand desired, and handed over a box.

“Juth put it on my bill,” said Saladine. He stood for a moment staring after the Hawaiian, who was disappearing through the front door. As he swung round his eyes encountered those of John Quincy. He looked quickly away and hurried out.

===Chinese===
他转身走开，这回脚步飞快。职员招呼起在雪茄柜近旁打转的萨拉迪尼先生。「先生，要点什么？」

「希加雷特（香烟）。」遭了劫的萨拉迪尼先生说。

那日本人显然知道他要的牌子，递过去一盒。

「就希记在我账上。」萨拉迪尼说。他站了片刻，目送那夏威夷人的背影消失在前门外。他一转身，目光同约翰·昆西撞了个正着。他慌忙移开眼，急匆匆出去了。

===Original===
The two policemen and the girl entered from the corridor. “Well, Mr. Winterslip,” said Hallet, “the bird has flown.”

“So I understand,” John Quincy answered.

“But we’ll find him,” continued Hallet. “I’ll go over these islands with a dragnet. First of all, I want a talk with his wife.” He turned to Carlota Egan. “Get her in here,” he ordered. The girl looked at him. “Please,” he added.

She motioned to the clerk, who went out the door.

===Chinese===
两名警察和那姑娘从走廊那头进来。「唉，温特斯利普先生，」哈利特说，「鸟儿飞了。」

「我也听说了。」约翰·昆西答道。

「不过我们抓得着他。」哈利特接着说。「我要撒下一张天罗地网，把这些岛屿统统筛一遍。头一件事，我要同他太太谈一谈。」他转向卡洛塔·伊根。「把她叫进来。」他吩咐道。姑娘望着他。「劳驾。」他添了一句。

她朝职员递了个眼色，职员便出门去了。

===Original===
“By the way,” remarked John Quincy, “someone was just here asking for Brade.”

“What’s that!” Hallet was interested.

“A young Hawaiian, about twenty, I should say. Tall and slim. If you go to the door, you may catch a glimpse of him.”

Hallet hurried over and glanced out into the garden. In a second he returned. “Humph,” he said. “I know him. Did he say he’d come again?”

“He did.”

Hallet considered. “I’ve changed my mind,” he announced. “I won’t question Mrs. Brade, after all. For the present, I don’t want her to know we’re looking for her husband. I’ll trust you to fix that up with your clerk,” he added to the girl. She nodded. “Lucky we left things as we found them in nineteen,” he went on. “Unless she misses that letter and the cigarettes, which isn’t likely, we’re all right. Now, Miss Egan, we three will go into your father’s office there behind the desk, and leave the door open. When Mrs. Brade comes in, I want you to question her about her husband’s absence. Get all you can out of her. I’ll be listening.”

===Chinese===
「对了，」约翰·昆西说，「方才有个人到这儿来找布雷德。」

「什么！」哈利特起了兴致。

「一个夏威夷青年，依我看，二十岁上下。高挑，瘦长。您这会儿到门口去，兴许还能望见他的背影。」

哈利特疾步走过去，朝花园里张了一眼。转眼他就回来了。「哼。」他说。「这人我认得。他说还要再来吗？」

「说了。」

哈利特思量了一下。「我改主意了。」他宣布道。「布雷德太太到底还是不盘问了。眼前我还不想让她知道我们在找她丈夫。这事儿就托你去同你们的职员打点妥当。」他向姑娘补了一句。她点点头。「幸亏十九号房里的东西我们原样放回。」他接着说。「只要她不察觉少了那封信和那些香烟——多半不会——我们就稳当。这样，伊根小姐，我们三个进你父亲账台后面那间办公室去，把门敞着。布雷德太太进来后，我要你就她丈夫的外出盘问她。能掏出多少掏多少。我自会听着。」

===Original===
“I understand,” the girl said.

Hallet, Chan and John Quincy went into Jim Egan’s sanctum. “You found nothing else in the room?” the latter inquired of the Chinaman.

Chan shook his head. “Even so, fates are in smiling mood. What we have now are plentiful.”

“Sh!” warned Hallet.

“Mrs. Brade, a young man was just here inquiring, for your husband.” It was Carlota Egan’s voice.

===Chinese===
「我明白了。」姑娘说。

哈利特、陈查理和约翰·昆西进了吉姆·伊根的密室。「房里没再搜出别的？」后者问那华人。

陈查理摇摇头。「即便如此，命运诸神正处在微笑的心情之中。我们如今所持有的，是丰足有余。」

「嘘！」哈利特提醒道。

「布雷德太太，方才有位年轻人到这儿来，打听您丈夫来着。」那是卡洛塔·伊根的声音。

===Original===
“Really?” The accent was unmistakably British.

“He wanted to know where he could find him. We couldn’t say.”

“No﻿—of course not.”

“Your husband has left town, Mrs. Brade?”

===Chinese===
「当真？」那口英国腔错不了。

「他想知道上哪儿能找到他。我们说不上来。」

「是啊——当然说不上。」

「您丈夫离城了，布雷德太太？」

===Original===
“Yes. I fancy he has.”

“You know when he will return, perhaps?”

“I really couldn’t say. Is the mail in?”

“Not yet. We expect it about one.”

“Thank you so much.”

===Chinese===
「是的。我想是走了。」

「您也许知道他什么时候回来？」

「我实在说不上。邮件到了吗？」

「还没有。我们估摸一点钟到。」

「多谢你了。」

===Original===
“Go to the door,” Hallet directed John Quincy.

“She’s gone to her room,” announced the boy.

The three of them emerged from Egan’s office.

“Oh, Captain,” said the girl, “I’m afraid I wasn’t very successful.”

“That’s all right,” replied Hallet. “I didn’t think you would be.” The clerk was again at his post behind the desk. Hallet turned to him. “Look here,” he said. “I understand someone was here a minute ago asking for Brade. It was Dick Kaohla, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「到门口去。」哈利特指挥约翰·昆西。

「她回自己房里去了。」那青年报告道。

三人从伊根的办公室里走了出来。

「唉，警长，」姑娘说，「恐怕我没能问出什么名堂。」

「不要紧。」哈利特答道。「我本也没指望你成。」职员已重新守在账台后的老位置上。哈利特转向他。「喂，」他说。「我听说方才有人来这儿找布雷德。是迪克·考赫拉，对不对？」

===Original===
“Yes-s,” answered the Jap.

“Had he been here before to see Brade?”

“Yes-s. Sunday night. Mr. Brade and him have long talk on the beach.”

Hallet nodded grimly. “Come on, Charlie,” he said. “We’ve got our work cut out for us. Wherever Brade is, we must find him.”

John Quincy stepped forward. “Pardon me, Captain,” he remarked. “But if you don’t mind﻿—just who is Dick Kaohla?”

Hallet hesitated. “Kaohla’s father﻿—he’s dead now﻿—was a sort of confidential servant to Dan Winterslip. The boy’s just plain no good. And oh, yes﻿—he’s the grandson of that woman who’s over at your place now. Kamaikui﻿—is that her name?”

===Chinese===
「是——」日本人答道。

「他从前到这儿找过布雷德吗？」

「找过。星期天夜里。布雷德先生跟他在沙滩上谈了很久。」

哈利特面色森然，点了点头。「走吧，查理。」他说。「咱们有的忙了。布雷德不管在哪儿，都得找出来。」

约翰·昆西上前一步。「恕我冒昧，警长。」他说。「不过您要是不介意——这位迪克·考赫拉究竟是谁？」

哈利特迟疑了一下。「考赫拉的父亲——如今不在人世了——从前算是丹·温特斯利普的一名心腹仆役。这小子纯粹是个不成器的坯子。哦，对了——他就是眼下在你们府上的那个女人的孙子。卡迈库伊——是叫这个名字吧？」

## XIV What Kaohla Carried / 十四 考赫拉携带的东西

===Original===
Several days slipped by so rapidly John Quincy scarcely noted their passing. Dan Winterslip was sleeping now under the royal palms of the lovely island where he had been born. Sun and moon shone brightly in turn on his last dwelling place, but those who sought the person he had encountered that Monday night on his lanai were still groping in the dark.
Hallet had kept his word, he was combing the Islands for Brade. But Brade was nowhere. Ships paused at the crossroads and sailed again; the name of Thomas Macan Brade was on no sailing list. Through far settlements that were called villages but were nothing save clusters of Japanese huts, in lonely coves where the surf moaned dismally, over pineapple and sugar plantations, the emissaries of Hallet pursued their quest. Their efforts came to nothing.
John Quincy drifted idly with the days. He knew now the glamour of Waikiki waters; he had felt their warm embrace. Every afternoon he experimented with a board in the malihini surf, and he was eager for the moment when he could dare the big rollers farther out. Boston seemed like a tale that is told, State Street and Beacon memories of another more active existence now abandoned. No longer was he at a loss to understand his aunt’s reluctance to depart these friendly shores.

===Chinese===
一连几天飞快地溜了过去，约翰·昆西几乎没有察觉它们的消逝。丹·温特斯利普如今长眠在他出生的那座可爱岛屿的王室棕榈树下。太阳和月亮轮番朗照着他最后的居所，然而追寻他星期一夜里在露台上遇到的那个人的人们，仍在黑暗中摸索。
哈利特没有食言，他正把各岛梳篦一般搜寻布雷德。可布雷德杳无踪影。轮船在这海上十字路口稍停又启航；哪一份航行名单上都没有托马斯·马坎·布雷德的名字。哈利特派出的密探穿过那些名为村庄、实则不过一片片日本棚屋的偏远聚落，踏进浪涛呜咽的荒凉海湾，越过菠萝园和甘蔗园，一路追寻。这一切辛劳尽付东流。
约翰·昆西随着日子慵懒地漂流。如今他领略了怀基基海水的魅力；也感受过它们温暖的拥抱。每天下午他都抱着冲浪板在玛利希尼新手浪里练习，急切盼着能到更远处闯荡大浪的日子。波士顿像一个讲完了的故事，州街和灯塔街成了另一种更活跃的、如今业已放弃的人生的记忆。他再也不难理解姑婆为何恋恋不舍这片友善的海岸。

===Original===
Early Friday afternoon Miss Minerva found him reading a book on the lanai. Something in the nonchalance of his manner irritated her. She had always been for action, and the urge was on her even in Hawaii.
“Have you seen Mr. Chan lately?” she inquired.
“Talked with him this morning. They’re doing their best to find Brade.”
“Humph,” sniffed Miss Minerva. “Their best is none too good. I’d like to have a few Boston detectives on this case.”
“Oh, give them time,” yawned John Quincy.
“They’ve had three days,” she snapped. “Time enough. Brade never left this island of Oahu, that’s certain. And when you consider that you can drive across it in a motor in two hours, and around it in about six, Mr. Hallet’s brilliance does not impress. I’ll have to end by solving this thing myself.”

===Chinese===
星期五下午刚过，米纳瓦小姐发现他在露台上看书。他那副漫不经心的模样里，有点什么让她着恼。她一辈子都主张行动，即便到了夏威夷，这股冲动仍在她身上发作。
「你近来见过陈先生吗？」她问。
「今天早上还同他谈过。他们正在竭尽全力寻找布雷德。」
「哼。」米纳瓦小姐嗤了一声。「他们的竭尽全力也高明不到哪儿去。我倒想从波士顿调几个侦探来办这个案子。」
「哦，给他们点儿时间嘛。」约翰·昆西打着哈欠说。
「已经给他们三天了。」她厉声道。「时间够长的了。布雷德压根儿没离开瓦胡岛，这是明摆着的。再想想，坐汽车横穿这岛只消两个钟头，绕一圈也不过六个钟头，哈利特警长的英明实在叫人不敢恭维。到头来，怕是得我亲自动手把这案子破了。」

===Original===
John Quincy laughed. “Yes, maybe you will.”
“Well, I’ve given them the two best clues they have. If they’d keep their eyes open the way I do﻿—”
“Charlie’s eyes are open,” protested John Quincy.
“Think so? They look pretty sleepy to me.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西笑了。「是啊，没准儿真会让你破了。」
「哼，他们手上那两条最好的线索都是我给的。他们要能像我这样把眼睛睁大点儿——」
「陈查理的眼睛可是睁着的。」约翰·昆西抗辩道。
「是吗？我看它们倒挺困的。」

===Original===
Barbara appeared on the lanai, dressed for a drive. Her eyes were somewhat happier; a bit of color had come back to her cheeks. “What are you reading, John Quincy?” she asked.
He held up the book. “*The City by the Golden Gate*,” he told her.
“Oh, really? If you’re interested, I believe dad had quite a library on San Francisco. I remember there was a history of the stock exchange﻿—he wanted me to read it, but I couldn’t.”
“You missed a good one,” John Quincy informed her. “I finished it this morning. I’ve read five other books on San Francisco since I came.”
His aunt stared at him. “What for?” she asked.

===Chinese===
芭芭拉来到露台上，一身驾车出游的装束。她的眼里多了几分喜色；脸颊上也恢复了一点血色。「你在读什么，约翰·昆西？」她问。
他举起那本书。「《金门畔的城市》。」他告诉她。
「哦，真的？你要是感兴趣，我想爸爸存了一大批关于旧金山的藏书。我记得有一本证券交易所的历史——他还想让我读，我可读不下去。」
「那你可错过了一本好书。」约翰·昆西告诉她。「我今天早上刚读完。来这儿以后，我还读了另外五本讲旧金山的书。」
他的姑婆直勾勾望着他。「读它做什么？」她问。

===Original===
“Well﻿—” He hesitated. “I’ve taken sort of a fancy to the town. I don’t know﻿—sometimes I think I’d rather like to live there.”
Miss Minerva smiled grimly. “And they sent you out to take me back to Boston,” she remarked.
“Boston’s all right,” said her nephew hastily. “It’s Winterslip headquarters﻿—but its hold has never been strong enough to prevent an occasional Winterslip from hitting the trail. You know, when I came into San Francisco harbor, I had the oddest feeling.” He told them about it. “And the more I saw of the city, the better I liked it. There’s a snap and sparkle in the air, and the people seem to know how to get the most out of life.”
Barbara smiled on him approvingly. “Follow that impulse, John Quincy,” she advised.
“Maybe I will. All this reminds me﻿—I must write a letter.” He rose and left the lanai.

===Chinese===
「这个嘛——」他迟疑了一下。「我有点儿爱上那座城了。说不清——有时候我想，我倒挺愿意住在那儿。」
米纳瓦小姐冷冷一笑。「他们把你派出来，就是要接我回波士顿去的。」她说。
「波士顿没什么不好。」她的侄子连忙说。「那是温特斯利普家的大本营——不过它的吸力向来不够强大，拦不住偶尔冒出来的温特斯利普家人上路远走。你知道，我进旧金山港的时候，有过一种顶顶古怪的感觉。」他把那感觉讲给她们听。「而且这城市我越看越喜欢。空气里有一股爽利的劲儿、亮闪闪的味儿，那里的人似乎最懂得怎么把生活的甜头榨到最足。」
芭芭拉赞许地朝他微笑。「跟着那股冲动走吧，约翰·昆西。」她劝道。
「也许我会的。这些话倒提醒了我——我得写封信。」他起身离开了露台。

===Original===
“Does he really intend to desert Boston?” Barbara asked.
Miss Minerva shook her head. “Just a moment’s madness,” she explained. “I’m glad he’s going through it﻿—he’ll be more human in the future. But as for leaving Boston! John Quincy! As well expect Bunker Hill Monument to emigrate to England.”

===Chinese===
「他当真打算抛弃波士顿吗？」芭芭拉问。
米纳瓦小姐摇摇头。「不过是一时痴念。」她解释道。「我倒高兴他经历这一遭——往后他会更有人情味儿。可要说离开波士顿！约翰·昆西！那还不如指望邦克山纪念碑移民英国去。」

===Original===
In his room upstairs, however, John Quincy’s madness was persisting. He had never completed that letter to Agatha Parker, but he now plunged into his task with enthusiasm. San Francisco was his topic, and he wrote well. He pictured the city in words that glowed with life, and he wondered﻿—just a suggestion﻿—how she’d like to live there.
Agatha was now, he recalled, on a ranch in Wyoming﻿—her first encounter with the West﻿—and that was providential. She had felt for herself the lure of the wide open spaces. Well, the farther you went the wider and opener they got. In California life was all color and light. Just a suggestion, of course.
As he sealed the flap of the envelope, he seemed to glimpse Agatha’s thin patrician face, and his heart sank. Her gray eyes were cool, so different from Barbara’s, so very different from those of Carlota Maria Egan.

===Chinese===
然而在楼上的房间里，约翰·昆西的痴念还在继续。给阿加莎·帕克的那封信他一直没写完，此刻却满腔热忱地扑到这桩差事上。旧金山是他的题目，他写得文采斐然。他用鲜活发亮的文字描画那座城市，又试探着——仅仅是一点暗示——问她愿不愿意住到那儿去。
他想起阿加莎如今正在怀俄明州的一座牧场上——她头一回见识西部——这真是天意。她已经亲身体会过辽阔旷野的诱惑。可不是嘛，走得越远，天地就越宽、越敞亮。在加利福尼亚，生活处处是色彩和阳光。当然，只是一点暗示而已。
封上信封的时候，他仿佛瞥见阿加莎那张清瘦的贵胄面孔，心便沉了下去。她那双灰眼睛是清冷的，同芭芭拉的眼睛多么不同，同卡洛塔·玛丽亚·伊根的眼睛又是多么、多么不同。

===Original===
On Saturday afternoon John Quincy had an engagement to play golf with Harry Jennison. He drove up Nuuanu Valley in Barbara’s roadster﻿—for Dan Winterslip’s will had been read and everything he possessed was Barbara’s now. In that sheltered spot a brisk rain was falling, as is usually the case, though the sun was shining brightly. John Quincy had grown accustomed to this phenomenon; “liquid sunshine” the people of Hawaii call such rain, and pay no attention to it. Half a dozen different rainbows added to the beauty of the Country Club links.
Jennison was waiting on the veranda, a striking figure in white. He appeared genuinely glad to see his guest, and they set out on a round of golf that John Quincy would long remember. Never before had he played amid such beauty. The low hills stood on guard, their slopes bright with tropical colors﻿—the yellow of kukui trees, the gray of ferns, the emerald of ohia and banana trees, here and there a splotch of brick-red earth. The course was a green velvet carpet beneath their feet, the showers came and went. Jennison was a proficient driver, but the boy was his superior on approaches, and at the end of the match John Quincy was four up. They putted through a rainbow and returned to the locker room.

===Chinese===
星期六下午，约翰·昆西约好同哈里·詹尼森打高尔夫。他开着芭芭拉的敞篷小跑车上努乌阿努谷去——丹·温特斯利普的遗嘱已经宣读，他名下的一切如今都归了芭芭拉。在那处避风的谷地里，一阵急雨正下着——事情往往如此——尽管头顶阳光灿烂。约翰·昆西已经习惯了这种天象；夏威夷人管这种雨叫「液体阳光」，根本不把它放在心上。五六道各不相同的彩虹，更为乡村俱乐部的球场增色不少。
詹尼森在游廊上候着，一身白衣，仪表堂堂，惹人注目。他见到客人显得由衷高兴，两人随即下场，这一轮高尔夫让约翰·昆西久久难忘。他从不曾在这样的美景中打过球。低低的丘陵列队守望，山坡上亮着热带的色彩——库库伊树的金黄，蕨类的灰绿，奥希亚树和香蕉树的翠青，间或一团砖红色的土斑。球场在他们脚下是一幅绿丝绒地毯，阵雨来了又去。詹尼森长打娴熟，可论攻上果岭的一杆，小伙子更胜一筹，打到终局，约翰·昆西赢了四洞。他们推杆穿过一道彩虹，回到更衣室去了。

===Original===
In the roadster going home, Jennison brought up the subject of Dan Winterslip’s murder. John Quincy was interested to get the reaction of a lawyer to the evidence.
“I’ve kept more or less in touch with the case,” Jennison said. “Egan is still my choice.”
Somehow, John Quincy resented this. A picture of Carlota Egan’s lovely but unhappy face flashed through his mind. “How about Leatherbee and the Compton woman?” he asked.
“Well, of course, I wasn’t present when they told their story,” Jennison replied. “But Hallet claims it sounded perfectly plausible. And it doesn’t seem likely that if he’d had anything to do with the murder, Leatherbee would have been fool enough to keep that page from the guest book.”

===Chinese===
回家的路上，在敞篷车里，詹尼森提起了丹·温特斯利普遇害的事。约翰·昆西很想听听一位律师对证据的反应。
「这案子我一直多少留意着。」詹尼森说。「依我看还是伊根。」
不知怎么，约翰·昆西听着有气。卡洛塔·伊根那张可爱而不快的脸庞在他脑中一闪而过。「那么莱瑟比和康普顿夫人呢？」他问。
「这个嘛，他们交代的时候我自然不在场。」詹尼森答道。「可哈利特说，听上去完全合情合理。再说，倘若莱瑟比同这凶案有什么干系，他总不至于蠢到还把登记簿上撕下的那页纸留着吧。」

===Original===
“There’s Brade, too,” John Quincy suggested.
“Yes﻿—Brade complicates things. But when they run him down﻿—if they do﻿—I imagine the result will be nil.”
“You know that Kamaikui’s grandson is mixed up somehow with Brade?”
“So I understand. It’s a matter that wants looking into. But mark my words, when all these trails are followed to the end, everything will come back to Jim Egan.”
“What have you against Egan?” inquired John Quincy, swerving to avoid another car.
“I have nothing against Egan,” Jennison replied. “But I can’t forget the look on Dan Winterslip’s face that day he told me he was afraid of the man. Then there is the stub of the Corsican cigarette. Most important of all, Egan’s silence regarding his business with Winterslip. Men who are facing a charge of murder, my boy, talk, and talk fast. Unless it so happens that what they have to say would further incriminate them.”

===Chinese===
「还有布雷德呢。」约翰·昆西提醒道。
「是啊——布雷德把事情搅复杂了。不过等他们把他逮住——倘若真能逮住——我想结果仍旧是一场空。」
「你知道卡迈库伊的孙子不知怎么同布雷德搅在一块儿了吧？」
「我是听说了。这事得查一查。不过记住我的话，等这些线索一一追到头，一切还是会绕回吉姆·伊根身上。」
「你同伊根有什么过节？」约翰·昆西问着，一打方向盘避开来车。
「我同伊根毫无过节。」詹尼森答道。「可我忘不掉那天丹·温特斯利普告诉我他怕这个人时，脸上的那副神情。再有，那半截科西嘉牌香烟。最要紧的，是伊根对他同温特斯利普之间的勾当缄口不言。我的孩子，眼看要背上谋杀罪名的男人是会开口的，而且抢着开口。除非碰巧，他们说出来的话会把自己陷得更深。」

===Original===
They drove on in silence into the heart of the city. “Hallet tells me you’re doing a little detective work yourself,” smiled Jennison.
“I’ve tried, but I’m a duffer,” John Quincy admitted. “Just at present my efforts consist of a still hunt for that watch Aunt Minerva saw on the murderer’s wrist. Whenever I see a wrist watch I get as close to it as I can, and stare. But as most of my sleuthing is done in the day time, it isn’t so easy to determine whether the numeral two is bright or dim.”
“Persistence,” urged Jennison. “That’s the secret of a good detective. Stick to the job and you may succeed yet.”
The lawyer was to dine with the family at Waikiki. John Quincy set him down at his office, where he had a few letters to sign, and then drove him out to the beach. Barbara was gowned in white; she was slim and wistful and beautiful, and considering the events of the immediate past, the dinner was a cheerful one.

===Chinese===
两人默默前行，驶进了市中心。「哈利特告诉我，你自己也做了点儿侦探工作。」詹尼森微笑道。
「试是试过，可我是个十足的笨手。」约翰·昆西承认道。「眼下我的全部努力，就是悄悄搜查米纳瓦姑婆在凶手手腕上瞧见的那块表。每逢看见一块腕表，我都尽量凑上去盯着瞧。只是我的侦查大半在白天进行，要断定那个数字『二』是亮还是暗，可不太容易。」
「贵在坚持。」詹尼森勉励道。「那正是当好侦探的秘诀。锲而不舍地干下去，说不定你还能成功呢。」
律师当晚要到怀基基同这一家人共进晚餐。约翰·昆西先把他送到他事务所门口——他有几封信要签——随后又开车送他到海滩。芭芭拉一身白色长裙；她纤瘦、含愁而美丽，鉴于新近发生的那一切，这顿晚饭算是气氛欢畅的了。

===Original===
They had coffee on the lanai. Presently Jennison rose and stood by Barbara’s chair. “We’ve something to tell you,” he announced. He looked down at the girl. “Is that right, my dear?”
Barbara nodded.
“Your cousin and I”﻿—the lawyer turned to the two from Boston﻿—“have been fond of each other for a long time. We shall be married very quietly in a week or so﻿—”
“Oh, Harry﻿—not a week,” said Barbara.
“Well, as you wish. But very soon.”
“Yes, very soon,” she repeated.
“And leave Honolulu for a time,” Jennison continued. “Naturally, Barbara feels she can not stay here for the present﻿—so many memories﻿—you both understand. She has authorized me to put this house up for sale﻿—”

===Chinese===
他们在露台上喝咖啡。过了一会儿，詹尼森起身站到芭芭拉的椅子旁。「我们有件事要告诉你们。」他宣布道。他低头望着姑娘。「是这样吧，亲爱的？」
芭芭拉点了点头。
「你们的堂妹和我」——律师转向两位波士顿来客——「彼此倾心已经很久了。大约一个星期后，我们将悄悄地完婚——」
「哦，哈里——别等一个星期。」芭芭拉说。
「好吧，随你的意。不过要快。」
「是的，要快。」她重复道。
「还要离开檀香山一阵子。」詹尼森接着说。「自然，芭芭拉觉得眼下没法待在这儿——太多回忆——你们二位都明白。她已授权我把这座宅子挂牌出售——」

===Original===
“But, Harry,” Barbara protested, “you make me sound so inhospitable. Telling my guests that the house is for sale and I am leaving﻿—”
“Nonsense, my dear,” said Aunt Minerva. “John Quincy and I understand, quite. I sympathize with your desire to get away.” She rose.
“I’m sorry,” said Jennison. “I did sound a little abrupt. But I’m naturally eager to take care of her now.”
“Of course,” John Quincy agreed.
Miss Minerva bent over and kissed the girl. “If your mother were here, dear child,” she said, “she couldn’t wish for your happiness any more keenly than I do.” Barbara reached up impulsively and put her arms about the older woman.

===Chinese===
「可是，哈里，」芭芭拉抗议道，「你把我说得这么怠慢客人。告诉我的客人们，房子要卖了，人也要走了——」
「胡说，亲爱的。」米纳瓦姑婆说。「约翰·昆西和我都明白，十分明白。你想离开这儿，我完全体谅。」她站起身来。
「抱歉。」詹尼森说。「我方才确实说得急了些。不过如今我自然巴不得赶紧照顾好她。」
「那当然。」约翰·昆西应道。
米纳瓦小姐俯下身去吻了吻姑娘。「要是你母亲还在，亲爱的孩子，」她说，「她盼你幸福的殷切，也断不能胜过我。」芭芭拉一时情动，伸手上去搂住了这位年长的妇人。

===Original===
John Quincy shook Jennison’s hand. “You’re mighty lucky.”
“I think so,” Jennison answered.
The boy went over to Barbara, “All﻿—all good wishes,” he said. She nodded, but did not reply. He saw there were tears in her eyes.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西握住詹尼森的手。「你的福气可真不小。」
「我也这么想。」詹尼森答道。
小伙子走到芭芭拉跟前。「万——万事如意。」他说。她点了点头，却没有答话。他看见她眼里噙着泪。

===Original===
Presently Miss Minerva withdrew to the living-room, and John Quincy, feeling like a fifth wheel, made haste to leave the two together. He went out on the beach. The pale moon rode high amid the golden stars, romance whispered through the coconut palms. He thought of the scene he had witnessed that breathless night on the *President Tyler*﻿—only two in the world, love quick and overwhelming﻿—well, this was the setting for it. Here on this beach they had walked two and two since the beginning of time, whispering the same vows, making the same promises, whatever their color and creed. Suddenly the boy felt lonely.
Barbara was a Winterslip, and not for him. Why then did he feel again that frustrated pang in his heart? She had chosen and her choice was fitting, what affair was it of his?

===Chinese===
不多时，米纳瓦小姐退进起居室去了，约翰·昆西自觉像个多余的第五个轮子，赶紧把这两人留在一处。他踱到海滩上。苍白的月亮高悬在金色的群星之间，浪漫在椰林间低语。他想起那个闷热难当的夜晚在泰勒总统号上目睹的那一幕——世上只此两人，爱情来得迅疾而排山倒海——是啊，眼前正是那出戏的布景。自开天辟地以来，人们便在这片海滩上成双成对地漫步，呢喃着同样的誓言，许下同样的承诺，无论肤色信仰。忽然，小伙子觉得孤单起来。
芭芭拉是温特斯利普家的人，不是他的。那他心头为何又泛起那阵怅然的刺痛？她已经做出了选择，选择得也般配，又关他什么事呢？

===Original===
He found himself moving slowly toward the Reef and Palm Hotel. For a chat with Carlota Egan? But why should he want to talk with this girl, whose outlook was so different from that of the world he knew? The girls at home were on a level with the men in brains﻿—often, indeed, they were superior, seemed to be looking down from a great height. They discussed that article in the latest *Atlantic*, Shaw’s grim philosophy, the new Sargent at the Art Gallery. Wasn’t that the sort of talk he should be seeking here? Or was it? Under these palms on this romantic beach, with the moon riding high over Diamond Head?

===Chinese===
不知不觉，他已缓缓朝「礁石棕榈」旅馆走去。去找卡洛塔·伊根聊聊？可他为什么要同这个姑娘说话呢——她的眼界同他熟知的那个世界何其不同？家乡的姑娘们论头脑同男人们不相上下——实际上还常常高出一头，仿佛正从高处在俯视他们。她们谈论最新一期《大西洋月刊》上的那篇文章，萧伯纳冷峻的哲学，美术馆里新挂的那幅萨金特。他在这儿寻觅的，难道不该正是这一类谈吐吗？还是说不是？在这些棕榈树下，在这片浪漫的海滩上，在月亮高悬于钻石头山之上的夜色里？

===Original===
Carlota Egan was seated behind the desk in the deserted lobby of the Reef and Palm, a worried frown on her face.
“You’ve come at the psychological moment,” she cried, and smiled. “I’m having the most awful struggle.”
“Arithmetic?” John Quincy inquired.
“Compound fractions, it seems to me. I’m making out the Brades’ bill.”
He came round the desk and stood at her side. “Let me help you.”
“It’s so fearfully involved.” She looked up at him, and he wished they could do their sums on the beach. “Mr. Brade has been away since Tuesday morning, and we don’t charge for any absence of more than three days. So that comes out of it. Maybe you can figure it﻿—I can’t.”
“Charge him anyhow,” suggested John Quincy.
“I’d like to﻿—that would simplify everything. But it’s not dad’s way.”
John Quincy took up a pencil. “What rate are they paying?” he inquired. She told him, and he began to figure. It wasn’t a simple matter, even for a bond expert. John Quincy frowned too.

===Chinese===
卡洛塔·伊根坐在「礁石棕榈」空荡荡的门厅里账台后面，眉心蹙着一团愁云。
「你来得正是心理上的关键时刻。」她喊道，接着又笑了。「我正在进行一场顶顶可怕的搏斗。」
「算术？」约翰·昆西问。
「依我看是复式分数。我正在开布雷德夫妇的账单。」
他绕过账台，站到她身旁。「让我来帮你。」
「这笔账缠人得厉害。」她抬眼望着他，他倒希望能们是在海滩上做这道算术。「布雷德先生从星期二早上起就不在了，住客外出超过三天我们是不收费的。这笔就得从中扣掉。也许你算得出来——我是不成了。」
「照收不误嘛。」约翰·昆西出主意道。
「我倒想呢——那样什么都省事了。可那不是爸爸的做派。」
约翰·昆西拿起一支铅笔。「他们按什么价钱住？」他问。她告诉了他，他便算了起来。这可不是桩简单事，即便对一位债券专家来说也是如此。约翰·昆西也皱起了眉头。

===Original===
Someone entered the front door of the Reef and Palm. Looking up, John Quincy beheld the Hawaiian boy, Dick Kaohla. He carried a bulky object, wrapped in newspapers.
“Mr. Brade here now?” he asked.
Carlota Egan shook her head. “No, he hasn’t returned.”
“I will wait,” said the boy.
“But we don’t know where he is, or when he will come back,” the girl protested.
“He will be here soon,” the Hawaiian replied. “I wait on the lanai.” He went out the side door, still carrying his clumsy burden. John Quincy and the girl stared at each other.

===Chinese===
有人从「礁石棕榈」的正门进来。约翰·昆西抬眼一看，是那个夏威夷青年迪克·考赫拉。他抱着一件笨重的东西，用报纸裹着。
「布雷德先生这会儿在吗？」他问。
卡洛塔·伊根摇摇头。「不在，他还没回来。」
「我等。」那青年说。
「可我们不知道他在哪儿，也不知道他什么时候回来。」姑娘分辩道。
「他很快就会到这儿。」那夏威夷人答道。「我在露台上等。」他抱着那件累赘的行李，出了侧门。约翰·昆西同姑娘面面相觑。

===Original===
“ ‘We move, we advance!’ ” John Quincy quoted in a low voice. “Brade will be here soon! Would you mind going out on the lanai and telling me where Kaohla is now?”
Quickly the girl complied. She returned in a few seconds. “He’s taken a chair at the far end.”
“Out of earshot?”
“Quite. You want the telephone﻿—”
But John Quincy was already in the booth. Charlie Chan’s voice came back over the wire.

===Chinese===
「『我们在行动，我们在推进！』」约翰·昆西低声引道。「布雷德很快就会到这儿！劳驾你到露台上走一趟，告诉我考赫拉眼下待在哪儿，好不好？」
姑娘依言而去，几秒钟便回来了。「他在最那头寻了把椅子坐下。」
「隔得听不见说话？」
「完全听不见。你要用电话——」
可约翰·昆西已经进了电话亭。陈查理的声音顺着电线传了回来。

===Original===
“Most warm congratulations. You are number one detective yourself. Should my self-starter not indulge in stubborn spasm, I will make immediate connection with you.”
John Quincy returned to the desk, smiling. “Charlie’s flying to us in his Ford. Begins to look as though we were getting somewhere now. But about this bill. Mrs. Brade’s board and room I make sixteen dollars. The charge against Mr. Brade﻿—one week’s board and room minus four days’ board﻿—totals nine dollars and sixty-two cents.”
“How can I ever thank you?” said the girl.
“By telling me again about your childhood on this beach.” A shadow crossed her face. “Oh, I’m sorry I’ve made you unhappy.”
“Oh, no﻿—you couldn’t.” She shook her head. “I’ve never been﻿—so very happy. Always an ‘if’ in it, as I told you before. That morning on the ferry I think I was nearest to real happiness. I seemed to have escaped from life for a moment.”
“I remember how you laughed at my hat.”
“Oh﻿—I hope you’ve forgiven me.”
“Nonsense. I’m mighty glad I was able to make you laugh like that.” Her great eyes stared into the future, and John Quincy pitied her. He had known others like her, others who loved their fathers, built high hopes for them, then saw them drift into a baffled old age. One of the girl’s slender, tanned hands lay on the desk, John Quincy put his own upon it. “Don’t be unhappy,” he urged. “It’s such a wonderful night. The moon﻿—you’re a what-you-may-call-it﻿—a kamaaina, I know, but I’ll bet you never saw the moon looking so well before. It’s like a thousand-dollar gold piece, pale but negotiable. Shall we go out and spend it?”

===Chinese===
「最热忱的祝贺。您本人便是头一号侦探。倘若在下的自动起动机不沉溺于固执的痉挛，在下将即刻同您会合。」
约翰·昆西微笑着回到账台前。「查理正开着他那辆福特飞也似的赶来。看来咱们现在总算要有进展了。不过先说这笔账。布雷德太太的膳宿费，我算出来是十六块。布雷德先生的账——一星期膳宿费，减去四天伙食——共计九块六毛二。」
「叫我怎么谢你才好？」姑娘说。
「再给我讲讲你在这片海滩上的童年。」一片阴影掠过她的脸。「哦，对不起，我惹得你不快了。」
「哦，不——你不会的。」她摇摇头。「我从来不曾——那么快活过。这里面总有一个『可是』，这我从前就对你说过。渡船上的那个早晨，我想是我离真正的幸福最近的一回。那一会儿，我仿佛从生活里逃了出去。」
「我还记得你当时怎么笑话我的帽子。」
「哦——但愿你早已原谅我了。」
「哪里的话。能让你那样大笑一场，我高兴还来不及呢。」她那双大眼睛凝望着未来，约翰·昆西不由得怜惜起她来。他认识过同她一样的姑娘，一样爱着自己的父亲，为他们构筑起高远的指望，然后眼看着他们漂进一个郁郁不得志的晚年。姑娘一只晒得黝黑的纤手搁在账台上，约翰·昆西把自己的手覆了上去。「别不快活。」他劝道。「这么好的夜色。月亮——你是个那个什么来着——卡玛艾纳，这我知道，可我敢打赌，你从前从没见过月亮气色这么好。它就像一枚一千块的金币，虽发白，却花得出去。咱们出去把它花了，好不好？」

===Original===
Gently she drew her hand away. “There were seven bottles of charged water sent to the room. Thirty-five cents each﻿—”
“What? Oh, the Brades’ bill. Yes, that means two forty-five more. I’d like to mention the stars too. Isn’t it odd how close the stars seem in the tropics﻿—”
She smiled. “We mustn’t forget the trunks and bags. Three dollars for bringing them up from the dock.”
“Say﻿—that’s rather steep. Well, it goes down on the record. Have I ever told you that all this natural beauty out here has left its imprint on your face? In the midst of so much loveliness, one couldn’t be anything but﻿—”
“Mrs. Brade had three trays to the room. That’s seventy-five cents more.”
“Extravagant lady! Brade will be sorry he came back, for more reasons than one. Well, I’ve got that. Anything else?”
“Just the laundry. Ninety-seven cents.”
“Fair enough. Adding it all up, I get thirty-two dollars and sixty-nine cents. Let’s call it an even thirty-three.”
She laughed. “Oh, no. We can’t do that.”

===Chinese===
她轻轻把手抽了回去。「房间里送过七瓶汽水。每瓶三毛五——」
「什么？哦，布雷德家的账。是了，那又得多算两块四毛五。我还想顺带提一提星星。热带的星星显得这么近，你说怪不怪——」
她微微一笑。「咱们可不能忘了那几口箱子和提包。从码头搬上来，三块钱。」
「嘿——这价钱够狠的。好吧，照记不误。我从前可曾告诉过你，这边天地间这一切自然之美，都在你脸上留了印记？身处这许多可爱的事物中间，人不可能不——」
「布雷德太太往房间里叫过三次送餐。那又是七毛五。」
「好一位挥霍的太太！布雷德回来可有得懊悔了，懊悔的理由还不止一桩。好，这笔我记下了。还有别的吗？」
「只剩洗衣费了。九毛七。」
「公道。全加起来，一共三十二块六毛九。就算个整数三十三吧。」
她笑出声来。「哦，不。这可不行。」

===Original===
Mrs. Brade came slowly into the lobby from the lanai. She paused at the desk. “Has there been a message?” she inquired.
“No, Mrs. Brade,” the girl answered. She handed over the slip of paper. “Your bill.”
“Ah, yes. Mr. Brade will attend to this the moment he returns.”
“You expect him soon?”
“I really can’t say.” The Englishwoman moved on into the corridor leading to nineteen.
“Full of information, as usual,” smiled John Quincy. “Why, here’s Charlie now.”

===Chinese===
布雷德太太从露台那边慢慢走进门厅。她在账台前停了停。「有我的口信吗？」她问。
「没有，布雷德太太。」姑娘答道。她递过那张纸条。「您的账单。」
「啊，好的。布雷德先生一回来就料理这个。」
「您指望他快回来了？」
「我实在说不上。」这位英国妇人径自走进通往十九号房的走廊。
「消息灵通，一如既往。」约翰·昆西笑道。「哟，查理这不是来了。」

===Original===
Chan came briskly to the desk, followed by another policeman, also in plain clothes.
“Automobile act noble,” he announced, “having fondly feeling for night air.” He nodded toward his companion. “Introducing Mr. Spencer. Now, what are the situation? Humbly hinting you speak fast.”
John Quincy told him Kaohla was waiting on the lanai, and mentioned the unwieldy package carried by the boy. Chan nodded.
“Events are turning over rapidly,” he said. He addressed the girl. “Please kindly relate to this Kaohla that Brade has arrived and would wish to encounter him here.” She hesitated. “No, no,” added Chan hastily, “I forget nice heathen delicacy. It is not pretty I should ask a lady to scatter false lies from ruby lips. I humbly demand forgiveness. Content yourself with a veiled pretext bringing him here.”
The girl smiled and went out. “Mr. Spencer,” said Chan, “I make bold to suggest you interrogate this Hawaiian. My reckless wanderings among words of unlimitable English language often fail to penetrate sort of skulls plentiful round here.”
Spencer nodded and went to the side door, standing where he would not be seen by anyone entering there. In a moment Kaohla appeared, followed by the girl. The Hawaiian came in quickly but seeing Chan, stopped, and a frightened look crossed his face. Spencer startled him further by seizing his arm.

===Chinese===
陈查理快步走到账台前，身后跟着另一名警察，也一身便衣。
「汽车行为高贵，」他宣布道，「对夜气怀有深厚的感情。」他朝同伴点点头。「容在下引荐斯宾塞先生。敢问如今的形势是些什么？在下谦卑暗示：请讲快些。」
约翰·昆西告诉他考赫拉正在露台上等，又说起那青年抱着的那只笨重难携的包裹。陈查理点了点头。
「事件正在迅速翻身。」他说。他转向姑娘。「烦请向这位考赫拉转达：布雷德已经到达，愿在此地同他相会。」她迟疑了。「不，不，」陈查理赶忙补充，「在下忘了那美妙的异教徒式矜持。要让在下请求一位女士从红宝石般的朱唇间撒播虚假的谎言，实在不美。在下谦卑乞求宽恕。您只消用一个含而不露的托词，把他带到此处便成。」
姑娘微微一笑，出去了。「斯宾塞先生，」陈查理说，「在下斗胆建议，由您来盘问这位夏威夷人。在下在浩瀚无涯的英语字词之间鲁莽游荡，其所得常常穿不透本地盛产的这一类脑壳。」
斯宾塞点点头，走到侧门跟前，站在从侧门进来的人瞧不见的地方。片刻之后，考赫拉出现了，姑娘跟在后面。那夏威夷人快步进来，一看见陈查理却收住了脚，一道惊惶掠过他的脸。斯宾塞一把攥住他的手臂，更吓得他一跳。

===Original===
“Come over here,” said the detective. “We want to talk to you.” He led the boy to a far corner of the room, Chan and John Quincy followed. “Sit down﻿—here, I’ll take that.” He removed the heavy package from under the boy’s arm. For a moment the Hawaiian seemed about to protest, but evidently he thought better of it. Spencer placed the package on a table and stood over Kaohla.
“Want to see Brade, eh?” he began in a threatening tone.
“Yes.”
“What for?”
“Business is private.”
“Well, I’m telling you to come across. You’re in bad. Better change your mind and talk.”
“No.”
“All right. We’ll see about that. What have you got in that package?” The boy’s eyes went to the table, but he made no answer.

===Chinese===
「到这边来。」那侦探说。「我们想同你谈谈。」他把青年领到房间远处的角落里，陈查理和约翰·昆西跟在后面。「坐下——来，这个我来拿。」他从青年胳膊底下取下那只沉重的包裹。那夏威夷人有一瞬似乎想要抗议，但显然又改了主意。斯宾塞把包裹搁到一张桌子上，居高临下地立在考赫拉跟前。
「想见布雷德，嗯？」他威吓地开了腔。
「想。」
「为了什么？」
「私事。」
「好，我劝你老实交代。你的麻烦大了。最好回心转意，开口说话。」
「不。」
「好啊。咱们走着瞧。你那只包裹里装的是什么？」那青年的眼睛瞟向桌子，却不答话。

===Original===
Chan took out a pocket knife. “Simple matter to discover,” he said. He cut the rough twine, unwound several layers of newspapers. John Quincy pressed close, he felt that something important was about to be divulged.
The last layer of paper came off. “Hot dog!” cried Chan. He turned quickly to John Quincy. “Oh, I am so sorry﻿—I pick up atrocious phrase like that from my cousin Mr. Willie Chan, Captain of All Chinese baseball team﻿—”
But John Quincy did not hear, his eyes were glued to the object that lay on the table. An ohia wood box, bound with copper﻿—the initials T.M.B.
“We will unlatch it,” said Chan. He made an examination. “No, locked most strongly. We will crash into it at police station, where you and I and this silent Hawaiian will now hasten. Mr. Spencer, you will remain on spot here. Should Brade appear, you know your duty.”
“I do,” said Spencer.
“Mr. Kaohla, do me the honor to accompany,” continued Chan. “At police headquarters much talk will be extracted out of you.”

===Chinese===
陈查理掏出一把折刀。「查明此事乃举手之劳。」他说。他割断粗麻绳，解开一层层报纸。约翰·昆西紧紧凑上前去，他感到一样要紧的东西即将水落石出。
最后一层纸剥掉了。「好家伙（Hot dog）！」陈查理喊道。他急忙转向约翰·昆西。「哦，万分抱歉——这般恶劣的词句，在下是从表弟威利·陈先生那儿学来的，他是全华人棒球队的队长——」
可约翰·昆西什么也没听见，他的两眼死死粘在躺在桌上的那样东西上。一只奥希亚木盒，包着铜箍——上面有首字母 T.M.B.
「我们将把它开启。」陈查理说。他检查了一番。「不，锁得万分牢固。我们将到警察局里砸开它，您、在下与这位缄默的夏威夷人即刻火速前往。斯宾塞先生，您原地留守此地。布雷德若现身，您自知职责所在。」
「我知道。」斯宾塞说。
「考赫拉先生，请赏光随行。」陈查理接着说。「到了警察总局，自会从您身上榨出许多话来。」

===Original===
They turned toward the door. As they did so, Carlota Egan came up. “May I speak to you a moment?” she said to John Quincy.
“Surely.” He walked with her to the desk.
“I went to the lanai just now,” she whispered breathlessly. “Someone was crouching outside the window near where you were talking. I went closer and it was﻿—Mr. Saladine!”
“Aha,” said John Quincy. “Mr. Saladine had better drop that sort of thing, or he’ll get himself in trouble.”
“Should we tell Chan?”
“Not yet. You and I will do a little investigating ourselves first. Chan has other things to think about. And we don’t want any of our guests to leave unless it’s absolutely necessary.”
“We certainly don’t,” she smiled. “I’m glad you’ve got the interests of the house at heart.”

===Chinese===
他们转身朝门口走去。正在此时，卡洛塔·伊根走上前来。「能同你说句话吗？」她对约翰·昆西说。
「当然可以。」他同她走到账台前。
「我方才到露台上去来着。」她气都不匀地低声道。「有人蹲在你们说话那地方近旁的窗外。我走近一看，竟是——萨拉迪尼先生！」
「啊哈。」约翰·昆西说。「萨拉迪尼先生最好趁早收了这一套，不然他要惹祸上身了。」
「要不要告诉陈查理？」
「先不。你我先自己来做点儿侦查。陈查理还有别的事要操心。再说，不到万不得已，咱们也不想放走任何一位客人。」
「当然不想。」她笑了。「你把这家旅馆的利益放在心上，我很高兴。」

===Original===
“That’s just where I’ve got them﻿—” John Quincy began, but Chan cut in.
“Humbly begging pardon,” he said, “we must speed. Captain Hallet will have high delight to encounter this Kaohla, to say nothing of ohia wood box.”
In the doorway, Kaohla crowded close to John Quincy, and the latter was startled by the look of hate he saw in the boy’s stormy eyes. “You did this,” muttered the Hawaiian. “I don’t forget.”

===Chinese===
「我放的地方正是那儿——」约翰·昆西刚开了个头，陈查理插了进来。
「谦卑乞恕，」他说，「我等必须火速行动。哈利特警长遇见这位考赫拉将大喜过望，更不必说那只奥希亚木盒了。」
门洞里，考赫拉紧挨到约翰·昆西身边，后者被这青年翻腾着风暴的眼睛里那股仇恨的光吓了一跳。「这是你干的。」那夏威夷人咕哝道。「我不会忘记。」

## XV The Man from India / 十五 来自印度的人

===Original===
They clattered along Kalakaua Avenue in Chan's car.

John Quincy sat alone on the rear seat; at the detective's request he held the ohia wood box on his knees.

He rested his hands upon it. Once it had eluded him, but he had it now. His mind went back to that night in the attic two thousand miles away, the shadow against the moonlit window, the sting of a jewel cutting across his cheek. Roger's heartfelt cry of "Poor old Dan!" Did they hold at last, in this ohia wood box, the answer to the mystery of Dan's death?

===Chinese===
他们坐着陈查理的车，轰隆隆地驶过卡拉卡瓦大道。

约翰·昆西独自坐在后座上；应侦探的请求，他把那只奥希亚木盒抱在膝头。

他把双手搭在盒上。它曾一度从他手中溜走，如今总算攥在了他手里。他的思绪回到两千英里外阁楼上的那一夜：月光映照的窗前那道黑影，一件珠宝划过脸颊的刺痛，罗杰发自心底的一声呼喊「可怜的老丹！」这只奥希亚木盒里，他们终于握住了丹之死谜团的答案吗？

===Original===
Hallet was waiting in his room. With him was a keen-eyed, efficient looking man evidently in his late thirties.

"Hello, boys," said the captain. "Mr. Winterslip, meet Mr. Greene, our district court prosecutor."

Greene shook hands cordially. "I've been wanting to meet you, sir," he said. "I know your city rather well. Spent three years at your Harvard Law School."

"Really?" replied John Quincy with enthusiasm.

"Yes. I went there after I got through at New Haven. I'm a Yale man, you know."

"Oh," remarked John Quincy, without any enthusiasm at all. But Greene seemed a pleasant fellow, despite his choice of college.

===Chinese===
哈利特正在自己的房间里等着。同他在一起的，是个目光锐利、干练模样的人，显见三十八九岁年纪。

「你们好啊，小伙子们。」警长说。「温特斯利普先生，来见见格林先生，我们的地方法院检察官。」

格林热情地握手。「我一直想见见您，先生。」他说。「您那座城我相当熟。在你们的哈佛法学院念过三年书。」

「当真？」约翰·昆西热情地答道。

「是啊。我从纽黑文毕业之后就去了那儿。要知道，我可是耶鲁人。」

「哦。」约翰·昆西应了一声，热情荡然无存。不过格林看上去倒是个和气的人，尽管他挑了那么所大学。

===Original===
Chan had set the box on the table before Hallet, and was explaining how they had come upon it. The captain's thin face had brightened perceptibly. He inspected the treasure. "Locked, eh?" he remarked. "You got the key, Kaohla?"

The Hawaiian shook his head sullenly. "No."

"Watch your step, boy," warned Hallet. "Go over him, Charlie."

Chan went over him, rapidly and thoroughly. He found a key ring, but none of the keys fitted the lock on the box. He also brought to light a fat roll of bills.

"Where'd you get all that money, Dick?" Hallet inquired.

"I got it," glowered the boy.

===Chinese===
陈查理已把木盒放到哈利特面前的桌上，正说明他们如何寻到了它。警长那张瘦脸明显地亮了起来。他把这件宝贝验看了一番。「锁着呢，嗯？」他说。「钥匙在你手里吧，考赫拉？」

那夏威夷青年阴沉着脸摇头。「没有。」

「放明白点儿，小子。」哈利特警告道。「搜搜他，查理。」

陈查理把他搜了一遍，动作麻利，丝毫不漏。搜出一串钥匙，但没有一把对得上盒子上的锁。他还搜出了一厚沓钞票。

「这么些钱是哪儿来的，迪克？」哈利特问道。

「弄来的。」小伙子怒目而视。

===Original===
But Hallet was more interested in the box. He tapped it lovingly. "This is important, Mr. Greene. We may find the solution of our puzzle in here." He took a small chisel from his desk, and after a brief struggle, pried open the lid.

John Quincy, Chan and the prosecutor pressed close, their eyes staring eagerly as the captain lifted the lid. The box was empty.

"Filled with nothing," murmured Chan. "Another dream go smash against stone wall."

The disappointment angered Hallet. He turned on Kaohla. "Now, my lad," he said. "I want to hear from you. You've been in touch with Brade, you talked with him last Sunday night, you've heard he's returning tonight. You've got some deal on with him. Come across and be quick about it."

"Nothing to tell," said the Hawaiian stubbornly.

Hallet leaped to his feet. "Oh, yes you have. And by heaven, you're going to tell it. I'm not any too patient tonight and I warn you if you don't talk and talk quick I'm likely to get rough." He stopped suddenly and turned to Chan. "Charlie, that Inter-Island boat is due from Maui about now. Get down to the dock and watch for Brade. You've got his description?"

===Chinese===
但哈利特更上心的是那只盒子。他爱惜地敲了敲它。「这可是要紧东西，格林先生。咱们那谜团的谜底，兴许就装在这里面。」他从桌上取过一把小凿子，略一较劲，撬开了盒盖。

约翰·昆西、陈查理和检察官凑上前去，瞪大了眼睛，热切地望着警长掀起盒盖。盒子是空的。

「满装着虚无。」陈查理喃喃道。「又一场梦撞碎在石墙之上。」

这失落惹恼了哈利特。他转身冲着考赫拉。「好了，小子。」他说。「我要听你交代。你一直同布雷德有联络，上星期天夜里还同他谈过话，又听说他今夜回来。你跟他有桩交易。乖乖招来，麻利点儿。」

「没什么可交代的。」那夏威夷青年死硬地说。

哈利特一跃而起。「哼，你有的是可交代的。老天作证，你非说不可。今儿夜里我的耐性可不怎么多，我警告你，你要是不赶紧招，我可能就要不客气了。」他猛地打住，转向陈查理。「查理，那艘岛际航船这会儿差不多该从毛伊岛到了。赶到码头上去盯着布雷德。他的模样特征你有吧？」

===Original===
"Sure," answered Chan. "Thin pale face, one shoulder descended below other, gray mustaches that droop in saddened mood."

"That's right. Keep a sharp lookout. And leave this lad to us. He won't have any secrets when we get through with him, eh, Mr. Greene?"

The prosecutor, more discreet, merely smiled.

"Mr. Winterslip," said Chan. "The night is delicious. A little stroll to moonly dock—"

"I'm with you," John Quincy replied. He looked back over his shoulder as he went, and reflected that he wouldn't care to be in Kaohla's shoes.

===Chinese===
「没错。」陈查理答道。「清瘦苍白的面孔，一边肩膀塌得比另一边低，灰白的胡子在悲戚的心绪中下垂。」

「就是这副模样。睁大眼睛盯着。这小子交给我们。等我们收拾完他，他就什么秘密都剩不下了，呃，格林先生？」

检察官比较谨慎，只是微微一笑。

「温特斯利普先生，」陈查理说，「夜色可人。往月光朗照的码头小小散步一回——」

「我跟您去。」约翰·昆西答道。走时他回头望了一眼，心想自己可千万不愿落到考赫拉那样的处境里去。

===Original===
The pier-shed was dimly lighted and a small but diversified group awaited the incoming boat. Chan and John Quincy walked to the far end and there, seated on a packing-case, they found the waterfront reporter of the evening paper.

"Hello, Charlie," cried Mr. Mayberry. "What you doing here?"

"Maybe friend arrive on boat," grinned Chan.

"Is that so?" responded Mayberry. "You boys over at the station have certainly become pretty mysterious all of a sudden. What's doing, Charlie?"

"All pronouncements come from captain," advised Chan.

"Yeah, we've heard his pronouncements," sneered Mayberry. "The police have unearthed clues and are working on them. Nothing to report at present. It's sickening. Well, sit down, Charlie. Oh—Mr. Winterslip—good evening. I didn't recognize you at first."

"How are you," said John Quincy. He and Chan also found packing-cases. There was a penetrating odor of sugar in the air. Through a wide opening in the pier-shed they gazed along the waterfront and out upon the moonlit harbor. A rather exotic and intriguing scene, John Quincy reflected, and he said as much.

===Chinese===
码头棚屋里灯光昏暗，一小群成分驳杂的人正等候着进港的班船。陈查理和约翰·昆西走到最里头，在那儿发现晚报的滨水区记者正坐在一只货箱上。

「你好啊，查理。」梅伯里先生嚷道。「你上这儿来干什么？」

「兴许有朋友搭船到港。」陈查理咧嘴一笑。

「是吗？」梅伯里应道。「你们警察局那帮小伙子，最近可真是突然神秘起来了。有什么动静，查理？」

「一切声明皆出自长官之口。」陈查理进言道。

「可不是，他的声明我们听过了。」梅伯里讥诮道。「警方已发掘出若干线索，正全力追查。目前尚无可奉告。真叫人作呕。坐吧，查理。哦——温特斯利普先生——晚上好。方才没认出您来。」

「你好。」约翰·昆西说。他和陈查理也寻到两只货箱坐下。空气里透着一股钻鼻子的糖味。透过码头棚屋的一道宽大豁口，他们顺着滨水区望出去，一直望到月光朗照的海港。一幅颇有异域情调、引人入胜的图景，约翰·昆西心想，他也这么说了出来。

===Original===
"Think so?" answered Mayberry. "Well, I don't. To me it's just like Seattle or Galveston or any of those stereotyped ports. But you see—I knew it when—"

"I think you mentioned that before," John Quincy smiled.

"I'm likely to mention it at any moment. As far as I'm concerned, the harbor of Honolulu has lost its romance. Once this was the most picturesque waterfront in the world, my boy. And now look at the damned thing!" The reporter relighted his pipe. "Charlie can tell you—he remembers. The old ramshackle, low-lying wharves. Old Naval Row with its sailing ships. The wooden-hulled steamers with a mast or two—not too proud to use God's good winds occasionally. The bright little rowboats, the *Aloha*, the *Manu*, the *Emma*. Eh, Chan?"

"All extinct," agreed Chan.

===Chinese===
「这么觉得吗？」梅伯里答道。「哼，我可不。依我看，它跟西雅图、加尔维斯顿，跟那些千篇一律的港口没什么两样。可你要明白——想当年我就识得它——」

「我想这话您先前已经说过了。」约翰·昆西莞尔。

「我随时都打算再提它。就我而言，檀香山这座海港的浪漫已经完了。想当年，这儿是全世界最富画意的滨水区，我的小伙子。如今你瞧瞧这该死的东西吧！」记者重新点上烟斗。「查理能告诉你——他还记得。那些摇摇欲坠的老式矮码头。泊着一列列帆船的老海军船列。竖着一两根桅杆的木壳轮船——并不傲慢到不肯偶尔借一借上帝的好风。还有那些鲜亮的小划艇，『阿罗哈号』、『马努号』、『埃玛号』。呃，查理？」

「尽已绝迹。」陈查理深表同意。

===Original===
"You wouldn't see a Rotary Club gang like this on a pier in those days," Mayberry continued. "Just Hawaiian stevedores with leis on their hats and ukuleles in their hands. Fishermen with their nets, and maybe a breezy old-time purser—a glad-hander and not a mere machine." He puffed a moment in sad silence. "Those were the days, Mr. Winterslip, the days of Hawaii's isolation, and her charm. The cable and the radio hadn't linked us up with the so-called civilization of the mainland. Every boat that came in we'd scamper over it, hunting a newspaper with the very latest news of the outside world. Remember those steamer days, Charlie, when everybody went down to the wharf in the good old hacks of yesteryear, when the women wore holokus and lauhala hats, and Berger was there with his band, and maybe a prince or two—"

"And the nights," suggested Charlie.

"Yeah, old-timer, I was coming to the nights. The soft nights when the serenaders drifted about the harbor in rowboats, and the lanterns speared long paths on the water—"

He seemed about to weep. John Quincy's mind went back to books he had read in his boyhood.

"And occasionally," he said, "I presume somebody went aboard a ship against his will?"

"I'll say he did," replied Mr. Mayberry, brightening at the thought. "Why, it was only in the 'nineties I was sitting one night on a dock a few yards down, when I saw a scuffle near the landing, and one of my best friends shouted to me: 'Goodbye, Pete!' I was up and off in a minute, and I got him away from them—I was younger in those days. He was a good fellow, a sailorman, and he wasn't intending to take the journey that bunch had planned for him. They'd got him into a saloon and drugged him, but he pulled out of it just in time—oh, well, those days are gone forever now. Just like Galveston or Seattle. Yes, sir, this harbor of Honolulu has lost its romance."

===Chinese===
「那种年月，你可看不见扶轮社这号人乌泱泱挤在码头上。」梅伯里接着说。「只有帽子上挂着花环（leis）、手里拿着尤克里里琴的夏威夷码头工人。张着渔网的渔夫，兴许再来一位快人快语的老式事务长——一个热络应酬的活人，而不是一具纯粹的机器。」他伤感地默默吸了片刻烟斗。「那才是好日子啊，温特斯利普先生，夏威夷与世隔绝的日子，她自有魅力的日子。海底电缆和无线电还没把我们跟大陆那所谓的文明连到一块儿。每有船进港，我们都要蹿上船去，搜罗一份载有外界最新消息的报纸。还记得那些轮船日吗，查理？人人都坐着往年那种老式出租马车赶到码头来，女人们穿着荷洛库长袍（holoku），戴着劳哈拉草帽（lauhala），伯格带着他的乐队到场，兴许还有一两位王孙——」

「还有那些夜晚。」查理提了一句。

「对，老伙计，我正要说那些夜晚呢。温柔的夜晚，唱小夜曲的人们划着小船在港中漂荡，灯笼在水面投下一道道长长的光路——」

他眼看就要落泪了。约翰·昆西的思绪回到少年时代读过的那些书里。

「而且我猜想，」他说，「偶尔总有人是不情不愿地被弄上船去的吧？」

「可不是嘛。」梅伯里先生答道，想到这里眼睛亮了。「嗐，就在十九世纪九十年代，有天夜里我坐在下游几码外的一座码头上，看见登岸处附近一阵扭打，我顶要好的一个朋友冲我大喊：『再见，皮特！』我一骨碌爬起来就冲了过去，把他从那帮人手里夺了下来——那些年我还年轻。他是个好人，一个水手，压根不打算去走那帮人替他盘算好的航程。他们把他骗进酒馆灌了药，幸亏他及时挣脱了出来——唉，罢了，那样的日子如今是一去不返了。跟加尔维斯顿、西雅图一个样。是啊先生，檀香山这座海港的浪漫算是完了。」

===Original===
The little Inter-Island boat was drawing up to the pier, and they watched it come. As the gangplank went down, Chan rose.

"Who you expecting, Charlie?" asked Mayberry.

"We grope about," said Chan. "Maybe on this boat are Mr. Brade."

"Brade!" Mayberry leaped to his feet.

"Not so sure," warned Chan. "Only a matter we suppose. If correct, humbly suggest you follow to the station. You might capture news."

===Chinese===
那艘小小的岛际航船正靠向码头，他们望着它驶来。跳板一放下来，陈查理便起身了。

「你等谁呢，查理？」梅伯里问。

「我们且摸索着。」陈查理说。「兴许布雷德先生就在这条船上。」

「布雷德！」梅伯里一跃而起。

「尚未笃定。」陈查理提醒道。「不过是我们的一桩揣测。倘若属实，谦卑地建议您随我们前往警察局。您或许能捕获新闻。」

===Original===
John Quincy and Chan moved up to the gangplank as the passengers descended. There were not many aboard. A few Island business men, a scattering of tourists, a party of Japanese in western clothes, ceremoniously received by friends ashore—a quaint little group all bowing from the waist. John Quincy was watching them with interest when Chan touched his arm.

A tall stooped Englishman was coming down the plank. Thomas Macan Brade would have been easily spotted in any crowd. His mustache was patterned after that of the Earl of Pawtucket, and to make identification even simpler, he wore a white pith helmet. Pith helmets are not necessary under the kindly skies of Hawaii; this was evidently a relic of Indian days.

Chan stepped forward. "Mr. Brade?"

The man had a tired look in his eyes. He started nervously. "Y—yes," he hesitated.

===Chinese===
乘客们下船时，约翰·昆西和陈查理挪到跳板跟前。船上人不多。几位本岛商人，零零落落的几名游客，还有一群西装打扮的日本人，被岸上的朋友们郑重其事地迎接着——一小群古雅的人，个个从腰间躬身行礼。约翰·昆西正看得有趣，陈查理碰了碰他的手臂。

一个高个儿、佝偻着背的英国人正走下跳板。托马斯·马坎·布雷德这个人，搁在哪个人堆里都容易认出来。他那两撇胡子是照着波塔基特伯爵的样式修剪的，为了让人认起来更加省事，他还戴着一顶白色木髓盔。在夏威夷和煦的天空下，木髓盔全无必要；这显然是他印度岁月的一件遗物。

陈查理上前一步。「布雷德先生？」

这人眼里带着倦意。他紧张地一惊。「是——是的。」他迟疑道。

===Original===
"I am Detective-Sergeant Chan, Honolulu police. You will do me the great honor to accompany me to the station, if you please."

Brade stared at him, then shook his head. "It's quite impossible," he said.

"Pardon me, please," answered Chan. "It are unevitable."

"I—I have just returned from a journey," protested the man. "My wife may be worried regarding me. I must have a talk with her, and after that—"

"Regret," purred Chan, "are scorching me. But duty remains duty. Chief's words are law. Humbly suggest we squander valuable time."

===Chinese===
「本人是檀香山警察局侦探警士陈。请您赏个脸面，随我到警察局走一遭。」

布雷德瞪着他，随即摇了摇头。「这万万办不到。」他说。

「恕我冒昧，」陈查理答道，「这可是免不了的一桩。」

「我——我刚旅行回来。」那人申辩道。「我太太也许正为我不安。我必须先同她谈一谈，然后——」

「歉疚，」陈查理柔声道，「正灼烤着我。但职责终归是职责。长官之言即是律法。谦卑地建议：咱们切莫再挥霍宝贵光阴了。」

===Original===
"Am I to understand that I'm under arrest?" flared Brade.

"The idea is preposterous," Chan assured him. "But the captain waits eager for statement from you. You will walk this way, I am sure. A moment's pardon. I introduce my fine friend, Mr. John Quincy Winterslip, of Boston."

At mention of the name, Brade turned and regarded John Quincy with deep interest. "Very good," he said. "I'll go with you."

They went out to the street, Brade carrying a small handbag. The flurry of arrival was dying fast. Honolulu would shortly return to its accustomed evening calm.

===Chinese===
「我是否应当理解为，我被捕了？」布雷德勃然作色。

「这想法未免荒谬绝伦。」陈查理宽慰他道。「但长官正热切等候您的陈述。您会往这边走的，我深信不疑。失敬片刻。容我介绍我美好的朋友，波士顿的约翰·昆西·温特斯利普先生。」

一听这名字，布雷德转过身来，怀着深深的兴味打量约翰·昆西。「很好。」他说。「我跟你们去。」

他们走到街上，布雷德提着一只小手提包。到港的一阵忙乱正迅速平息。檀香山不久就要回到惯常的夜晚宁静里去。

===Original===
When they reached the police station, Hallet and the prosecutor seemed in high good humor. Kaohla sat in a corner, hopeless and defeated; John Quincy saw at a glance that the boy's secret was his no longer.

"Introducing Mr. Brade," said Chan.

"Ah," cried Hallet, "we're glad to see you, Mr. Brade. We'd been getting pretty worried about you."

"Really, sir," said Brade, "I am completely at a loss—"

"Sit down," ordered Hallet. The man sank into a chair. He too had a hopeless, defeated air. No one can appear more humble and beaten than a British civil servant, and this man had known thirty-six years of baking under the Indian sun, looked down on by the military, respected by none. Not only his mustache but his whole figure drooped "in saddened mood." Yet now and then, John Quincy noted, he flashed into life, a moment of self-assertion and defiance.

===Chinese===
到警察局时，哈利特和检察官似乎兴致极高。考赫拉坐在一个角落里，绝望而溃败；约翰·昆西一眼便看出，这小子的秘密已不再归他所有了。

「容我引见，布雷德先生。」陈查理说。

「啊，」哈利特喊道，「见到您真高兴，布雷德先生。我们一直为您担着老大一份心呢。」

「说真的，先生，」布雷德说，「我完全摸不着头脑——」
