「坐下。」哈利特吩咐道。那人陷进椅子里。他也带着一股绝望、溃败的神气。再没有谁比一位英国文职官员显得更卑微、更败落的了，而这个人足足三十六年在印度的烈日下烘烤，受军方的白眼，无一人敬重。不仅他那两撇胡子，连他整个人的身形都「在悲戚的心绪中」耷拉着。然而时不时地，约翰·昆西注意到，他会倏地亮出生气来，露出片刻的自恃与倔强。

===Original===
"Where have you been, Mr. Brade?" Hallet inquired.

"I have visited one of the other islands. Maui."

"You went last Tuesday morning?"

"Yes. On the same steamer that brought me back."

"Your name was not on the sailing list," Hallet said.

"No. I went under another name. I had—reasons."

"Indeed?"

===Chinese===
「您上哪儿去了，布雷德先生？」哈利特问道。

「我去了一座别的岛。毛伊岛。」

「上星期二早晨走的？」

「是的。坐的就是把我送回来的这艘班轮。」

「离港乘客名单上没有您的名字。」哈利特说。

「没有。我用的是另一个名字。我有——缘故。」

「是吗？」

===Original===
The flash of life. "Just why am I here, sir?" He turned to the prosecutor. "Perhaps you will tell me that?"

Greene nodded toward the detective. "Captain Hallet will enlighten you," he said.

"You bet I will," Hallet announced. "As perhaps you know, Mr. Brade, Mr. Dan Winterslip has been murdered."

Brade's washed-out eyes turned to John Quincy. "Yes," he said. "I read about it in a Hilo newspaper."

"You didn't know it when you left last Tuesday morning?" Hallet asked.

"I did not. I sailed without seeing a paper here."

===Chinese===
那倏然亮起的一瞬。「我到底为什么到这儿来，先生？」他转向检察官。「也许您肯告诉我？」

格林朝侦探那边点点头。「哈利特警长会给您开窍的。」他说。

「那还用说。」哈利特宣布道。「布雷德先生，您或许已经听闻，丹·温特斯利普先生被人谋杀了。」

布雷德那双洗褪了色似的眼睛转向约翰·昆西。「是的。」他说。「我在一份希洛的报纸上读到了。」

「上星期二早晨动身时，您还不知道这事？」哈利特问。

「不知道。我开船前没见到这儿的报纸。」

===Original===
"Ah, yes. When did you see Mr. Dan Winterslip last?"

"I never saw him."

"What! Be careful, sir."

"I never saw Dan Winterslip in my life."

"All right. Where were you last Tuesday morning at twenty minutes past one?"

"I was asleep in my room at the Reef and Palm Hotel. I'd retired at nine-thirty, as I had to rise early in order to board my boat. My wife can verify that."

"A wife's testimony, Mr. Brade, is not of great value—"

===Chinese===
「啊，是这样。您最后一次见丹·温特斯利普先生是什么时候？」

「我从来没见过他。」

「什么！说话小心点，先生。」

「我平生从未见过丹·温特斯利普。」

「好吧。上星期二凌晨一点二十分，您在什么地方？」

「我在『礁石棕榈』旅馆自己的房间里睡觉。我九点半就歇了，因为次日得早起赶船。我太太可以证实这一点。」

「妻子的证词嘛，布雷德先生，价值不大——」

===Original===
Brade leaped to his feet. "Look here, sir! Do you mean to insinuate—"

"Take it easy," said Hallet smoothly. "I have a few matters to call to your attention, Mr. Brade. Mr. Dan Winterslip was murdered at one-twenty or thereabouts last Tuesday morning. We happen to know that in his youth he served as first officer aboard the *Maid of Shiloh*, a blackbirder. The master of that vessel had the same name as yourself. An investigation of your room at the Reef and Palm—"

"How dare you!" cried Brade. "By what right—"

"I am hunting the murderer of Dan Winterslip," broke in Hallet coolly. "And I follow the trail wherever it leads. In your room I found a letter from the British Consul here addressed to you, and informing you that Winterslip was alive and in Honolulu. I also found this tin of Corsican cigarettes. Just outside the living-room door of Winterslip's house, we picked up the stub of a Corsican cigarette. It's a brand not on sale in Honolulu."

Brade had dropped back into his chair, and was staring in a dazed way at the tin box in Hallet's hand. Hallet indicated the Hawaiian boy in the corner. "Ever see this lad before, Mr. Brade?" Brade nodded.

===Chinese===
布雷德霍地跳起来。「你听着，先生！你是想含沙射影——」

「消消气。」哈利特不紧不慢地说。「有几件事要提请您留意，布雷德先生。丹·温特斯利普先生是在上星期二凌晨一点二十分前后遇害的。我们恰好知道，他青年时代曾在一艘贩奴船希洛少女号上任大副。那条船的船长与您同名同姓。我们对您在『礁石棕榈』的房间作了一番查检——」

「你好大的胆子！」布雷德嚷道。「你凭什么——」

「我在缉拿谋杀丹·温特斯利普的凶手。」哈利特冷冷地插进来。「线索引到哪里，我就追到哪里。我在您房间里发现一封本地英国领事写给您的信，通知您温特斯利普还活着，就在檀香山。我还发现了这听科西嘉牌香烟。就在温特斯利普家客厅门外，我们拾到一个科西嘉牌烟蒂。这种牌子檀香山没有发售。」

布雷德已瘫回椅子里，恍恍惚惚地盯着哈利特手里那听铁盒香烟。哈利特朝角落里的夏威夷小伙子指了指。「从前见过这小子吗，布雷德先生？」布雷德点了点头。

===Original===
"You had a talk with him last Sunday night on the beach?"

"Yes."

"The boy's told us all about it. He read in the paper that you were coming to Honolulu. His father was a confidential servant in Dan Winterslip's employ and he himself was brought up in the Winterslip household. He could make a pretty good guess at your business with Winterslip, and he figured you'd be pleased to lay hands on this ohia wood box. In his boyhood he'd seen it in a trunk in the attic of Winterslip's San Francisco house. He went down to the *President Tyler* and arranged with a friend aboard that boat, the quartermaster, to break into the house and steal the box. When he saw you last Sunday night he told you he'd have the box as soon as the *President Tyler* got in, and he arranged to sell it to you for a good sum. Am I right so far, Mr. Brade?"

"You are quite right," said Brade.

"The initials on the box are T.M.B." Hallet persisted. "They are your initials, are they not?"

"They happen to be," said Brade. "But they were also the initials of my father. My father died aboard ship in the South Seas many years ago, and that box was stolen from his cabin after his death. It was stolen by the first officer of the *Maid of Shiloh*—by Mr. Dan Winterslip."

===Chinese===
「上星期天夜里，您在沙滩上同他谈过话？」

「谈过。」

「这小子全招了。他在报上读到您要来檀香山。他父亲是丹·温特斯利普雇用的心腹仆役，他本人就是在温特斯利普家长大的。您找温特斯利普有什么事，他大抵猜得出个七八分，他断定您若能把这只奥希亚木盒弄到手，必定喜出望外。他小时候在温特斯利普旧金山宅子的阁楼上一口衣箱里见过它。他找到泰勒总统号，同船上的一位朋友——那舵手——商量好，闯进宅子把盒子偷出来。上星期天夜里见您时，他说泰勒总统号一进港，盒子就到手，并谈妥以一笔可观的钱卖给您。到目前为止我说得对不对，布雷德先生？」

「你说得完全对。」布雷德说。

「盒子上的姓名首字母是T.M.B.。」哈利特追问道。「那正是您的首字母，不是吗？」

「碰巧是。」布雷德说。「但那也是我父亲的首字母。我父亲多年前死在南太平洋的船上，他死后，那只盒子被人从他舱里偷走了。偷它的是希洛少女号的大副——丹·温特斯利普先生。」

===Original===
For a moment no one spoke. A cold shiver ran down the spine of John Quincy Winterslip and a hot flush suffused his cheek. Why, oh, why, had he strayed so far from home? In Boston he traveled in a rut, perhaps, but ruts were safe, secure. There no one had ever brought a charge such as this against a Winterslip, no whisper of scandal had ever sullied the name. But here Winterslips had run amuck, and there was no telling what would next be dragged into the light.

===Chinese===
一时间没有人说话。一线寒颤顺着约翰·昆西·温特斯利普的脊梁滑下去，一股热辣辣的红晕漫上他的脸颊。为什么，噢，为什么，他要离家闯得这么远？在波士顿，他日子过得像车辙一样刻板，也许吧，可车辙是安稳的、牢靠的。在那儿，从没有人对温特斯利普家提出过这样的指控，从没有一丝流言蜚语玷污过这个姓氏。可是在这里，温特斯利普家的人撒开了野，谁也说不准下一件被拖到光天化日之下的又会是什么。

===Original===
"I think, Mr. Brade," said the prosecutor slowly, "you had better make a full statement."

===Chinese===
「我想，布雷德先生，」检察官慢条斯理地说，「您最好把事情原原本本讲个清楚。」

===Original===
Brade nodded. "I intend to do so. My case against Winterslip is not complete and I should have preferred to remain silent for a time. But under the circumstances, of course I must speak out. I'll smoke, if you don't mind." He took a cigarette from his case and lighted it. "I'm a bit puzzled just how to begin. My father disappeared from England in the 'seventies, leaving my mother and me to shift for ourselves. For a time we heard nothing of him, then letters began to arrive from various points in Australia and the South Seas. Letters with money in them, money we badly needed. I have since learned that he had gone into the blackbirding trade; it is nothing to be proud of, God knows, but I like to recall in his favor that he did not entirely abandon his wife and boy."

===Chinese===
布雷德点点头。「我正打算如此。我控告温特斯利普的案子还没有备齐，按说我宁愿先保持一段沉默。但事到如今，自然必须说出来了。你们不介意我抽支烟吧。」他从烟盒里取出一支烟，点上。「从哪儿说起，我有点犯难。我父亲十九世纪七十年代从英国失踪，撇下我母亲和我自谋生路。有一阵子我们全无他的音讯，后来信开始从澳大利亚和南太平洋的各个地方寄来。信里夹着钱，我们急需的钱。我后来才知道，他干上了贩奴那行当；天知道，这没什么可夸耀的，不过我愿意念他一点好：他没有完全抛弃妻子和孩子。」

===Original===
"In the 'eighties we got word of his death. He died aboard the *Maid of Shiloh* and was buried on the island of Apiang in the Gilbert Group—buried by Dan Winterslip, his first officer. We accepted the fact of his death, the fact of no more letters with remittances, and took up our struggle again. Six months later we received, from a friend of my father in Sydney, a brother captain, a most amazing letter."

===Chinese===
「八十年代，我们得到他的死讯。他死在希洛少女号上，葬在吉尔伯特群岛的阿皮昂岛——下葬的正是他的大副丹·温特斯利普。我们接受了这个死讯，接受了再没有汇款来信的事实，重新拾起我们的挣扎。半年之后，我们收到我父亲在悉尼的一位朋友——一位同行船长——写来的一封信，一封令人瞠目的信。」

===Original===
"This letter said that, to the writer's certain knowledge, my father had carried a great deal of money in his cabin on the *Maid of Shiloh*. He had done no business with banks, instead he had had this strong box made of ohia wood. The man who wrote us said that he had seen the inside of it, and that it contained jewelry and a large quantity of gold. My father had also shown him several bags of green hide, containing gold coins from many countries. He estimated that there must have been close to twenty thousand pounds, in all. Dan Winterslip, the letter said, had brought the *Maid of Shiloh* back to Sydney and turned over to the proper authorities my father's clothing and personal effects, and a scant ten pounds in money. He had made no mention of anything further. He and the only other white man aboard the *Maid*, an Irishman named Hagin, had left at once for Hawaii. My father's friend suggested that we start an immediate investigation."

===Chinese===
「那封信说，写信人确知，我父亲在希洛少女号的舱里携有巨款。他不与银行打交道，而是请人打造了这只奥希亚木的坚固木箱。写信人对我们说，他亲眼见过箱子里面，说里面装着珠宝和大量的黄金。我父亲还给他看过几只生牛皮口袋，里面装着许多国家的金币。他估计，总计当有两万英镑上下。信上说，丹·温特斯利普把希洛少女号开回悉尼，把我父亲的衣物和随身财物移交给有关当局，外加区区十英镑现款。其余的一概未提。他和船上唯一另一个白人——一个名叫哈金的爱尔兰人——随即动身去了夏威夷。我父亲的那位朋友建议我们立即着手调查。」

===Original===
"Well, gentlemen"—Brade looked about the circle of interested faces—"what could we do? We were in pitiful circumstances, my mother and I. We had no money to employ lawyers, to fight a case thousands of miles away. We did make a few inquiries through a relative in Sydney, but nothing came of them. There was talk for a time, but the talk died out, and the matter was dropped. But I—I have never forgotten.

"Dan Winterslip returned here, and prospered. He built on the foundation of the money he found in my father's cabin a fortune that inspired the admiration of Honolulu. And while he prospered, we were close to starvation. My mother died, but I carried on. For years it has been my dream to make him pay. I have not been particularly successful, but I have saved, scrimped. I have the money now to fight this case.

"Four months ago I resigned my post in India and set out for Honolulu. I stopped over in Sydney—my father's friend is dead, but I have his letter. I have the depositions of others who knew about that money—about the ohia wood box. I came on here, ready to face Dan Winterslip at last. But I never faced him. As you know, gentlemen"—Brade's hand trembled slightly as he put down his cigarette—"someone robbed me of that privilege. Some unknown hand removed from my path the man I have hated for more than forty years."

===Chinese===
「唉，诸位——」布雷德环视一圈兴致盎然的脸——「我们又能怎么样呢？我母亲和我处境凄惨。我们没有钱延请律师，去几千英里外打一场官司。我们确实托悉尼的一位亲戚打听过几回，但毫无结果。议论了一阵子，议论渐渐平息，事情也就撂下了。可是我——我从来没有忘。

「丹·温特斯利普回到这里，发达了。他在从我父亲舱里找到的那笔钱的地基上，垒起了令檀香山啧啧称羡的一份家业。他步步兴旺，我们却濒于饥馑。我母亲死了，我撑了下去。多年来，让他付出代价一直是我的梦想。我算不上特别得志，但我省了又省，抠了又抠。如今我有了打这场官司的钱。

「四个月前，我辞去印度的职位，动身来檀香山。我在悉尼作了停留——我父亲的那位朋友已经故去，但他的信还在。我还拿到了几位知情者的口供——关于那笔钱，关于那只奥希亚木盒。我一路前来，终于要与丹·温特斯利普当面对质了。可我始终没能与他对面。诸位都知道——」布雷德放下烟卷，手微微发颤——「有人剥夺了我这份权利。某只不为人知的手，把我恨了四十多年的那个人，从我路上移开了。」

===Original===
"You arrived last Saturday—a week ago," said Hallet, after a pause. "On Sunday evening Kaohla here called on you. He offered you the strong box?"

"He did," Brade replied. "He'd had a cable from his friend, and expected to have the box by Tuesday. I promised him five thousand dollars for it—a sum I intended Winterslip should pay. Kaohla also told me that Hagin was living on a ranch in a remote part of the Island of Maui. That explains my journey there—I took another name, as I didn't want Winterslip to follow my movements. I had no doubt he was watching me."

"You didn't tell Kaohla you were going, either?"

"No, I didn't think it advisable to take him completely into my confidence. I found Hagin, but could get nothing out of him. Evidently Winterslip had bought his silence long ago. I realized the box was of great importance to me, and I cabled Kaohla to bring it to me immediately on my return. It was then that the news of Winterslip's death came through. It was a deep disappointment, but it will not deter me." He turned to John Quincy. "Winterslip's heirs must pay. I am determined they shall make my old age secure."

John Quincy's face flushed again. A spirit of rebellion, of family pride outraged, stirred within him. "We'll see about that, Mr. Brade," he said. "You have unearthed the box, but so far as any proof about valuables—money—"

===Chinese===
「您是上星期六到的——整整一星期前。」哈利特顿了顿，说。「星期天晚上，这位考赫拉拜访了您。他把那口箱子卖给您了？」

「卖了。」布雷德答道。「他收到了他朋友拍来的电报，指望星期二拿到盒子。我许了他五千美元——这笔钱我原打算让温特斯利普来出。考赫拉还告诉我，哈金住在毛伊岛一个偏僻地方的牧场上。我那趟毛伊岛之行就是这个缘故——我用了假名，因为不想让温特斯利普盯住我的行踪。我毫不怀疑他在盯着我。」

「您也没告诉考赫拉您要走？」

「没有，我认为不宜对他全然交底。我找到了哈金，却没能从他嘴里掏出半句话。显然，温特斯利普早就收买了他，让他闭嘴。我明白那箱子对我事关重大，便拍电报告诉考赫拉，我一回来就立刻把箱子送到我手上。就在那时，传来了温特斯利普的死讯。这令我深深失望，但它吓不退我。」他转向约翰·昆西。「温特斯利普的继承人必须付账。我拿定主意，要让他们保住我的晚年。」

约翰·昆西的脸又红了。一种反叛的情绪，一种家族尊严被冒犯了的情绪，在他胸中翻腾。「那咱们走着瞧，布雷德先生。」他说。「盒子是叫您挖出来了，可要说到有什么凭据证明那些财物——那些钱——」

===Original===
"One moment," cut in Greene, the prosecutor. "Mr. Brade, have you a description of any article of value taken from your father?"

Brade nodded. "Yes. In my father's last letter to us—I was looking through it only the other day—he spoke of a brooch he had picked up in Sydney. A tree of emeralds, rubies and diamonds against an onyx background. He said he was sending it to my mother—but it never came."

The prosecutor looked at John Quincy. John Quincy looked away. "I'm not one of Dan Winterslip's heirs, Mr. Brade," he explained. "As a matter of fact, he was a rather distant relative of mine. I can't presume to speak for his daughter, but I'm reasonably sure that when she knows your story, this matter can be settled out of court. You'll wait, of course?"

"I'll wait," agreed Brade. "And now, Captain—"

===Chinese===
「慢着。」检察官格林插进来。「布雷德先生，对于从您父亲手里拿走的任何一件值钱物件，您可有具体的描述？」

布雷德点头。「有。在我父亲写给我们的最后一封信里——前几天我还翻过——他提到在悉尼买到的一枚胸针。绿宝石、红宝石和钻石在缟玛瑙底衬上缀成的一棵树。他说要寄给我母亲——可它始终没有寄到。」

检察官望向约翰·昆西。约翰·昆西把目光移开了。「我不是丹·温特斯利普的继承人之一，布雷德先生。」他解释道。「说实在的，他只是我相当远的一位亲戚。他女儿那边我不便僭越代她表态，不过我相当有把握：等她知道了您的遭遇，这件事可以在庭外了结。您自然愿意等一等？」

「我等。」布雷德应道。「那么现在，警长——」

===Original===
Hallet raised his hand. "Just a minute. You didn't call on Winterslip? You didn't go near his house?"

"I did not," said Brade.

"Yet just outside the door of his living-room we found, as I told you, the stub of a Corsican cigarette. It's a matter still to be cleared up."

Brade considered briefly. "I don't want to get anyone into trouble," he said. "But the man is nothing to me, and I must clear my own name. In the course of a chat with the proprietor of the Reef and Palm Hotel, I offered him a cigarette. He was delighted when he recognized the brand—said it had been years since he'd seen one. So I gave him a handful, and he filled his case."

===Chinese===
哈利特抬起手。「且慢。您没有拜访过温特斯利普？没有走近过他的宅子？」

「没有。」布雷德说。

「可是，就在他客厅门外，正如我方才所说，我们找到了一个科西嘉牌烟蒂。这件事还没弄清楚。」

布雷德略一思忖。「我不想连累任何人。」他说。「但那人与我毫无干系，而我必须洗清自己的名声。同『礁石棕榈』旅馆的老板闲谈时，我敬了他一支烟。他认出这牌子时喜出望外——说有好些年没见着这种烟了。于是我给了他一把，他把烟盒装满了。」

===Original===
"You're speaking of Jim Egan," suggested Hallet delightedly.

"Of Mr. James Egan, yes," Brade replied.

"That's all I want to know," said Hallet. "Well, Mr. Greene—"

The prosecutor addressed Brade. "For the present, we can't permit you to leave Honolulu," he said. "But you are free to go to your hotel. This box will remain here until we can settle its final disposition."

"Naturally." Brade rose.

===Chinese===
「您说的是吉姆·伊根。」哈利特喜形于色地提示道。

「是詹姆斯·伊根先生，没错。」布雷德答道。

「我要知道的就是这个。」哈利特说。「那么，格林先生——」

检察官对布雷德说。「目前我们不能允许您离开檀香山。但您可以回您的旅馆去。这只盒子要留在这里，直到我们定下它的最终处置。」

「那当然。」布雷德起身。

===Original===
John Quincy faced him. "I'll call on you very soon," he promised.

"What? Oh, yes—yes, of course." The man stared nervously about him. "If you'll pardon me, gentlemen, I must run—I really must—"

He went out. The prosecutor looked at his watch. "Well, that's that. I'll have a conference with you in the morning, Hallet. My wife's waiting for me at the Country Club. Good night, Mr. Winterslip." He saw the look on John Quincy's face, and smiled. "Don't take those revelations about your cousin too seriously. The 'eighties are ancient history, you know."

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西迎着他。「过不了几天我就来拜访您。」他允诺道。

「什么？哦，是的——当然。」那人紧张地朝四下里张望。「诸位恕我失陪，我得赶紧走——实在得赶紧——」

他走了出去。检察官看了看表。「好了，就这么着。明早我同你碰个头，哈利特。我太太在乡村俱乐部等我。晚安，温特斯利普先生。」他看见约翰·昆西脸上的神色，莞尔一笑。「别把您堂叔那些被抖落出来的事太当真。八十年代嘛，您知道，早就是老古董的历史了。」

===Original===
As Greene disappeared, Hallet turned to John Quincy. "What about this Kaohla?" he inquired. "It will be a pretty complicated job to prosecute him and his housebreaking friend on the *President Tyler*, but it can be done—"

A uniformed policeman appeared at the door, summoning Chan outside.

"Oh, no," said John Quincy. "Let the boy go. We don't want any publicity about this. I'll ask you, Captain, to keep Brade's story out of the papers."

"I'll try," Hallet replied. He turned to the Hawaiian. "Come here!" The boy rose. "You heard what this gentleman said. You ought to be sent up for this, but we've got more important things to attend to now. Run along—beat it—"

===Chinese===
格林一走，哈利特便转向约翰·昆西。「这个考赫拉怎么处置？」他问道。「要起诉他和他那泰勒总统号上的破门行窃朋友，是桩相当麻烦的活计，不过不是办不成——」

一名穿制服的警察出现在门口，把陈查理唤了出去。

「哦，别。」约翰·昆西说。「放这小子走吧。我们不想让这事张扬见报。警长，我要请您别让布雷德的事走漏到报纸上。」

「我尽力。」哈利特答道。他转向那夏威夷青年。「过来！」小伙子站起来。「这位先生的话你听见了。照理该为此把你关起来，可眼下我们还有更要紧的事要办。走吧——滚吧——」

===Original===
Chan came in just in time to hear the last. At his heels followed a sly little Jap and a young Chinese boy. The latter was attired in the extreme of college-cut clothes; he was an American and he emphasized the fact.

"Only one moment," Chan cried. "New and interesting fact emerge into light. Gentlemen, my Cousin Willie Chan, captain All Chinese baseball team and demon back-stopper of the Pacific!"

"Pleased to meetchu," said Willie Chan.

"Also Okamoto, who have auto stand on Kalakaua Avenue, not far from Winterslip household—"

"I know Okamoto," said Hallet. "He sells okolehau on the side."

"No, indeed," protested the Jap. "Auto stand, that is what."

===Chinese===
陈查理进来得正巧，听见了最后这句。他脚后跟着一个狡黠的日本小个子和一个中国少年。后者穿一身登峰造极的大学生款式西服；他是美国人，而且处处刻意强调这一事实。

「只占诸位片刻。」陈查理扬声道。「一件新鲜而有趣的事实浮现于光天之下。诸位，我堂弟威利·陈，全华人棒球队队长，威震太平洋的魔王捕手！」

「幸会幸会。」威利·陈说。

「还有这位冈本，他在卡拉卡瓦大道上开着一处汽车候客站，离温特斯利普府上不远——」

「我认识冈本。」哈利特说。「他还私下卖奥科勒豪烈酒（okolehau）。」

「没有的事。」那日本人抗议道。「汽车候客站，就是这么回事。」

===Original===
"Willie do small investigating to help out crowded hours," went on Chan. "He have dug up strange event out of this Okamoto here. On early morning of Tuesday, July first, Okamoto is roused from slumber by fierce knocks on door of room. He go to door—"

"Let him tell it," suggested Hallet. "What time was this?"

"Two of the morning," said the Jap. "Knocks were as described. I rouse and look at watch, run to door. Mr. Dick Kaohla here is waiting. Demand I drive him to home over in Iwilei district. I done so."

"All right," said Hallet. "Anything else? No? Charlie—take them out and thank them—that's your specialty." He waited until the Orientals had left the room, then turned fiercely on Kaohla. "Well, here you are back in the limelight," he cried. "Now, come across. What were you doing out near Winterslip's house the night of the murder?"

===Chinese===
「威利做了点小小的查访，为诸事纷繁的时辰分忧。」陈查理接着说。「他从这位冈本身上挖出一桩离奇之事。七月一日星期二凌晨，冈本被房门上一阵凶猛的敲击从睡梦中惊起。他走向门去——」

「让他自己说。」哈利特提议道。「那是几点钟的事？」

「凌晨两点。」那日本人说。「敲门声确如所述。我惊醒看表，奔到门口。迪克·考赫拉先生正等在那儿。他要求我开车送他回伊维莱区的家。我照办了。」

「好。」哈利特说。「还有别的吗？没有？查理——带他们出去，谢谢他们——这是你的专长。」他等到那几个东方人出了屋，才猛地转向考赫拉。「好啊，你又回到聚光灯底下来了。」他喝道。「快招。凶案那天夜里，你在温特斯利普宅子附近干什么？」

===Original===
"Nothing," said the Hawaiian.

"Nothing! A little late to be up doing nothing, wasn't it? Look here, my boy, I'm beginning to get you. For years Dan Winterslip gave you money, supported you, until he finally decided you were no good. So he stopped the funds and you and he had a big row. Now, didn't you?"

"Yes," admitted Dick Kaohla.

"On Sunday night Brade offered you five thousand for the box. You thought it wasn't enough. The idea struck you that maybe Dan Winterslip would pay more. You were a little afraid of him, but you screwed up your courage and went to his house—"

"No, no," the boy cried. "I did not go there."

"I say you did. You'd made up your mind to double-cross Brade. You and Dan Winterslip had another big scrap, you drew a knife—"

===Chinese===
「没干什么。」那夏威夷人说。

「没干什么！深更半夜爬起来什么都不干，是不是有点儿晚了？你听着，小子，我开始把你摸透了。多年来丹·温特斯利普给你钱、养着你，直到他终于认定你是个不成器的坯子。于是他断了接济，你们大吵了一场。是不是这样？」

「是。」迪克·考赫拉承认了。

「星期天夜里，布雷德出五千美元买那盒子。你觉得不够。你灵机一动，心想丹·温特斯利普没准肯出更多的钱。你有点怕他，可你还是鼓足了勇气，上了他家的门——」

「不，不，」小伙子喊道，「我没有去那儿。」

「我说你去了。你打定主意要坑布雷德一道。你和丹·温特斯利普又大干了一架，你抽出了刀子——」

===Original===
"Lies, all lies," the boy shouted, terrified.

"Don't tell me I lie! You killed Winterslip and I'll get it out of you! I got the other and I'll get this." Hallet rose threateningly from his chair.

Chan suddenly reentered the room, and handed Hallet a note. "Arrive this moment by special messenger," he explained.

Hallet ripped open the envelope and read. His expression altered. He turned disgustedly to Kaohla. "Beat it!" he scowled.

The boy fled gratefully. John Quincy and Chan looked wonderingly at the captain. Hallet sat down at his desk. "It all comes back to Egan," he said. "I've known it from the first."

"Wait a minute," cried John Quincy. "What about that boy?"

===Chinese===
「谎话，全是谎话。」小伙子吓坏了，大声喊道。

「别跟我说我在撒谎！就是你杀了温特斯利普，我会让你招出来的！别的东西我拿到了，这个我也一定拿得到。」哈利特威吓地从椅子上站起身来。

陈查理突然重新进屋，递给哈利特一张字条。「方才由专差送到。」他解释道。

哈利特撕开信封，读了一遍。他的神色变了。他厌恶地转向考赫拉。「滚吧！」他沉下脸喝道。

小伙子如蒙大赦，一溜烟跑了。约翰·昆西和陈查理讶异地望着警长。哈利特在办公桌前坐下。「到头来还是伊根。」他说。「打一开始我就知道是他。」

「等一等。」约翰·昆西喊道。「那小子怎么算？」

===Original===
Hallet crumpled the letter in his hand. "Kaohla? Oh, he's out of it now."

"Why?"

"That's all I can tell you. He's out of it."

"That's not enough," John Quincy said. "I demand to know—"

Hallet glared at him. "You know all you're going to," he answered angrily. "I say Kaohla's out, and that settles it. Egan killed Winterslip, and before I get through with him—"

===Chinese===
哈利特把手里的信揉成一团。「考赫拉？哦，他现在脱了干系了。」

「为什么？」

「我只能告诉你这些。他脱了干系。」

「这不够。」约翰·昆西说。「我要求知道——」

哈利特瞪着他。「该让你知道的都让你知道了。」他没好气地答道。「我说考赫拉脱了干系，这事就这么定了。伊根杀了温特斯利普，等我收拾完他——」

===Original===
"Permit me to say," interrupted John Quincy, "that you have the most trusting nature I ever met. Everybody's story goes with you. The Compton woman and that rat Leatherbee come in here and spin a yarn, and you bow them out. And Brade! What about Brade! In bed at one-twenty last Tuesday morning, eh? Who says so? He does. Who can prove it? His wife can. What was to prevent his stepping out on the balcony of the Reef and Palm and walking along the beach to my cousin's house? Answer me that!"

Hallet shook his head. "It's Egan. That cigarette—"

"Yes—that cigarette. Has it occurred to you that Brade may have given him those cigarettes purposely—"

"Egan did it," cut in Hallet stubbornly. "All I need now is his story; I'll get it. I have ways and means—"

===Chinese===
「请恕我直言，」约翰·昆西打断道，「您是我平生所见天性最轻信的人。谁的说法到您那儿都行得通。康普顿那个女人和莱瑟比那只耗子上这儿来编套瞎话，您客客气气把他们送出门。还有布雷德！布雷德怎么说？上星期二凌晨一点二十他在床上，嗯？谁说的？他自己说的。谁能证明？他太太能。他要是走出『礁石棕榈』的露台，沿着沙滩走到我堂叔家去，有什么拦得住？您倒是回答我！」

哈利特摇头。「是伊根。那支烟——」

「是——那支烟。您可曾想过，布雷德把那些香烟给他，没准是有意的——」

「就是伊根干的。」哈利特执拗地插进来。「如今我只缺他的一套口供；我会拿到手的。我自有办法——」

===Original===
"I congratulate you on your magnificent stupidity," cried John Quincy. "Good night, sir."

He walked along Bethel Street, Chan at his side.

"You are partly consumed by anger," said the Chinaman. "Humbly suggest you cool. Calm heads needed."

"But what was in that note? Why wouldn't he tell us?"

"In good time, we know. Captain honest man. Be patient."

"But we're all at sea again," protested John Quincy. "Who killed Cousin Dan? We get nowhere."

===Chinese===
「为您这辉煌的愚蠢，我谨致祝贺。」约翰·昆西喊道。「晚安，先生。」

他沿着贝特尔街走，陈查理陪在身边。

「您有一部分正被怒火吞噬着。」那中国人说。「谦卑地建议您冷却。此处需要镇定的头脑。」

「可那张字条上写的是什么？他为什么不肯告诉我们？」

「时机一到，自然知晓。长官是诚实之人。且耐下性子。」

「可咱们又全都茫然无绪了。」约翰·昆西抗议道。「到底是谁杀了堂叔丹？咱们寸步难行。」

===Original===
"So very true," agreed Chan. "More clues lead us into presence of immovable stone wall. We sway about, seeking still other path."

"I'll say we do," answered John Quincy. "There comes my car. Good night!"

Not until the trolley was halfway to Waikiki did he remember Mr. Saladine. Saladine crouching outside that window at the Reef and Palm. What did that mean? But Saladine was a comic figure, a lisping searcher after bridgework in the limpid waters of Waikiki. Even so, perhaps his humble activities should be investigated.

===Chinese===
「千真万确。」陈查理深表同意。「更多线索把我们引至那堵岿然不动的高墙跟前。我们左右逡巡，仍在另觅他路。」

「可不是嘛。」约翰·昆西应道。「我的车来了。晚安！」

直到有轨电车行到半路、快到怀基基时，他才想起萨拉迪尼先生。萨拉迪尼蜷在『礁石棕榈』那扇窗子外头的样子。那意味着什么？可萨拉迪尼不过是个滑稽角色，一个口齿漏风、在怀基基清澈碧波里寻摸假牙桥的小个子。饶是如此，他那些不上台面的活动，或许也该查访一番。

## XVI The Return of Captain Cope / 十六 科佩船长归来

===Original===
After breakfast on Sunday morning, John Quincy followed Miss Minerva to the lanai. It was a neat world that lay outside the screen, for Dan Winterslip’s yard boy had been busy until a late hour the night before, sweeping the lawn with the same loving thoroughness a housewife might display on a precious Oriental rug.

Barbara had not come down to breakfast, and John Quincy had seized the opportunity to tell his aunt of Brade’s return, and repeat the man’s story of Dan Winterslip’s theft on board the *Maid of Shiloh*. Now he lighted a cigarette and sat staring seriously out at the distant water.

===Chinese===
星期日早晨吃过早饭，约翰·昆西跟着米纳瓦小姐来到露台上。纱窗外是一个纤尘不染的世界，丹·温特斯利普家打理院子的仆役前一夜一直忙到很晚，把草坪扫得干干净净，那份爱惜周到的劲儿，就像家庭主妇打理一块珍贵的东方地毯。

芭芭拉没有下楼来吃早饭，约翰·昆西便趁这个机会，把布雷德回来的事告诉了姑婆，又复述了那人讲的丹·温特斯利普在「希洛少女号」上行窃的往事。此刻他点燃一支香烟，坐下来神情严肃地凝望着远处的海面。

===Original===
“Cheer up,” said Miss Minerva. “You look like a judge. I presume you’re thinking of poor Dan.”

“I am.”

“Forgive and forget. None of us ever suspected Dan of being a saint.”

“A saint! Far from it! He was just a plain﻿—”

“Never mind,” put in his aunt sharply. “Remember, John Quincy, man is a creature of environment. And the temptation must have been great. Picture Dan on that ship in these easygoing latitudes, wealth at his feet and not a soul in sight to claim it. Ill-gotten wealth, at that. Even you﻿—”

“Even I,” said John Quincy sternly, “would have recalled I am a Winterslip. I never dreamed I’d live to hear you offering apologies for that sort of conduct.”

===Chinese===
「振作点，」米纳瓦小姐说，「你瞧着像位法官。我猜你是在想可怜的丹。」

「正是在想他。」

「既往不咎吧。我们谁也没疑心过丹是圣徒。」

「圣徒！差得远了！他不过是个不折不扣的——」

「不必往下说了，」他姑婆厉声插进来。「记住，约翰·昆西，人是环境的造物。那诱惑必定大得很。想想丹在那条船上，身处这等闲散的纬度，财富就在脚边，四下不见一个人影来认领。况且那还是不义之财。就连你——」

「就连我，」约翰·昆西声色俱厉地说，「也会想起自己是温特斯利普家的人。我做梦也没想到，有生之年竟会听见您为那等行径辩解。」

===Original===
She laughed. “You know what they say about white women who go to the tropics. They lose first their complexion, then their teeth, and finally their moral sense.” She hesitated. “I’ve had to visit the dentist a good deal of late,” she added.

John Quincy was shocked “My advice to you is to hurry home,” he said.

“When are you going?”

“Oh, soon﻿—soon.”

===Chinese===
她笑了起来。「人们怎么说上热带来的白种女人，你是知道的：先是丢了脸色，再丢了牙齿，最后连道德感也丢了。」她迟疑了一下。「近来我可没少跑牙医那儿。」她补了一句。

约翰·昆西大为震惊。「我劝您还是赶紧回家吧。」他说。

「您什么时候走？」

「哦，快了——快了。」

===Original===
“That’s what we all say. Returning to Boston, I suppose?”

“Of course.”

“How about San Francisco?”

“Oh, that’s off. I did suggest it to Agatha, but I’m certain she won’t hear of it. And I’m beginning to think she’d be quite right.” His aunt rose. “You’d better go to church,” said John Quincy severely.

===Chinese===
「我们个个都这么说。回去是回波士顿，我猜得对吧？」

「当然。」

「旧金山那趟呢？」

「哦，那计划取消了。我确实向阿加莎提议过，可我敢肯定她连听都不要听。而且我也开始觉得，她的拒绝完全在理。」他姑婆站起身来。「您还是上教堂去吧。」约翰·昆西正色道。

===Original===
“That’s just where I am going,” she smiled. “By the way, Amos is coming to dinner tonight, and he’d best hear the Brade story from us, rather than in some garbled form. Barbara must hear it too. If it proves to be true, the family ought to do something for Mr. Brade.”

“Oh, the family will do something for him, all right,” John Quincy remarked. “Whether it wants to or not.”

“Well, I’ll let you tell Barbara about him,” Miss Minerva promised.

“Thank you so much,” replied her nephew sarcastically.

“Not at all. Are you coming to church?”

“No,” he said. “I don’t need it the way you do.”

===Chinese===
「我正是要去教堂。」她微笑道。「对了，今晚阿莫斯要来吃饭，布雷德这桩事，最好由我们亲口讲给他听，免得传到他耳朵里已走了样。芭芭拉也必须听听。这事若查证属实，家里应当为布雷德先生做点什么。」

「哦，家里自会为他做点什么的，错不了，」约翰·昆西说，「不管它乐意不乐意。」

「那么，布雷德的事就由你去讲给芭芭拉听吧。」米纳瓦小姐允诺道。

「多谢您啦。」她侄子语带讥诮地答道。

「不必客气。你去做礼拜吗？」

「不去，」他说，「我可不像您那样需要它。」

===Original===
She left him there to face a lazy uneventful day. By five in the afternoon Waikiki was alive with its usual Sunday crowd﻿—not the unsavory holiday throng seen on a mainland beach, but a scattering of good-looking people whose tanned straight bodies would have delighted the heart of a physical culture enthusiast. John Quincy summoned sufficient energy to don a bathing suit and plunge in.

There was something soothing in the warm touch of the water, and he was becoming more at home there every day. With long powerful strokes he drew away from the malihini breakers to dare the great rollers beyond. Surfboard riders flashed by him; now and then he had to alter his course to avoid an outrigger canoe.

On the farthest float of all he saw Carlota Egan. She sat there, a slender lovely figure vibrant with life, and awaited his coming. As he climbed up beside her and looked into her eyes he was﻿—perhaps from his exertion in the water﻿—a little breathless.

===Chinese===
她把他留在那儿，去面对一个懒散无事的白昼。到了下午五点，怀基基照例聚起星期天的人群——不是大陆海滩上那种不堪入目的假日乌合之众，而是三三两两、零零落落的一群好看人物，他们晒成古铜色的挺直身躯，准会叫任何一个健美运动的热心人看了心花怒放。约翰·昆西攒够了力气，换上泳衣，一头扎进水里。

海水温暖的抚触里有一种抚慰人心的东西，他一天比一天觉得在那水里自在如归。他抡开长而有力的臂膀，游离了外来客们嬉戏的近岸碎浪，去闯外头那些大浪。踏冲浪板的人影从他身边飞掠而过；他不时要改换水路，避开一条支架独木舟。

在最远的那座浮台上，他望见了卡洛塔·伊根。她坐在那儿，一个纤细可爱的身影，浑身洋溢着生气，正等候他游来。他爬上浮台，坐到她身旁，凝视她的眼睛时——也许是水里的力气用尽了——竟微微有些气喘。

===Original===
“I rather hoped I’d find you,” he panted.

“Did you?” She smiled faintly. “I hoped it too. You see, I need a lot of cheering up.”

“On a perfect day like this!”

“I’d pinned such hopes on Mr. Brade,” she explained. “Perhaps you know he’s back﻿—and from what I can gather, his return hasn’t meant a thing so far as dad’s concerned. Not a thing.”

“Well, I’m afraid it hasn’t,” John Quincy admitted. “But we mustn’t get discouraged. As Chan puts it, we sway about, seeking a new path. You and I have a bit of swaying to do. How about Mr. Saladine?”

===Chinese===
「我还真盼着能在这儿找着你呢。」他喘着气说。

「是吗？」她浅浅一笑。「我也盼着呢。你是知道的，我太需要人给我宽宽心了。」

「这么好的天，你还这样！」

「我本来在布雷德先生身上寄托了那么大的指望，」她解释道，「他回来了，这你也许知道——可据我看，他的归来对我爸爸的事没有起一丝作用。一丝也没有。」

「呃，恐怕确实没起，」约翰·昆西承认道，「可咱们不能灰心。用陈查理的话说：我们左右逡巡，另觅他路。你我还有些逡巡的功夫要下。萨拉迪尼先生那条线怎么样？」

===Original===
“I’ve been thinking about Mr. Saladine. But I can’t get excited about him, somehow. He’s so ridiculous.”

“We mustn’t pass him up on that account,” admonished John Quincy. “I caught a glimpse of his purple bathing suit on the first float. Come on﻿—we’ll just casually drop in on him. I’ll race you there.”

She smiled again, and leaped to her feet. For a second she stood poised, then dived in a way that John Quincy could never hope to emulate. He slipped off in pursuit, and though he put forth every effort, she reached Saladine’s side five seconds before he did.

===Chinese===
「我一直在琢磨萨拉迪尼先生。可不知怎么，我就是热乎不起来。他这人太可笑了。」

「咱们可不能因为这个就把他放过，」约翰·昆西告诫道，「我刚才在第一座浮台上瞥见他那一身紫色泳衣了。来——我们就装作不经意地拜访他一下。咱们比赛游过去。」

她又笑了，一跃而起。有那么一秒钟，她凝立在浮台边缘纹丝不动，随即纵身入水，那一手功夫，约翰·昆西一辈子也休想学得来。他滑下水去追，尽管使出了浑身解数，还是比她晚五秒钟游到萨拉迪尼身边。

===Original===
“Hello, Mr. Saladine,” she said. “This is Mr. Winterslip, of Boston.”

“Ah, yeth,” responded Mr. Saladine, gloomily. “Mr. Winterthlip.” He regarded the young man with interest.

“Any luck, sir?” inquired John Quincy sympathetically.

“Oh﻿—you heard about my accthident?”

“I did, sir, and I’m sorry.”

“I am, too,” said Mr. Saladine feelingly. “Not a thrath of them tho far. And I muth go home in a few dath.”

===Chinese===
「你好，萨拉迪尼先生，」她说，「这位是波士顿来的温特斯利普先生。」

「啊，伊希（是的），」萨拉迪尼先生怏怏不乐地应道，「温特希利普先生。」他饶有兴味地打量着这个青年。

「有收获吗，先生？」约翰·昆西关切地问。

「哦——您听说我那场希外（意外）了？」

「听说了，先生，我很遗憾。」

「我也遗憾哪，」萨拉迪尼先生感慨系之地说。「到希（现）在为止，它们连一丝踪影也没有。再过几希（天），我就非回家不可了。」

===Original===
“I believe Miss Egan said you lived in Des Moines?”

“Yeth. Deth﻿—Deth﻿—I can’t thay it.”

“In business there?” inquired John Quincy nonchalantly.

“Yeth. Wholethale grothery buthineth,” answered Mr. Saladine, slowly but not very successfully.

John Quincy turned away to hide a smile. “Shall we go along?” he said to the girl. “Good luck to you, sir.” He dove off, and as they swam toward the shore, he reflected that they were on a false trail there﻿—a trail as spurious as the teeth. That little business man was too conventional a figure to have any connection with the murder of Dan Winterslip. He kept these thoughts to himself, however.

===Chinese===
「我记得伊根小姐说过，您住在得梅因？」

「伊希（是的）。得希——得希——我说不上希（来）。」

「在那边经商吗？」约翰·昆西漫不经心地问。

「伊希（是的）。批发杂货的生希（意）。」萨拉迪尼先生答道，答得很慢，却不见得怎么成功。

约翰·昆西扭过脸去，掩住一丝笑意。「我们走吧？」他对姑娘说。「祝您好运，先生。」他潜入水中。两人朝岸边游去时，他心里琢磨：这条线索走岔了——岔得跟那副假牙一样假。这位小个子生意人太规行矩步，同丹·温特斯利普的凶案扯不上半点干系。不过这些念头，他谁也没有告诉。

===Original===
Halfway to the beach, they encountered an enormous figure floating languidly on the water. Just beyond the great stomach John Quincy perceived the serene face of Charlie Chan.

“Hello, Charlie,” he cried. “It’s a small ocean, after all! Got your Ford with you?”

Chan righted himself and grinned. “Little pleasant recreation,” he explained. “Forget detective worries out here floating idle like leaf on stream.”

“Please float ashore,” suggested John Quincy. “I have something to tell you.”

“Only too happy,” agreed Chan.

===Chinese===
游到离海滩一半路程的地方，他们撞见一个庞大的身形，正懒洋洋地浮在水面上。越过那偌大的肚皮，约翰·昆西认出了陈查理那张安详的脸。

「你好啊，查理！」他喊道，「这海洋毕竟还是小啊！您的福特车也随身带来了？」

陈查理翻身立起，咧嘴一笑。「小小怡情之消遣，」他解释道，「闲浮此间如溪上之叶，侦探的烦忧尽忘。」

「请漂到岸上来吧，」约翰·昆西提议道，「我有话要跟您说。」

「求之不得。」陈查理欣然应道。

===Original===
He followed them in and they sat, an odd trio, on the white sand. John Quincy told the detective about Saladine’s activities outside the window the night before, and repeated the conversation he had just had with the middle westerner. “Of course, the man seems almost too foolish to mean anything,” he added.

Chan shook his head. “Begging most humble pardon,” he said, “that are wrong attitude completely. Detective business made up of unsignificant trifles. One after other our clues go burst in our countenance. Wise to pursue matter of Mr. Saladine.”

“What do you suggest?” John Quincy asked.

“Tonight I visit city for night work to drive off my piled tasks,” Chan replied. “After evening meal, suggest you join with me at cable office. We despatch message to postmaster of this Des Moines, inquiring what are present locality of Mr. Saladine, expert in wholeselling provisions. Your name will be signed to message, much better than police meddling.”

“All right,” John Quincy agreed, “I’ll meet you there at eight-thirty.”

===Chinese===
他跟着他们游进浅水，三个人作为一个古怪的三人组，在白沙滩上坐了下来。约翰·昆西把头天夜里萨拉迪尼在窗外的一举一动讲给侦探听，又复述了方才同这位中西部人的对话。「当然啦，这人蠢得几乎不像有什么名堂。」他补了一句。

陈查理摇了摇头。「谦卑乞恕，」他说，「此态度全然大错。侦探之业，本由毫不起眼的琐屑堆叠而成。我们的线索一条接一条，当面炸为泡影。穷究萨拉迪尼先生之事，方为明智。」

「您有何高见？」约翰·昆西问。

「今夜我进城，连夜赶工，驱散我积压的事务，」陈查理答道，「晚饭之后，建议您到电报局与我会合。我们发一封电报给那得梅因的邮政局长，询问那位批发杂货行家萨拉迪尼先生如今身在何处。电报上署您的名字，远胜警方出面搅扰。」

「好，」约翰·昆西应承道，「八点半我在那儿同您碰头。」

===Original===
Carlota Egan rose. “I must get back to the Reef and Palm. You’ve no idea all I have to do﻿—”

John Quincy stood beside her. “If I can help, you know﻿—”

“I know,” she smiled. “I’m thinking of making you assistant manager. They’d be so proud of you﻿—in Boston.”

===Chinese===
卡洛塔·伊根站起身来。「我得回『礁石棕榈』去了。你根本想不到我有多少事要做——」

约翰·昆西站到她身旁。「要是我能搭把手，你知道——」

「我知道，」她微笑道，「我在考虑聘你做协理呢。他们准会为你骄傲得了不得——波士顿那边的人。」

===Original===
She moved off toward the water for her homeward swim, and John Quincy dropped down beside Chan. The Chinaman’s little amber eyes followed the girl. “Endeavoring to make English language my slave,” he said, “I pursue poetry. Who were the great poet who said﻿—‘She walks in beauty like the night?’ ”

“Why, that was﻿—er﻿—who was it?” remarked John Quincy helpfully.

“Name is slippery,” went on Chan. “But no matter. Lines pop into brain whenever I see this Miss Egan. Beauty like the night, Hawaiian night maybe, lovely as purest jade. Most especially on this beach. Spot of heartbreaking charm, this beach.”

“Surely is,” agreed John Quincy, amused at Chan’s obviously sentimental mood.

===Chinese===
她朝水边走去，要游水回家。约翰·昆西在陈查理身边坐了下来。那华人琥珀色的小眼睛紧随着姑娘的背影。「我正发愤使英语做我的奴仆，」他说，「因而于诗歌一道苦苦追索。那位大诗人是谁说的来着——『她走在美的光彩中，像夜晚』？」

「哎呀，那是——呃——那是谁来着？」约翰·昆西好心地搭腔道。

「姓名这东西滑不留手，」陈查理接着说，「但无妨。每见这位伊根小姐，这些诗句便蹦进我脑中。美如夜，也许是夏威夷之夜，可爱如至纯之玉。尤其在这片海滩上。令人心痛神驰的胜境，这片海滩。」

「可不是嘛。」约翰·昆西附和道，陈查理这副显而易见的伤感情调让他暗自好笑。

===Original===
“Here on gleaming sand I first regard my future wife,” continued Chan. “Slender as the bamboo is slender, beautiful as blossom of the plum﻿—”

“Your wife,” repeated John Quincy. The idea was a new one.

“Yes, indeed.” Chan rose. “Recalls I must hasten home where she attends the children who are now, by actual count, nine in number.” He looked down at John Quincy thoughtfully. “Are you well-fitted with the armor of preparation?” he said. “Consider. Some night the moon has splendor in this neighborhood, the coco-palms bow lowly and turn away their heads so they do not see. And the white man kisses without intending to do so.”

“Oh, don’t worry about me,” John Quincy laughed; “I’m from Boston, and immune.”

“Immune,” repeated Chan. “Ah, yes, I grasp meaning. In my home I have idol brought from China with insides of solid stone. He would think he is﻿—immune. But even so I would not entrust him on this beach. As my cousin Willie Chan say with vulgarity, see you later.”

===Chinese===
「就在这闪光的沙滩上，我初次注目我未来的妻子，」陈查理继续道，「纤秀如竹之纤秀，美丽如梅之初绽——」

「您的妻子？」约翰·昆西重复道。这可是桩新鲜事。

「正是，千真万确。」陈查理起身。「这提醒我必须赶紧回家了，她正在家中照看孩子们——如今照实数一数，已有九个之多。」他低头若有所思地望着约翰·昆西。「您可已披挂齐整戒备之铠甲？」他说，「想想看。哪一夜月亮在此间大放光华，椰子树深深躬下身去，扭过头不敢看。白种人便无意之间吻了下去。」

「哦，甭替我操心，」约翰·昆西笑道，「我是波士顿来的，免疫的。」

「免疫的，」陈查理重复道，「啊，是了，我领会此意。我家中有一尊从中国带来的偶像，五脏六腑全是实心石头。他准会自以为——免疫。但即便如此，我也不敢托付他来这片海滩。正如我表亲威利·陈用粗俗之语所说：回头见。」

===Original===
John Quincy sat for a time on the sand, then rose and strolled toward home. His path lay close to the lanai of Arlene Compton’s cottage, and he was surprised to hear his name called from behind the screen. He stepped to the door and looked in. The woman was sitting there alone.

“Come in a minute, Mr. Winterslip,” she said.

John Quincy hesitated. He did not care to make any social calls on this lady, but he did not have it in him to be rude. He went inside and sat down gingerly, poised for flight. “Got to hurry back for dinner,” he explained.

“Dinner? You’ll want a cocktail.”

“No, thanks. I’m﻿—I’m on the wagon.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西在沙滩上又坐了一阵，才起身信步往家走。他走的路紧挨着阿琳·康普顿小屋的露台，忽听纱窗后有人唤他的名字，不由得吃了一惊。他走到门口朝里看。那女人正独自坐在那里。

「进来坐一分钟吧，温特斯利普先生。」她说。

约翰·昆西踌躇了一下。他并不想同这位太太做什么礼节性拜访，可他也生不出粗鲁相待的心肠。他走进屋里，小心翼翼地坐下，随时准备开溜。「我得赶回去吃晚饭。」他解释道。

「晚饭？那你得来杯鸡尾酒。」

「不用了，谢谢。我——我戒着酒呢。」

===Original===
“You’ll find it hard to stick out here,” she said a little bitterly. “I won’t keep you long. I just want to know﻿—are those boneheads down at the station getting anywhere, or ain’t they?”

“The police,” smiled John Quincy. “They seem to be making progress. But it’s slow. It’s very slow.”

“I’ll tell the world it’s slow. And I got to stick here till they pin it on somebody. Pleasant outlook, ain’t it?”

“Is Mr. Leatherbee still with you?” inquired John Quincy.

===Chinese===
「要在这儿守着，你可坚持不了多久，」她的话音里带着几分苦涩。「我不多留你。我就想知道——局子里那帮榆木脑袋到底有没有进展，还是压根儿没有？」

「警察嘛，」约翰·昆西含笑说，「他们似乎是在往前走。只是慢。非常慢。」

「可不是慢到家了嘛。我还得在这儿干耗着，等他们把罪名安到谁头上。前景美妙吧，嗯？」

「莱瑟比先生还同您在一起吗？」约翰·昆西问道。

===Original===
“What do you mean is he still with me?” she flared.

“Pardon me. Is he still in town?”

“Of course he’s in town. They won’t let him go, either. But I ain’t worrying about him. I got troubles of my own. I want to go home.” She nodded toward a newspaper on the table. “I just got hold of an old *Variety* and seen about a show opening in Atlantic City. A lot of the gang is in it, working like dogs, rehearsing night and day, worrying themselves sick over how long the thing will last. Gee, don’t I envy them. I was near to bawling when you came along.”

“You’ll get back all right,” comforted John Quincy.

“Say﻿—if I ever do! I’ll stop everybody I meet on Broadway and promise never to leave ’em again.” John Quincy rose. “You tell that guy Hallet to get a move on,” she urged.

===Chinese===
「什么叫还同我在一起？」她火了起来。

「对不起。我是说，他还在城里吗？」

「他当然在城里。他们也不放他走。不过我不为他操心。我有我自己的苦处。我要回家。」她朝桌上一份报纸点了点头。「我刚弄到一份旧的《综艺》（*Variety*），看到大西洋城有一出戏开演。一帮老伙计都在里头，像狗一样卖力，白天黑夜地排练，为那出戏能撑多久愁得要命。天哪，我可真羡慕他们。你来的时候，我差一点儿没哭出来。」

「您总会回去的。」约翰·昆西宽慰道。

「嘿——真要有那一天！我要拦下百老汇上碰见的每一个人，向他们保证再也不离开他们了。」约翰·昆西站起身。「你告诉哈利特那家伙，叫他抓紧点儿。」她催促道。

===Original===
“I’ll tell him,” he agreed.

“And drop in to see me now and then,” she added wistfully. “Us easterners ought to stick together out here.”

“That’s right, we should,” John Quincy answered. “Goodbye.”

As he walked along the beach, he thought of her with pity. The story she and Leatherbee had told might be entirely false; even so, she was a human and appealing figure and her homesickness touched his heart.

===Chinese===
「我一定转告。」他应道。

「得空也常来看看我，」她怅然地添了一句，「咱们东部人在这儿该抱成团才是。」

「说的是，是该这样。」约翰·昆西答道，「再见。」

沿着海滩一路走去，他想着她，心里怜悯。她同莱瑟比讲的那套说辞也许纯属虚构；即便如此，她终究是个有人情味、招人怜见的角色，她那股思乡的愁绪触动了他的心。

===Original===
Later that evening when John Quincy came downstairs faultlessly attired for dinner, he encountered Amos Winterslip in the living-room. Cousin Amos’s lean face was whiter than ever; his manner listless. He had been robbed of his hate; his evenings beneath the algaroba tree had lost their savor; life was devoid of spice.

Dinner was not a particularly jolly affair. Barbara seemed intent on knowing now the details of the search the police were conducting, and it fell to John Quincy to enlighten her. Reluctantly he came at last to the story of Brade. She listened in silence. After dinner she and John Quincy went out into the garden and sat on a bench under the hau tree, facing the water.

===Chinese===
当晚晚些时候，约翰·昆西衣冠齐整、一丝不苟地下楼来吃晚饭，在客厅里遇见了阿莫斯·温特斯利普。阿莫斯堂亲那张清瘦的脸比往常更加苍白；举止百无聊赖。他的恨被人夺走了；他在牧豆树下的那些夜晚失了滋味；生活里再无半点辛辣。

这顿晚饭吃得并不怎么欢畅。芭芭拉此刻似乎一心要知道警方搜查的种种详情，这开导的差事便落到了约翰·昆西头上。他磨磨蹭蹭，最后总算讲到布雷德那段故事。她默默听着。饭后，她同约翰·昆西走进花园，在桐棉树下的一条长凳上坐下，面朝着海水。

===Original===
“I’m terribly sorry I had to tell you that about Brade,” John Quincy said gently. “But it seemed necessary.”

“Of course,” she agreed. “Poor dad! He was weak﻿—weak﻿—”

“Forgive and forget,” John Quincy suggested. “Man is a creature of environment.” He wondered dimly where he had heard that before. “Your father was not entirely to blame﻿—”

“You’re terribly kind, John Quincy,” she told him.

“No﻿—but I mean it,” he protested. “Just picture the scene to yourself. That lonely ocean, wealth at his feet for the taking, no one to see or know.”

She shook her head. “Oh, but it was wrong, wrong. Poor Mr. Brade. I must make things right with him as nearly as I can. I shall ask Harry to talk with him tomorrow﻿—”

===Chinese===
「布雷德的事，我不得不那样告诉你，实在抱歉得很，」约翰·昆西轻声说，「可这似乎又是必须的。」

「当然，」她应道，「可怜的爸爸！他是软弱——软弱——」

「既往不咎吧，」约翰·昆西劝道，「人是环境的造物。」他隐隐纳闷，这话先前是打哪儿听来的。「你父亲也不能全怪他——」

「你心肠太好了，约翰·昆西。」她对他说。

「不——可我是真心的，」他分辩道，「你自己想象一下那番情景吧。那片孤零零的大洋，财富就在脚边唾手可得，没有一个人看见，没有一个人知晓。」

她摇了摇头。「哦，可那是错的，是错的。可怜的布雷德先生。我必须尽我所能，把事情给他一个公道。我要请哈里明天去同他谈谈——」

===Original===
“Just a suggestion,” interposed John Quincy. “Whatever you agree to do for Brade must not be done until the man who killed your father is found.”

She stared at him. “What! You don’t think that Brade﻿—”

“I don’t know. Nobody knows. Brade is unable to prove where he was early last Tuesday morning.”

They sat silent for a moment; then the girl suddenly collapsed and buried her face in her hands. Her slim shoulders trembled convulsively and John Quincy, deeply sympathetic, moved closer. He put his arm about her. The moonlight shone on her bright hair, the trades whispered in the hau tree, the breakers murmured on the beach. She lifted her face, and he kissed her. A cousinly kiss he had meant it to be, but somehow it wasn’t﻿—it was a kiss he would never have been up to on Beacon Street.

===Chinese===
「提个建议，」约翰·昆西插话道，「你们答应为布雷德做的任何事，都必须等找到杀害你父亲的凶手之后再办。」

她直瞪瞪地望着他。「什么！你不会认为布雷德——」

「我不知道。谁也不知道。布雷德拿不出证据，证明上星期二清晨他自己身在何处。」

两人默默坐了片刻；随后姑娘突然支撑不住，把脸埋进双手里。她纤细的肩膀一阵阵地抽动，约翰·昆西深感同情，挪近了些。他伸手揽住她。月光照在她明亮的头发上，信风在桐棉树间低语，碎浪在沙滩上呢喃。她抬起脸来，他吻了她。他本意只当是表亲之间的一吻，可不知怎么竟不是——那一吻，在灯塔街上他是无论如何也使不出来的。

===Original===
“Miss Minerva said I’d find you here,” remarked a voice behind them.

John Quincy leaped to his feet and found himself staring into the cynical eyes of Harry Jennison. Even though you are the girl’s cousin, it is a bit embarrassing to have a man find you kissing his fiancée. Particularly if the kiss wasn’t at all cousinly﻿—John Quincy wondered if Jennison had noticed that.

“Come in﻿—I mean, sit down,” stammered John Quincy. “I was just going.”

“Goodbye,” said Jennison coldly.

===Chinese===
「米纳瓦小姐说，会在这儿找到你们。」身后一个声音开口道。

约翰·昆西一跃而起，发现自己正瞪视着哈里·詹尼森那双带讥带诮的眼睛。即便你是姑娘的表亲，叫人撞见你正吻着他的未婚妻，也总有几分难堪。尤其那一吻压根儿算不得表亲之吻——约翰·昆西暗自琢磨，詹尼森注意到这一点没有。

「进来——我是说，请坐，」约翰·昆西结结巴巴地说，「我正要走呢。」

「再见。」詹尼森冷冷地说。

===Original===
John Quincy went hastily through the living-room, where Miss Minerva sat with Amos. “Got an appointment downtown,” he explained, and picking up his hat in the hall, fled into the night.

He had intended taking the roadster, but to reach the garage he would have to pass that bench under the hau tree. Oh, well, the colorful atmosphere of a trolley was more interesting, anyhow.

In the cable office on the ground floor of the Alexander Young Hotel, Chan was waiting, and they sent off their inquiry to the postmaster at Des Moines, signing John Quincy’s name and address. That attended to, they returned to the street. In the park across the way an unseen group of young men strummed steel guitars and sang in soft haunting voices; it was the only sign of life in Honolulu.

“Kindly deign to enter hotel lobby with me,” suggested Chan. “It is my custom to regard names in register from time to time.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西匆匆穿过客厅——米纳瓦小姐正陪阿莫斯坐在那里。「在市中心有个约会。」他解释了一句，便在大厅里抓起帽子，逃进了夜色。

他本打算开那辆双座跑车，可要去车库，就得打桐棉树下那条长凳前经过。唉，罢了——反正电车里的各色光景，本来也更有意思些。

在亚历山大·扬旅馆底层的电报局里，陈查理已经等着了，两人把发给得梅因邮政局长的查询电报发了出去，署的是约翰·昆西的名字和地址。这件事办妥，他们回到街上。对面公园里，一群看不见的青年拨弄着钢弦吉他，用轻柔萦回的嗓音唱着歌；这是檀香山仅剩的一丝生气。

「敬请屈尊随我步入旅馆大堂，」陈查理提议道，「不时浏览登记簿上的姓名，乃是本人的习惯。」

===Original===
At the cigar stand just inside the door, the boy paused to light his pipe, while Chan went on to the desk. As John Quincy turned he saw a man seated alone in the lobby, a handsome, distinguished man who wore immaculate evening clothes that bore the stamp of Bond Street. An old acquaintance, Captain Arthur Temple Cope.

At sight of John Quincy, Cope leaped to his feet and came forward. “Hello, I’m glad to see you,” he cried, with a cordiality that had not been evident at former meetings. “Come over and sit down.”

John Quincy followed him. “Aren’t you back rather soon?” he inquired.

“Sooner than I expected,” Cope rejoined. “Not sorry, either.”

“Then you didn’t care for your little flock of islands?”

===Chinese===
在门里那座雪茄柜旁，小伙子停下来点烟斗，陈查理径自朝账台走去。约翰·昆西一转身，看见大堂里独坐着一个人——一个英俊出众的男人，一身无可挑剔的晚礼服，透着伦敦邦德街名匠的印记。是位旧相识，阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩船长。

一见约翰·昆西，科佩霍地站起身，迎上前来。「你好，见到你真高兴，」他嚷道，那份热络劲儿，从前几次会面里可不曾显过。「过来坐。」

约翰·昆西跟了他过去。「您回来得不是有些早么？」他问道。

「比我预想的还早，」科佩答道，「我倒也不觉得遗憾。」

「这么说，您那一小群岛屿不合胃口？」

===Original===
“My boy, you should visit there. Thirty-five white men, two hundred and fifty natives, and a cable station. Jolly place of an evening, what?”

Chan came up, and John Quincy presented him. Captain Cope was the perfect host. “Sit down, both of you,” he urged. “Have a cigarette.” He extended a silver case.

“Thanks, I’ll stick to the pipe,” John Quincy said. Chan gravely accepted a cigarette and lighted it.

“Tell me, my boy,” Cope said when they were seated, “is there anything new on the Winterslip murder? Haven’t run down the guilty man, by any chance.”

===Chinese===
「小伙子，你真该去见识见识。三十五个白人，二百五十个土人，外加一座电报站。晚间的快活去处，是不是？」

陈查理走了过来，约翰·昆西替两人作了引见。科佩船长一副尽善尽美的主人做派。「你们两位都坐，」他劝道，「来支烟。」他递出一只银烟盒。

「多谢，我还是抽我的烟斗。」约翰·昆西说。陈查理郑重其事地接过一支香烟，点着了。

「跟我说说，小伙子，」落座之后，科佩开口道，「温特斯利普那桩凶案有什么新动静吗？该不会凑巧已经把罪人追拿归案了吧。」

===Original===
“No, not yet,” John Quincy replied.

“That’s a great pity. I﻿—er﻿—understand the police are holding a chap named Egan?”

“Yes﻿—Jim Egan, of the Reef and Palm Hotel.”

“Just what evidence have they against Egan, Mr. Winterslip?”

John Quincy was suddenly aware of Chan looking at him in a peculiar way. “Oh, they’ve dug up several things,” he answered vaguely.

===Chinese===
「没有，还没有。」约翰·昆西答道。

「那太遗憾了。我——呃——听说警方扣押着一个叫伊根的家伙？」

「是的——吉姆·伊根，『礁石棕榈』旅馆的老板。」

「他们手里究竟握着什么证据来指控伊根，温特斯利普先生？」

约翰·昆西忽然察觉，陈查理正用一种异样的目光望着他。「哦，他们翻出了几样东西。」他含含糊糊地答道。

===Original===
“Mr. Chan, you are a member of the police force,” Captain Cope went on. “Perhaps you can tell me?”

Chan’s little eyes narrowed. “Such matters are not yet presented to public,” he replied.

“Ah, yes, naturally.” Captain Cope’s tone suggested disappointment.

“You have interest in this murder, I think?” Chan said.

“Why, yes﻿—everyone out this way is puzzling about it, I fancy. The thing has so many angles.”

===Chinese===
「陈先生，您是警界中人，」科佩船长接着说，「或许您能告知一二？」

陈查理眯起小眼睛。「此类事项，尚未呈交公众。」他答道。

「啊，是了，理所当然。」科佩船长的语气里透着失望。

「依我看，您对这桩凶案颇有关注之心？」陈查理说。

「哎呀，正是——我看这一带的人，个个都在为它绞脑汁。这件事的角度太多了。」

===Original===
“Is it possible that you were an acquaintance with Mr. Dan Winterslip?” the detective persisted.

“I﻿—I knew him slightly. But that was many years ago.”

Chan stood. “Humbly begging pardon to be so abrupt,” he said. He turned to John Quincy. “The moment of our appointment is eminent﻿—”

“Of course,” agreed John Quincy. “See you again, Captain.” Perplexed, he followed Chan to the street. “What appointment﻿—” he began, and stopped. Chan was carefully extinguishing the light of the cigarette against the stone façade of the hotel. That done, he dropped the stub into his pocket.

“You will see,” he promised. “First we visit police station. As we journey, kindly relate all known facts concerning this Captain Cope.”

===Chinese===
「您从前可曾与丹·温特斯利普先生有过交往？」侦探追问道。

「我——我与他略识一二。不过那已是许多年前的事了。」

陈查理站起身来。「谦卑乞恕，如此唐突。」他说着，转向约翰·昆西。「我们约会之时刻已然迫近——」

「当然，」约翰·昆西应道，「再会，船长。」他满心疑惑，跟着陈查理走到街上。「什么约会——」他刚起头便顿住了。陈查理正把那支香烟的火头，仔仔细细摁灭在旅馆的石砌门面上。摁灭之后，他把烟蒂收进了口袋。

「您即将会知晓，」他许诺道，「我们先赴警察局。一路行去，有劳您细说有关这位科佩船长的全部已知事实。」

===Original===
John Quincy told of his first meeting with Cope in the San Francisco club, and repeated the conversation as he recalled it.

“Evidence of warm dislike for Dan Winterslip were not to be concealed?” inquired Chan.

“Oh, quite plain, Charlie. He certainly had no love for Cousin Dan. But what﻿—”

“Immediately he was leaving for Hawaii﻿—pardon the interrupt. Does it happily chance you know his date of arrival here?”

“I do. I saw him in the Alexander Young Hotel last Tuesday evening when I was looking for you. He was rushing off to the Fanning Islands, and he told me he had got in the previous day at noon﻿—”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西讲起他在旧金山俱乐部与科佩的初遇，又凭记忆复述了那番对话。

「他对丹·温特斯利普怀有热烈厌憎的证据，无法掩藏，是么？」陈查理问道。

「哦，明摆着的，查理。他对丹堂亲肯定没有半点好感。可是那又——」

「紧接着他便动身前来夏威夷——乞恕插话。您可碰巧知晓他抵达此地的日期？」

「知晓。上星期二晚上，我在亚历山大·扬旅馆里四下找您的时候，见着了他。他正要赶去范宁群岛，他告诉我，他是前一天中午到的——」

===Original===
“Monday noon to put it lucidly.”

“Yes﻿—Monday noon. But Charlie﻿—what are you trying to get at?”

“Groping about,” Chan smiled. “Seeking to seize truth in my hot hands.”

They walked on in silence to the station, where Chan led the way into the deserted room of Captain Hallet. He went directly to the safe and opened it. From a drawer he removed several small objects, which he carried over to the captain’s table.

===Chinese===
「说得明白些，星期一中午。」

「对——星期一中午。可是查理——您到底想查个什么？」

「四下摸索，」陈查理微笑道，「欲将真理攥入我滚烫的双手。」

两人默默走到警察局，陈查理领路进了哈利特警长那间空无一人的屋子。他径直走到保险柜前，开了柜门，从一只抽屉里取出几件小东西，端到警长的桌上。

===Original===
“Property Mr. Jim Egan,” he announced, and laid a case of tarnished silver before John Quincy. “Open it﻿—what do you find now? Corsican cigarettes.” He set down another exhibit. “Tin box found in room of Mr. Brade. Open that, also. You find more Corsican cigarettes.”

He removed an envelope from his pocket and taking out a charred stub, laid that too on the table. “Fragment found by walk outside door of Dan Winterslip’s mansion,” he elucidated. “Also Corsican brand.”

Frowning deeply, he removed a second charred stub from his pocket and laid it some distance from the other exhibits. “Cigarette offered just now with winning air of hospitality by Captain Arthur Temple Cope. Lean close and perceive. More Corsican brand!”

===Chinese===
「吉姆·伊根先生之财物，」他宣布道，把一只失了光泽的银烟盒摆在约翰·昆西面前。「打开——您现在发现了什么？科西嘉香烟。」他又摆下一件证物。「布雷德先生房中寻获之铁盒。也把它打开。您发现更多的科西嘉香烟。」

他从口袋里取出一只信封，拿出半截烧焦的烟蒂，也搁在桌上。「于丹·温特斯利普宅邸门外小径旁寻获之残片，」他讲解道，「亦是科西嘉牌子。」

他眉头深锁，又从口袋里取出第二截烧焦的烟蒂，摆在离其余证物稍远的地方。「方才由阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩船长以殷勤好客之迷人风度敬上之香烟。俯身细察。又是科西嘉牌子！」

===Original===
“Good lord!” John Quincy cried.

“Can it be you are familiar with these Corsicans?” inquired Chan.

“Not at all.”

“I am more happily located. This afternoon before the swim I pause at public library for listless reading. In Australian newspaper I encounter advertising talk of Corsican cigarette. It are assembled in two distinct fashions, one, labeled on tin 222, holds Turkish tobacco. Note 222 on tin of Brade. Other labeled 444 made up from Virginia weeds. Is it that you are clever to know difference between Turkish and Virginia tobacco?”

===Chinese===
「天哪！」约翰·昆西叫出声来。

「莫非您与这些科西嘉人素有深交？」陈查理问道。

「素不相识。」

「我所处的境地则幸运得多。今日下午游泳之前，我在公共图书馆驻足，作无精打采之阅读。在一份澳大利亚报纸上，我撞见了谈论科西嘉香烟的广告文字。此烟以两种不同方式配制：一种，铁盒上标着 222，装的是土耳其烟叶。请留意布雷德铁盒上的 222。另一种标着 444，由弗吉尼亚烟叶卷成。您可有本事分辨土耳其烟叶与弗吉尼亚烟叶之不同？」

===Original===
“Well, I think so﻿—” began John Quincy.

“Same with me, but thinking are not enough now. The moment are serious. We will interrogate expert opinion. Honor me by a journey to smoking emporium.”

He took a cigarette from Brack’s tin, put it in an envelope and wrote something on the outside, then did the same with one from Egan’s case. The two stubs were similarly classified.

They went in silence to the street. John Quincy, amazed by this new turn of events, told himself the idea was absurd. But Chan’s face was grave, his eyes awake and eager.

===Chinese===
「呃，我想我分得出——」约翰·昆西开口道。

「与我一样，但此刻光会想是不够的。时刻已然严重。我们将质询专家之意见。请赏光随我赴烟草商铺一游。」

他从布雷德的铁盒里取出一支烟，装进信封，在外皮上写了几个字，又从伊根的银盒里取出一支，照样办理。两截烟蒂也如此分门别类。

两人默默走到街上。约翰·昆西被这新的转折惊得目瞪口呆，暗自宽慰自己说，这念头未免荒唐。可陈查理面色凝重，双眼清醒而急切，灼灼有光。

===Original===
John Quincy was vastly more amazed when they emerged from the tobacco shop after a brisk interview with the young man in charge. Chan was jubilant now.

“Again we advance! You hear what he tells us. Cigarette from Brade’s tin and little brother from Egan’s case are of identical contents, both being of Turkish tobacco. Stub found near walk are of Virginia stuff. So also are remnant received by me from the cordial hand of Captain Arthur Temple Cope!”

“It’s beyond me,” replied John Quincy. “By gad﻿—that lets Egan out. Great news for Carlota. I’ll hurry to the Reef and Palm and tell her﻿—”

===Chinese===
同管店的年轻人干脆利落问过一番话之后，两人走出烟草店，约翰·昆西愈发惊得无以复加。陈查理这会儿已是欢欣鼓舞。

「我们又向前推进了！您听见他怎么说了。布雷德铁盒中的香烟与伊根银盒中的小兄弟，内容物一模一样，皆属土耳其烟叶。小径旁寻获之烟蒂，乃弗吉尼亚货色。由阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩船长热忱之手递到我手中的那截残烟，亦是如此！」

「这我可懵了，」约翰·昆西答道，「天哪——这下伊根脱了干系。对卡洛塔是天大的好消息。我要赶紧去『礁石棕榈』告诉她——」

===Original===
“Oh, no, no,” protested Chan. “Please to let that happy moment wait. For the present, indulge only in silence. Before asking Captain Cope for statement we spy over his every move. Much may be revealed by the unsuspecting. I go to station to make arrangements﻿—”

“But the man’s a gentleman,” John Quincy cried. “A captain in the British Admiralty. What you suggest is impossible.”

Chan shook his head. “Impossible in Rear Bay at Boston,” he said, “but here at moorly crossroads of Pacific, not so much so. Twenty-five years of my life are consumed in Hawaii, and I have many times been witness when the impossible roused itself and occurred.”

===Chinese===
「哦，不，不，」陈查理反对道，「请让那幸福的时刻再等一等。眼下，只请纵情于沉默。在请科佩船长作出陈述之前，我们先暗中盯住他的一举一动。毫无戒心之人，往往会吐露极多。我去局里作些安排——」

「可那人是个绅士，」约翰·昆西喊道，「英国海军部的船长。您这提议是不可能成立的。」

陈查理摇了摇头。「在波士顿后湾，是不可能的，」他说，「可在这太平洋上烟波茫茫的十字路口，便不尽然了。我的生命有二十五年消耗在夏威夷，曾多次亲眼见证：不可能之事奋然起身，随即发生。」

## XVII Night Life in Honolulu / 十七 檀香山的夜生活

===Original===
Monday brought no new developments, and John Quincy spent a restless day. Several times he called Chan at the police station, but the detective was always out.

Honolulu, according to the evening paper, was agog. This was not, as John Quincy learned to his surprise, a reference to the Winterslip case. An American fleet had just left the harbor of San Pedro bound for Hawaii. This was the annual cruise of the graduating class at Annapolis; the warships were overflowing with future captains and admirals. They would linger at the port of Honolulu for several days and a gay round of social events impended﻿—dinners, dances, moonlight swimming parties.

===Chinese===
星期一没有带来任何新进展，约翰·昆西在焦躁不安中过了一天。他好几次打电话到警察局找陈查理，可这位侦探总是不在。

据晚报的报道，檀香山全城正翘首若狂。约翰·昆西诧异地得知，这回轰动的话题并不是温特斯利普家的案子。一支美国舰队刚刚驶离圣佩德罗（San Pedro）港，开赴夏威夷。这是安纳波利斯海军军官学校毕业班一年一度的远航；军舰上挤满了未来的舰长与将军。他们将在檀香山港盘桓数日，一连串欢快的社交活动眼看接踵而至——宴请啊，舞会啊，还有月光泳会。

===Original===
John Quincy had not seen Barbara all day; the girl had not appeared at breakfast and had lunched with a friend down the beach. They met at dinner, however, and it seemed to him that she looked more tired and wan than ever. She spoke about the coming of the warships.

“It’s always such a happy time,” she said wistfully. “The town simply blooms with handsome boys in uniform. I don’t like to have you miss all the parties, John Quincy. You’re not seeing Honolulu at its best.”

“Why﻿—that’s all right,” John Quincy assured her.

She shook her head. “Not with me. You know, we’re not such slaves to convention out here. If I should get you a few invitations﻿—what do you think, Cousin Minerva?”

“I’m an old woman,” said Miss Minerva. “According to the standards of your generation, I suppose it would be quite the thing. But it’s not the sort of conduct I can view approvingly. Now, in my day﻿—”

“Don’t you worry, Barbara,” John Quincy broke in. “Parties mean nothing to me. Speaking of old women, I’m an old man myself﻿—thirty my next birthday. Just my pipe and slippers by the fire﻿—or the electric fan﻿—that’s all I ask of life now.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西一整天没见着芭芭拉；姑娘早饭没露面，午饭又是陪一位朋友在海滩那头吃的。不过晚餐时两人碰了面，他只觉得她看上去比以往任何时候都更疲惫、更憔悴。她谈起了军舰将至的事。

「那向来是顶快活的时光，」她怅然地说。「满城一下子开满了穿军装的俊俏小伙子。我不愿意让你错过那些舞会，约翰·昆西。你还没见识过檀香山最好的光景呢。」

「哦——没关系的。」约翰·昆西安慰她。

她摇头。「跟我在一起可不成。您知道的，我们这儿的人不那么受礼俗的辖制。要是我替你弄几张请帖来——您说呢，米纳瓦堂姑？」

「我是个老太婆了。」米纳瓦小姐说。「照你们这辈人的标准，我料想这再寻常不过。可这样的做派，我没法赞成。要在我们那个年头——」

「你别担心，芭芭拉。」约翰·昆西插进来。「舞会于我算不得什么。说起老太婆，我自己就是个老头儿了——过完下个生日就三十。守着炉火——或者电风扇——旁边搁上我的烟斗和拖鞋，这便是我如今对生活的全部所求。」

===Original===
She smiled and dropped the matter. After dinner, she followed John Quincy to the lanai. “I want you to do something for me,” she began.

“Anything you say.”

“Have a talk with Mr. Brade, and tell me what he wants.”

“Why, I thought that Jennison﻿—” said John Quincy.

“No, I didn’t ask him to do it,” she replied. For a long moment she was silent. “I ought to tell you﻿—I’m not going to marry Mr. Jennison, after all.”

===Chinese===
她笑了笑，把这事撂下了。晚饭后，她跟着约翰·昆西来到露台。「我想请你办件事。」她开了口。

「你说什么都行。」

「去找布雷德先生谈一谈，然后告诉我他要什么。」

「咦，我还以为詹尼森——」约翰·昆西说。

「不，我没托他办这件事。」她答道。好半晌，她一言不发。「我该告诉你——我终究不打算嫁给詹尼森先生了。」

===Original===
A shiver of apprehension ran down John Quincy’s spine. Good lord﻿—that kiss! Had she misunderstood? And he hadn’t meant a thing by it. Just a cousinly salute﻿—at least, that was what it had started out to be. Barbara was a sweet girl, yes, but a relative, a Winterslip, and relatives shouldn’t marry, no matter how distant the connection. Then, too, there was Agatha. He was bound to Agatha by all the ties of honor. What had he got himself into, anyhow?

“I’m awfully sorry to hear that,” he said. “I’m afraid I’m to blame﻿—”

“Oh, no,” she protested.

“But surely Mr. Jennison understood. He knows we’re related, and that what he saw last night meant﻿—nothing.” He was rather proud of himself. Pretty neat the way he’d got that over.

“If you don’t mind,” Barbara said, “I’d rather not talk about it any more. Harry and I will not be married﻿—not at present, at any rate. And if you’ll see Mr. Brade for me﻿—”

“I certainly will,” John Quincy promised. “I’ll see him at once.” He was glad to get away, for the moon was rising on that “spot of heartbreaking charm.”

===Chinese===
一阵惊惧顺着约翰·昆西的脊梁骨直溜下去。老天爷——那一吻！她会错意了？而他原本没有半分意思。不过是亲戚间的一下致意——至少，起初是那样的。芭芭拉是个可爱的姑娘，不错，可到底是亲戚，是温特斯利普家的人，而亲戚之间不该谈婚论嫁，任凭血缘隔得多远都不成。再说，还有阿加莎呢。他受道义上重重纽带的约束，是阿加莎的人。他到底把自己弄到什么田地了？

「听了这消息我很难过。」他说。「恐怕这得怪我——」

「哦，不。」她分辩道。

「可詹尼森先生总归是明白的。他知道我们是亲戚，也知道他昨晚看见的那一幕——毫无意思。」他倒有点儿为自己得意。这话说得干净利落。

「你要是不介意，」芭芭拉说，「我宁愿不再谈这件事了。哈里和我不结婚了——至少眼下不结。要是你肯替我去见布雷德先生——」

「我一定去。」约翰·昆西许诺道。「我这就去见他。」他庆幸能脱身走开，因为月亮正冉冉升起，照着那处「令人心痛神驰的胜境」。

===Original===
A fellow ought to be more careful, he reflected as he walked along the beach. Fit upon himself the armor of preparation, as Chan had said. Strange impulses came to one here in this far tropic land; to yield to them was weak. Complications would follow, as the night the day. Here was one now, Barbara and Jennison estranged, and the cause was clear. Well, he was certainly going to watch his step hereafter.

On the far end of the Reef and Palm’s first floor balcony, Brade and his wife sat together in the dusk. John Quincy went up to them.

===Chinese===
一个男人该更加小心才是，他沿着海滩一路走一路想。像陈查理说过的那样，给自己披挂上戒备的铠甲。在这遥远的热带地方，人会涌起种种古怪的冲动；向冲动屈服便是软弱。麻烦势必随之而来，就像黑夜接着白昼一样确凿。眼前便是一桩：芭芭拉和詹尼森疏远了，缘由一清二楚。好吧，往后他可要处处留神自己的脚步了。

『礁石棕榈』一楼露台的尽头，布雷德和他的太太在暮色里并肩坐着。约翰·昆西朝他们走去。

===Original===
“May I speak with you, Mr. Brade?” he said.

The man looked up out of a deep reverie. “Ah, yes﻿—of course﻿—”

“I’m John Quincy Winterslip. We’ve met before.”

“Oh, surely, surely sir.” Brade rose and shook hands. “My dear﻿—” he turned to his wife, but with one burning glance at John Quincy, the woman had fled. The boy tingled﻿—in Boston a Winterslip was never snubbed. Well, Dan Winterslip had arranged it otherwise in Hawaii.

===Chinese===
「能同您谈谈吗，布雷德先生？」他说。

那人从深沉的冥想里抬起头来。「啊，是的——当然——」

「我是约翰·昆西·温特斯利普。咱们从前见过面。」

「哦，正是，正是，先生。」布雷德起身握手。「亲爱的——」他转向太太，可那女人朝约翰·昆西投去灼灼的一瞥，人已经躲出去了。小伙子只觉脸上火辣辣的——在波士顿，温特斯利普家的人可从来没受过这种冷遇。唉，在夏威夷，丹·温特斯利普把这一切弄成了另一副样子。

===Original===
“Sit down, sir,” said Brade, somewhat embarrassed by his wife’s action. “I’ve been expecting someone of your name.”

“Naturally. Will you have a cigarette, sir.” John Quincy proffered his case, and when the cigarettes were lighted, seated himself at the man’s side. “I’m here, of course, in regard to that story you told Saturday night.”

“Story?” flashed Brade.

===Chinese===
「请坐，先生。」布雷德说，太太的举动叫他有些窘迫。「我一直在候着贵府的人来。」

「想必如此。请吸烟，先生。」约翰·昆西递上烟盒，两人点着了烟，他在那人身边坐下。「我此来，自然是为您星期六晚上讲的那套说法。」

「说法？」布雷德一下子冒了火。

===Original===
John Quincy smiled. “Don’t misunderstand me. I’m not questioning the truth of it. But I do want to say this, Mr. Brade﻿—you must be aware that you will have considerable difficulty establishing your claim in a court of law. The ’eighties are a long time back.”

“What you say may be true,” Brade agreed. “I’m relying more on the fact that a trial would result in some rather unpleasant publicity for the Winterslip family.”

“Precisely,” nodded John Quincy. “I am here at the request of Miss Barbara Winterslip, who is Dan Winterslip’s sole heir. She’s a very fine girl, sir﻿—”

“I don’t question that,” cut in Brade impatiently.

“And if your demands are not unreasonable﻿—” John Quincy paused, and leaned closer. “Just what do you want, Mr. Brade?”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西微微一笑。「别误会我。我不是质疑它是否属实。不过我确实要说一句，布雷德先生——您必定明白，要在法庭上确立您的主张，您会遇上相当大的难处。八十年代已是久远的事了。」

「您说的许是实情。」布雷德表示同意。「我更多的指望在于：一场官司会替温特斯利普家招来相当难堪的报端风闻。」

「正是。」约翰·昆西点头。「我此来是受芭芭拉·温特斯利普小姐之托，她是丹·温特斯利普唯一的继承人。她是位极好的姑娘，先生——」

「这我不置疑。」布雷德不耐烦地插进来。

「倘若您的要求不算过分——」约翰·昆西顿了顿，凑近了些。「您究竟想要什么，布雷德先生？」

===Original===
Brade stroked those gray mustaches that drooped “in saddened mood.” “No money,” he said, “can make good the wrong Dan Winterslip did. But I’m an old man, and it would be something to feel financially secure for the rest of my life. I’m not inclined to be grasping﻿—particularly since Dan Winterslip has passed beyond my reach. There were twenty thousand pounds involved. I’ll say nothing about interest for more than forty years. A settlement of one hundred thousand dollars would be acceptable.”

John Quincy considered. “I can’t speak definitely for my cousin,” he said, “but to me that sounds fair enough. I have no doubt Barbara will agree to give you that sum”﻿—he saw the man’s tired old eyes brighten in the semidarkness﻿—“the moment the murderer of Dan Winterslip is found,” he added quickly.

“What’s that you say?” Brade leaped to his feet.

===Chinese===
布雷德捋着那两撇「在悲戚的心绪中」垂落的灰白胡子。「什么钱财，」他说，「都补不起丹·温特斯利普造下的罪孽。可我是个老人了，后半辈子若能在钱财上有个安稳，总算是一点指望。我无意贪婪勒索——何况丹·温特斯利普已经去到我够不着的地方。当年这笔钱是两万英镑。至于四十多年来的利息，我就一字不提了。十万美元的了结，是可以接受的。」

约翰·昆西斟酌片刻。「我无法替我堂妹把话说死，」他说，「不过在我听来，这够公道的了。我毫不怀疑芭芭拉会同意把这笔钱付给您」——他看见那老人疲惫的眼睛在半明半暗里亮了起来——「就在丹·温特斯利普的凶手被拿获的那一刻。」他赶忙补上一句。

「你说什么？」布雷德腾地跳起身。

===Original===
“I say she’ll very likely pay you when this mystery is cleared up. Surely you don’t expect her to do so before that time?” John Quincy rose too.

“I certainly do!” Brade cried. “Why, look here, this thing may drag on indefinitely. I want England again﻿—the Strand, Piccadilly﻿—it’s twenty-five years since I saw London. Wait! Damn it, why should I wait! What’s this murder to me﻿—by gad, sir﻿—” He came close, erect, flaming, the son of Tom Brade, the blackbirder, now. “Do you mean to insinuate that I﻿—”

John Quincy faced him calmly. “I know you can’t prove where you were early last Tuesday morning,” he said evenly. “I don’t say that incriminates you, but I shall certainly advise my cousin to wait. I’d not care to see her in the position of having rewarded the man who killed her father.”

“I’ll fight,” cried Brade. “I’ll take it to the courts﻿—”

“Go ahead,” John Quincy said. “But it will cost you every penny you’ve saved, and you may lose in the end. Good night, sir.”

“Good night!” Brade answered, standing as his father might have stood on the *Maid of Shiloh*’s deck.

===Chinese===
「我是说，这桩疑案水落石出之后，她多半会付给您的。您总不至于指望她在那之前就付吧？」约翰·昆西也站了起来。

「我正是这么指望的！」布雷德嚷道。「咳，你听着，这案子没准会没完没了地拖下去。我要回英国——斯特兰德大街，皮卡迪利——我有二十五年没见伦敦了。等！见鬼，凭什么要我等！这桩凶案与我什么相干——老天在上，先生——」他逼近前来，腰杆笔挺，怒火熊熊，此刻他成了贩奴船员汤姆·布雷德的儿子。「你莫不是想含沙射影，说我——」

约翰·昆西安然面对着他。「我知道您拿不出凭据，证明上星期二凌晨您身在何处。」他平静地说。「我并不是说这就定了您的罪，但我肯定要劝我堂妹等一等。我不愿眼看她落到那步田地——酬谢了杀害她父亲的凶手。」

「我要斗。」布雷德喊道。「我要把这官司打到法庭上去——」

「悉听尊便。」约翰·昆西说。「可这会耗尽您攒下的每一个铜板，到头来您兴许还要输。晚安，先生。」

「晚安！」布雷德应道，那站姿，俨然他父亲当年立在希洛少女号甲板上的模样。

===Original===
John Quincy had gone halfway down the balcony when he heard quick footsteps behind him. He turned. It was Brade, Brade the civil servant, the man who had labored thirty-six years in the oven of India, a beaten, helpless figure.

“You’ve got me,” he said, laying a hand on John Quincy’s arm. “I can’t fight. I’m too tired, too old﻿—I’ve worked too hard. I’ll take whatever your cousin wants to give me﻿—when she’s ready to give it.”

“That’s a wise decision, sir,” John Quincy answered. A sudden feeling of pity gripped his heart. He felt toward Brade as he had felt toward that other exile, Arlene Compton. “I hope you see London very soon,” he added, and held out his hand.

Brade took it. “Thank you, my boy. You’re a gentleman, even if your name is Winterslip.”

Which, John Quincy reflected as he entered the lobby of the Reef and Palm, was a compliment not without its flaw.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西沿露台走出去一半，听见身后响起急促的脚步声。他回过头。是布雷德——文官布雷德，在印度的火炉里熬煎了三十六年的那个人，一副被打垮了的、无可奈何的身影。

「您把我拿捏住了。」他说着，把一只手搭上约翰·昆西的手臂。「我斗不动了。我太累，太老——我这辈子苦干得太凶了。您堂妹愿意给什么我就收什么——等她乐意给的时候。」

「这是个明智的决定，先生。」约翰·昆西答道。一阵突如其来的怜悯攫住了他的心。他对布雷德生出的这番情意，正如当初对另一个流落者阿琳·康普顿的那样。「愿您很快见到伦敦。」他添了一句，伸出手去。

布雷德握住了。「谢谢您，我的孩子。您是位君子，哪怕您姓的偏偏是温特斯利普。」

走进『礁石棕榈』的大堂时，约翰·昆西暗自思忖，这句恭维话并非全无瑕疵。

===Original===
He didn’t worry over that long, however, for Carlota Egan was behind the desk. She looked up and smiled, and it occurred to John Quincy that her eyes were happier than he had seen them since that day on the Oakland ferry.

“Hello,” he said. “Got a job for a good bookkeeper?”

She shook her head. “Not with business the way it is now. I was just figuring my payroll. You know, we’ve no undertow at Waikiki, but all my life I’ve had to worry about the overhead.”

He laughed. “You talk like a brother Kiwanian. By the way, has anything happened? You seem considerably cheered.”

“I am,” she replied. “I went to see poor dad this morning in that horrible place﻿—and when I left someone else was going in to visit him. A stranger.”

===Chinese===
不过这事他没有多想，因为卡洛塔·伊根正坐在账台后面。她抬头嫣然一笑，约翰·昆西忽然觉得，自奥克兰渡轮那一日以来，还没见她的眼睛这般欢快过。

「你好。」他说。「有差事给一位出色的管账人吗？」

她摇头。「生意眼下这光景，可请不起。我方才正在算工资单呢。你是知道的，怀基基的海里没有回头浪，可我这一辈子，总得为开销发愁。」

他笑了。「听你说这话，倒像基瓦尼会（Kiwanis，商人社团）里的弟兄了。对了，出什么事了吗？你像是大大开心了。」

「是开心了。」她答道。「今天上午我到那可怕的地方去探了可怜的爸爸——我走的时候，正有另一个人要进去看他。一个陌生人。」

===Original===
“A stranger?”

“Yes﻿—and the handsomest thing you ever saw﻿—tall, gray, capable-looking. He had such a friendly air, too﻿—I felt better the moment I saw him.”

“Who was he?” John Quincy inquired, with sudden interest.

“I’d never seen him before, but one of the men told me he was Captain Cope, of the British Admiralty.”

===Chinese===
「陌生人？」

「是的——而且是你从来没见过的那么个漂亮人物——高个子，头发花白，一副精明干练的相貌。他神情又是那样和蔼——我一见他，心里就松快了。」

「他是谁？」约翰·昆西问道，蓦然起了兴致。

「我从前没见过他，不过有个男的告诉我，他是英国海军部的科佩船长。」

===Original===
“Why should Captain Cope want to see your father?”

“I haven’t a notion. Do you know him?”

“Yes﻿—I’ve met him,” John Quincy told her.

“Don’t you think he’s wonderful-looking?” Her dark eyes glowed.

“Oh, he’s all right,” replied John Quincy without enthusiasm. “You know, I can’t help feeling that things are looking up for you.”

===Chinese===
「科佩船长为什么要见你父亲？」

「我一点儿也摸不着头脑。你认识他？」

「认识——见过面的。」约翰·昆西告诉她。

「你不觉得他相貌堂堂吗？」她那双黑眼睛熠熠发亮。

「哦，他倒是挺好。」约翰·昆西答道，兴致缺缺。「你知道，我不由得觉得，你家的光景正在好转呢。」

===Original===
“I feel that too,” she said.

“What do you say we celebrate?” he suggested. “Go out among ’em and get a little taste of night life. I’m a bit fed up on the police station. What do people do here in the evening? The movies?”

“Just at present,” the girl told him, “everybody visits Punahou to see the night-blooming cereus. It’s the season now, you know.”

“Sounds like a big evening,” John Quincy laughed. “Go and look at the flowers. Well, I’m for it. Will you come?”

“Of course.” She gave a few directions to the clerk, then joined him by the door. “I can run down and get the roadster,” he offered.

“Oh, no,” she smiled. “I’m sure I’ll never own a motorcar, and it might make me discontented to ride in one. The trolley’s my carriage﻿—and it’s lots of fun. One meets so many interesting people.”

===Chinese===
「我也有这个感觉。」她说。

「咱们庆祝一下去，怎么样？」他提议。「到大伙儿中间去，尝一点夜生活的滋味。警察局我可有点儿受够了。这儿的人晚上都干些什么？看电影？」

「眼下这个时节，」姑娘告诉他，「人人都去普纳荷（Punahou）看夜开的昙花。现在正是花期，你知道的。」

「听起来真是个盛大的夜晚。」约翰·昆西笑道。「去看看花。好啊，我赞成。你去不去？」

「当然去。」她向职员交代了几句，随后到门口同他会合。「我可以跑下去把跑车开来。」他主动说。

「哦，不用。」她笑了。「我准知道自己这辈子永远买不起汽车，坐上一回，怕就要心有不甘了。有轨电车就是我的马车——而且乐趣多着呢。能遇上多少有意思的人物啊。」

===Original===
On the stone walls surrounding the campus of Oahu College, the strange flower that blooms only on a summer night was heaped in snowy splendor. John Quincy had been a bit lukewarm regarding the expedition when they set out, but he saw his error now. For here was beauty, breathtaking and rare. Before the walls paraded a throng of sightseers; they joined the procession. The girl was a charming companion, her spirits had revived and she chatted vivaciously. Not about Shaw and the art galleries, true enough, but bright human talk that John Quincy liked to hear.

He persuaded her to go to the city for a maidenly ice-cream soda, and it was ten o’clock when they returned to the beach. They left the trolley at a stop some distance down the avenue from the Reef and Palm, and strolled slowly toward the hotel. The sidewalk was lined at their right by dense foliage, almost impenetrable. The night was calm; the street lamps shone brightly; the paved street gleamed white in the moonlight. John Quincy was talking of Boston.

===Chinese===
欧胡学院（Oahu College）校园四周的石墙上，那种只在夏夜开放的奇花堆叠出一片雪白的盛景。出发时约翰·昆西对这趟出游还有三分冷淡，此刻方知自己错了。因为眼前是美，令人屏息的、稀世的美。墙前徐行着一队队观光的人；他们也加入了这支行列。姑娘是位可人的旅伴，她兴致复苏，谈笑风生。诚然，谈的不是萧伯纳和美术馆，却是约翰·昆西爱听的、鲜活明亮的人间话语。

他说动她进城喝了一客规规矩矩的冰淇淋苏打，两人回到海滩时已是十点钟。他们在离『礁石棕榈』还隔着一大截路的大道边一个车站下了电车，朝旅馆缓缓踱去。人行道右侧是一列浓密的草木，几乎密不透人。夜静无风；街灯灼灼；铺过的路面在月光下泛着白光。约翰·昆西正谈着波士顿。

===Original===
“I think you’d like it there. It’s old and settled, but﻿—”

From the foliage beside them came the flash of a pistol, and John Quincy heard a bullet sing close to his head. Another flash, another bullet. The girl gave a startled little cry.

John Quincy circled round her and plunged into the bushes. Angry branches stung his cheek. He stopped; he couldn’t leave the girl alone. He returned to her side.

“What did that mean?” he asked, amazed. He stared in wonder at the peaceful scene before him.

===Chinese===
「我想你会喜欢那儿的。那座城古老而安稳，不过——」

身旁的草木丛中倏地迸出手枪的火光，约翰·昆西听见一颗子弹贴着他的头呼啸而过。又是一道火光，又是一颗子弹。姑娘发出一声吃惊的轻呼。

约翰·昆西绕过她，一头扎进灌木丛。恼怒的枝条刺痛了他的脸颊。他停住了；总不能把姑娘一个人撂下。他回到她身边。

「这是什么意思？」他愕然问道。他惊疑不定地凝望着眼前一派安宁的景象。

===Original===
“I﻿—I don’t know.” She took his arm. “Come﻿—hurry!”

“Don’t be afraid,” he said reassuringly.

“Not for myself,” she answered.

===Chinese===
「我——我不知道。」她挽住他的手臂。「走——快！」

「别怕。」他宽慰道。

「不是为我自己。」她答道。

===Original===
They went on to the hotel, greatly puzzled. But when they entered the lobby, they had something else to think about. Captain Arthur Temple Cope was standing by the desk, and he came at once to meet them.

“This is Miss Egan, I believe. Ah, Winterslip, how are you?” He turned again to the girl. “I’ve taken a room here, if you don’t mind.”

“Why, not at all,” she gasped.

“I talked with your father this morning. I didn’t know about his trouble until I had boarded a ship for the Fanning Islands. I came back as quickly as I could.”

“You came back﻿—” She stared at him.

===Chinese===
两人满腹狐疑地走到了旅馆。可一进大堂，他们便有别的事要思量了。阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩船长正立在账台旁，一见他们便迎上前来。

「这位想必就是伊根小姐。啊，温特斯利普，你好吗？」他又转向姑娘。「我在这儿开了个房间，你不介意吧。」

「哦，一点儿也不。」她喘着气说。

「今天上午我同你父亲谈过了。我登上去范宁群岛（Fanning Islands）的船之后，才听说他遭了难。我便尽快赶了回来。」

「您赶回来了——」她怔怔地望着他。

===Original===
“Yes. I came back to help him.”

“That’s very kind of you,” the girl said. “But I’m afraid I don’t understand﻿—”

“Oh, no, you don’t understand. Naturally.” The captain smiled down at her. “You see, Jim’s my young brother. You’re my niece, and your name is Carlota Maria Cope. I fancy I’ve persuaded old Jim to own up to us at last.”

===Chinese===
「是的。我赶回来帮他。」

「您真是太好心了。」姑娘说。「只怕我不明白——」

「哦，是啊，你不明白。这也自然。」船长含笑俯视着她。「你瞧，吉姆是我的弟弟。你是我的侄女，你的名字是卡洛塔·玛丽亚·科佩。我想我总算劝得老吉姆肯认下我们这门亲了。」

===Original===
The girl’s dark eyes were wide. “I﻿—I think you’re a very nice uncle,” she said at last.

“Do you really?” The captain bowed. “I aim to be,” he added.

John Quincy stepped forward. “Pardon me,” he said. “I’m afraid I’m intruding. Good night, Captain.”

“Good night, my boy,” Cope answered.

===Chinese===
姑娘那双黑眼睛睁得大大的。「我——我觉得您是位非常好的伯父。」她终于说道。

「当真？」船长躬身一礼。「我正有志于此呢。」他补了一句。

约翰·昆西走上前来。「告罪了。」他说。「恐怕是我搅扰了二位。晚安，船长。」

「晚安，我的孩子。」科佩答道。

===Original===
The girl went with John Quincy to the balcony. “I﻿—don’t know what to make of it,” she said.

“Things are coming rather fast,” John Quincy admitted. He remembered the Corsican cigarette. “I wouldn’t trust him too far,” he admonished.

“But he’s so wonderful﻿—”

“Oh, he’s all right, probably. But looks are often deceptive. I’ll go along now and let you talk with him.”

===Chinese===
姑娘陪约翰·昆西走到露台上。「我——真不知道这事该怎么看。」她说。

「事情来得太快了些。」约翰·昆西承认。他记起那支科西嘉香烟。「对他可别信得太深。」他告诫道。

「可他那么出众——」

「哦，他多半没问题。可相貌往往是靠不住的。我这就走，你好同他谈。」

===Original===
She laid one slim tanned hand on his white-clad arm. “Do be careful!”

“Oh, I’m all right,” he told her.

“But someone shot at you.”

“Yes, and a very poor aim he had, too. Don’t worry about me.” She was very close, her eyes glowing in the dark. “You said you weren’t afraid for yourself,” he added. “Did you mean﻿—”

“I meant﻿—I was afraid﻿—for you.”

===Chinese===
她把一只苗条的、晒得黝黑的手搭在他那白衣的臂膀上。「千万小心！」

「哦，我没事的。」他对她说。

「可是有人朝你开了枪。」

「是啊，何况他的枪法也糟透了。别替我担心。」她离得那么近，眼睛在黑暗里灼灼放光。「你说过你不是为自己害怕。」他添了一句。「你的意思是——」

「我的意思是——我害怕的——是你。」

===Original===
The moon, of course, was shining. The coco-palms turned their heads away at the suggestion of the trades. The warm waters of Waikiki murmured nearby. John Quincy Winterslip, from Boston and immune, drew the girl to him and kissed her. Not a cousinly kiss, either﻿—but why should it have been? She wasn’t his cousin.

“Thank you, my dear,” he said. He seemed to be floating dizzily in space. It came to him that he might reach out and pluck her a handful of stars.

It came to him a second later that, despite his firm resolve, he had done it again. Kissed another girl. Three﻿—that made three with whom he was sort of entangled.

“Good night,” he said huskily, and leaping over the rail, fled hastily through the garden.

===Chinese===
月亮自然正照着。椰子树感应着信风的示意，把头别了过去。怀基基温暖的海水在近旁絮语。约翰·昆西·温特斯利普——那位波士顿来的、免疫的人——把姑娘揽到身边，吻了她。这回可不是亲戚式的一吻——可它又何必是呢？她又不是他的堂妹。

「谢谢你，亲爱的。」他说。他仿佛晕晕乎乎地飘浮在太空里。他忽然觉得，自己一伸手，也许能替她摘下一捧星星来。

一秒钟后他才醒悟过来：尽管决心下得铁定，他又干了一回。吻了另一个姑娘。三个——这样算来，跟他有几分纠缠的姑娘已有三个了。

「晚安。」他嗓音沙哑地说，随即翻身跃过栏杆，仓皇地穿过花园逃走了。

===Original===
Three girls now﻿—but he hadn’t a single regret. He was living at last. As he hurried through the dark along the beach, his heart was light. Once he fancied he was being followed, but he gave it little thought. What of it?

On the bureau in his room he found an envelope with his name typewritten on the outside. The note within was typewritten too. He read:

===Chinese===
如今是三个姑娘了——可他一丝一毫的懊悔也没有。他总算活出滋味来了。他沿着海滩在黑暗中匆匆而行，心头轻快。有一阵他恍惚觉得身后有人跟踪，但他没大放在心上。跟就跟吧？

在自己房间的五斗柜上，他发现一只信封，封面上用打字机打着他的名字。里面的字条也是打字的。他读到：

===Original===
> “You are too busy out here. Hawaii can manage her affairs without the interference of a malihini. Boats sail almost daily. If you are still here forty-eight hours after you get this﻿—look out! Tonight’s shots were fired into the air. The aim will quickly improve!”

===Chinese===
> 「你在这儿管的事太多了。夏威夷自己的事务，用不着一个玛利希尼来插手。开船的日子几乎天天都有。你接到此信四十八小时之后，要是还在这儿——小心！今晚的枪是朝天上放的。枪法很快就会见长！」

===Original===
Delighted, John Quincy tossed the note aside. Threatening him, eh? His activities as a detective were bearing fruit. He recalled the glowering face of Kaohla when he said: “You did this. I don’t forget.” And a remark of Dan Winterslip’s his aunt had quoted: “Civilized﻿—yes. But far underneath there are deep dark waters flowing still.”

Boats were sailing almost daily, were they? Well, let them sail. He would be on one some day﻿—but not until he had brought Dan Winterslip’s murderer to justice.

Life had a new glamour now. Look out? He’d be looking﻿—and enjoying it, too. He smiled happily to himself as he took off his coat. This was better than selling bonds in Boston.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西喜滋滋地把字条丢在一边。威胁他，嗯？他干侦探的这番营生开花结果了。他想起考赫拉怒目而视的那张脸，那小子当时说：「这是你干的。我不会忘记。」又想起姑婆引用过的丹·温特斯利普的一句话：「文明——是的。可在那底下深处，幽暗的深水依然在流淌。」

开船的日子几乎天天都有，是吗？好哇，让它们开去。总有一天他也会搭上一艘——但那得等他把杀害丹·温特斯利普的凶手缉拿归案之后。

生活如今有了一层新的光彩。小心？他会小心的——而且还要乐在其中呢。他脱下外衣，独自快活地微笑起来。这可比在波士顿卖债券强多了。

## XVIII A Cable from the Mainland / 十八 来自大陆的电报

===Original===
John Quincy awoke at nine the following morning, and slipped from under his mosquito netting eager to face the responsibilities of a new day. On the floor near his bureau lay the letter designed to speed the parting guest. He picked it up and read it again with manifest enjoyment.

When he reached the dining-room Haku informed him that Miss Minerva and Barbara had breakfasted early and gone to the city on a shopping tour.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨九点，约翰·昆西醒来，从蚊帐底下钻出，急欲迎接新一天的重任。五斗柜旁的地板上，躺着那封专诚催客上路的书信。他把它拾起来，又读了一遍，读得津津有味。

他到饭厅时，哈库禀报说，米纳瓦小姐和芭芭拉一早用过早餐，进城购物去了。

===Original===
“Look here, Haku,” the boy said. “A letter came for me late last night?”

“Yes-s,” admitted Haku.

“Who delivered it?”

“Can not say. It were found on floor of hall close by big front door.”

“Who found it?”

“Kamaikui.”

“Oh, yes—Kamaikui.”

“I tell her to put in your sleeping room.”

“Did Kamaikui see the person who brought it?”

“Nobody see him. Nobody on place.”

“All right,” John Quincy said.

===Chinese===
「我说，哈库，」小伙子开口道，「昨天夜里很晚，有人给我送来一封信？」

「是——」哈库承认道。

「谁送来的？」

「说不上来。信是在前厅地上捡到的，紧挨着那扇大门。」

「谁捡到的？」

「卡迈库伊。」

「哦，对——卡迈库伊。」

「我叫她放进您睡房去的。」

「卡迈库伊瞧见送信的人了吗？」

「没人瞧见他。宅里一个人也没有。」

「好了。」约翰·昆西说。

===Original===
He spent a leisurely hour on the lanai with his pipe and the morning paper. At about half past ten he got out the roadster and drove to the police station.

Hallet and Chan, he was told, were in a conference with the prosecutor. He sat down to wait, and in a few moments word came for him to join them. Entering Greene’s office, he saw the three men seated gloomily about the prosecutor’s desk.

===Chinese===
他叼着烟斗、看着晨报，在露台上悠然消磨了一个钟头。十点半左右，他开出那辆双座跑车，向警察局驶去。

有人告诉他，哈利特和陈查理正与检察官会谈。他坐下等候，不多时便传话请他进去。走进格林的办公室，只见三个人神色阴沉地围坐在检察官的办公桌旁。

===Original===
“Well, I guess I’m some detective,” he announced.
