Greene looked up quickly. “Found anything new?”

“Not precisely,” John Quincy admitted. “But last night when I was walking along Kalakaua Avenue with a young woman, somebody took a couple of wild shots at me from the bushes. And when I got home I found this letter waiting.”

He handed the epistle to Hallet, who read it with evident disgust, then passed it on to the prosecutor. “That doesn’t get us anywhere,” the captain said.

“It may get me somewhere, if I’m not careful,” John Quincy replied. “However, I’m rather proud of it. Sort of goes to show that my detective work is hitting home.”

“Maybe,” answered Hallet, carelessly.

===Chinese===
「嘿，我瞧自己也算得上号侦探了。」他扬声宣布。

格林霍地抬起头。「有新发现？」

「倒也说不上。」约翰·昆西承认道。「不过昨晚我陪一位年轻姑娘沿卡拉卡瓦大道散步，有人从树丛里朝我胡乱开了两枪。等我到家，又发现这么一封信等着我。」

他把那封书信递给哈利特，哈利特一脸厌恶地读完，又转递给检察官。「这玩意儿帮不了咱们什么忙。」警长说。

「一个不留神，它倒可能把我送到什么地方去呢。」约翰·昆西答道。「不过我颇以为荣。多少可以说明，我这侦探的营生正打在要害上。」

「也许吧。」哈利特漫不经心地应道。

===Original===
Greene laid the letter on his desk. “My advice to you,” he said, “is to carry a gun. That’s unofficial, of course.”

“Nonsense, I’m not afraid,” John Quincy told him. “I’ve got a pretty good idea who sent this thing.”

“You have?” Greene said.

“Yes. He’s a friend of Captain Hallet’s. Dick Kaohla.”

“What do you mean he’s a friend of mine?” flared Hallet.

“Well, you certainly treated him pretty tenderly the other night.”

“I knew what I was doing,” said Hallet grouchily.

“I hope you did. But if he puts a bullet in me some lovely evening, I’m going to be pretty annoyed with you.”

“Oh, you’re in no danger,” Hallet answered. “Only a coward writes anonymous letters.”

“Yes, and only a coward shoots from ambush. But that isn’t saying he can’t take a good aim.”

===Chinese===
格林把信搁在桌上。「我对你的忠告，」他说，「是随身带支枪。当然，这不算官方意见。」

「胡说，我不怕。」约翰·昆西告诉他。「这玩意儿是谁送的，我心里颇有数。」

「你有数？」格林说。

「有。他是哈利特警长的朋友。迪克·考赫拉。」

「你说他是我的朋友，这是什么话？」哈利特火了起来。

「嘿，那天晚上你待他可是够温柔的了。」

「我清楚自己在干什么。」哈利特没好气地说。

「但愿如此。可他要是哪个美妙的黄昏赏我一颗子弹，我对你可就要大为光火了。」

「哦，你不会有危险的。」哈利特答道。「只有懦夫才写匿名信。」

「不错，也只有懦夫才躲在暗处放枪。可这并不等于说，他就瞄不准了。」

===Original===
Hallet picked up the letter. “I’ll keep this. It may prove to be evidence.”

“Surely,” agreed John Quincy. “And you haven’t got any too much evidence, as I see it.”

“Is that so?” growled Hallet. “We’ve made a rather important discovery about that Corsican cigarette.”

“Oh, I’m not saying Charlie isn’t good,” smiled John Quincy. “I was with him when he worked that out.”

===Chinese===
哈利特把信拾起来。「这个我留下。说不定能充个证据。」

「那是自然。」约翰·昆西表示同意。「而且依我看，你们的证据本来就不算宽裕。」

「是吗？」哈利特咆哮道。「关于那支科西嘉香烟，我们已经有了一个相当重要的发现。」

「哦，我不是说查理不厉害。」约翰·昆西微笑道。「他琢磨出这一层时，我就在旁边。」

===Original===
A uniformed man appeared at the door. “Egan and his daughter and Captain Cope,” he announced to Greene. “Want to see them now, sir?”

“Send them in,” ordered the prosecutor.

“I’d like to stay, if you don’t mind,” John Quincy suggested.

“Oh, by all means,” Greene answered. “We couldn’t get along without you.”

The policeman brought Egan to the door, and the proprietor of the Reef and Palm came into the room. His face was haggard and pale; his long siege with the authorities had begun to tell. But a stubborn light still flamed in his eyes. After him came Carlota Egan, fresh and beautiful, and with a new air of confidence about her. Captain Cope followed, tall, haughty, a man of evident power and determination.

===Chinese===
一名穿制服的警员出现在门口。「伊根和他女儿，还有科佩船长。」他向格林通报。「现在就见他们吗，先生？」

「让他们进来。」检察官吩咐道。

「我想留下听听，你们不介意吧。」约翰·昆西提议道。

「哦，尽管请便。」格林答道。「少了您，我们可办不成事。」

警察把伊根领到门口，「礁石棕榈」的老板走进了房间。他面容憔悴苍白；与当局的这一场长期围困，已开始在他身上显出痕迹。但他眼中仍燃着一团倔强的光。随后进来的是卡洛塔·伊根，娇嫩美丽，周身透着一股新的自信。科佩船长跟在最后，身材高大，神态倨傲，一看便是个有权势、有决断的人物。

===Original===
“This is the prosecutor, I believe?” he said. “Ah, Mr. Winterslip; I find you everywhere I go.”

“You don’t mind my staying?” inquired John Quincy.

“Not in the least, my boy. Our business here will take but a moment.” He turned to Greene. “Just as a preliminary,” he continued, “I am Captain Arthur Temple Cope of the British Admiralty, and this gentleman”—he nodded toward the proprietor of the Reef and Palm—“is my brother.”

“Really?” said Greene. “His name is Egan, as I understand it.”

“His name is James Egan Cope,” the captain replied. “He dropped the Cope many years ago for reasons that do not concern us now. I am here simply to say, sir, that you are holding my brother on the flimsiest pretext I have ever encountered in the course of my rather extensive travels. If necessary, I propose to engage the best lawyer in Honolulu and have him free by night. But I’m giving you this last chance to release him and avoid a somewhat painful exposé of the sort of nonsense you go in for.”

John Quincy glanced at Carlota Egan. Her eyes were shining but not on him. They were on her uncle.

===Chinese===
「这位想必就是检察官？」他说。「啊，温特斯利普先生；我走到哪儿都能遇见您。」

「您不介意我留下？」约翰·昆西问道。

「一点也不，我的孩子。我们在这儿的事只需片刻工夫。」他转向格林。「先做个开场白，」他接着说，「我是英国海军部的阿瑟·坦普尔·科佩船长，而这一位」——他朝「礁石棕榈」的老板点了点头——「是我的弟弟。」

「当真？」格林说。「据我所知，他姓伊根。」

「他的名字是詹姆斯·伊根·科佩。」船长答道。「多年前他丢掉了科佩这个姓，缘由与我们此刻的事无关。我在这里只想说一句，先生：你们扣留我弟弟所凭的借口，是我相当广泛的游历中所遇到过的最站不住脚的一个。如有必要，我打算聘请檀香山最好的律师，天黑之前就让他获释。不过我先给你这最后一个机会——放了他，免得你们惯玩的那套把戏，遭到一番颇为难堪的曝光。」

约翰·昆西瞥了卡洛塔·伊根一眼。她双眼发亮，但望的不是他。那目光落在她伯父身上。

===Original===
Greene flushed slightly. “A good bluff, Captain, is always worth trying,” he said.

“Oh, then you admit you’ve been bluffing,” said Cope quickly.

“I was referring to your attitude, sir,” Greene replied.

“Oh, I see,” Cope said. “I’ll sit down, if you don’t mind. As I understand it, you have two things against old Jim here. One is that he visited Dan Winterslip on the night of the murder, and now refuses to divulge the nature of that call. The other is the stub of a Corsican cigarette which was found by the walk outside the door of Winterslip’s living-room.”

Greene shook his head. “Only the first,” he responded. “The Corsican cigarette is no longer evidence against Egan.” He leaned suddenly across his desk. “It is, my dear Captain Cope, evidence against you.”

Cope met his look unflinchingly. “Really?” he remarked.

John Quincy noted a flash of startled bewilderment in Carlota Egan’s eyes.

===Chinese===
格林微微涨红了脸。「一手漂亮的虚张声势，船长，总是值得一试的。」他说。

「哦，这么说，您承认自己一直在虚张声势了。」科佩立刻接道。

「我指的是您的态度，先生。」格林答道。

「哦，明白了。」科佩说。「不介意的话，我就坐下。据我理解，你们用来对付我这位老吉姆的，有两样东西。一是凶案当晚他拜访过丹·温特斯利普，如今又拒绝透露那次拜访的性质。二是那支科西嘉香烟的烟蒂，在温特斯利普起居室门外的小径旁被人发现。」

格林摇了摇头。「只有第一样。」他应道。「那支科西嘉香烟，已不再是针对伊根的证据。」他忽然俯身探过办公桌。「我亲爱的科佩船长，它如今是针对您的证据。」

科佩毫不畏缩地迎住他的目光。「当真？」他说。

约翰·昆西注意到，卡洛塔·伊根眼中掠过一丝惊惶的迷乱。

===Original===
“That’s what I said,” Greene continued. “I’m very glad you dropped in this morning, sir. I’ve been wanting to talk to you. I’ve been told that you were heard to express a strong dislike for Dan Winterslip.”

“I may have. I certainly felt it.”

“Why?”

“As a midshipman on a British warship, I was familiar with Australian gossip in the ’eighties. Mr. Dan Winterslip had an unsavory reputation. It was rumored on good authority that he rifled the sea chest of his dead captain on the *Maid of Shiloh*. Perhaps we’re a bit squeamish, but that is the sort of thing we sailors can not forgive. There were other quaint deeds in connection with his blackbirding activities. Yes, my dear sir, I heartily disliked Dan Winterslip, and if I haven’t said so before, I say it now.”

===Chinese===
「我就是这么说的。」格林继续道。「您今早顺路进来，我非常高兴，先生。我一直想同您谈谈。有人告诉我，曾听见您表示过对丹·温特斯利普的强烈厌恶。」

「也许说过。这份厌恶，我确确实实怀有。」

「为什么？」

「十九世纪八十年代，我作为英国军舰上的见习军官，对澳大利亚的种种传闻相当熟悉。丹·温特斯利普先生名声不佳。有可靠的说法流传，说他在希洛少女号上洗劫了他那位亡故船长的水手箱。也许我们有些神经过敏，可这种事，是我们当水手的人无法原谅的。至于他的贩奴勾当，还有别的种种古怪名堂。是的，我亲爱的先生，我由衷地厌恶丹·温特斯利普，从前若没有说过，现在我亲口说了。」

===Original===
“You arrived in Honolulu a week ago yesterday,” Greene continued. “At noon—Monday noon. You left the following day. Did you, by any chance, call on Dan Winterslip during that period?”

“I did not.”

“Ah, yes. I may tell you, sir, that the Corsican cigarettes found in Egan’s case were of Turkish tobacco. The stub found near the scene of Dan Winterslip’s murder was of Virginia tobacco. So also, my dear Captain Cope, was the Corsican cigarette you gave our man Charlie Chan in the lobby of the Alexander Young Hotel last Sunday night.”

Cope looked at Chan, and smiled. “Always the detective, eh?” he said.

“Never mind that!” Greene cried. “I’m asking for an explanation.”

“The explanation is very simple,” Cope replied. “I was about to give it to you when you launched into this silly cross-examination. The Corsican cigarette found by Dan Winterslip’s door was, naturally, of Virginia tobacco. I never smoke any other kind.”

===Chinese===
「您抵达檀香山，是昨天往回算的一个星期之前。」格林继续道。「中午到的——星期一中午。第二天您就离开了。在那段时间里，您有没有碰巧拜访过丹·温特斯利普？」

「没有。」

「啊，是这样。我可以告诉您，先生，在伊根烟盒里发现的那些科西嘉香烟，是土耳其烟叶卷的。在丹·温特斯利普遇害现场附近发现的烟蒂，是弗吉尼亚烟叶的。还有，我亲爱的科佩船长，上星期天晚上，您在亚历山大·扬旅馆大堂里递给我们的人陈查理的那支科西嘉香烟，也是弗吉尼亚烟叶的。」

科佩望着陈查理，微微一笑。「时刻不忘当侦探，嗯？」他说。

「别扯那个！」格林喝道。「我要的是解释。」

「解释起来非常简单。」科佩答道。「您刚开始这场愚蠢的盘问时，我正要奉告呢。在丹·温特斯利普门边发现的那支科西嘉香烟，自然是弗吉尼亚烟叶的。别的烟叶，我从来不抽。」

===Original===
“What!”

“There can be no question about it, sir. I dropped that cigarette there myself.”

“But you just told me you didn’t call on Dan Winterslip.”

“That was true. I didn’t. I called on Miss Minerva Winterslip, of Boston, who is a guest in the house. As a matter of fact, I had tea with her last Monday at five o’clock. You may verify that by telephoning the lady.”

Greene glanced at Hallet, who glanced at the telephone, then turned angrily to John Quincy. “Why the devil didn’t she tell me that?” he demanded.

John Quincy smiled. “I don’t know, sir. Possibly because she never thought of Captain Cope in connection with the murder.”

“She’d hardly be likely to,” Cope said. “Miss Winterslip and I had tea in the living-room, then went out and sat on a bench in the garden, chatting over old times. When I returned to the house I was smoking a cigarette. I dropped it just outside the living-room door. Whether Miss Winterslip noted my action or not, I don’t know. She probably didn’t, it isn’t the sort of thing one remembers. You may call her on the telephone if you wish, sir.”

Again Greene looked at Hallet, who shook his head. “I’ll talk with her later,” announced the Captain of Detectives. Evidently Miss Minerva had an unpleasant interview ahead.

===Chinese===
「什么！」

「这一点毫无可疑之处，先生。那支烟是我自己丢在那儿的。」

「可您刚才告诉我，您没有拜访过丹·温特斯利普。」

「那是实话。我没有。我拜访的是波士顿的米纳瓦·温特斯利普小姐，她是在那宅子里做客的。事实上，上星期一下午五点，我同她一起用过茶。您可以打电话向那位女士核实。」

格林瞥了哈利特一眼，哈利特瞥了一眼电话机，随后愤怒地转向约翰·昆西。「她到底为什么没告诉我这个？」他责问道。

约翰·昆西微微一笑。「我不知道，先生。多半是因为，她压根儿没想到把科佩船长同这桩凶案联系起来。」

「她也几乎不可能想到。」科佩说。「温特斯利普小姐和我在起居室用的茶，随后出去坐在花园里一条长凳上，聊叙旧日时光。我回到宅子里时，正抽着一支烟。就在起居室门外，我把它丢下了。温特斯利普小姐注意到我这个动作没有，我不知道。多半没有——这种事，谁也不会记在心上。您若愿意，先生，可以打电话问她。」

格林又看向哈利特，哈利特摇了摇头。「回头我再同她谈。」刑警队长宣布道。看来，米纳瓦小姐面前摆着一场不愉快的谈话了。

===Original===
“At any rate,” Cope continued to the prosecutor, “you had yourself disposed of the cigarette as evidence against old Jim. That leaves only the fact of his silence—”

“His silence, yes,” Greene cut in, “and the fact that Winterslip had been heard to express a fear of Jim Egan.”

Cope frowned. “Had he, really?” He considered a moment. “Well, what of it? Winterslip had good reason to fear a great many honest men. No, my dear sir, you have nothing save my brother’s silence against him, and that is not enough. I demand—”

Greene raised his hand. “Just a minute. I said you were bluffing, and I still think so. Any other assumption would be an insult to your intelligence. Surely you know enough about the law to understand that your brother’s refusal to tell me his business with Winterslip, added to the fact that he was presumably the last person to see Winterslip alive, is sufficient excuse for holding him. I can hold him on those grounds, I am holding him, and, my dear Captain, I shall continue to hold him until hell freezes over.”

“Very good,” said Cope, rising. “I shall engage a capable lawyer—”

“That is, of course, your privilege,” snapped Greene. “Good morning.”

Cope hesitated. He turned to Egan. “It means more publicity, Jim,” he said. “Delay, too. More unhappiness for Carlota here. And since everything you did was done for her—”

===Chinese===
「无论如何，」科佩继续对检察官说，「那支香烟作为对付老吉姆的证据，您自己已经把它处理掉了。剩下的只有他保持沉默这一条——」

「他的沉默，不错，」格林插进来，「还有一桩事实：有人曾听见温特斯利普表示过，他惧怕吉姆·伊根。」

科佩皱起眉头。「当真有这回事？」他思忖片刻。「唔，那又怎样？温特斯利普有充分的理由，惧怕许许多多诚实的人。不，我亲爱的先生，除了我弟弟的沉默，您手里什么也没有，而那是远远不够的。我要求——」

格林举起一只手。「且慢。我说过您是在虚张声势，如今依然这么认为。换任何别的想法，都是对您智力的侮辱。想必您对法律也懂得不少，足以明白：您弟弟拒绝告诉我他与温特斯利普的往来，再加上他多半是最后一个见到活着的温特斯利普的人，这两条就足以构成扣押他的理由。我可以凭这些理由扣他，我正在扣他，而且，我亲爱的船长，我要一直扣下去，扣到地狱结冰为止。」

「很好。」科佩说着站起身。「我会聘请一位能干的律师——」

「那自然是您的权利。」格林厉声道。「不送了。」

科佩犹豫了一下。他转向伊根。「那就意味着闹得更满城风雨，吉姆。」他说。「还有拖延。也给你身边的卡洛塔添更多的不快。而你做的一切，都是为了她——」

===Original===
“How did you know that?” asked Egan quickly.

“I’ve guessed it. I can put two and two together, Jim. Carlota was to return with me for a bit of schooling in England. You said you had the money, but you hadn’t. That was your pride again, Jim. It’s got you into a lifetime of trouble. You cast about for the funds, and you remembered Winterslip. I’m beginning to see it all now. You had something on Dan Winterslip, and you went to his house that night to—er—”

“To blackmail him,” suggested Greene.

“It wasn’t a pretty thing to do, Jim,” Cope went on. “But you weren’t doing it for yourself. Carlota and I know you would have died first. You did it for your girl, and we both forgive you.” He turned to Carlota. “Don’t we, my dear?”

The girl’s eyes were wet. She rose and kissed her father. “Dear old dad,” she said.

“Come on, Jim,” pleaded Captain Cope. “Forget your pride for once. Speak up, and we’ll take you home with us. I’m sure the prosecutor will keep the thing from the newspapers—”

“We’ve promised him that a thousand times,” Greene said.

Egan lifted his head. “I don’t care anything about the newspapers,” he explained. “It’s you, Arthur—you and Cary—I didn’t want you two to know. But since you’ve guessed, and Cary knows too—I may as well tell everything.”

===Chinese===
「这个您怎么知道的？」伊根急忙问。

「我猜到的。我会把二加二拼成四，吉姆。卡洛塔本要随我回英国，去受一点教育。你说钱你有，可你并没有。又是你的自尊心作祟，吉姆。它把你拖进了半辈子的麻烦。你四处张罗这笔钱，于是想起了温特斯利普。如今我全看明白了。你手里握着丹·温特斯利普的把柄，那天夜里你到他家里去，是为了——呃——」

「去敲诈他。」格林提示道。

「这可不是什么光彩事，吉姆。」科佩继续道。「可你并不是为你自己。卡洛塔和我都明白，你宁死也不会干的。你是为了你的女儿才做的，我们俩都原谅你。」他转向卡洛塔。「是不是，亲爱的？」

姑娘的眼眶湿了。她起身吻了吻她的父亲。「亲爱的老爸爸。」她说。

「说吧，吉姆。」科佩船长恳求道。「就这一次，忘掉你的自尊心。痛痛快快说出来，我们就带你一起回家。我确信检察官会不让这事见报——」

「这句话我们向他许过一千次了。」格林说。

伊根抬起头来。「报不报的，我一点都不在乎。」他解释道。「是你，阿瑟——你同卡莉——我不愿让你们两个知道。可既然你已经猜到，卡莉也知道了——我不妨把一切都说了吧。」

===Original===
John Quincy stood up. “Mr. Egan,” he said. “I’ll leave the room, if you wish.”

“Sit down, my boy,” Egan replied. “Cary’s told me of your kindness to her. Besides, you saw the check—”

“What check was that?” cried Hallet. He leaped to his feet and stood over John Quincy.

“I was honor bound not to tell,” explained the boy gently.

“You don’t say so!” Hallet bellowed. “You’re a fine pair, you and that aunt of yours—”

“One minute, Hallet,” cut in Greene. “Now, Egan, or Cope, or whatever your name happens to be—I’m waiting to hear from you.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西站起身。「伊根先生，」他说，「如果您愿意，我这就回避。」

「坐下，我的孩子。」伊根答道。「卡莉同我说过你对她的好意。再说，那张支票你也见过——」

「什么支票？」哈利特嚷道。他跳将起来，逼到约翰·昆西跟前。

「我受了名誉的约束，不能说。」小伙子温和地解释道。

「是嘛！」哈利特吼道。「你们倒真是天生一对，你和你那位姑婆——」

「等一下，哈利特。」格林插进来。「好了，伊根——或者说科佩，不管你如今姓什么——我等着听你说。」

===Original===
Egan nodded. “Back in the ’eighties I was teller in a bank in Melbourne, Australia,” he said. “One day a young man came to my window—Williams or some such name he called himself. He had a green hide bag full of gold pieces—Mexican, Spanish and English coins, some of them crusted with dirt—and he wanted to exchange them for banknotes. I made the exchange for him. He appeared several times with similar bags, and the transaction was repeated. I thought little of it at the time, though the fact that he tried to give me a large tip did rather rouse my suspicion.

===Chinese===
伊根点点头。「早在十九世纪八十年代，我在澳大利亚墨尔本一家银行当出纳员。」他说。「有一天，一个年轻人来到我的窗口——他自称姓威廉斯还是别的什么来着。他带来一只生牛皮口袋，装满了金币——墨西哥的、西班牙的、英国的硬币都有，有几枚还结着泥土的硬壳——他想把它们换成钞票。我替他换了。他后来又拿过几回同样的口袋来，交易一次次做成。当时我没怎么在意，只是他曾想给我一大笔赏钱，这件事倒着实引起过我的疑心。

===Original===
“A year later, when I had left the bank and gone to Sydney, I heard rumors of what Dan Winterslip had done on the *Maid of Shiloh*. It occurred to me that Williams and Winterslip were probably the same man. But no one seemed to be prosecuting the case, the general feeling was that it was blood money anyhow, that Tom Brade had not come by it honestly himself. So I said nothing.

===Chinese===
「一年之后，我已离开那家银行去了悉尼，听说了丹·温特斯利普在希洛少女号上干下的事的传闻。我想到，那威廉斯和温特斯利普多半是同一个人。可似乎没有人要追究此案，众人都觉得那反正是笔血钱，汤姆·布雷德自己得来也不干净。于是我什么也没说。

===Original===
“Twelve years later I came to Hawaii, and Dan Winterslip was pointed out to me. He was Williams, right enough. And he knew me, too. But I’m not a blackmailer—I’ve been in some tight places, Arthur, but I’ve always played fair—so I let the matter drop. For more than twenty years nothing happened.

===Chinese===
「十二年后我来到夏威夷，有人指给我看，那就是丹·温特斯利普。他正是那个威廉斯，错不了。他也认出了我。可我不是敲诈勒索之徒——阿瑟，我也落难过几回，可我做事向来正派——于是我把这事搁下了。此后二十多年，风平浪静。

===Original===
“Then, a few months ago, my family located me at last, and Arthur here wrote me that he was coming to Honolulu and would look me up. I’d always felt that I’d not done the right thing by my girl—that she was not taking the place in the world to which she was entitled. I wanted her to visit my old mother and get a bit of English training. I wrote to Arthur and it was arranged. But I couldn’t let her go as a charity child—I couldn’t admit I’d failed and was unable to do anything for her—I said I’d pay her way. And I—I didn’t have a cent.

===Chinese===
「后来，几个月前，我家里的人终于寻到了我，这位阿瑟写信告诉我，说他要来檀香山，会来看我。我一向觉得，我亏待了我的女儿——她在这个世界上，没有得到她应得的地位。我想让她去看看我的老母亲，受一点英国的教育。我给阿瑟去了信，事情便定了下来。可我不能让她像个受施舍的孩子那样去——我不能承认自己一事无成、什么都给不了她——我说，她的路费我来出。而我——我连一分钱也没有。

===Original===
“And then Brade came. It seemed providential. I might have sold my information to him, but when I talked with him I found he had very little money, and I felt that Winterslip would beat him in the end. No, Winterslip was my man—Winterslip with his rotten wealth. I don’t know just what happened—I was quite mad, I fancy—the world owed me that, I figured, just for my girl, not for me. I called Winterslip up and made an appointment for that Monday night.

===Chinese===
「就在这时，布雷德来了。那简直像是上天的安排。我本可以把我知道的事卖给他，可同他一谈，我发现他钱很少，而且我料定，到头来温特斯利普会赢他。不，我的人是温特斯利普——带着他那腐朽家财的温特斯利普。我自己也说不清当时是怎么回事——我想我是疯了——我认定这世界欠我这一笔，只为我的女儿，不为我自己。我打电话给温特斯利普，约在了那个星期一的晚上。

===Original===
“But somehow—the standards of a lifetime—it’s difficult to change. The moment I had called him, I regretted it. I tried to slip out of it—I told myself there must be some other way—perhaps I could sell the Reef and Palm—anyhow, I called him again and said I wasn’t coming. But he insisted, and I went.

===Chinese===
「可是不知怎么——一辈子做人的规矩——改起来难。电话一挂，我就后悔了。我想法子推掉——我告诉自己，总还有别的路子——没准儿我可以把『礁石棕榈』卖掉——总之，我又给他打了电话，说我不去了。可他执意要我过去，我就去了。

===Original===
“I didn’t have to tell him what I wanted. He knew. He had a check ready for me—a check for five thousand dollars. It was Cary’s happiness, her chance. I took it, and came away—but I was ashamed. I’m not trying to excuse my action; however, I don’t believe I would ever have cashed it. When Cary found it in my desk and brought it to me, I tore it up. That’s all.” He turned his tired eyes toward his daughter. “I did it for you, Cary, but I didn’t want you to know.” She went over and put her arm about his shoulder, and stood smiling down at him through her tears.

===Chinese===
「我用不着告诉他我要什么。他知道。他备好了一张支票等我——一张五千元的支票。那是卡莉的幸福，她的机会。我收下它，走了——可我心里羞愧。我不想为自己的行为开脱；不过，我相信我永远不会去兑现它。卡莉在我书桌里发现那张支票，拿来给我时，我把它撕了。就这些。」他把疲惫的目光转向女儿。「我是为你做的，卡莉，可我不愿让你知道。」她走过去，一条手臂搂住他的肩，含着泪，带笑俯视着他。

===Original===
“If you’d told us that in the first place,” said Greene, “you could have saved everybody a lot of trouble, yourself included.”

Cope stood up. “Well, Mr. Prosecutor, there you are. You’re not going to hold him now?”

Greene rose briskly. “No. I’ll arrange for his release at once.” He and Egan went out together, then Hallet and Cope. John Quincy held out his hand to Carlota Egan—for by that name he thought of her still.

“I’m mighty glad for you,” he said.

“You’ll come and see me soon?” she asked. “You’ll find a very different girl. More like the one you met on the Oakland ferry.”

“She was very charming,” John Quincy replied. “But then, she was bound to be—she had your eyes.” He suddenly remembered Agatha Parker. “However, you’ve got your father now,” he added. “You won’t need me.”

She looked up at him and smiled. “I wonder,” she said, and went out.

===Chinese===
「你要是一开始就把这些告诉我们，」格林说，「就能省去大家许多麻烦，也包括你自己。」

科佩站起身。「好了，检察官先生，情形就是这样。现在你总不会扣他了吧？」

格林利落地站起身。「不会了。我马上安排释放他。」他和伊根一同出去，随后是哈利特和科佩。约翰·昆西向卡洛塔·伊根伸出手去——因为在心里，他仍以这个名字惦念着她。

「我真为你高兴。」他说。

「你很快会来看我的吧？」她问。「你会见到一个非常不一样的姑娘。更像你在奥克兰渡轮上遇见的那一个。」

「她非常迷人。」约翰·昆西答道。「不过，她也注定如此——她有你这双眼睛。」他忽然想起了阿加莎·帕克。「再说，如今你父亲回到你身边了，」他补了一句，「你不再需要我了。」

她抬眼望着他，微微一笑。「那可说不准。」说着，她走了出去。

===Original===
John Quincy turned to Chan. “Well, that’s that,” he remarked. “Where are we now?”

“Speaking personally for myself,” grinned Chan, “I am static in same place as usual. Never did have fondly feeling for Egan theory.”

“But Hallet did,” John Quincy answered. “A black morning for him.”

In the small anteroom they encountered the Captain of Detectives. He appeared disgruntled.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西转向陈查理。「唉，事情就这样了结了。」他说。「咱们如今走到哪一步了？」

「仅就我本人个人而言，」陈查理咧嘴笑道，「我仍旧静处于惯常之原地。对伊根理论，我从来不曾怀有亲切的好感。」

「可哈利特信这个。」约翰·昆西答道。「对他说来，今早真是黑暗。」

在小前厅里，他们撞见了刑警队长。他一脸怏怏不乐。

===Original===
“We were just remarking,” said John Quincy pleasantly, “that there goes your little old Egan theory. What have you left?”

“Oh, I’ve got plenty,” growled Hallet.

“Yes, you have. One by one your clues have gone up in smoke. The page from the guest book, the brooch, the torn newspaper, the ohia wood box, and now Egan and the Corsican cigarette.”

“Oh, Egan isn’t out of it. We may not be able to hold him, but I’m not forgetting Mr. Egan.”

“Nonsense,” smiled John Quincy. “I asked what you had left. A little button from a glove—useless. The glove was destroyed long ago. A wrist watch with an illuminated dial and a damaged numeral two—”

===Chinese===
「我们正说呢，」约翰·昆西和颜悦色地说，「您那套宝贝的伊根理论，这下子完啦。您手里还剩什么？」

「哦，我手里有的是。」哈利特咆哮道。

「是啊，多得很。您的线索一条接一条，都化作了青烟。留名簿的那一页，胸针，撕碎的报纸，奥希亚木盒，如今又是伊根和科西嘉香烟。」

「哦，伊根还没脱干系。我们或许扣不住他，可伊根先生这个人，我不会忘。」

「胡扯。」约翰·昆西微笑道。「我问的是您还剩什么。手套上掉落的一颗小纽扣——没用。手套早毁掉了。一块带夜光表盘的腕表，表盘上的 2 字坏了——」

===Original===
Chan’s amber eyes narrowed. “Essential clue,” he murmured. “Remember how I said it.”

Hallet banged his fist on a table. “That’s it—the wrist watch! If the person who wore it knows anyone saw it, it’s probably where we’ll never find it now. But we’ve kept it pretty dark—perhaps he doesn’t know. That’s our only chance.” He turned to Chan. “I’ve combed these islands once hunting that watch,” he cried, “now I’m going to start all over again. The jewelry stores, the pawn shops, every nook and corner. You go out, Charlie, and start the ball rolling.”

Chan moved with alacrity despite his weight. “I will give it one powerful push,” he promised, and disappeared.

“Well, good luck,” said John Quincy, moving on.

Hallet grunted. “You tell that aunt of yours I’m pretty sore,” he remarked. He was not in the mood for elegance of diction.

===Chinese===
陈查理那双琥珀色的眼睛眯了起来。「关键之线索。」他喃喃道。「请记得我当初如何言说。」

哈利特一拳捶在桌上。「就是它——那块腕表！要是戴它的人知道有人瞧见过，它这会儿多半已藏在咱们永远找不着的地方了。可这事我们一直捂得很严——兴许他不知道。那是咱们唯一的机会。」他转向陈查理。「为找这块表，这些岛屿我已经像篦头发似的篦过一遍了，」他喊道，「现在我要从头再来。珠宝店，当铺，犄角旮旯。你出去，查理，先让球滚起来。」

陈查理不顾体重，行动敏捷。「我将给它有力之一推。」他许诺道，随即消失不见。

「那么，祝好运。」约翰·昆西说罢，继续赶路。

哈利特哼了一声。「你告诉你那位姑婆，我火气大得很。」他说。他这会儿可没心思讲究措辞的雅致。

===Original===
John Quincy’s opportunity to deliver the message did not come at lunch, for Miss Minerva remained with Barbara in the city. After dinner that evening he led his aunt out to sit on the bench under the hau tree.

“By the way,” he said, “Captain Hallet is very much annoyed with you.”

“I’m very much annoyed with Captain Hallet,” she replied, “so that makes us even. What’s his particular grievance now?”

“He believes you knew all the time the name of the man who dropped that Corsican cigarette.”

She was silent for a moment. “Not all the time,” she said at length. “What has happened?”

===Chinese===
午饭时，约翰·昆西一直没得到传话的机会，因为米纳瓦小姐陪着芭芭拉留在城里没回来。当晚晚饭之后，他领着姑婆出来，坐在桐棉树下的长凳上。

「对了，」他说，「哈利特警长对您大为恼火。」

「我对哈利特警长也大为恼火。」她答道，「这样咱们就两讫了。他这回具体怨什么？」

「他认定您从头到尾都知道，丢下那支科西嘉香烟的人叫什么名字。」

她沉默了片刻。「并非从头到尾。」她终于开口。「出什么事了？」

===Original===
John Quincy sketched briefly the events of the morning at the police station. When he had finished he looked at her inquiringly.

“In the first excitement I didn’t remember, or I should have spoken,” she explained. “It was several days before the thing came to me. I saw it clearly then—Arthur—Captain Cope—tossing that cigarette aside as we reentered the house. But I said nothing about it.”

“Why?”

“Well, I thought it would be a good test for the police. Let them discover it for themselves.”

“That’s a pretty weak explanation,” remarked John Quincy severely. “You’ve been responsible for a lot of wasted time.”

“It—it wasn’t my only reason,” said Miss Minerva softly.

“Oh—I’m glad to hear that. Go on.”

“Somehow, I couldn’t bring myself to link up that call of Captain Cope’s with—a murder mystery.”

Another silence. And suddenly—he was never dense—John Quincy understood.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西扼要讲了那天上午警察局里的事。讲完，他探询似的望着她。

「起初乱糟糟的，我没想起来，否则我早就说了。」她解释道。「过了好几天，这事才回到我脑子里。那时我看得分明——阿瑟——科佩船长——在我们重回宅子时，把那支烟随手一丢。可我什么也没说。」

「为什么？」

「唔，我想这给警方出了道好考题。让他们自己去发现吧。」

「这个解释可够牵强的。」约翰·昆西正色道。「许许多多白白浪费掉的时间，都该记在您账上。」

「这——这不是我唯一的缘由。」米纳瓦小姐轻声说。

「哦——听你这么说我才高兴。说下去。」

「不知怎么，我总没法让自己把科佩船长的那次拜访，同一桩——凶案联系起来。」

又是一阵沉默。突然间——他并不迟钝——约翰·昆西明白了。

===Original===
“He told me you were very beautiful in the ’eighties,” said the boy gently. “The captain, I mean. When I met him in that San Francisco club.”

Miss Minerva laid her own hand on the boy’s. When she spoke her voice, which he had always thought firm and sharp, trembled a little. “On this beach in my girlhood,” she said, “happiness was within my grasp. I had only to reach out and take it. But somehow—Boston—Boston held me back. I let my happiness slip away.”

“Not too late yet,” suggested John Quincy.

She shook her head. “So he tried to tell me that Monday afternoon. But there was something in his tone—I may be in Hawaii, but I’m not quite mad. Youth, John Quincy, youth doesn’t return, whatever they may say out here.” She pressed his hand, and stood. “If your chance comes, dear boy,” she added, “don’t be such a fool.”

She moved hastily away through the garden, and John Quincy looked after her with a new affection in his eyes.

===Chinese===
「他告诉我，您在十九世纪八十年代非常美丽。」小伙子柔声说。「我是说那位船长。我在旧金山那家俱乐部遇见他的时候。」

米纳瓦小姐把自己的手覆在小伙子的手上。她开口时，那副他一向觉得坚定而干脆的嗓音，微微颤抖起来。「少女时代，在这片海滩上，」她说，「幸福曾近在我的掌握之中。我只要伸出手，就能拿到它。可不知怎么——波士顿——是波士顿拖住了我。我眼看着自己的幸福溜走了。」

「现在还为时不晚。」约翰·昆西提声道。

她摇了摇头。「那个星期一下午，他也想这么告诉我。可他话音里有种什么东西——我人虽在夏威夷，脑子还没全疯。青春，约翰·昆西，青春是一去不返的，任凭这边的人把话说得多么好听。」她按了按他的手，站起身来。「要是机会来到你面前，好孩子，」她添了一句，「可别做那样的傻瓜。」

她匆匆穿过花园走远了，约翰·昆西望着她的背影，眼中含着一种新的深情。

===Original===
Presently he saw the yellow glare of a match beyond the wire. Amos again, still loitering under his algaroba tree. John Quincy rose and strolled over to him.

“Hello, Cousin Amos,” he said. “When are you going to take down this fence?”

“Oh, I’ll get round to it some time,” Amos answered. “By the way, I wanted to ask you. Any new developments?”

“Several,” John Quincy told him. “But nothing that gets us anywhere. So far as I can see, the case has blown up completely.”

“Well, I’ve been thinking it over,” Amos said. “Maybe that would be the best outcome, after all. Suppose they do discover who did for Dan—it may only reveal a new scandal, worse than any of the others.”

“I’ll take a chance on that,” replied John Quincy. “For my part, I intend to see this thing through—”

===Chinese===
不多时，他望见铁丝网外一根火柴黄色的光焰。又是阿莫斯，还在他那棵牧豆树下流连。约翰·昆西起身，踱了过去。

「你好啊，阿莫斯堂叔。」他说。「您打算什么时候把这道栅栏拆掉？」

「哦，哪天抽空就拆。」阿莫斯答道。「对了，我正想问你。有什么新的进展没有？」

「有几桩。」约翰·昆西告诉他。「可没有一桩顶用的。照我看来，这案子已经彻底砸了。」

「唔，这些天我把这事前后想了一遍。」阿莫斯说。「没准儿到头来，那样的结局反倒最好。就算他们查出是谁害了丹——揭开来的，也许不过是一桩新的丑闻，比先前哪一桩都更难看。」

「那我甘愿冒这个险。」约翰·昆西答道。「至于我，我打算把这事查个水落石出——」

===Original===
Haku came briskly through the garden. “Cable message for Mr. John Quincy Winterslip. Boy say collect. Requests money.”

John Quincy followed quickly to the front door. A bored small boy awaited him. He paid the sum due and tore open the cable. It was signed by the postmaster at Des Moines, and it read:

“No one named Saladine ever heard of here.”

John Quincy dashed to the telephone. Someone on duty at the station informed him that Chan had gone home, and gave him an address on Punchbowl Hill. He got out the roadster, and in five minutes more was speeding toward the city.

===Chinese===
哈库快步穿过花园走来。「电报，给约翰·昆西·温特斯利普先生的。送报的孩子说是到付，要收钱的。」

约翰·昆西快步跟到前门。一个无精打采的小童等着他。他付清了应付的款项，撕开电报。电报由得梅因的邮政局长签发，上面写着：

「此地从未听说过有名叫萨拉迪尼的人。」

约翰·昆西冲向电话。警察局里值班的人告诉他，陈查理已经回家，并给了他一个碗盆山上的地址。他开出那辆双座跑车，五分钟后，便向城里飞驰而去了。

## XIX “Goodbye, Pete!” / 十九 「再见，皮特！」

===Original===
Charlie Chan lived in a bungalow that clung precariously to the side of Punchbowl Hill. Pausing a moment at the Chinaman’s gate, John Quincy looked down on Honolulu, one great gorgeous garden set in an amphitheater of mountains. A beautiful picture, but he had no time for beauty now. He hurried up the brief walk that lay in the shadow of the palm trees.
A Chinese woman﻿—a servant, she seemed﻿—ushered him into Chan’s dimly-lighted living-room. The detective was seated at a table playing chess; he rose with dignity when he saw his visitor. In this, his hour of ease, he wore a long loose robe of dark purple silk, which fitted closely at the neck and had wide sleeves. Beneath it showed wide trousers of the same material, and on his feet were shoes of silk, with thick felt soles. He was all Oriental now, suave and ingratiating but remote, and for the first time John Quincy was really conscious of the great gulf across which he and Chan shook hands.
“You do my lowly house immense honor,” Charlie said. “This proud moment are made still more proud by opportunity to introduce my eldest son.” He motioned for his opponent at chess to step forward, a slim sallow boy with amber eyes﻿—Chan himself before he put on weight. “Mr. John Quincy Winterslip, of Boston, kindly condescend to notice Henry Chan. When you appear I am giving him lesson at chess so he may play in such manner as not to tarnish honored name.”

===Chinese===
陈查理住在一座平房里，房子紧贴着碗盆山的山坡，摇摇欲坠。约翰·昆西在那位华人的院门前驻足片刻，俯瞰檀香山——好一座绚烂的大花园，安放在群山环抱的圆形剧场正中。一幅美景，可他此刻无暇欣赏。他匆匆走上棕榈树荫下那条短短的小径。
一个中国妇人——看样子是个仆人——把他领进陈查理灯光昏暗的客厅。这位侦探正坐在桌旁下棋，见有客来，庄重地起身。在这闲暇的时刻，他身穿一件深紫色绸料的宽松长袍，领口收得妥帖，衣袖宽大。袍下露出同色料子的宽腿裤，脚上是厚毡底的绸鞋。此刻他通身上下是个东方人，温文、殷勤，却又遥不可及，而约翰·昆西头一回真切地意识到，他和陈查理是隔着一道深深的鸿沟在握手。
「您光临我这寒微的居所，予它莫大的荣耀，」陈查理说，「而有机会引见长子，这自豪的时刻更添自豪了。」他示意棋局上的对手上前——那是个清瘦微黄的少年，生着一双琥珀色的眼睛——正是发福之前的陈查理本人。「波士顿来的约翰·昆西·温特斯利普先生，恳请屈尊垂注亨利·陈。您到来之时，我正给他上棋课，好使他行棋有方，不至玷污那受人尊敬的姓氏。」

===Original===
The boy bowed low; evidently he was one member of the younger generation who had a deep respect for his elders. John Quincy also bowed. “Your father is my very good friend,” he said. “And from now on, you are too.”
Chan beamed with pleasure. “Condescend to sit on this atrocious chair. Is it possible you bring news?”
“It certainly is,” smiled John Quincy. He handed over the message from the postmaster at Des Moines.
“Most interesting,” said Chan. “Do I hear impressive chug of rich automobile engine in street?”

===Chinese===
少年深深鞠躬；他显然是年轻一代中深深敬重长辈的一员。约翰·昆西也鞠了一躬。「令尊是我的挚友，」他说，「从今往后，你也是。」
陈查理喜形于色。「请屈尊坐上这把蹩脚的椅子。您可是有可能带来消息了？」
「正是。」约翰·昆西微笑着，递上得梅因邮局长的回电。
「趣味无穷，」陈查理说，「街上富丽汽车的引擎发出雄浑的突突声，我可听得属实？」

===Original===
“Yes, I came in the car,” John Quincy replied.
“Good. We will hasten at once to home of Captain Hallet, not far away. I beg of you to pardon my disappearance while I don more appropriate costume.”
Left alone with the boy, John Quincy sought a topic of conversation. “Play baseball?” he asked.
The boy’s eyes glowed. “Not very good, but I hope to improve. My cousin Willie Chan is great expert at that game. He has promised to teach me.”

===Chinese===
「对，我开车来的。」约翰·昆西答道。
「好。我们即刻赶赴哈利特警长府上，就在不远。趁我换上更合宜的装束，恳请恕我暂且失陪。」
只剩他和少年在一起，约翰·昆西找起了话头。「打棒球吗？」他问。
少年双目放光。「打得不算好，但我盼望精进。我堂叔威利·陈是此项运动的大行家。他已应承教我。」

===Original===
John Quincy glanced about the room. On the back wall hung a scroll with felicitations, the gift of some friend of the family at New Year’s. Opposite him, on another wall, was a single picture, painted on silk, representing a bird on an apple bough. Charmed by its simplicity, he went over to examine it. “That’s beautiful,” he said.
“Quoting old Chinese saying, a picture is a voiceless poem,” replied the boy.
Beneath the picture stood a square table, flanked by straight, low-backed armchairs. On other elaborately carved teakwood stands distributed about the room were blue and white vases, porcelain wine jars, dwarfed trees. Pale golden lanterns hung from the ceiling; a soft-toned rug lay on the floor. John Quincy felt again the gulf between himself and Charlie Chan.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西环顾室内。后墙上挂着一幅贺喜的字轴，是家中某位朋友新年所赠的礼物。他对面的另一面墙上，孤零零挂着一幅绢画，画着苹果枝头的一只鸟。他为那画面的简洁所动，走过去细看。「真美。」他说。
「引一句中国古话：画乃无声之诗。」少年答道。
画下方立着一张方桌，两侧摆着靠背笔直低矮的扶手椅。屋里各处还分布着雕工繁复的柚木架，架上陈着青花瓷瓶、瓷酒坛和盆栽的矮树。天花板上悬着淡金色的灯笼，地上铺着一块色调柔和的地毯。约翰·昆西再度感到自己与陈查理之间的那道鸿沟。

===Original===
But when the detective returned, he wore the conventional garb of Los Angeles or Detroit, and the gulf did not seem so wide. They went out together and entering the roadster, drove to Hallet’s house on Iolani Avenue.
The captain lolled in pajamas on his lanai. He greeted his callers with interest.
“You boys are out late,” he said. “Something doing?”
“Certainly is,” replied John Quincy, taking a proffered chair. “There’s a man named Saladine﻿—”

===Chinese===
可待侦探再出来时，他已换上洛杉矶或底特律人的寻常装束，那道鸿沟便显得不那么宽了。两人一同出门，上了敞篷车，开往伊奥拉尼大道（Iolani Avenue）哈利特警长的家。
警长穿着睡衣歪在露台上，饶有兴味地迎接来客。
「你们两个小伙子夜出不早啊，」他说，「有事儿？」
「当然有。」约翰·昆西答道，在递过来的椅子上落座。「有个叫萨拉迪尼的人——」

===Original===
At mention of the name, Hallet looked at him keenly. John Quincy went on to tell what he knew of Saladine, his alleged place of residence, his business, the tragedy of the lost teeth.
“Some time ago we got on to the fact that every time Kaohla figured in the investigation, Saladine was interested. He managed to be at the desk of the Reef and Palm the day Kaohla inquired for Brade. On the night Kaohla was questioned by your men, Miss Egan saw Mr. Saladine crouching outside the window. So Charlie and I thought it a good scheme to send a cable of inquiry to the postmaster at Des Moines, where Saladine claimed to be in the wholesale grocery business.” He handed an envelope to Hallet. “That answer arrived tonight,” he added.
An odd smile had appeared on Hallet’s usually solemn face. He took the cable and read it, then slowly tore it into bits.

===Chinese===
一听这名字，哈利特目光锐利地打量他。约翰·昆西接着讲起他所知道的萨拉迪尼：他自称的住处、营生，还有他丢掉假牙的那出悲剧。
「前些日子我们察觉到一个事实：每逢考赫拉在调查中露面，萨拉迪尼必感兴趣。考赫拉到『礁石棕榈』柜台打听布雷德的那天，他设法守在柜台边；你们的人审问考赫拉那晚，伊根小姐看见萨拉迪尼先生蹲伏在窗外。于是查理和我想到一条妙计：向得梅因的邮政局长发一封查询电报——萨拉迪尼自称在那边做杂货批发生意。」他把一个信封递给哈利特。「回电今晚到了。」他补了一句。
哈利特那张素来严肃的脸上浮起一丝古怪的笑。他接过电报读了，然后慢慢把它撕成碎片。

===Original===
“Forget it, boys,” he said calmly.
“Wha﻿—what!” gasped John Quincy.
“I said forget it. I like your enterprise, but you’re on the wrong trail there.”
John Quincy was greatly annoyed. “I demand an explanation,” he cried.
“I can’t give it to you,” Hallet answered. “You’ll have to take my word for it.”

===Chinese===
「把它忘了吧，小伙子们。」他平静地说。
「什——什么！」约翰·昆西倒抽一口气。
「我说，把它忘了。我赏识你们的干劲，可那条线索你们追错了。」
约翰·昆西大为光火。「我要求一个解释。」他嚷道。
「我没法给你。」哈利特答道，「你只能信我的话。」

===Original===
“I’ve taken your word for a good many things,” said John Quincy hotly. “This begins to look rather suspicious to me. Are you trying to shield somebody?”
Hallet rose and laid his hand on John Quincy’s shoulder. “I’ve had a hard day,” he remarked, “and I’m not going to get angry with you. I’m not trying to shield anybody. I’m as anxious as you are to discover who killed Dan Winterslip. More anxious, perhaps.”
“Yet when we bring you evidence you tear it up﻿—”
“Bring me the right evidence,” said Hallet. “Bring me that wrist watch. I can promise you action then.”

===Chinese===
「你的话我已经信过一大堆了，」约翰·昆西火气腾地起来，「这事在我看来可有点蹊跷。你是想包庇什么人吗？」
哈利特站起来，把一只手搭在约翰·昆西肩上。「我今天辛苦了一整天，」他说，「不想跟你动气。我没有包庇任何人。查出谁杀了丹·温特斯利普，我跟你一样急。也许更急。」
「可我们给你送来证据，你倒把它撕了——」
「给我送来对路的证据，」哈利特说，「把那块手表给我拿来。到那时候，我保证你看到行动。」

===Original===
John Quincy was impressed by the sincerity in his tone. But he was sadly puzzled, too. “All right,” he said, “that’s that. I’m sorry if we’ve troubled you with this trivial matter﻿—”
“Don’t talk like that,” Hallet broke in. “I’m glad of your help. But as far as Mr. Saladine is concerned﻿—” he looked at Chan﻿—“let him alone.”
Chan bowed. “You are undisputable chief,” he replied.

===Chinese===
他语气里的诚恳打动了约翰·昆西。可他心里也苦恼地一团疑惑。「好吧，」他说，「就这样。若是拿这桩小事搅扰了您，抱歉——」
「别这么说，」哈利特插话，「你们的帮忙我欢迎。可至于萨拉迪尼先生嘛——」他望了望陈查理——「别去动他。」
陈查理躬身一礼。「您乃无可争辩之首领。」他答道。

===Original===
They went back to Punchbowl Hill in the roadster, both rather dejected. As Chan alighted at his gate, John Quincy spoke: “Well, I’m *pau*. Saladine was my last hope.”
The Chinaman stared for a moment at the moonlit Pacific that lay beyond the waterfront lamps. “Stone wall surround us,” he said dreamily. “But we circle about, seeking loophole. Moment of discovery will come.”
“I wish I thought so,” replied John Quincy.
Chan smiled. “Patience are a very lovely virtue,” he remarked. “Seem that way to me. But maybe that are my Oriental mind. Your race, I perceive, regard patience with ever-swelling disfavor.”

===Chinese===
两人乘敞篷车回到碗盆山，都颇为丧气。陈查理在自家院门口下车时，约翰·昆西开口道：「唉，我帕乌了。萨拉迪尼是我最后的指望。」
那位华人凝望了片刻滨水灯火之外月光下的太平洋。「石墙将我等团团围住，」他悠悠地说，「但我们绕行周旋，寻觅墙上的裂隙。发现的时刻终将来临。」
「但愿我也这么想。」约翰·昆西答道。
陈查理微微一笑。「忍耐乃一样极可爱之美德，」他说，「在我眼中似乎如此。但或许那是我这颗东方头脑使然。我察觉，贵种族看待忍耐，反感之心正持续膨胀。」

===Original===
It was with swelling disfavor that John Quincy regarded it as he drove back to Waikiki. Yet he had great need of patience in the days immediately following. For nothing happened.
The forty-eight-hour period given him to leave Hawaii expired, but the writer of that threatening letter failed to come forward and relieve the tedium. Thursday arrived, a calm day like the others; Thursday night, peaceful and serene.
On Friday afternoon Agatha Parker broke the monotony by a cable sent from the Wyoming ranch.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西驱车返回怀基基的一路，正是怀着持续膨胀的反感看待忍耐的。然而在随后的几天里，他大大地用得着忍耐。因为什么事都没有发生。
限他离开夏威夷的四十八小时期限到了头，可那封恐吓信的作者并未现身来解此烦闷。星期四来了，同别的日子一样平静；星期四夜里，安宁祥和。
星期五下午，阿加莎·帕克从怀俄明州的牧场拍来一封电报，打破了这单调。

===Original===
“You must be quite mad. I find the West crude and impossible.”
John Quincy smiled; he could picture her as she wrote it, proud, haughty, unyielding. She must have been popular with the man who transmitted the message. Or was he, too, an exile from the East?
And perhaps the girl was right. Perhaps he was mad, after all. He sat on Dan Winterslip’s lanai, trying to think things out. Boston, the office, the art gallery, the theaters. The Common on a winter’s day, with the air bracing and full of life. The thrill of a new issue of bonds, like the thrill of a theatrical first night﻿—would it get over big or flop at his feet? Tennis at Longwood, long evenings on the Charles, golf with people of his own kind at Magnolia. Tea out of exquisite cups in dim old drawing-rooms. Wasn’t he mad to think of giving up all that? But what had Miss Minerva said? “If your chance ever comes﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你准是彻底疯了。我发觉西部粗野得让人没法忍受。」
约翰·昆西笑了；他能想象她写电文时的模样，高傲，倨傲，寸步不让。拍发这封电报的报务员，想必也很喜欢她。又或者，他也是个从东部放逐出来的人？
说不定姑娘说得对。说不定他到底是疯了。他坐在丹·温特斯利普家的露台上，想把事情理出个头绪。波士顿，写字楼，美术馆，剧院。冬日里的波士顿公园，空气清冽，生机充盈。新一期债券发行时那阵心跳，如同戏剧首演之夜的心跳——是大红大紫，还是砸在他脚边？朗伍德的网球，查尔斯河上悠长的黄昏，在玛格诺利亚同自己同类的人打高尔夫。昏暗的老客厅里，用精美的茶杯喝下午茶。想着要放弃这一切，他不是疯了是什么？可米纳瓦小姐当时是怎么说的？「一旦你的机会来了——」

===Original===
The problem was a big one, and big problems were annoying out here where the lotus grew. He yawned, and went aimlessly downtown. Drifting into the public library, he saw Charlie Chan hunched over a table that held an enormous volume. John Quincy went closer. The book was made up of back numbers of the Honolulu morning paper, and it was open at a time-yellowed sporting page.
“Hello, Chan. What are you up to?”
The Chinaman gave him a smile of greeting. “Hello. Little bit of careless reading while I gallop about seeking loophole.” He closed the big volume casually. “You seem in the best of health.”
“Oh, I’m all right.”
“No more fierce shots out of bushes?”

===Chinese===
这是个天大的难题，而在这莲花滋长的忘忧之乡，天大的难题最叫人心烦。他打个哈欠，漫无目的地进了城。信步走进公共图书馆，他看见陈查理弓身伏在一张桌子前，桌上摊着一大册合订本。约翰·昆西凑近去看。那册书是檀香山晨报的过刊合订本，翻开在一页被时光熏黄的体育版上。
「你好啊，查理。你在忙什么呢？」
那位华人含笑相迎。「你好。趁我纵马驰骋、四处寻觅裂隙的间隙，做一点儿漫不经心的阅读。」他随手合上那册大书。「您看来康健无比。」
「哦，我好着呢。」
「灌木丛里再没有凶猛的枪击了？」

===Original===
“Not a trigger pulled. I imagine that was a big bluff﻿—nothing more.”
“What do you say﻿—bluff?”
“I mean the fellow’s a coward, after all.”
Chan shook his head solemnly. “Pardon humble suggestion﻿—do not lose carefulness. Hot heads plenty in hot climate.”

===Chinese===
「扳机一下也没扣过。我看那不过是场大大的虚张声势——仅此而已。」
「您说什么——虚张声势？」
「我是说，那家伙到底是个胆小鬼。」
陈查理郑重其事地摇头。「恕我进一卑微之言——切勿失去小心。炎热气候里，滚烫的脑袋多的是。」

===Original===
“I’ll look before I leap,” John Quincy promised. “But I’m afraid I interrupted you.”
“Ridiculous thought,” protested Chan.
“I’ll go along. Let me know if anything breaks.”
“Most certainly. Up to present, everything are intact.”

===Chinese===
「我会三思而后行。」约翰·昆西应承道。「不过我怕是打扰你了。」
「荒谬的念头。」陈查理否认道。
「我这就走。有什么动静，告诉我。」
「必然奉告。迄今为止，一切原封未动。」

===Original===
John Quincy paused at the door of the reference room. Charlie Chan had promptly opened the big book, and was again bending over it with every show of interest.
Returning to Waikiki, John Quincy faced a dull evening. Barbara had gone to the island of Kauai for a visit with old friends of the family. He had not been sorry when she went, for he didn’t feel quite at ease in her presence. The estrangement between the girl and Jennison continued; the lawyer had not been at the dock to see her off. Yes, John Quincy had parted from her gladly, but her absence cast a pall of loneliness over the house on Kalia Road.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西在参考阅览室门口驻了驻足。陈查理早已重新翻开那册大书，正再度俯身其上，一副兴致盎然的样子。
回到怀基基，约翰·昆西面对的是一个乏味的夜晚。芭芭拉到考艾岛（Kauai）拜访家里的老朋友去了。她走的时候他并不惋惜，因为在她跟前他总觉得不甚自在。姑娘和詹尼森之间的疏远仍在继续；她启程那天，律师没有到码头送行。是的，约翰·昆西乐得与她作别，可她一走，卡利亚路上的宅子便罩上了一层寂寞的暗影。

===Original===
After dinner, he sat with his pipe on the lanai. Down the beach at the Reef and Palm pleasant company was available﻿—but he hesitated. He had seen Carlota Egan several times by day, on the beach or in the water. She was very happy now, though somewhat appalled at thought of her approaching visit to England. They’d had several talks about that﻿—daylight talks. John Quincy was a bit afraid to entrust himself﻿—as Chan had said in speaking of his stone idol﻿—of an evening. After all, there was Agatha, there was Boston. There was Barbara, too. Being entangled with three girls at once was a rather wearing experience. He rose, and went downtown to the movies.

===Chinese===
晚饭后，他叼着烟斗坐在露台上。海滩那头的「礁石棕榈」里有的是宜人的陪伴——可他犹豫了。白日里他见过卡洛塔·伊根几回，在沙滩上，或在海水里。她如今很快活，只是想到不久后的英国之行，不免有些发怵。这事他们谈过几次——都是白天的谈话。约翰·昆西有点不敢把自己托付出去——正如陈查理说起他那尊石头偶像时所言——尤其不敢在夜里托付。毕竟，还有阿加莎，还有波士顿。还有芭芭拉呢。同时跟三个姑娘纠缠不清，是相当磨人的一种经历。他起身进城看电影去了。

===Original===
On Saturday morning he was awakened early by the whir of aeroplanes above the house. The American fleet was in the offing, and the little brothers of the air service hastened out to hover overhead in friendly welcome. That day a spirit of carnival prevailed in Honolulu, flags floated from every masthead, and the streets bloomed, as Barbara had predicted, with handsome boys in spotless uniforms. They were everywhere, swarming in the souvenir stores, besieging the soda fountains, skylarking on the trolley-cars. Evening brought a great ball at the beach hotel, and John Quincy, out for a walk, saw that every spic and span uniform moved toward Waikiki, accompanied by a fair young thing who was only too happy to serve as sweetheart in that particular port.

===Chinese===
星期六早晨，屋顶上空飞机的嗡鸣早早把他吵醒。美国舰队已到近海，空军的小兄弟们急忙飞出来，盘旋在头顶上空，以示友好的欢迎。那一天檀香山全城一片狂欢气象，每一根桅顶都飘着旗子，街上也正如芭芭拉所预言的那样，开满了穿一尘不染制服的英俊少年。他们无处不在，成群涌进纪念品商店，围攻冷饮柜台，在电车上嬉闹。入夜，海滨旅馆里有一场盛大的舞会，出门散步的约翰·昆西看见，每一套干净笔挺的制服都在向怀基基移动，身边伴着一位白皙的年轻姑娘——在这座特别的港城充当一回甜心情人，她们求之不得。

===Original===
John Quincy felt, suddenly, rather out of things. Each pretty girl he saw recalled Carlota Egan. He turned his wandering footsteps toward the Reef and Palm, and oddly enough, his pace quickened at once.
The proprietor himself was behind the desk, his eyes calm and untroubled now.
“Good evening, Mr. Egan﻿—or should I say Mr. Cope,” remarked John Quincy.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西忽然觉得自己颇有些格格不入。他见到的每一个漂亮姑娘都让他想起卡洛塔·伊根。他信步转向「礁石棕榈」，说来也怪，脚步顿时快了起来。
旅馆老板本人站在柜台后面，如今他眼里一片平静，再无忧色。
「晚上好，伊根先生——还是我该称您科佩先生？」约翰·昆西说。

===Original===
“Oh, we’ll stick to the Egan, I guess,” the man replied. “Sort of got out of the hang of the other. Mr. Winterslip, I’m happy to see you. Cary will be down in a moment.”
John Quincy gazed about the big public room. It was a scene of confusion, spattered ladders, buckets of paint, rolls of new wallpaper. “What’s going on?” he inquired.
“Freshening things up a bit,” Egan answered. “You know, we’re in society now.” He laughed. “Yes, sir, the old Reef and Palm has been standing here a long time without so much as a glance from the better element of Honolulu. But now they know I’m related to the British Admiralty, they’ve suddenly discovered it’s a quaint and interesting place. They’re dropping in for tea. Just fancy. But that’s Honolulu.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我想我们还是沿用伊根这个姓吧，」那人答道，「那另一个姓，我多少有些生疏了。温特斯利普先生，见到您真高兴。卡丽马上就下来。」
约翰·昆西环视这间宽大的公共客厅。一片忙乱景象：溅满漆点的梯子，一桶桶油漆，一卷卷新墙纸。「这是干什么呢？」他问。
「把屋里翻新翻新，」伊根答道，「您知道的，我们如今跻身社交界了。」他哈哈一笑。「可不是嘛，先生，『礁石棕榈』这爿老店在这儿立了这么多年，檀香山的上流人物连正眼都没瞧过它一回。可如今他们知道了我同英国海军部沾亲，忽然又发现这是个古雅有趣的地方。他们要顺道来喝茶了。您想想吧。可这就是檀香山。」

===Original===
“That’s Boston, too,” John Quincy assured him.
“Yes﻿—and precisely the sort of thing I ran away from England to escape, a good many years ago. I’d tell them all to go to the devil﻿—but there’s Cary. Somehow, women feel differently about those things. It will warm her heart a bit to have these dowagers smile upon her. And they’re smiling﻿—you know, they’ve even dug up the fact that my Cousin George has been knighted for making a particularly efficient brand of soap.” He grimaced. “It’s nothing I’d have mentioned myself﻿—a family skeleton, as I see it. But society has odd standards. And I mustn’t be hard on poor old George. As Arthur says, making soap is good clean fun.”

===Chinese===
「波士顿也一样。」约翰·昆西向他保证。
「是啊——而多年前我逃离英国要躲开的，恰恰就是这种事。我真想叫他们统统见鬼去——可还有卡丽呢。女人家对这类事的感受，总归有些不一样。有这些贵妇人向她青眼相加，多少能暖暖她的心。而且她们还真的笑脸相待——您知道吗，他们居然连我表兄乔治因制造一种格外灵光的肥皂而受封爵士的事，都给刨出来了。」他做了个苦脸。「这本是我绝不会自己提起的——依我看，这是一桩家丑。可社交界的是非标准古怪得很。再说，也不该苛待可怜的老乔治。正如阿瑟所说，造肥皂是桩干净的好消遣。」

===Original===
“Is your brother still with you?”
“No. He’s gone back to finish his job in the Fanning Group. When he returns, I’m sending Cary to England for a long stop. Yes, that’s right﻿—*I’m* sending her,” he added quickly. “I’m paying for these repairs, too. You see, I’ve been able to add a second mortgage to the one already on the poor tottering Reef and Palm. That’s another outcome of my newfound connection with the British Admiralty and the silly old soap business. Here’s Cary now.”

===Chinese===
「令兄还同您在一起吗？」
「不在了。他回范宁群岛收尾他那桩差事去了。等他回来，我就送卡丽去英国长住。是的，没错——是*我*送她去，」他赶忙补了一句，「这些翻修费也是我出。您瞧，我已经在那可怜的、摇摇欲坠的『礁石棕榈』原有的一道按揭之上，又添上了第二道按揭。这又是我同英国海军部新攀的亲、同那桩可笑的老肥皂营生沾光的一桩结果。卡丽这不就来了。」

===Original===
John Quincy turned. And he was glad he had, for he would not willingly have missed the picture of Carlota on the stairs. Carlota in an evening gown of some shimmering material, her dark hair dressed in a new and amazingly effective way, her white shoulders gleaming, her eyes happy at last. As she came quickly toward him he caught his breath, never had he seen her look so beautiful. She must have heard his voice in the office, he reflected, and with surprising speed arrayed herself thus to greet him. He was deeply grateful as he took her hand.
“Stranger,” she rebuked. “We thought you’d deserted us.”
“I’d never do that,” he answered. “But I’ve been rather busy﻿—”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西回过头去。他庆幸自己回了头，因为卡洛塔在楼梯上的这幅画面，他可绝不愿错过。卡洛塔穿着一身闪着微光的料子做的晚礼服，乌黑的头发梳了个新颖别致、效果惊人的发式，雪白的肩膀莹莹生辉，眼里终于盛满快乐。她快步向他走来，他不觉屏住了呼吸——他从没见她显得这样美过。想必是她在办公室里听见了他的声音，他寻思，便以惊人的速度装扮成这样来迎他。他执起她的手，心中深深感激。
「稀客呀，」她嗔道，「我们还当你把我们抛下了呢。」
「我绝不会的，」他答道，「只是近来颇有些忙——」

===Original===
A step sounded behind him. He turned, and there stood one of those ubiquitous navy boys, a tall, blond Adonis who held his cap in his hand and smiled in a devastating way.
“Hello, Johnnie,” Carlota said. “Mr. Winterslip, of Boston, this is Lieutenant Booth, of Richmond, Virginia.”
“How are you,” nodded the boy, without removing his eyes from the girl’s face. Just one of the guests, this Winterslip, no account at all﻿—such was obviously the lieutenant’s idea. “All ready, Cary? The car’s outside.”

===Chinese===
身后响起一阵脚步。他转过身，那儿站着又一个无处不在的海军小伙子——高挑个儿，金发的美少年，手里拿着军帽，笑得摄人心魄。
「你好，约翰尼。」卡洛塔说。「波士顿来的温特斯利普先生，这位是布思中尉，弗吉尼亚州里士本人。」
「你好。」小伙子点点头，眼睛没离开姑娘的脸。这位温特斯利普不过是客人中的一个，全然无足轻重——中尉的心思显然如此。「都准备好了吗，卡丽？车在外面。」

===Original===
“I’m frightfully sorry, Mr. Winterslip,” said the girl, “but we’re off to the dance. This weekend belongs to the navy, you know. You’ll come again, won’t you?”
“Of course,” John Quincy replied. “Don’t let me keep you.”
She smiled at him and fled with Johnnie at her side. Looking after them, John Quincy felt his heart sink to his boots, an unaccountable sensation of age and helplessness. Youth, youth was going through that door, and he was left behind.

===Chinese===
「实在对不住，温特斯利普先生，」姑娘说，「可我们要去舞会。这个周末是属于海军的，您知道的。您还会再来的，是不是？」
「当然。」约翰·昆西答道，「别让我耽搁你们。」
她朝他一笑，同约翰尼并肩飞奔而去。望着他们的背影，约翰·昆西觉得一颗心直沉到靴底，泛起一种莫可名状的衰老与无助之感。青春，青春正走出那道门去，而他给留在了门外。

===Original===
“A great pity she had to run,” said Egan in a kindly voice.
“Why, that’s all right,” John Quincy assured him. “Old friend of the family, this Lieutenant Booth?”
“Not at all. Just a lad Cary met at parties in San Francisco. Won’t you sit down and have a smoke with me?”
“Some other time, thanks,” John Quincy said wearily. “I must hurry back to the house.”

===Chinese===
「真遗憾她非走不可。」伊根语气和善地说。
「嗨，没什么。」约翰·昆西向他保证。「这位布思中尉，是府上的老朋友？」
「不是。不过是卡丽在旧金山舞会上认识的一个小伙子。坐下来陪我抽支烟，如何？」
「改日吧，谢谢。」约翰·昆西倦倦地说，「我得赶紧回宅子里去。」

===Original===
He wanted to escape, to get out into the calm lovely night, the night that was ruined for him now. He walked along the beach, savagely kicking his toes into the white sand. “Johnnie!” She had called him Johnnie. And the way she had looked at him, too! Again John Quincy felt that sharp pang in his heart. Foolish, foolish; better go back to Boston and forget. Peaceful old Boston, that was where he belonged. He was an old man out here﻿—thirty, nearly. Better go away and leave these children to love and the moonlit beach.

===Chinese===
他只想逃开，逃进那宁静可爱的夜色里去——如今这夜色在他算是毁了。他沿着海滩走，狠狠地把脚尖踢进白沙滩里。「约翰尼！」她叫过他约翰尼。还有她望他的那种眼神！约翰·昆西心口又是一阵锐痛。糊涂，糊涂；还是回波士顿去忘掉吧。宁静的老波士顿，那才是他该待的地方。在这边他已是个老人——快三十的人了。还是走吧，把这些孩子留给爱情和月光下的海滩。

===Original===
Miss Minerva had gone in the big car to call on friends, and the house was quiet as the tomb. John Quincy wandered aimlessly about the rooms, gloomy and bereft. Down at the Moana an Hawaiian orchestra was playing and Lieutenant Booth, of Richmond, was holding Carlota close in the intimate manner affected these days by the young. Bah! If he hadn’t been ordered to leave Hawaii, by gad, he’d go tomorrow.
The telephone rang. None of the servants appeared to answer it, so John Quincy went himself.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐坐大汽车访友去了，宅子静得像座坟。约翰·昆西在各房间里漫无目的地踱步，郁郁寡欢，若有所失。莫阿纳（Moana）旅馆那边，一支夏威夷乐队正在演奏，里士满来的布思中尉正以如今年轻人时兴的那种亲昵姿势，把卡洛塔紧紧搂在怀里。呸！要不是有人勒令他离开夏威夷，天哪，他明天就走。
电话铃响了。仆人们一个也没露面来接，约翰·昆西只好自己去。

===Original===
“Charlie Chan speaking,” said a voice. “That is you, Mr. Winterslip? Good. Big events will come to pass very quick. Meet me drug and grocery emporium of Liu Yin, number 927 River Street, soon as you can do so. You savvy locality?”
“I’ll find it,” cried John Quincy, delighted.
“By bank of stream. I will await. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「我是陈查理。」一个声音说。「是您吗，温特斯利普先生？好。大事即将很快发生。请尽快到河街（River Street）927 号刘因（Liu Yin）的药料杂货大铺来见我。这地方您 savvy（懂）吗？」
「我会找到的。」约翰·昆西喜出望外地喊道。
「在溪岸边。我恭候。再见。」

===Original===
Action﻿—action at last! John Quincy’s heart beat fast. Action was what he wanted tonight. As usually happens in a crisis, there was no automobile available; the roadster was at a garage undergoing repairs, and the other car was in use. He hastened over to Kalakaua Avenue intending to rent a machine, but a trolley approaching at the moment altered his plans and he swung aboard.
Never had a trolley moved at so reluctant a pace. When they reached the corner of Fort Street in the center of the city, he left it and proceeded on foot. The hour was still fairly early, but the scene was one of somnolent calm. A couple of tourists drifted aimlessly by. About the bright doorway of a shooting gallery loitered a group of soldiers from the fort, with a sprinkling of enlisted navy men. John Quincy hurried on down King Street, past Chinese noodle cafés and pawn shops, and turned presently off into River Street.

===Chinese===
行动——终于有行动了！约翰·昆西心跳加速。今晚他要的就是行动。而每逢危急关头总赶巧没有汽车可用：敞篷车在车行里修着，另一辆车又让人用走了。他匆匆赶往卡拉卡瓦大道，打算租一辆，恰在此时驶来一辆电车，改变了他的计划，他一跃上了车。
从没有一辆电车开得这么不情愿。到了市中心的福特街街口，他下车步行。时候还相当早，可街景已是一片昏昏欲睡的宁静。两个游客漫无目的地飘然而过。一家打靶场灯光明亮的门口，闲荡着一群要塞来的士兵，夹杂着几名海军士兵。约翰·昆西匆匆沿国王街往前赶，越过一家家中式面馆和当铺，不多时便拐进了河街。

===Original===
On his left was the river, on his right an array of shabby stores. He paused at the door of number 927, the establishment of Liu Yin. Inside, seated behind a screen that revealed only their heads, a number of Chinese were engrossed in a friendly little game. John Quincy opened the door; a bell tinkled, and he stepped into an odor of must and decay. Curious sights met his quick eye, dried roots and herbs, jars of seahorse skeletons, dejected ducks flattened out and varnished to tempt the palate, gobbets of pork. An old Chinaman rose and came forward.
“I’m looking for Mr. Charlie Chan,” said John Quincy.

===Chinese===
左边是那条河，右边是一溜寒酸的铺面。他在 927 号门前停下，这便是刘因的铺子。铺子里，几个人坐在一道屏风后面，只露出脑袋，正全神贯注于一场和气的小牌局。约翰·昆西推门进去；铃铛叮的一响，他迈进门内一股霉烂的气味。敏锐的眼睛撞见种种奇景：干树根和草药，一坛坛海马骨架，压得扁扁、上了漆以勾人食欲的蔫头蔫脑的板鸭，一块块猪肉。一个中国老头起身迎上来。
「我找陈查理先生。」约翰·昆西说。

===Original===
The old man nodded and led the way to a red curtain across the rear of the shop. He lifted it, and indicated that John Quincy was to pass. The boy did so, and came into a bare room furnished with a cot, a table on which an oil lamp burned dimly behind a smoky chimney, and a couple of chairs. A man who had been sitting on one of the chairs rose suddenly; a huge red-haired man with the smell of the sea about him.
“Hello,” he said.
“Is Mr. Chan here?” John Quincy inquired.

===Chinese===
老头点点头，领他走到店铺后部一道红帘子跟前。他掀起帘子，示意约翰·昆西进去。小伙子依言而入，来到一间四壁萧然的屋子，屋里陈设着一张帆布床、一张桌子——油灯在熏黑的玻璃罩后幽幽地燃着——还有两把椅子。一个坐在椅子上的人霍地站起；是个红头发的大个子，浑身一股海腥气。
「你好。」他说。
「陈查理先生在吗？」约翰·昆西问。

===Original===
“Not yet. He’ll be along in a minute. What say to a drink while we’re waiting. Hey, Liu, a coupla glasses that rotten rice wine!”
The Chinaman withdrew. “Sit down,” said the man. John Quincy obeyed; the sailor sat too. One of his eyelids drooped wickedly; he rested his hands on the table﻿—enormous hairy hands. “Charlie’ll be here pretty quick,” he said. “Then I got a little story to tell the two of you.”

===Chinese===
「还没有。他一会儿就到。等着的时候来一杯怎么样。喂，老刘，来两杯那烂米酒！」
那华人退了出去。「坐吧。」那人说。约翰·昆西坐下了；水手也坐下了。他一只眼皮不怀好意地耷拉着；他把双手搁在桌上——两只毛茸茸的巨手。「查理很快就到，」他说，「到时我有个小故事，要讲给你们两位听。」

===Original===
“Yes?” John Quincy replied. He glanced about the little vile-smelling room. There was a door, a closed door, at the back. He looked again at the red-haired man. He wondered how he was going to get out of there.
For he knew now that Charlie Chan had not called him on the telephone. It came to him belatedly that the voice was never Charlie’s. “You savvy locality?” the voice had said. A clumsy attempt at Chan’s style, but Chan was a student of English; he dragged his words painfully from the poets; he was careful to use nothing that savored of “pidgin.” No, the detective had not telephoned; he was no doubt at home now bending over his chessboard, and here was John Quincy shut up in a little room on the fringe of the River District with a husky sailorman who leered at him knowingly.

===Chinese===
「是吗？」约翰·昆西应道。他扫了一眼这间恶臭的小屋。后面有一道门，一道关着的门。他又看了看那红头发大汉。他琢磨起自己怎么才能从这儿脱身。
因为他现在明白了，打电话召他来的并不是陈查理。他迟迟才醒悟，那声音根本就不是陈查理的。「这地方您 savvy 吗？」那声音曾这样问。那是模仿陈查理腔调的一次笨拙尝试，可陈查理是研习英语的人；他的字句都是从诗人那里艰难地拖曳而来；他措辞谨慎，绝不用任何沾着「洋泾浜」味儿的词。不，侦探没有来过电话；他此刻多半正俯身在自家棋盘上，而约翰·昆西却被关在河区边缘的一间小屋里，同他关在一起的，是个知情狞笑的彪形水手。

===Original===
The old Chinaman returned with two small glasses into which the liquor had already been poured. He set them on the table. The red-haired man lifted one of them. “Your health, sir,” he said.
John Quincy took up the other glass and raised it to his lips. There was a suspicious eagerness in the sailor’s one good eye. John Quincy put the glass back on the table. “I’m sorry,” he said. “I don’t want a drink, thank you.”
The great face with its stubble of red beard leaned close to his. “Y’ mean you won’t drink with me?” said the red-haired man belligerently.

===Chinese===
那中国老头端着两只小杯回来了，酒已经斟好。他把杯子放到桌上。红头发大汉举起其中一只。「祝您健康，先生。」他说。
约翰·昆西拿起另一只杯，举到唇边。水手那只完好的眼睛里透着一股可疑的热切。约翰·昆西把杯子放回桌上。「很抱歉，」他说，「我不想喝，谢谢。」
那张带着红胡茬的大脸凑到他面前。「你是说，你不肯陪我喝？」红头发大汉挑衅道。

===Original===
“That’s just what I mean,” John Quincy answered. Might as well get it over with, he felt; anything was better than this suspense. He stood up. “I’ll be going along,” he announced.
He took a step toward the red curtain. The sailor, evidently a fellow of few words, rose and got in his way. John Quincy, himself feeling the futility of talk, said nothing, but struck the man in the face. The sailor struck back with efficiency and promptness. In another second the room was full of battle, and John Quincy saw red everywhere, red curtain, red hair, red lamp flame, great red hairy hands cunningly seeking his face. What was it Roger had said? “Ever fought with a ship’s officer﻿—the old-fashioned kind with fists like flying hams?” No, he hadn’t up to then, but that sweet experience was his now, and it came to John Quincy pleasantly that he was doing rather well at his new trade.

===Chinese===
「正是这个意思。」约翰·昆西答道。他心想，索性摊牌了事；怎么都好过这样悬着。他站起身。「我这就走了。」他宣布。
他朝红帘子迈了一步。那水手显然是个惜字如金的家伙，站起来拦住去路。约翰·昆西自己也觉得多说无益，便一言不发，一拳打在那人脸上。水手回击得又利落又及时。转眼间满屋子都是战斗，约翰·昆西满眼皆红：红帘子，红头发，红灯焰，一双双巨大的红毛手狡诈地摸索他的脸。罗杰当初是怎么说的来着？「可曾跟船上的军官干过架——老式的那种，一对拳头抡起来像两只飞舞的火腿？」没有，在那之前他没有，可这份美妙的体验如今归他了，而且约翰·昆西愉快地发现，自己在这门新行当里干得还算不赖。

===Original===
This was better than the attic; here he was prepared and had a chance. Time and again he got his hands on the red curtain, only to be dragged back and subjected to a new attack. The sailor was seeking to knock him out, and though many of his blows went home, that happy result﻿—from the standpoint of the red-haired man﻿—was unaccountably delayed. John Quincy had a similar aim in life; they lunged noisily about the room, while the surprising Orientals in the front of the shop continued their quiet game.

===Chinese===
这可比阁楼那一回强多了；这回他有备而来，有一线机会。他一次又一次把手抓到红帘子上，却一次次被拖回来，挨一顿新的攻击。水手一心要把他打晕过去，虽然许多拳都结结实实落了地，那圆满的结果——在红头发大汉看来——却莫名其妙地迟迟不来。约翰·昆西的人生目标与之相仿；两人在屋里轰轰烈烈地跌撞缠斗，而前面铺子里那班处变不惊的东方人，仍继续着他们安静的牌局。

===Original===
John Quincy felt himself growing weary; his breath came painfully; he realized that his adversary had not yet begun to fight. Standing with his back to the table in an idle moment while the red-haired man made plans for the future, the boy hit on a plan of his own. He overturned the table; the lamp crashed down; darkness fell over the world. In the final glimmer of light he saw the big man coming for him and dropping to his knees he tackled in the approved manner of Soldiers’ Field, Cambridge, Massachusetts. Culture prevailed; the sailor went on his head with a resounding thump; John Quincy let go of him and sought the nearest exit. It happened to be the door at the rear, and it was unlocked.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西觉得自己渐渐乏了；喘气都带着疼；他明白对手还没真正开打。趁红头发大汉运筹下一步的空当，他背靠着桌子站着，自己也想出了一条计。他掀翻桌子；油灯轰然砸落；黑暗笼罩了世界。在最后一线灯光里，他看见大汉朝自己扑来，便双膝一屈，以马萨诸塞州坎布里奇士兵球场（Soldiers' Field）公认的标准姿势抱腿猛铲。文化占了上风；水手一声轰响，头朝下栽了过去；约翰·昆西撒开手，寻找最近的出口。那恰是后门，而且没上锁。

===Original===
He passed hurriedly through a cluttered back yard and climbing a fence, found himself in the neighborhood known as the River District. There in crazy alleys that have no names, no sidewalks, no beginning and no end, five races live together in the dark. Some houses were above the walk level, some below, all were out of alignment. John Quincy felt he had wandered into a futurist drawing. As he paused he heard the whine and clatter of Chinese music, the clicking of a typewriter, the rasp of a cheap phonograph playing American jazz, the distant scream of an auto horn, a child wailing Japanese lamentations. Footsteps in the yard beyond the fence roused him, and he fled.

===Chinese===
他匆匆穿过一个堆满杂物的后院，翻过一道栅栏，发觉自己已站在人称河区（River District）的地界上。在那里，疯疯癫癫的小巷没有名字，没有人行道，没有起点也没有终点，五个种族在黑暗中杂居一处。有的房子高出路面，有的低于路面，全都歪歪斜斜不成行列。约翰·昆西觉得自己闯进了一幅未来派画作。他驻脚时，听见中国音乐的呜咽与铿锵，打字机的嗒嗒声，廉价留声机沙哑放着的美国爵士乐，远处汽车喇叭的尖叫，还有一个孩子哭唱日本挽歌。栅栏那边院子里响起的脚步声惊醒了他，他拔腿便逃。

===Original===
He must get out of this mystic maze of mean alleys, and at once. Odd painted faces loomed in the dusk: pasty-white faces with just a suggestion of queer costumes beneath. A babel of tongues, queer eyes that glittered, once a lean hand on his arm. A group of moon-faced Chinese children under a lamp who scattered at his approach. And when he paused again, out of breath, the patter of many feet, bare feet, sandaled feet, the clatter of wooden clogs, the squeak of cheap shoes made in his own Massachusetts. Then suddenly the thump of large feet such as might belong to a husky sailor. He moved on.

===Chinese===
他必须逃出这座由卑陋小巷织成的迷魂阵，而且要快。暮色里浮出一张张涂画得古怪的脸：浆白的花脸，底下隐隐透出古怪的衣裳。众声嘈杂如巴别塔，怪眼睛灼灼放光，有一回一只干瘦的手还搭上了他的胳膊。灯下一群月亮脸的中国孩子，见他走近便四散而去。待他再次驻足，已喘作一团，只听得无数脚步的轻响：光脚，草鞋脚，木屐的呱嗒，还有他自己马萨诸塞州出产的廉价皮鞋的吱嘎声。接着突然响起大脚的咚咚声，像是某个彪形水手的。他又继续前行。

===Original===
Presently he came into the comparative quiet of River Street, and realized that he had traveled in a circle, for there was Liu Yin’s shop again. As he hurried on toward King Street, he saw, over his shoulder, that the red-haired man still followed. A big touring car, with curtains drawn, waited by the curb. John Quincy leaped in beside the driver.
“Get out of here, quick!” he panted.
A sleepy Japanese face looked at him through the gloom. “Busy now.”

===Chinese===
不多时，他来到相对安静的河街，这才发觉自己兜了个圈子——刘因的铺子又到了。他朝国王街匆匆赶去，回头瞥见那红头发大汉仍在追赶。一辆大型游览车，帘子拉着，停在路边。约翰·昆西一跃跳上车，坐到司机旁边。
「快离开这儿，快！」他喘着气说。
昏暗里一张睡眼惺忪的日本人的脸望着他。「这会儿忙着呢。」

===Original===
“I don’t care if you are﻿—” began John Quincy, and glanced down at one of the man’s arms resting on the wheel. His heart stood still. In the dusk he saw a wrist watch with an illuminated dial, and the numeral two was very dim.
Even as he looked, strong hands seized him by the collar and dragged him into the dark tonneau. At the same instant, the red-haired man arrived.

===Chinese===
「你忙不忙我不管——」约翰·昆西刚一开口，目光落到那人搭在方向盘上的一条胳膊上。他的心顿时停住了。暮色中，他看见一块带夜光表盘的手表，而数字二非常暗淡。
正看着，几只强有力的手已经揪住他的衣领，把他拖进后座黑暗的车厢里。同一瞬间，红头发大汉也赶到了。

===Original===
“Got him, Mike? Say, that’s luck!” He leaped into the rear of the car. Quick able work went forward, John Quincy’s hands were bound behind his back, a vile-tasting gag was put in his mouth. “Damned if this bird didn’t land me one in the eye,” said the red-haired man. “I’ll pay him for it when we get aboard. Hey you﻿—Pier 78. Show us some speed!”
The car leaped forward. John Quincy lay on the dusty floor, bound and helpless. To the docks? But he wasn’t thinking of that, he was thinking of the watch on the driver’s wrist.

===Chinese===
「抓到他了，迈克？嘿，真走运！」他跳上汽车后座。一番麻利干练的活计随即开工：约翰·昆西的双手被反绑起来，嘴里塞进一团味道恶臭的堵嘴布。「这小子居然给我眼上来了一拳，真见鬼。」红头发大汉说，「等上了船我再跟他算账。喂，你——78 号码头。拿出点速度来！」
汽车猛地窜了出去。约翰·昆西躺在满是尘土的车厢地板上，被捆着，动弹不得。去码头？可他想的不是这个，他想的是司机腕上那块表。

===Original===
A brief run, and they halted in the shadow of a pier-shed. John Quincy was lifted and propelled none too gently from the car. His cheek was jammed against one of the buttons holding the side curtain, and he had sufficient presence of mind to catch the gag on it and loosen it. As they left the car he tried to get a glimpse of its license plate, but he was able to ascertain only the first two figures﻿—33﻿—before it sped away.
His two huge chaperons hurried him along the dock. Some distance off he saw a little group of men, three in white uniforms, one in a darker garb. The latter was smoking a pipe. John Quincy’s heart leaped. He maneuvered the loosened gag with his teeth, so that it dropped about his collar. “Goodbye, Pete!” he shouted at the top of his lungs, and launched at once into a terrific struggle to break away from his startled captors.

===Chinese===
跑了一小段，车在一座码头货棚的阴影里停住。约翰·昆西被抬起来，毫不温柔地推下车。他的脸颊顶在固定侧帘的一颗钮扣上，他足够镇定，把嘴里的堵布挂上那颗钮扣，扯松了它。他们下车时，他试图瞟一眼车牌，却只来得及看清头两个数字——33——车便飞驰而去。
那两位大块头「护卫」押着他沿码头匆匆前行。不远处立着一小群人，三个穿白制服，一个穿深色衣裳。后者正抽着烟斗。约翰·昆西的心猛地一跳。他用牙齿拨弄那扯松的堵布，让它垂落到衣领边。「再见，皮特！」他扯开最大的嗓门大喊，随即发足蛮劲挣扎起来，要从吓了一跳的抓捕者手里脱身。

===Original===
There was a moment’s delay, and then the clatter of feet along the dock. A stocky boy in a white uniform began an enthusiastic debate with Mike, and the other two were prompt to claim the attention of the red-haired man. Pete Mayberry was at John Quincy’s back, cutting the rope on his wrists.
“Well, I’ll be damned, Mr. Winterslip,” he cried.
“Same here,” laughed John Quincy. “Shanghaied in another minute but for you.” He leaped forward to join the battle, but the red-haired man and his friend had already succumbed to youth and superior forces, and were in full retreat. John Quincy followed joyously along the dock, and planted his fist back of his old adversary’s ear. The sailor staggered, but regained his balance and went on.

===Chinese===
略停了一停，码头上随即响起奔跑的脚步声。一个穿白制服的矮壮小伙子同迈克热火朝天地争执起来，另两位也当即分头去招呼红头发大汉。皮特·梅伯里在约翰·昆西背后，割着他腕上的绳子。
「哎呀，这可真见鬼了，温特斯利普先生。」他喊道。
「我也是。」约翰·昆西大笑。「要不是你，再过一分钟我就要被绑上海船了。」他跃身加入战团，可红头发大汉和他的朋友早已屈服于青春与优势兵力，全线溃退了。约翰·昆西快活地沿着码头追上去，照着老对手的耳后结结实实奉上一拳。水手踉跄了一下，又稳住身子，接着逃。

===Original===
John Quincy returned to his rescuers. “The last blow is the sweetest,” he remarked.
“I can place those guys,” said Mayberry. “They’re off that tramp steamer that’s been lying out in the harbor the past week. An opium runner, I’ll gamble on it. You go to the police station right away﻿—”
“Yes,” said John Quincy, “I must. But I want to thank you, Mr. Mayberry. And”﻿—he turned to the white uniforms﻿—“you fellows too.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西回到救兵们身边。「最后一拳最是甜美。」他说。
「这两个家伙我有处可查，」梅伯里说，「他们是从上周起一直泊在港外的那艘不定期货船上下来的。八成是条鸦片走私船，我敢打赌。你这就去警察局——」
「是，」约翰·昆西说，「我这就去。不过我要谢谢你，梅伯里先生。还有——」他转向那些白制服——「也谢谢你们几位。」

===Original===
The stocky lad was picking up his cap. “Why, that’s all right,” he said. “A real pleasure, if you ask me. But look here, old timer,” he added, addressing Mayberry, “how about your Honolulu waterfront and its lost romance? You go tell that to the marines.”
As John Quincy hurried away Pete Mayberry was busily explaining that the thing was unheard of﻿—not in twenty years﻿—maybe more than that﻿—his voice died in the distance.

===Chinese===
那矮壮小伙子正捡他的军帽。「嗨，没什么，」他说，「要我说，实在是件乐事。不过你听着，老前辈，」他冲着梅伯里补了一句，「你那檀香山滨水区和它失落的罗曼史，这回可怎么说？你把那一套讲给海军陆战队听去吧。」
约翰·昆西匆匆离去时，皮特·梅伯里还在忙着解释：这种事闻所未闻——二十年里没出过——兴许还不止——他的声音消失在远处。

===Original===
Hallet was in his room, and John Quincy detailed his evening’s adventure. The captain was incredulous, but when the boy came to the wrist watch on the driver of the car, he sat up and took notice.
“Now you’re talking,” he cried. “I’ll start the force after that car tonight. First two figures 33, you say. I’ll send somebody aboard that tramp, too. They can’t get away with stuff like that around here.”
“Oh, never mind them,” said John Quincy magnanimously. “Concentrate on the watch.”

===Chinese===
哈利特正在自己房里，约翰·昆西详细讲了他这一夜的历险。警长起初满腹狐疑，可等小伙子讲到汽车司机腕上的手表，他立刻坐直了身子，郑重其事起来。
「这才像话。」他喊道，「今晚我就派全部警力去追那辆车。头两个数字是 33，你说。那艘不定期货船，我也要派人上去查。在这一带干出这种事来，休想逍遥法外。」
「哦，别管他们了，」约翰·昆西宽宏大量地说，「把力气都使在那块表上。」

===Original===
Back in the quiet town he walked with his head up, his heart full of the joy of battle. And while he thought of it, he stepped into the cable office. The message he sent was addressed to Agatha Parker on that Wyoming ranch. “San Francisco or nothing,” was all it said.
As he walked down the deserted street on his way to the corner to wait for his trolley, he heard quick footsteps on his trail again. Who now? He was sore and weary, a bit fed up on fighting for one evening. He quickened his pace. The steps quickened too. He went even faster. So did his pursuer. Oh, well, might as well stop and face him.
John Quincy turned. A young man rushed up, a lean young man in a cap.

===Chinese===
回到静静的城里，他昂首而行，满心都是战斗的欢愉。趁着还没忘，他踱进电报局。他发出的电报，是拍给怀俄明牧场上的阿加莎·帕克的。电文只有一句：「旧金山，否则免谈。」
沿着空无一人的街道走向街角去等电车时，他又听见身后响起急促的脚步声。这回又是谁？他浑身酸痛，疲乏不堪，一个晚上打一场架已经够受的了。他加快脚步。那脚步也加快了。他走得更快。追赶的人也一样。唉，也罢，索性停下身来照面。
约翰·昆西回过身。一个年轻人奔上前来，是个戴便帽的瘦削青年。

===Original===
“Mr. Winterslip, ain’t it?” He thrust a dark brown object into John Quincy’s hand. “Your July *Atlantic*, sir. Came in on the *Maui* this morning.”
“Oh,” said John Quincy limply. “Well, I’ll take it. My aunt might like to look at it. Keep the change.”
“Thank you, sir,” said the newsman, touching his cap.

===Chinese===
「温特斯利普先生，是吧？」他把一个深褐色的物件塞进约翰·昆西手里。「您的七月号《大西洋月刊》，先生。今早随毛伊号（*Maui*）轮到的。」
「哦。」约翰·昆西有气无力地说。「好吧，我收下。我姑婆没准想翻一翻。零钱不用找了。」
「谢谢您，先生。」报贩碰了碰帽檐。

===Original===
John Quincy rode out to Waikiki on the last seat of the car. His face was swollen and cut, every muscle ached. Under his arm, clasped tightly, he held the July *Atlantic*. But he didn’t so much as look at the table of contents. “We move, we advance,” he told himself exultantly. For he had seen the watch with the illuminated dial﻿—the dial on which the numeral two was very dim.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西坐在车厢最后一排座位上，坐车回怀基基。他脸也肿了，皮也破了，每块肌肉都在酸痛。腋下紧紧夹着那本七月号《大西洋月刊》。可他连目录都懒得看上一眼。「我们行动了，我们推进了。」他扬扬得意地对自己说。因为他已经见过那块带夜光表盘的手表——那表盘上，数字二非常暗淡。

## XX The Story of Lau Ho / 二十 刘何的故事

===Original===
Early Sunday morning John Quincy was awakened by a sharp knock on his door. Rising sleepily and donning dressing-gown and slippers, he opened it to admit his Aunt Minerva. She had a worried air.

“Are you all right, John Quincy?” she inquired.

“Surely. That is, I would be if I hadn’t been dragged out of bed a full hour before I intended to get up.”

“I’m sorry, but I had to have a look at you.” She took a newspaper from under her arm and handed it to him. “What’s all this?”

An eight-column head on the first page caught even John Quincy’s sleepy eye. “Boston Man has Strange Adventure on Waterfront.” Smaller heads announced that Mr. John Quincy Winterslip had been rescued from an unwelcome trip to China, “in the nick of time” by three midshipmen from the *Oregon*. Poor Pete Mayberry! He had been the real hero of the affair, but his own paper would not come out again until Monday evening, and rivals had beaten him to the story.

===Chinese===
星期日清晨，约翰·昆西被一阵急促的敲门声惊醒。他睡眼惺忪地爬起来，披上晨衣、趿上拖鞋，开了门，请进米纳瓦姑婆。她一脸焦急的神色。

「你没事吧，约翰·昆西？」她问。

「当然没事。我是说，要不是比原定起床的钟点整整早一个钟头被人从床上拖起来，我本来没事的。」

「对不起，可我非得亲眼看看你不可。」她从臂弯底下抽出一份报纸，递给他。「这到底是怎么回事？」

头版上一条八栏通栏大标题，连约翰·昆西惺忪的睡眼都攫住了：「波士顿男子码头奇遇」。小标题则宣布：约翰·昆西·温特斯利普先生险些被迫作一趟中国之旅，幸得「俄勒冈号」（*Oregon*）上三名海军见习生「千钧一发」之际搭救。可怜的皮特·梅伯里！他才是这件事真正的英雄，可惜他自己那份报纸要到星期一晚上才再出刊，同行们抢先把这则新闻登了出去。

===Original===
John Quincy yawned. “All true, my dear,” he said. “I was on the verge of leaving you when the navy saved me. Life, you perceive, has become a musical comedy.”

“But why should anyone want to shanghai you?” cried Miss Minerva.

“Ah, I hoped you’d ask me that. It happens that your nephew has a brain. His keen analytical work as a detective is getting someone’s goat. He admitted as much in a letter he sent me the night he took a few shots at my head.”

“Someone shot at you!” gasped Miss Minerva.

“I’ll say so. You rather fancy yourself as a sleuth, but is anybody taking aim at you from behind bushes? Answer me that.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西打了个哈欠。「千真万确，亲爱的，」他说。「我眼看就要离你而去了，海军救了我。生活嘛，你瞧，已经变成一出轻歌剧了。」

「可为什么竟有人要把你绑上船拐走呢？」米纳瓦小姐嚷道。

「啊，我就盼着你问这一句。你这位侄子恰好长了颗脑子。他当侦探的那些敏锐分析功夫，惹得某人大动肝火。有天晚上他朝我脑袋开了几枪，还写了封信来，自己亲口承认了这一点。」

「有人朝你开枪！」米纳瓦小姐倒抽一口冷气。

「可不是嘛。你自命是个大侦探，可有人躲在树丛后面朝你瞄准吗？回答我这个问题。」

===Original===
Miss Minerva sat down weakly on a chair. “You’re going home on the next boat,” she announced.

He laughed. “About two weeks ago I made that suggestion to you. And what was your reply? Ah, my dear, the tables are turned. I’m not going home on the next boat. I may never go home. This gay, carefree, sudden country begins to appeal to me. Let me read about myself.”

He returned to the paper. “The clock was turned back thirty years on the Honolulu waterfront last night,” began the somewhat imaginative account. It closed with the news that the tramp steamer *Mary S. Allison* had left port before the police could board her. Evidently she’d had steam up and papers ready, and was only awaiting the return of the red-haired man and his victim. John Quincy handed the newspaper back to his aunt.

“Too bad,” he remarked. “They slipped through Hallet’s fingers.”

“Of course they did,” she snapped. “Everybody does. I’d like a talk with Captain Hallet. If I could only tell him what I think of him, I’d feel better.”

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐浑身无力，跌坐在一把椅子上。「下一班船你就给我回家去，」她宣布。

他笑了。「约莫两个星期前，这话还是我对你说来着。当时你是怎么回答的？啊，亲爱的，如今风水轮流转了。我不打算乘下一班船回家。说不定永远不回去了。这个快活、无忧无虑、说变就变的国度，开始让我着迷了。让我看看报上怎么写我。」

他重新看起报来。「昨夜，檀香山码头的时钟被倒拨了三十年，」那篇想象力颇丰的报道这样开了头。结尾的消息是：那艘不定期货轮「玛丽·S·艾利森号」（*Mary S. Allison*）赶在警察登船之前驶离了港口。显而易见，它锅炉早已升汽、证件一应俱全，专等那个红头发男人和他的猎物归来。约翰·昆西把报纸递还给姑婆。

「真糟糕，」他说。「他们从哈利特警长的指缝里溜掉了。」

「他们当然溜掉了，」她尖刻地说。「谁不从他指缝里溜掉啊。我倒想跟哈利特警长谈谈。要是能把我对他的看法痛痛快快说出来，我心里还痛快些。」

===Original===
“Save that paper,” John Quincy said. “I want to send it to mother.”

She stared at him. “Are you mad? Poor Grace—she’d have a nervous breakdown. I only hope she doesn’t hear of this until you’re back in Boston safe and sound.”

“Oh, yes—Boston,” laughed John Quincy. “Quaint old town, they tell me. I must visit there some day. Now if you’ll leave me a minute, I’ll prepare to join you at breakfast and relate the story of my adventurous life.”

“Very well,” agreed Miss Minerva, rising. She paused at the door. “A little witch-hazel might help your face.”

“The scars of honorable battle,” said her nephew. “Why remove them?”

“Honorable fiddlesticks,” Miss Minerva answered. “After all, the Back Bay has its good points.” But in the hall outside she smiled a delighted little smile.

===Chinese===
「把那份报纸留着，」约翰·昆西说。「我要寄给母亲。」

她直勾勾地瞪着他。「你疯了吗？可怜的格蕾丝——她会精神崩溃的。我只盼着她别听说这件事，一直瞒到你平平安安回到波士顿。」

「哦，是啊——波士顿，」约翰·昆西笑道。「听说是个古雅的老城。哪天我一定去游览一番。现在请你回避一分钟，我收拾一下就来陪你吃早饭，讲讲我这充满冒险的一生。」

「好吧，」米纳瓦小姐起身应允。她在门口停了停。「敷点金缕梅水，兴许对你的脸有好处。」

「这是荣誉之战留下的疤，」她的侄子说。「干吗去掉？」

「荣誉个鬼，」米纳瓦小姐回敬道。「说到底，后湾区自有它的好处。」可到了外面的门厅里，她脸上漾开一丝小小的、欢喜的笑。

===Original===
When John Quincy and his aunt were leaving the dining-room after breakfast Kamaikui, stiff and dignified in a freshly-laundered holoku, approached the boy.

“So very happy to see you safe this morning,” she announced.

“Why, thank you, Kamaikui,” he answered. He wondered. Was Kaohla responsible for his troubles, and if so, did this huge silent woman know of her grandson’s activities?

“Poor thing,” Miss Minerva said as they entered the living-room. “She’s been quite downcast since Dan went. I’m sorry for her. I’ve always liked her.”

“Naturally,” smiled John Quincy. “There’s a bond between you.”

“What’s that?”

“Two vanishing races, yours and hers. The Boston Brahman and the pure Hawaiian.”

===Chinese===
早餐过后，约翰·昆西和姑婆正要离开饭厅，卡迈库伊迎上这小伙子。她穿着浆洗一新的霍洛库长裙，身板笔挺，仪态庄重。

「今天早晨见你平安无事，非常高兴。」她说。

「哎呀，谢谢你，卡迈库伊。」他答道。心里却在犯嘀咕：给他惹麻烦的是不是考赫拉？如果是，这位高大的沉默女人，知不知道自己孙子的那些勾当？

「可怜的人，」两人走进客厅时米纳瓦小姐说。「自从丹去了以后，她一直郁郁寡欢。我很替她难过。我一向喜欢她。」

「这很自然，」约翰·昆西微笑道。「你们之间有一条纽带。」

「什么纽带？」

「两个正在消逝的种族，你的和她的。波士顿婆罗门，和纯粹的夏威夷人。」

===Original===
Later in the morning Carlota Egan telephoned him, greatly excited. She had just seen the Sunday paper.

“All true,” he admitted. “While you were dancing your heart out, I was struggling to sidestep a Cook’s tour of the Orient.”

“I shouldn’t have had a happy moment if I’d known.”

“Then I’m glad you didn’t. Big party, I suppose?”

“Yes. You know, I’ve been terribly worried about you ever since that night on the avenue. I want to talk with you. Will you come to see me?”

“Will I? I’m on my way already.”

He hung up the receiver and hastened down the beach. Carlota was sitting on the white sand not far from the Reef and Palm, all in white herself. A serious wide-eyed Carlota quite different from the gay girl who had been hurrying to a party the night before.

===Chinese===
上午迟些时候，卡洛塔·伊根打来电话，激动万分。她刚看过星期天的报纸。

「全是真的，」他承认。「你跳得心花怒放的时候，我正竭力躲闪，不肯上一趟东方的库克旅行社观光游呢。」

「我要是知道了，一秒钟也快活不起来。」

「那我很高兴你没知道。盛大的舞会吧，我猜？」

「是的。你知道，自从那天夜里在大街上出事以后，我一直为你担惊受怕。我想跟你谈谈。你肯来见我吗？」

「肯不肯？我已经在路上了。」

他挂上听筒，快步沿着海滩走去。卡洛塔坐在离「礁石棕榈」不远的白沙滩上，自己也是一身素白。此时的卡洛塔神情严肃、睁大着一双眼睛，与前一夜匆匆赶赴舞会的快活姑娘判若两人。

===Original===
John Quincy dropped down beside her, and for a time they talked of the dance and of his adventure. Suddenly she turned to him.

“I have no right to ask it, I know, but—I want you to do something for me.”

“It will make me very happy—anything you ask.”

“Go back to Boston.”

“What! Not that. I was wrong—that wouldn’t make me happy.”

“Yes, it would. You don’t think so now, perhaps. You’re dazzled by the sun out here, but this isn’t your kind of place. We’re not your kind of people. You think you like us, but you’d soon forget. Back among your own sort—the sort who are interested in the things that interest you. Please go.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西在她身边坐下，有一会儿，两人聊着舞会，也聊着他的历险。忽然她转过脸来。

「我没有权利提这个要求，我知道，可是——我想请你为我做一件事。」

「你提什么都会让我非常快活——什么事都行。」

「回波士顿去。」

「什么！别是这个。刚才我说错了——那件事不会让我快活。」

「会的。眼下你兴许不这么想。你被这里的太阳晃花了眼，可这不是你该待的地方。我们不是你那一路人。你自以为喜欢我们，可你很快就会忘掉的。回到你自己的同类中间去——那些对你感兴趣的东西也感兴趣的人中间去。求你了，走吧。」

===Original===
“It would be retreating under fire,” he objected.

“But you proved your courage, last night. I’m afraid for you. Someone out here has a terrible grudge against you. I’d never forgive Hawaii if—if anything happened to you.”

“That’s sweet of you.” He moved closer. But—confound it—there was Agatha. Bound to Agatha by all the ties of honor. He edged away again. “I’ll think about it,” he agreed.

“I’m leaving Honolulu too, you know,” she reminded him.

“I know. You’ll have a wonderful time in England.”

===Chinese===
「那等于在炮火下撤退，」他表示反对。

「可昨天夜里，你已经证明了自己的勇气。我是为你害怕。这地方有人对你怀恨极深。要是——要是你出了什么事，我永远不能原谅夏威夷。」

「你真好。」他朝她挪近了些。可是——见鬼——还有阿加莎呢。道义上的千丝万缕，还把他和阿加莎拴在一起。他又悄悄挪开了。「我会考虑的。」他应道。

「你知道，我也要离开檀香山了。」她提醒他。

「我知道。你在英国会过得非常快活的。」

===Original===
She shook her head. “Oh, I dread the whole idea. Dad’s heart is set on it, and I shall go to please him. But I shan’t enjoy it. I’m not up to England.”

“Nonsense.”

“No, I’m not. I’m unsophisticated—crude, really—just a girl of the Islands.”

“But you wouldn’t care to stay here all your life?”

“No, indeed. It’s a beautiful spot—to loll about in. But I’ve too much northern blood to be satisfied with that. One of these days I want dad to sell and we’ll go to the mainland. I could get some sort of work—”

“Any particular place on the mainland?”

“Well, I haven’t been about much, of course. But all the time I was at school I kept thinking I’d rather live in San Francisco than anywhere else in the world—”

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。「哦，一想起这整件事我就发怵。爸爸一门心思认准了它，我会去，就为讨他高兴。可我不会快活的。我够不上英国的水准。」

「胡说。」

「不，我够不上。我不谙世故——说实在的，粗陋得很——不过是个海岛上的姑娘。」

「可你也不愿意在这儿待一辈子吧？」

「当然不愿意。这地方很美——闲躺着消磨时光是够美的。可我北方血统太重，不满足于此。将来哪一天，我要爸爸把店卖掉，咱们到大陆去。我能找份什么事做——」

「大陆上有没有特别想去的地方？」

「嗯，我自然没怎么出过门。可上学那些年，我一直想，世界上任何地方都比不上旧金山，我宁愿住在那儿——」

===Original===
“Good,” John Quincy cried. “That’s my choice too. You remember that morning on the ferry, how you held out your hand to me and said: ‘Welcome to your city—’”

“But you corrected me at once. You said you belonged in Boston.”

“I see my error now.”

She shook her head. “A moment’s madness, but you’ll recover. You’re an easterner, and you could never be happy anywhere else.”

“Oh, yes, I could,” he assured her. “I’m a Winterslip, a wandering Winterslip. Any old place we hang our hats—” This time he did lean rather close. “I could be happy anywhere—” he began. He wanted to add “with you.” But Agatha’s slim patrician hand was on his shoulder. “Anywhere,” he repeated, with a different inflection. A gong sounded from the Reef and Palm.

===Chinese===
「好，」约翰·昆西嚷道。「那也是我的选择。你还记得渡船上那个早晨吧，你朝我伸出手来说：『欢迎来到你的城市——』」

「可你立刻就纠正了我。你说你属于波士顿。」

「如今我明白自己的错了。」

她摇了摇头。「一时的痴狂罢了，你会醒过来的。你是东部人，换个地方你永远不会快活。」

「哦，不，我会的，」他向她保证。「我是温特斯利普家的人，流浪的温特斯利普。随便哪个地方，我们挂上帽子便安家——」这一回他当真凑得很近了。「在哪儿我都能快活——」他开了口。他想添上一句「和你在一起」。可阿加莎那只纤细的名门之手搭上了他的肩头。「哪儿都行，」他又重复了一遍，语调却不同了。「礁石棕榈」那边响起了一声锣。

===Original===
