Carlota rose. “That’s lunch.” John Quincy stood too. “It’s beside the point—where you go,” she went on. “I asked you to do something for me.”

“I know. If you’d asked anything else in the world, I’d be up to my neck in it now. But what you suggest would take a bit of doing. To leave Hawaii—and say goodbye to you—”

“I meant to be very firm about it,” she broke in.

“But I must have a little time to consider. Will you wait?”

She smiled up at him. “You’re so much wiser than I am,” she said. “Yes—I’ll wait.”

He went slowly along the beach. Unsophisticated, yes—and charming. “You’re so much wiser than I am.” Where on the mainland could one encounter a girl nowadays who’d say that? He had quite forgotten that she smiled when she said it.

===Chinese===
卡洛塔站起身。「该吃午饭了。」约翰·昆西也站了起来。「你去哪儿——那是枝节问题，」她继续说。「我求你为我做一件事来着。」

「我知道。世上随便什么别的事，只要你开口，我此刻早已一头扎进去不回头了。可你提的这件，办起来有点费事。要离开夏威夷——还要跟你道别——」

「我原打算在这件事上非常坚决的。」她插嘴道。

「可我总得有点时间考虑考虑。你肯等吗？」

她仰头朝他微笑。「你比我聪明得多，」她说。「好——我等。」

他沿着海滩缓缓走去。不谙世故，是的——却也迷人。「你比我聪明得多。」如今在大陆上，上哪儿还能遇见肯说这话的姑娘呢？只是她说这话时脸上的笑，他忘得一干二净了。

===Original===
In the afternoon, John Quincy visited the police station. Hallet was in his room in rather a grouchy mood. Chan was out somewhere hunting the watch. No, they hadn’t found it yet.

John Quincy was mildly reproving. “Well, you saw it, didn’t you?” growled Hallet. “Why in Sam Hill didn’t you grab it?”

“Because they tied my hands,” John Quincy reminded him. “I’ve narrowed the search, down for you to the taxi drivers of Honolulu.”

“Hundreds of them, my boy.”

“More than that, I’ve given you the first two numbers on the license plate of the car. If you’re any good at all, you ought to be able to land that watch now.”

“Oh, we’ll land it,” Hallet said. “Give us time.”

Time was just what John Quincy had to give them. Monday came and went. Miss Minerva was bitterly sarcastic.

===Chinese===
下午，约翰·昆西去了一趟警察局。哈利特待在自己屋里，情绪相当恶劣。陈查理外出到什么地方寻那只手表去了。没有，他们还没有找到。

约翰·昆西略带责备之意。「哼，你不是亲眼见过它了吗？」哈利特吼道。「你到底见什么鬼不把它抓住？」

「因为他们把我的手捆住了呀，」约翰·昆西提醒他。「我已经替你把搜寻范围缩小，缩到檀香山的出租车司机里头了。」

「那可有几百号人呢，我的小伙子。」

「不止如此，我还把那辆车牌照开头的两个数字给了你。你要是还有几分能耐，这会儿该能把那只手表弄到手了。」

「哦，我们会弄到手的，」哈利特说。「给我们点时间。」

时间，恰恰是约翰·昆西唯一拿得出手的东西。星期一来而复去。米纳瓦小姐冷嘲热讽，刻薄之至。

===Original===
“Patience are a very lovely virtue,” John Quincy told her. “I got that from Charlie.”

“At any rate,” she snapped, “it are a virtue very much needed with Captain Hallet in charge.”

In another direction, too, John Quincy was called upon to exercise patience. Agatha Parker was unaccountably silent regarding that short peremptory cable he had sent on his big night in town. Was she offended? The Parkers were notoriously not a family who accepted dictation. But in such a vital matter as this, a girl should be willing to listen to reason.

Late Tuesday afternoon Chan telephoned from the station-house—unquestionably Chan this time. Would John Quincy do him the great honor to join him for an early dinner at the Alexander Young café?

===Chinese===
「耐心，是顶可爱的美德，」约翰·昆西告诉她。「这是跟陈查理学来的。」

「不管怎样，」她抢白道，「只要哈利特警长还主着事，这美德眼下是大大地需要。」

在另一件事上，约翰·昆西也不得不拿出耐心来。他在城里狂欢的那个夜晚发出过一封简短霸道的电报，对此阿加莎·帕克却莫名其妙地杳无回音。她生气了吗？帕克家是出了名的不受人摆布的人家。可在这种性命攸关的大事上，姑娘总该肯听听道理吧。

星期二傍晚，陈查理从警察局打来电话——这回货真价实是陈查理。他问约翰·昆西能否赏他一个天大的脸面，到亚历山大·扬旅馆的咖啡厅同他共进一顿提前的晚餐？

===Original===
“Something doing, Charlie?” cried the boy eagerly.

“Maybe it might be,” answered Chan, “and maybe also not. At six o’clock in hotel lobby, if you will so far condescend.”

“I’ll be there,” John Quincy promised, and he was.

He greeted Chan with anxious, inquiring eyes, but the Chinaman was suave and entirely noncommittal. He led John Quincy to the dining-room and carefully selected a table by a front window.

“Do me the great favor to recline,” he suggested.

John Quincy reclined. “Charlie, don’t keep me in suspense,” he pleaded.

Chan smiled. “Let us not shade the feast with gloomy murder talk,” he replied. “This are social meeting. Is it that you are in the mood to dry up plate of soup?”

“Why, yes, of course,” John Quincy answered. Politeness, he saw, dictated that he hide his curiosity.

===Chinese===
「有动静了，陈查理？」小伙子急切地喊。

「也许有，也许也没有，」陈查理答道，「六点钟，旅馆大堂，若您肯如此屈尊俯就的话。」

「我一定到，」约翰·昆西许了诺，他也确实到了。

他迎向陈查理，眼里满是焦灼和探询，可这位中国人温文圆滑，滴水不漏。他把约翰·昆西领进餐厅，精心挑了一张临街前窗的桌子。

「务请赏光，宽坐。」他建议道。

约翰·昆西坐下了。「陈查理，别吊我胃口了，」他央求道。

陈查理微笑。「咱们莫让阴沉的凶案话头，给这场盛宴蒙上阴影，」他答道。「这是社交性质的会面。您可有兴致，喝干一盘汤水？」

「哎呀，当然有，」约翰·昆西答道。他明白，礼数要求他按捺住自己的好奇心。

===Original===
“Two of the soup,” ordered Chan of a white-jacketed waiter. A car drew up to the door of the Alexander Young. Chan half rose, staring at it keenly. He dropped back to his seat. “It is my high delight to entertain you thus humbly before you are restored to Boston. Converse at some length of Boston. I feel interested.”

“Really?” smiled the boy.

“Undubitably. Gentleman I meet once say Boston are like China. The future of both, he say, lies in graveyards where repose useless bodies of honored guests on high. I am fogged as to meaning.”

“He meant both places live in the past,” John Quincy explained. “And he was right, in a way. Boston, like China, boasts a glorious history. But that’s not saying the Boston of today isn’t progressive. Why, do you know—”

He talked eloquently of his native city. Chan listened, rapt.

“Always,” he sighed, when John Quincy finished, “I have unlimited yearning for travel.” He paused to watch another car draw up before the hotel. “But it are unavailable. I am policeman on small remuneration. In my youth, rambling on evening hillside or by moonly ocean, I dream of more lofty position. Not so now. But that other American citizen, my eldest son, he are dreaming too. Maybe for him dreams eventuate. Perhaps he become second Baby Ruth, home run emperor, applause of thousands making him deaf. Who knows it?”

===Chinese===
「汤，来两份。」陈查理对一位白衣侍者吩咐道。一辆汽车驶到亚历山大·扬旅馆门前。陈查理半抬起身，锐利地盯住它，随即又坐了回去。「在您被恭送回波士顿之前，能这般简慢地款待您，是我莫大的欣喜。请就波士顿畅谈一番。我颇感兴味。」

「当真？」小伙子笑道。

「确乎如此。我曾遇过一位先生说，波士顿就像中国。他说，两者的未来，都躺在墓园里，那里安卧着座上贵客们的无用之躯。此中深意，我如坠五里雾中。」

「他是说，这两个地方都活在过去，」约翰·昆西解释道。「某种程度上他说得对。波士顿和中国一样，有一段辉煌的历史。可这并不是说，今天的波士顿就不求进取。呃，你可知道——」

他滔滔不绝地谈起自己的家乡。陈查理听得入迷。

「一直以来，」约翰·昆西说完后他叹道，「我对旅行怀有无尽的渴望。」他停了停，望着又一辆汽车驶到旅馆门前。「然而它是求之不可得的。我是警察，薪俸微薄。年少时，在黄昏的山坡上、月色的大海边闲步，我梦想过更崇高的位置。如今不了。可另外那位美国公民，我的长子，他也在做梦呢。说不定他的梦想能够成真。也许他会成为第二个贝比·鲁斯（Baby Ruth），全垒打皇帝，千万人的掌声震聋他的耳朵。谁知道呢？」

===Original===
The dinner passed, unshaded by gloomy talk, and they went outside. Chan proffered a cigar of which he spoke in the most belittling fashion. He suggested that they stand for a time before the hotel door.

“Waiting for somebody?” inquired John Quincy, unable longer to dissemble.

“Precisely the fact. Barely dare to mention it, however. Great disappointment may drive up here any minute now.”

An open car stopped before the hotel entrance. John Quincy’s eyes sought the license plate, and he got an immediate thrill. The first two figures were 33.

A party of tourists, a man and two women, alighted. The doorman ran forward and busied himself with luggage. Chan casually strolled across the walk, and as the Japanese driver shifted his gears preparatory to driving away, put a restraining hand on the car door.

“One moment, please.” The Jap turned, fright in his eyes. “You are Okuda, from auto stand across way?”

===Chinese===
这顿饭吃完了，没有阴沉的话头扫兴，两人走到外面。陈查理敬上一支雪茄，言语间把它贬得一文不值。他建议两人在旅馆门前站上一会儿。

「在等什么人吧？」约翰·昆西问，再也装不下去了。

「正是此情。然而几乎不敢声张。天大的失望，随时可能驱车驶到此处。」

一辆敞篷汽车在旅馆入口前停了下来。约翰·昆西的眼睛去寻那车牌，登时心头一激灵。头两个数字正是 33。

一伙观光客下了车——一个男人和两个女人。门房跑上前去张罗行李。陈查理信步踱过人行道，趁那日本司机换挡准备开走的当口，一只手按住了车门。

「请留步。」那日本人转过脸来，眼里满是惊恐。「您是奥田（Okuda），对面出租车候客站的车主吧？」

===Original===
“Yes-s,” hissed the driver.

“You are now returned from exploring island with party of tourists? You leave this spot early Sunday morning?”

“Yes-s.”

“Is it possible that you wear wrist watch, please?”

“Yes-s.”

“Deign to reveal face of same.”

The Jap hesitated. Chan leaned far over into the car and thrust aside the man’s coat sleeve. He came back, a pleased light in his eyes, and held open the rear door. “Kindly embark into tonneau, Mr. Winterslip.” Obediently John Quincy got in. Chan took his place by the driver’s side. “The police station, if you will be so kind.” The car leaped forward.

The essential clue! They had it at last. John Quincy’s heart beat fast there in the rear of the car where, only a few nights before, he had been bound and gagged.

===Chinese===
「是——」司机嘶声答道。

「您现在是载着观光客环岛探险归来了？星期日一早，您是从这个地点出发的？」

「是——」

「请问，您腕上可是戴着一块手表？」

「是——」

「请恩准揭示它的面目。」

那日本人迟疑着。陈查理把身子深深探进车里，拨开那人的衣袖。他直起身回来，眼里闪着欣喜的光，拉开了后车门。「温特斯利普先生，请登上后座。」约翰·昆西顺从地钻了进去。陈查理在司机身旁坐定。「劳驾，去警察局。」汽车向前窜去。

关键的线索！他们终于到手了。约翰·昆西坐在汽车后座里，心跳得飞快——就在几个夜晚之前，他还在这后座上被人捆住手脚、堵住嘴巴来着。

===Original===
Captain Hallet’s grim face relaxed into happy lines when he met them at the door of his room. “You got him, eh? Good work.” He glanced at the prisoner’s wrist. “Rip that watch off him, Charlie.”

Charlie obeyed. He examined the watch for a moment, then handed it to his chief.

“Inexpensive timepiece of noted brand,” he announced. “Numeral two faint and far away. One other fact emerge into light. This Jap here have small wrist. Yet worn place on strap convey impression of being worn by man with wrist of vastly larger circumference.”

Hallet nodded. “Yes, that’s right. Some other man has owned this watch. He had a big wrist—but most men in Honolulu have, you know. Sit down, Okuda. I want to hear from you. You understand what it means to lie to me?”

“I do not lie, sir.”

“No, you bet your sweet life you don’t. First, tell me who engaged your car last Saturday night.”

“Saturday night?”

===Chinese===
哈利特警长在自己房门口迎着他们，铁板的面孔松弛下来，堆起欢喜的纹路。「抓到他了，嗯？干得漂亮。」他瞥了一眼俘虏的手腕。「把那手表从他腕上扯下来，陈查理。」

陈查理照办了。他把表端详了片刻，递给上司。

「名牌的廉价计时器，」他宣布。「数字二模糊而遥远。另有一桩事实浮现于光明之中。这位日本人手腕细小。然而表带上的磨痕给人的印象是，戴它的是位手腕粗壮得多的人士。」

哈利特点头。「对，是这样。这表是别的什么人的。那人手腕粗——不过檀香山多数男人手腕都粗，这你知道。坐下，奥田。我要听听你的交代。你明白对我撒谎是什么下场？」

「我不撒谎，长官。」

「对，谅你也不敢。第一，说说上星期六晚上是谁雇了你的车。」

「星期六晚上？」

===Original===
“That’s what I said!”

“Ah, yes. Two sailors from ship. Engage for evening paying large cash at once. I drive to shop on River Street, wait long time. Then off we go to dock with extra passenger in back.”

“Know the names of those sailors?”

“Could not say.”

“What ship were they from?”

“How can I know? Not told.”

“All right. I’m coming to the important thing. Understand? The truth—that’s what I want! Where did you get this watch?”

Chan and John Quincy leaned forward eagerly. “I buy him,” said the Jap.

===Chinese===
「我正是这么说的！」

「啊，是了。船上的两个水手。雇一整晚，当场先付一大笔现钱。我开车到河街（River Street）一家铺子门口，等了老半天。随后我们一道去码头，后座上多了一位乘客。」

「知道那两个水手的名字吗？」

「说不上来。」

「他们是哪条船上的？」

「我哪里知道？没人告诉我。」

「好。接下来是要紧话了。明白？我要的是实话！这表你是哪儿弄来的？」

陈查理和约翰·昆西急切地凑上前去。「我买的。」那日本人说。

===Original===
“You bought him? Where?”

“At jewel store of Chinese Lau Ho on Maunakea Street.”

Hallet turned to Chan. “Know the place, Charlie?”

Chan nodded. “Yes, indeed.”

“Open now?”

“Open until hour of ten, maybe more.”

“Good,” said Hallet. “Come along, Okuda. You can drive us there.”

===Chinese===
「买的？在哪儿买的？」

「在莫纳克亚街，中国人刘何（Lau Ho）开的珠宝铺。」

哈利特转向陈查理。「认识那地方吗，陈查理？」

陈查理点头。「确乎认识。」

「现在开着门吗？」

「开到十点钟光景，兴许更晚。」

「好，」哈利特说。「一块儿走吧，奥田。你开车送我们过去。」

===Original===
Lau Ho, a little wizened Chinaman, sat back of his work bench with a microscope screwed into one dim old eye. The four men who entered his tiny store filled it to overflowing, but he gave them barely a glance.

“Come on, Ho—wake up,” Hallet cried. “I want to talk to you.”

With the utmost deliberation the Chinaman descended from his stool and approached the counter. He regarded Hallet with a hostile eye. The captain laid the wrist watch on top of a showcase in which reposed many trays of jade.

“Ever see that before?” he inquired.

Lau Ho regarded it casually. Slowly he raised his eyes. “Maybe so. Can not say,” he replied in a high squeaky voice.

Hallet reddened. “Nonsense. You had it here in the store, and you sold it to this Jap. Now, didn’t you?”

Lau Ho dreamily regarded the taxi driver. “Maybe so. Can not say.”

===Chinese===
刘何，一个干瘪矮小的中国老头，坐在工作台后，一只昏花的老眼上旋着一具放大镜。四条汉子走进他那间小铺子，把铺子挤得满满当当，他却几乎没抬眼看一下。

「行了，老何——醒醒神儿，」哈利特喊道。「我要跟你说话。」

那中国人以极度的从容从高凳上下来，踱到柜台前。他带着敌意打量哈利特。警长把那块手表放在一只陈列柜上，柜里安卧着一盘一盘的玉石。

「以前见过它吗？」他问。

刘何漫不经心地瞥了它一眼，缓缓抬起眼来。「兴许见过。说不好。」他用高亢尖细的嗓音答道。

哈利特涨红了脸。「胡说。这表就摆在你铺子里，你还卖给了这个日本人。是不是？」

刘何悠悠惚惚地打量着那出租车司机。「兴许是。说不好。」

===Original===
“Damn it!” cried Hallet. “You know who I am?”

“Policeman, maybe.”

“Policeman maybe yes! And I want you to tell me about this watch. Now wake up and come across or by the Lord Harry—”

Chan laid a deferential hand on his chief’s arm. “Humbly suggest I attempt this,” he said.

Hallet nodded. “All right, he’s your meat, Charlie.” He drew back.

Chan bowed with a great show of politeness. He launched into a long story in Chinese. Lau Ho looked at him with slight interest. Presently he squeaked a brief reply. Chan resumed his flow of talk. Occasionally he paused, and Lau Ho spoke. In a few moments Chan turned beaming.

“Story are now completely extracted like aching tooth,” he said. “Wrist watch was brought to Lau Ho on Thursday, same week as murder. Offered him on sale by young man darkly colored with small knife scar marring cheek. Lau Ho buy and repair watch, interior works being in injured state. Saturday morning he sell at seemly profit to Japanese, presumably this Okuda here but Lau Ho will not swear. Saturday night dark young man appear much overwhelmed with excitement and demand watch again, please. Lau Ho say it is sold to Japanese. Which Japanese? Lau Ho is not aware of name, and can not describe, all Japanese faces being uninteresting outlook for him. Dark young man curse and fly. Appear frequently demanding any news, but Lau Ho is unable to oblige. Such are story of this jewel merchant here.”

===Chinese===
「该死！」哈利特喊道。「你知道我是谁吗？」

「警察，兴许是。」

「警察兴许是，没错！我要你交代这表的事。赶紧醒过来招了，否则老天爷在上——」

陈查理毕恭毕敬地把一只手搭在上司臂上。「卑职斗胆进言，容我来一试。」他说。

哈利特点头。「好吧，他是你碗里的菜了，陈查理。」他退到一旁。

陈查理打了一个极尽礼数的躬，随即用中国话滔滔讲起一个长长的故事。刘何略带兴味地望着他。片刻后，他尖着嗓子简短地回了一句。陈查理又接着讲下去。他不时停顿，刘何便说上几句。不多时，陈查理眉开眼笑地转过身来。

「故事现已如病牙一般，被完完整整拔出，」他说。「那块手表是星期四送到刘何铺子里的，正是凶案发生的那一个星期。一个肤色黝黑的年轻人拿它来求售，脸颊上有个小刀疤损了面相。刘何买下并修好了表，内部机件原是受损状态。星期六早晨，他以体面的利润卖给一个日本人，想必就是眼前这位奥田，但刘何不肯起誓。星期六夜里，那黑肤年轻人现身，激动得不可名状，恳请赎回手表。刘何说已经卖给日本人了。哪个日本人？刘何不知其姓名，也描述不出，盖因所有日本人的面孔在他看来均是无趣的景致。黑肤年轻人咒骂着飞奔而去。此后频频现身，打探任何消息，但刘何爱莫能助。此即这位珠宝商的故事。」

===Original===
They went out on the street. Hallet scowled at the Jap. “All right—run along. I’ll keep the watch.”

“Very thankful,” said the taxi driver, and leaped into his car.

Hallet turned to Chan. “A dark young man with a scar?” he queried.

“Clear enough to me,” Chan answered. “Same are the Spaniard José Cabrera, careless man about town with reputation not so savory. Mr. Winterslip, is it that you have forgotten him?”

John Quincy started. “Me? Did I ever see him?”

“Recall,” said Chan. “It are the night following murder. You and I linger in All American Restaurant engaged in debate regarding hygiene of pie. Door open, admitting Bowker, steward on *President Tyler*, joyously full of okolehau. With him are dark young man—this José Cabrera himself.”

“Oh, I remember now,” John Quincy answered.

===Chinese===
他们走到街上。哈利特朝那日本人皱了皱眉。「行了——去吧。表我留下了。」

「多谢多谢。」出租车司机说着，一跃跳进车里。

哈利特转向陈查理。「一个带疤的黑皮肤年轻人？」他问道。

「在我看来再清楚不过，」陈查理答道。「此人即西班牙人何塞·卡布雷拉（José Cabrera），城里一个吊儿郎当的浪荡子，名声不算太香。温特斯利普先生，您莫非把他忘了？」

约翰·昆西一怔。「我？我见过他吗？」

「请回忆，」陈查理说。「那是凶案次日之夜。您与我流连于全美餐馆，正为馅饼的卫生问题进行争辩。门开处，进来鲍克，泰勒总统号上的乘务员，满腹奥科勒豪，喜气洋洋。与他同来的是个黑肤年轻人——正是这位何塞·卡布雷拉本尊。」

「哦，我想起来了。」约翰·昆西答道。

===Original===
“Well, the Spaniard’s easy to pick up,” said Hallet. “I’ll have him inside an hour—”

“One moment, please,” interposed Chan. “Tomorrow morning at nine o’clock the *President Tyler* return from Orient. No gambler myself but will wager increditable sum Spaniard waits on dock for Mr. Bowker. If you present no fierce objection, I have a yearning to arrest him at that very moment.”

“Why, of course,” agreed Hallet. He looked keenly at Charlie Chan. “Charlie, you old rascal, you’ve got the scent at last.”

“Who—me?” grinned Chan. “With your gracious permission I would alter the picture. Stone walls are crumbling now like dust. Through many loopholes light stream in like rosy streaks of dawn.”

===Chinese===
「哼，那西班牙人好抓，」哈利特说。「不出一个钟头，我就让他进局子——」

「且慢，」陈查理插话。「明晨九点钟，泰勒总统号自东方返航抵埠。在下并非赌徒，却愿押上一笔骇人的巨款：那西班牙人必在码头恭候鲍克先生。您若无强烈异议，在下渴望于那一刻将他逮捕归案。」

「哎呀，当然可以，」哈利特应道。他锐利地端详着陈查理。「陈查理，你这老滑头，总算嗅到踪迹了。」

「谁——我吗？」陈查理咧嘴一笑。「蒙您恩准，在下愿将这幅图景更改。石墙此刻正纷纷崩塌，如尘土一般。透过许多孔隙，光明涌入，宛如黎明玫瑰色的霞缕。」

## XXI The Stone Walls Crumble / 二十一 石墙崩塌

===Original===
The stone walls were crumbling and the light streaming through﻿—but only for Chan. John Quincy was still groping in the dark, and his reflections were a little bitter as he returned to the house at Waikiki. Chan and he had worked together, but now that they approached the crisis of their efforts, the detective evidently preferred to push on alone, leaving his fellow-worker to follow if he could. Well, so be it﻿—but John Quincy’s pride was touched.

He had suddenly a keen desire to show Chan that he could not be left behind like that. If only he could, by some inspirational flash of deductive reasoning, arrive at the solution of the mystery simultaneously with the detective. For the honor of Boston and the Winterslips.

===Chinese===
石墙在崩塌，光在透了进来——可这只是对陈查理而言。约翰·昆西仍在黑暗中摸索，他回到怀基基的宅子时，心里泛着一丝苦涩。他和陈查理本是并肩做事的，可如今眼看努力就要到见分晓的关头，这位侦探却显然宁可独自奋进，把同伴撇在后头，能跟上便跟上。好吧，随他去——只是约翰·昆西的自尊心被刺痛了。

他陡然生出一股强烈的欲望，要向陈查理证明，自己可不是能这样随随便便被甩下的人。但愿他能凭推理演绎的一道灵光，与这位侦探同时抵达谜底。为了波士顿的荣誉，为了温特斯利普家的荣誉。

===Original===
Frowning deeply, he considered all the old discarded clues again. The people who had been under suspicion and then dropped﻿—Egan, the Compton woman, Brade, Kaohla, Leatherbee, Saladine, Cope. He even considered several the investigation had not touched. Presently he came to Bowker. What did Bowker’s reappearance mean?

For the first time in two weeks he thought of the little man with the fierce pompadour and the gold-rimmed eyeglasses. Bowker with his sorrowful talk of vanished barrooms and lost friends behind the bar. How was the steward on the *President Tyler* connected with the murder of Dan Winterslip? He had not done it himself, that was obvious, but in some way he was linked up with the crime. John Quincy spent a long and painful period seeking to join Bowker up with one or another of the suspects. It couldn’t be done.

===Chinese===
他深深地皱着眉，把那些弃置已久的旧线索又逐一琢磨了一遍。那些一度涉嫌、随后又被撂开的人——伊根、康普顿那个女人、布雷德、考赫拉、莱瑟比、萨拉迪尼、科佩。他甚至把调查从未触及的几个人也考虑到了。不多时，他想到鲍克身上。鲍克的重新露面，意味着什么？

两星期来，他头一回想起那个小个子男人：一头怒张的蓬蓬头发，一副金丝边眼镜。还有鲍克那一套忧伤的谈吐，说什么酒馆不见了，柜台后头的老朋友都散了。泰勒总统号上的这位乘务员，同丹·温特斯利普的被杀究竟有什么牵连？动手的显然不是他本人，可他总归以某种方式连着这桩罪案。约翰·昆西苦熬了很长一段时候，想把鲍克同这个或那个嫌疑犯拴到一处。拴不上。

===Original===
All through that Tuesday evening the boy puzzled, so silent and distrait that Miss Minerva finally gave him up and retired to her room with a book. He awoke on Wednesday morning with the problem no nearer solution.

Barbara was due to arrive at ten o’clock from Kauai, and taking the small car, John Quincy went downtown to meet her. Pausing at the bank to cash a check, he encountered his old shipmate on the *President Tyler*, the sprightly Madame Maynard.

===Chinese===
那个星期二的整个晚上，小伙子都在苦苦思索，一言不发，神思恍惚，米纳瓦小姐终于不再理会他，拿了本书回自己房里去了。星期三早晨醒来，那难题离解决仍不见近上半分。

芭芭拉定于十点钟从考爱岛（Kauai）回来，约翰·昆西开出那辆小车，进城去接她。在银行停下来兑支票的当口，他撞见了泰勒总统号上的老同船旅伴——精神矍铄的梅纳德夫人。

===Original===
“I really shouldn’t speak to you,” she said. “You never come to see me.”

“I know,” he answered. “But I’ve been so very busy.”

“So I hear. Running round with policemen and their victims. I have no doubt you’ll go back to Boston and report we’re all criminals and cutthroats out here.”

“Oh, hardly that.”

“Yes, you will. You’re getting a very biased view of Honolulu. Why not stoop to associate with a respectable person now and then?”

===Chinese===
「我真不该理你，」她说。「你从来不上我那儿去。」

「我知道，」他答道。「可我实在太忙了。」

「我也听说了。整天同警察还有他们的受害人间逛。毫无疑问，你回到波士顿就会汇报说，我们这儿的人净是罪犯和凶手。」

「哦，那倒不至于。」

「你就是会。你如今对檀香山的看法偏颇得很。何不偶尔屈尊，同体面人来往来往？」

===Original===
“I’d enjoy it﻿—if they’re all like you.”

“Like me? They’re much more intelligent and charming than I am. Some of them are dropping in at my house tonight for an informal little party. A bit of a chat, and then a moonlight swim. Won’t you come too?”

“I want to, of course,” John Quincy replied. “But there’s Cousin Dan﻿—”

Her eyes flashed. “I’ll say it, even if he was your relative. Ten minutes of mourning for Cousin Dan is ample. I’ll be looking for you.”

John Quincy laughed. “I’ll come.”

“Do,” she answered. “And bring your Aunt Minerva. Tell her I said she might as well be dead as hogtied by convention.”

===Chinese===
「我很乐意——要是他们都像您这样。」

「像我？他们可比我有教养、有魅力多了。今晚有几位要到我家里来，小小聚一聚，不拘礼数。聊聊天，然后月光下游泳。你也来好吗？」

「我当然想去，」约翰·昆西答道。「可是丹堂叔的事——」

她目光一闪。「就算他是你的亲戚，这话我也要说。为丹堂叔哀悼十分钟，尽够了。我等着你来。」

约翰·昆西笑了。「我一定来。」

「一定来啊，」她答道。「把你家米纳瓦姑婆也带来。告诉她这是我说的话：与其让繁文缛节捆住手脚，还不如死了干脆。」

===Original===
John Quincy went out to the corner of Fort and King Streets, near which he had parked the car. As he was about to climb into it, he paused. A familiar figure was jauntily crossing the street. The figure of Bowker, the steward, and with him was Willie Chan, demon back-stopper of the Pacific.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西走出去，到了福特街与国王街的路口，他的车就停在近旁。正要跨进车里，他忽然停住了。一个熟悉的身影正兴冲冲地穿过街来。是乘务员鲍克，身边还走着威利·陈——威震太平洋的魔王捕手。

===Original===
“Hello, Bowker,” John Quincy called.

Mr. Bowker came blithely to join him. “Well, well, well. My old friend Mr. Winterslip. Shake hands with William Chan, the local Ty Cobb.”

“Mr. Chan and I have met before,” John Quincy told him.

“Know all the celebrities, eh? That’s good. Well, we missed you on the *President Tyler*.”

===Chinese===
「你好啊，鲍克。」约翰·昆西招呼道。

鲍克先生欢欢喜喜地走过来。「哎呀，哎呀，哎呀。我的老朋友温特斯利普先生。来同威廉·陈握握手——咱们本地的泰·科布。」

「陈先生和我从前见过。」约翰·昆西告诉他。

「名人全认得，嗯？那挺好。唉，泰勒总统号上怪想你的。」

===Original===
Bowker was evidently quite sober. “Just got in, I take it,” John Quincy remarked.

“A few minutes ago. How about joining us?” He came closer and lowered his voice. “This intelligent young man tells me he knows a taxi-stand out near the beach where one may obtain a superior brand of fusel oil with a very pretty label on the bottle.”

“Sorry,” John Quincy answered. “My cousin’s coming in shortly on an Inter-island boat, and I’m elected to meet her.”

“I’m sorry, too,” said the graduate of Dublin University. “If my strength holds out I’m aiming to stage quite a little party, and I’d like to have you in on it. Yes, a rather large affair﻿—in memory of Tim, and as a last long lingering farewell to the seven seas.”

===Chinese===
鲍克这回显然相当清醒。「我猜是刚进港吧。」约翰·昆西说。

「几分钟前刚到。一块儿走走怎么样？」他凑近些，压低了嗓音。「这位聪明的年轻人告诉我，他知道海滩那头有一处出租汽车候客站，能弄到一种上等杂醇油，瓶子上贴的商标漂亮得很。」

「抱歉，」约翰·昆西答道。「我堂妹马上要坐岛际航船到，大家推了我去接。」

「我也遗憾，」这位都柏林大学的毕业生说。「只要力气撑得住，我打算操办一场小小盛会，很想请你来入一份。不错，排场相当可观——纪念蒂姆，也算同七大洋作最后一次悠悠长长的道别。」

===Original===
“What? You’re *pau*?”

“*Pau* it is. When I sail out of here tonight at nine on the old *P.T.* I’m through forever. You don’t happen to know a good country newspaper that can be bought for﻿—well, say ten grand.”

“This is rather sudden, isn’t it?” John Quincy inquired.

“This is sudden country out here, sir. Well, we must roll along. Sorry you can’t join us. If the going’s not too rough and I can find a nice smooth table top, I intend to turn down an empty glass. For poor old Tim. So long, sir﻿—and happy days.”

He nodded to Willie Chan, and they went on down the street. John Quincy stood staring after them, a puzzled expression on his face.

===Chinese===
「什么？您*pau*（完事儿）啦？」

「*Pau*定了。今晚九点我搭上老『P.T.』出港，就永远不干这一行了。你凑巧不知道哪家像样的乡下报纸肯卖——嗯，比方说，一万美元？」

「这可有点突然，不是吗？」约翰·昆西问道。

「这儿本来就是突然之乡啊，先生。好，我们得滚蛋了。你不能同路，可惜。要是风浪不太颠，又能寻着一张光滑的桌面，我打算倒扣一只空杯子。为了可怜的老蒂姆。再会了，先生——祝你快活。」

他朝威利·陈点点头，两人沿街去了。约翰·昆西站在原地目送他们，脸上满是大惑不解的神情。

===Original===
Barbara seemed paler and thinner than ever, but she announced that her visit had been an enjoyable one, and on the ride to the beach appeared to be making a distinct effort to be gay and sprightly. When they reached the house, John Quincy repeated to his aunt Mrs. Maynard’s invitation.

“Better come along,” he urged.

“Perhaps I will,” she answered. “I’ll see.”

===Chinese===
芭芭拉显得比先前更苍白、更清瘦了，可她声称这趟做客甚是尽兴，回海滩的一路上也明显在强作轻快。到了宅子里，约翰·昆西把梅纳德夫人的邀请说给他姑婆听。

「一起去吧。」他怂恿道。

「也许去吧，」她答道。「到时候看。」

===Original===
The day passed quietly, and it was not until evening that the monotony was broken. Leaving the dining-room with his aunt and Barbara, John Quincy was handed a cablegram. He hastily opened it. It had been sent from Boston; evidently Agatha Parker, overwhelmed by the crude impossibility of the West, had fled home again, and John Quincy’s brief “San Francisco or nothing” had followed her there. Hence the delay.

The cablegram said simply: “Nothing. Agatha.” John Quincy crushed it in his hand; he tried to suffer a little, but it was no use. He was a mighty happy man. The end of a romance﻿—no. There had never been any nonsense of that kind between them﻿—just an affectionate regard too slight to stand the strain of parting. Agatha was younger than he, she would marry some nice proper boy who had no desire to roam. And John Quincy Winterslip would read of her wedding﻿—in the San Francisco papers.

===Chinese===
这一天过得平平静静，直到晚间才打破了沉闷。约翰·昆西同姑婆和芭芭拉一起走出饭厅时，有人递给他一封电报。他急忙拆开。电报从波士顿发来；显然，阿加莎·帕克叫西部的荒蛮无理吓破了胆，又逃回家去了，约翰·昆西那句简短的「要么旧金山，要么作罢」也追到了那里。所以才耽搁了这些时候。

电文很简单：「作罢。阿加莎。」约翰·昆西把它攥进手心；他努力让自己难受一阵，可是没用。他是个快活透顶的人。一段罗曼史的终结——不。他们之间从来不曾有过那类情痴意迷——不过是一份情谊，深浅还经不住离别的拉扯。阿加莎比他年轻，日后会嫁给哪个安分守己、无意四处漂泊的好青年。而约翰·昆西·温特斯利普呢，会读到她结婚的消息——在旧金山出的报纸上。

===Original===
He found Miss Minerva alone in the living-room. “It’s none of my business,” she said, “but I’m wondering what was in your cablegram.”

“Nothing,” he answered truthfully.

“All the same, you were very pleased to get it.”

He nodded. “Yes. I imagine nobody was ever so happy over nothing before.”

“Good heavens,” she cried. “Have you given up grammar, too?”

“I’m thinking of it. How about going down the beach with me?”

She shook her head. “Someone is coming to look at the house﻿—a leading lawyer, I believe he is. He’s thinking of buying, and I feel I should be here to show him about. Barbara appears so listless and disinterested. Tell Sally Maynard I may drop in later.”

===Chinese===
他发现米纳瓦小姐独自在客厅里。「这不关我的事，」她说，「可我禁不住纳闷，你那封电报里写了什么。」

「作罢的事。」他如实答道。

「尽管如此，你接到它可是高兴得很。」

他点点头。「是啊。我想，从没有人为『作罢』这么高兴过。」

「天哪，」她叫道。「你连语法也戒掉了不成？」

「我正有此意。陪我上海滩那头走走怎么样？」

她摇了摇头。「有人要来看房子——我想是位大牌律师。他有买的打算，我觉得自己该在家领他四处看看。芭芭拉显得那么没精打采、心不在焉。告诉萨莉·梅纳德，我晚些时候也许会过去。」

===Original===
At a quarter to eight, John Quincy took his bathing suit and wandered down Kalia Road. It was another of those nights; a bright moon was riding high; from a bungalow buried under purple alamander came the soft croon of Hawaiian music. Through the hedges of flaming hibiscus he caught again the exquisite odors of this exotic island.

Mrs. Maynard’s big house was a particularly unlovely type of New England architecture, but a hundred flowering vines did much to conceal that fact. John Quincy found his hostess enthroned in her great airy drawing-room, surrounded by a handsome laughing group of the best people. Pleasant people, too; as she introduced him he began to wonder if he hadn’t been missing a great deal of congenial companionship.

===Chinese===
七点三刻，约翰·昆西带上游泳衣，沿着卡利亚路信步走去。又是一个那样的夜晚；一轮明月高悬；一座平房埋在紫色的阿拉曼达花丛底下，飘出夏威夷音乐轻柔的低唱。透过一丛丛红艳似火的木槿，他又一次嗅到这座异域岛屿美妙入骨的芬芳。

梅纳德夫人的大宅，是新英格兰建筑里格外不好看的那一路，可上百株花藤把这个事实遮掩了大半。约翰·昆西看到女主人端坐在轩敞通透的大客厅里，身边围着一群衣冠楚楚、谈笑风生的上流人士。而且都是些可亲的人；经她一一引见，他开始怀疑，自己是不是一直错失了大量意气相投的交往。

===Original===
“I dragged him here against his will,” the old lady explained. “I felt I owed it to Hawaii. He’s been associating with the riffraff long enough.”

They insisted that he take an enormous chair, pressed cigarettes upon him, showered him with hospitable attentions. As he sat down and the chatter was resumed, he reflected that here was as civilized a company as Boston itself could offer. And why not? Most of these families came originally from New England, and had kept in their exile the old ideals of culture and caste.

===Chinese===
「我是硬把他拖来的，」老妇人解释道。「我觉得这是欠夏威夷的份内事。他同那帮乌七八糟的人厮混得也够久了。」

众人执意让他坐进一把硕大无朋的椅子，把香烟一递再递，殷勤的款待如雨点般落在他身上。他落了座，谈笑声重新扬起，他心想，这样文雅的一个社交圈子，波士顿本地也未必拿得出来。何况有什么不能的？这些人家的祖上多半打新英格兰迁来，在流放一般的海外岁月里，仍守着旧日的教养与门第理想。

===Original===
“It might interest Beacon Street to know,” Mrs. Maynard said, “that long before the days of ’forty-nine the people of California were sending their children over here to be educated in the missionary schools. And importing their wheat from here, too.”

“Go on, tell him the other one, Aunt Sally,” laughed a pretty girl in blue. “That about the first printing press in San Francisco being brought over from Honolulu.”

Madame Maynard shrugged her shoulders. “Oh, what’s the use? We’re so far away, New England will never get us straight.”

===Chinese===
「灯塔街上的人若有兴趣，不妨知道，」梅纳德夫人说，「早在四九年淘金潮那些年头以前，加利福尼亚人就把孩子送到这儿来，进教会学校念书。麦子也是打这里进口的。」

「接着讲，把另一桩也讲给他听，萨莉姑妈，」一个穿蓝衣裳的漂亮姑娘笑道。「就讲旧金山的第一台印刷机是从檀香山运过去的那个。」

梅纳德夫人耸了耸肩。「唉，说了有什么用？我们离得太远，新英格兰永远弄不明白我们。」

===Original===
John Quincy looked up to see Carlota Egan in the doorway. A moment later Lieutenant Booth, of Richmond, appeared at her side. It occurred to the young man from Boston that the fleet was rather overdoing its stop at Honolulu.

Mrs. Maynard rose to greet the girl. “Come in, my dear. You know most of these people.” She turned to the others. “This is Miss Egan, a neighbor of mine on the beach.”

It was amusing to note that most of these people knew Carlota too. John Quincy smiled﻿—the British Admiralty and the soap business. It must have been rather an ordeal for the girl, but she saw it through with a sweet graciousness that led John Quincy to reflect that she would be at home in England﻿—if she went there.

Carlota sat down on a sofa, and while Lieutenant Booth was busily arranging a cushion at her back, John Quincy dropped down beside her. The sofa was, fortunately, too small for three.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西抬起头，看见卡洛塔·伊根站在门口。片刻之后，里士满来的布思中尉出现在她身旁。这位波士顿青年不禁暗想：舰队在檀香山这一站，也未免停得过分久了。

梅纳德夫人起身迎接姑娘。「进来吧，亲爱的。这些人你多半都认得。」她转向众人。「这位是伊根小姐，我在海滩上的邻居。」

有意思的是，这些人多半也认得卡洛塔。约翰·昆西笑了——英国海军部，还有肥皂生意。对这姑娘来说，这场面想必相当难熬，可她带着一种温婉大方从头应到尾，令约翰·昆西暗想：她到了英国也会如鱼得水——如果她去的话。

卡洛塔在一张沙发上落座，布思中尉忙着在她背后安放靠垫的当口，约翰·昆西一屁股坐到了她身边。所幸那沙发坐不下三个人。

===Original===
“I rather expected to see you,” he said in a low voice. “I was brought here to meet the best people of Honolulu, and the way I see it, you’re the best of all.”

She smiled at him, and again the chatter of small talk filled the room. Presently the voice of a tall young man with glasses rose above the general hubbub.

===Chinese===
「我早料到会见到你，」他低声说。「人家领我来结识檀香山的上等人，依我看，你是其中最好的一个。」

她朝他嫣然一笑，满屋又盈满了寒暄的絮语。不多时，一个戴眼镜的高个子青年的嗓门盖过了众声。

===Original===
“They got a cable from Joe Clark out at the Country Club this afternoon,” he announced.

The din ceased, and everyone listened with interest. “Clark’s our professional,” explained the young man to John Quincy. “He went over a month ago to play in the British Open.”

“Did he win?” asked the girl in blue.

“He was put out by Hagen in the semifinals,” the young man said. “But he had the distinction of driving the longest ball ever seen on the St. Andrews course.”

“Why shouldn’t he?” asked an older man. “He’s got the strongest wrists I ever saw on anybody?”

John Quincy sat up, suddenly interested. “How do you account for that?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「今天下午，乡村俱乐部那边收到了乔·克拉克打来的电报。」他宣布道。

喧声顿息，人人都听得饶有兴味。「克拉克是我们的职业球手，」那青年向约翰·昆西解释。「一个月前他渡海去参加英国公开赛了。」

「他赢了吗？」蓝衣姑娘问。

「半决赛叫哈根淘汰了，」青年说。「不过他打出了圣安德鲁斯球场上前所未见的最远一杆，这份光彩独一份儿。」

「这有什么可奇怪的？」一位年纪长些的人问。「他的手腕，是我在任何人身上见过的最强健有劲的。」

约翰·昆西坐直了身子，猛然来了兴致。「这你们怎么解释？」他问。

===Original===
The older man smiled. “We’ve all got pretty big wrists out here,” he answered. “Surfboarding﻿—that’s what does it. Joe Clark was a champion at one time﻿—body-surfing and board-surfing too. He used to disappear for hours in the rollers out by the reef. The result was a marvelous wrist development. I’ve seen him drive a golf ball three hundred and eighty yards. Yes, sir, I’ll bet he made those Englishmen sit up and take notice.”

While John Quincy was thinking this over, someone suggested that it was time for the swim, and confusion reigned. A Chinese servant led the way to the dressing-rooms, which opened off the lanai, and the young people trouped joyously after him.

===Chinese===
年长些的人微微一笑。「我们这儿的人手腕都相当粗壮，」他答道。「冲浪——就是这么练出来的。乔·克拉克当年是个冠军——徒身冲浪、俯板冲浪样样在行。他常常在礁石外的浪头里一泡就是几个钟头，不见人影。结果呢，手腕发育得出奇地好。我亲眼见他一杆把高尔夫球打出三百八十码。是啊，先生，我敢打赌，准叫那些英国人个个刮目相看。」

约翰·昆西正琢磨着这番话，有人提议该去游泳了，于是一片欢腾忙乱。一名中国仆人前头引路，去露台侧面开门进出的更衣室，年轻人们欢欢喜喜地成群跟了上去。

===Original===
“I’ll be waiting for you on the beach,” John Quincy said to Carlota Egan.

“I came with Johnnie, you know,” she reminded him.

“I know all about it,” he answered. “But it was the weekend you promised to the navy. People who try to stretch their weekend through the following Wednesday night deserve all they get.”

She laughed. “I’ll look for you,” she agreed.

===Chinese===
「我在海滩上等你。」约翰·昆西对卡洛塔·伊根说。

「你知道，我是同约翰尼一起来的。」她提醒他。

「我全知道，」他答道。「可那是你答应给海军的周末。想把一个周末一直拉长到下一个星期三夜里的人，落得什么下场都是活该。」

她笑了。「我等你。」她应承道。

===Original===
He donned his bathing suit hastily in a room filled with flying clothes and great waving brown arms. Lieutenant Booth, he noted with satisfaction, was proceeding at a leisurely pace. Hurrying through a door that opened directly on the beach, he waited under a nearby hau tree. Presently Carlota came, slender and fragile-looking in the moonlight.

“Ah, here you are,” John Quincy cried. “The farthest float.”

“The farthest float it is,” she answered.

They dashed into the warm silvery water and swam gaily off. Five minutes later they sat on the float together. The light on Diamond Head was winking; the lanterns of sampans twinkled out beyond the reef; the shore line of Honolulu was outlined by a procession of blinking stars controlled by dynamos. In the bright heavens hung a lunar rainbow, one colorful end in the Pacific and the other tumbling into the foliage ashore.

===Chinese===
他在一间满是飞舞的衣裳和挥动的棕色臂膀的屋子里，匆匆换上游泳衣。他满意地注意到，布思中尉正不紧不慢地磨蹭着。他赶紧钻出那扇直通海滩的门，在近旁一棵哈乌树（hau tree）下等候。不多时卡洛塔来了，月光里显得纤细柔弱。

「啊，你来了。」约翰·昆西喊道。「最远的那只浮台。」

「就是最远的那只。」她答道。

两人扎进温暖银亮的水里，快快活活地游了出去。五分钟后，他们并排坐上了浮台。钻石头山上的灯明明灭灭；礁石外舢板的渔灯点点闪烁；檀香山的海岸线，让一串由发电机操控的眨眼星辰勾出了轮廓。明亮的天宇间悬着一道月虹，彩色的一端落在太平洋里，另一端翻滚着泻进岸上的林木。

===Original===
A gorgeous setting in which to be young and in love, and free to speak at last. John Quincy moved closer to the girl’s side.

“Great night, isn’t it?” he said.

“Wonderful,” she answered softly.

“Cary, I want to tell you something, and that’s why I brought you out here away from the others﻿—”

“Somehow,” she interrupted, “it doesn’t seem quite fair to Johnnie.”

===Chinese===
一幅华丽的天造布景，正好安放年轻与爱情，而且终于可以畅所欲言了。约翰·昆西朝姑娘身边挪近了些。

「夜色真好，是不是？」他说。

「美极了。」她轻声答道。

「卡莉，我有话要对你讲，所以才把你带到这儿来，离开那些人——」

「不知怎么，」她打断他，「这对约翰尼好像不太公平。」

===Original===
“Never mind him. Has it ever occurred to you that my name’s Johnnie, too.”

She laughed. “Oh, but it couldn’t be.”

“What do you mean?”

“I mean I simply couldn’t call you that. You’re too dignified and﻿—and remote. John Quincy﻿—I believe I could call you John Quincy﻿—”

“Well, make up your mind. You’ll have to call me something, because I’m going to be hanging round pretty constantly in the future. Yes, my dear, I’ll probably turn out to be about the least remote person in the world. That is, if I can make you see the future the way I see it. Cary dearest﻿—”

===Chinese===
「别管他。你可曾想到，我的名字也是约翰尼。」

她笑了。「哦，那可不行。」

「你这话什么意思？」

「我是说，我实在没法那样叫你。你太庄重，太——太疏远。约翰·昆西——我想我叫得出『约翰·昆西』——」

「那好，拿定主意吧。你总得有个叫法，因为往后我会时时刻刻在你身边打转。是啊，亲爱的，我说不定到头来会是天底下最不疏远的人。当然啦，前提是我能让你像我一样看清将来。最亲爱的卡莉——」

===Original===
A gurgle sounded behind them, and they turned around. Lieutenant Booth was climbing on to the raft. “Swam the last fifty yards under water to surprise you,” he sputtered.

“Well, you succeeded,” said John Quincy without enthusiasm.

The lieutenant sat down with the manner of one booked to remain indefinitely. “I’ll tell the world it’s some night,” he offered.

“Speaking of the world, when do you fellows leave Honolulu?” asked John Quincy.

“I don’t know. Tomorrow, I guess. Me, I don’t care if we never go. Hawaii’s not so easy to leave. Is it, Cary?”

===Chinese===
身后响起一阵咕嘟咕嘟的水声，两人回过头去。布思中尉正往浮台上爬。「最后五十码是潜着水游过来的，好吓你们一跳。」他噗噗地吐着水说。

「嗯，你成功了。」约翰·昆西毫不带劲地说。

中尉坐了下来，那副架势像是预订了无限期的席位。「我敢向全世界宣布，这夜色真够意思。」他搭腔道。

「说到全世界，你们这帮人什么时候离开檀香山？」约翰·昆西问。

「不知道。大概明天吧。至于我，一辈子不走我也不在乎。夏威夷可不是那么容易离开的。对不对，卡莉？」

===Original===
She shook her head. “Hardest place I know of, Johnnie. I shall have to be sailing presently, and I know what a wrench it will be. Perhaps I’ll follow the example of Waioli the swimmer, and leave the boat when it passes Waikiki.”

They lolled for a moment in silence. Suddenly John Quincy sat up. “What was that you said?” he asked.

“About Waioli? Didn’t I ever tell you? He was one of our best swimmers, and for years they tried to get him to go to the mainland to take part in athletic meets, like Duke Kahanamoku. But he was a sentimentalist﻿—he couldn’t bring himself to leave Hawaii. Finally they persuaded him, and one sunny morning he sailed on the *Matsonia*, with a very sad face. When the ship was opposite Waikiki he slipped overboard and swam ashore. And that was that. He never got on a ship again. You see﻿—”

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。「这是我知道的最难舍的地方，约翰尼。我不久就得随船启航了，我知道那会是怎样一番撕扯。也许我会学游泳家瓦约利（Waioli）的样，船过怀基基时就跳下去。」

两人懒懒地静了片刻。约翰·昆西猛地坐起身来。「你刚才说什么？」他问。

「说瓦约利？我没同你讲过吗？他是我们最出色的游泳好手之一，多年来大家一直劝他去本土参加体育比赛，像杜克·卡哈纳莫库那样。可他是个多愁善感的人——怎么也下不了决心离开夏威夷。最后他们终于劝动了他，一个晴朗的早晨，他登上了马特森尼亚号，一脸愁容。船行到怀基基外海，他悄悄翻下船去，游上了岸。事情就这么完了。他从此再没上过船。你瞧——」

===Original===
John Quincy was on his feet. “What time was it when we left the beach?” he asked in a low tense voice.

“About eight-thirty,” said Booth.

John Quincy talked very fast. “That means I’ve got just thirty minutes to get ashore, dress, and reach the dock before the *President Tyler* sails. I’m sorry to go, but it’s vital﻿—vital. Cary, I’d started to tell you something. I don’t know when I’ll get back, but I must see you when I do, either at Mrs. Maynard’s or the hotel. Will you wait up for me?”

She was startled by the seriousness of his tone. “Yes, I’ll be waiting,” she told him.

“That’s great.” He hesitated a moment; it is a risky business to leave the girl you love on a float in the moonlight with a handsome naval officer. But it had to be done. “I’m off,” he said, and dove.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西一跃而起。「我们离开海滩时是几点？」他压着嗓子、绷紧了声音问。

「八点半左右。」布思说。

约翰·昆西飞快地说：「那就是说，我只有三十分钟，赶在泰勒总统号开船之前上岸、穿衣裳、到码头。对不起，我必须走——这事关紧要——紧要。卡莉，我本来有话要告诉你。不知道什么时候能回来，可回来后必须见你，要么在梅纳德夫人家，要么在旅馆。你肯等我别睡吗？」

他语气的郑重吓了她一跳。「好，我等你。」她对他说。

「那太好了。」他迟疑了片刻；把心爱的姑娘留在月光下的浮台上，交给一位英俊的海军军官，实在是桩冒险的营生。可是非做不可。「我走了。」他说完，一个猛子扎了下去。

===Original===
When he came up he heard the lieutenant’s voice. “Say, old man, that dive was all wrong. You let me show you﻿—”

“Go to the devil,” muttered John Quincy wetly, and swam with long powerful strokes toward the shore. Mad with haste, he plunged into the dressing-room, donned his clothes, then dashed out again. No time for apologies to his hostess. He ran along the beach to the Winterslip house. Haku was dozing in the hall.

“Wikiwiki,” shouted John Quincy. “Tell the chauffeur to get the roadster into the drive and start the engine. Wake up! Travel! Where’s Miss Barbara?”

“Last seen on beach﻿—” began the startled Haku.

On the bench under the hau tree he found Barbara sitting alone. He stood panting before her.

===Chinese===
他冒出水面时，听见中尉的声音。「喂，老兄，你这一跳全错了。让我来教你——」

「见鬼去吧。」约翰·昆西浑身滴着水咕哝了一句，长而有力地划着水向岸边游去。他急得发了疯似的扎进更衣室，套上衣裳，又冲了出去。没工夫向女主人告罪了。他沿着海滩一路跑回温特斯利普家。哈库正在门厅里打盹。

「维基维基（wikiwiki）——快！」约翰·昆西大喊。「叫司机把双座跑车开到车道上，发动引擎。醒醒！动起来！芭芭拉小姐在哪儿？」

「最后一眼瞧见是在海滩上——」吓了一跳的哈库开口道。

在哈乌树下那条长椅上，他找到了独自坐着的芭芭拉。他气喘吁吁地立在她跟前。

===Original===
“My dear,” he said. “I know at last who killed your father﻿—”

She was on her feet. “You do?”

“Yes﻿—shall I tell you?”

“No,” she said. “No﻿—I can’t bear to hear. It’s too horrible.”

“Then you’ve suspected?”

“Yes﻿—just suspicion﻿—a feeling﻿—intuition. I couldn’t believe it﻿—I didn’t want to believe it. I went away to get it out of my mind. It’s all too terrible﻿—”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，」他说。「我终于知道是谁杀了你父亲——」

她霍地站起来。「你知道了？」

「知道了——要我说给你听吗？」

「不，」她说。「不——我不忍听。太可怕了。」

「那你早有怀疑了？」

「是——只是怀疑——一种感觉——直觉。我不敢相信——我不愿相信。我出门远躲，想把它从脑子里赶出去。这一切太可怕了——」

===Original===
He put his hand on her shoulder. “Poor Barbara. Don’t you worry. You won’t appear in this in any way. I’ll keep you out of it.”

“What﻿—what has happened?”

“Can’t stop now. Tell you later.” He ran toward the drive. Miss Minerva appeared from the house. “Haven’t time to talk,” he cried, leaping into the roadster.

===Chinese===
他把手搭在她肩上。「可怜的芭芭拉。你别担心。这件事无论如何不会把你牵扯进去。我会让你干干净净地站在局外。」

「出——出什么事了？」

「现在停不得。回头告诉你。」他朝车道跑去。米纳瓦小姐从宅子里出来。「没工夫说话了。」他喊道，一跃跳进双座跑车。

===Original===
“But John Quincy﻿—a curious thing has happened﻿—that lawyer who was here to look at the house﻿—he said that Dan, just a week before he died, spoke to him about a new will﻿—”

“That’s good! That’s evidence!” John Quincy cried.

“But why a new will? Surely Barbara was all he had﻿—”

“Listen to me,” cut in John Quincy. “You’ve delayed me already. Get the big car and go to the station﻿—tell that to Hallet. Tell him too that I’m on the *President Tyler* and to send Chan there at once.”

===Chinese===
「可是约翰·昆西——出了件怪事——来看房子的那位律师——他说，丹在死前恰恰一个星期，同他谈起过一份新遗嘱——」

「太好了！那是证据！」约翰·昆西喊道。

「可为什么还要立新遗嘱？芭芭拉不是他仅有的一切吗——」

「听我说，」约翰·昆西打断她。「您已经耽搁我了。开那辆大车，到警察局去——把这事告诉哈利特。再告诉他，我在泰勒总统号上，叫他把陈查理立刻派到那儿去。」

===Original===
He stepped on the gas. By the clock in the automobile he had just seventeen minutes to reach the dock before the *President Tyler* would sail. He shot like a madman through the brilliant Hawaiian night. Kalakaua Avenue, smooth and deserted, proved a glorious speedway. It took him just eight minutes to travel the three miles to the dock. A bit of traffic and an angry policeman in the center of the city caused the delay.

A scattering of people in the dim pier-shed waited for the imminent sailing of the liner. John Quincy dashed through them and up the gangplank. The second officer, Hepworth, stood at the top.

===Chinese===
他一脚踩下油门。照汽车里的钟，离泰勒总统号开船，他只有十七分钟赶到码头。他像个疯子一般，射过灯火通明的夏威夷之夜。卡拉卡瓦大道平坦空旷，成了一条辉煌的高速赛道。到码头三英里的路，他只用了八分钟。耽搁出在市中心：一点堵塞的车辆，外加一位发火的警察。

昏暗的码头棚屋里，稀稀落落的人们正等着班船即刻启航。约翰·昆西从人丛中冲过，奔上跳板。二副赫普沃思站在跳板上端。

===Original===
“Hello, Mr. Winterslip,” he said. “You sailing?”

“No. But let me aboard!”

“I’m sorry. We’re about to draw in the plank.”

“No, no﻿—you mustn’t. This is life and death. Hold off just a few minutes. There’s a steward named Bowker﻿—I must find him at once. Life and death, I tell you.”

Hepworth stood aside. “Oh, well, in that case. But please hurry, sir﻿—”

“I will.” John Quincy passed him on the run. He was on his way to the cabins presided over by Bowker when a tall figure caught his eye. A man in a long green ulster and a battered green hat﻿—a hat John Quincy had last seen on the links of the Oahu Country Club.

===Chinese===
「您好，温特斯利普先生。」他说。「您随船走吗？」

「不走。可让我上船！」

「抱歉。我们就要收跳板了。」

「不，不——千万别收。这是人命关天的事。宽限几分钟。船上有个乘务员叫鲍克——我必须马上找到他。我告诉你，人命关天。」

赫普沃思侧身让开。「哦，那样的话——行。可请您务必抓紧，先生——」

「一定。」约翰·昆西跑着从他身边掠过。他正奔向鲍克掌管的那片舱房，一个高大的身影撞进他的眼角。那人穿一件绿色长风衣，戴一顶压瘪的绿帽子——那顶帽子，约翰·昆西上一回见到，是在瓦胡乡村俱乐部的高尔夫球场上。

===Original===
The tall figure moved on up a stairway to the topmost deck. John Quincy followed. He saw the ulster disappear into one of the de luxe cabins. Still he followed, and pushed open the cabin door. The man in the ulster was back to, but he swung round suddenly.

“Ah, Mr. Jennison,” John Quincy cried. “Were you thinking of sailing on this boat?”

For an instant Jennison stared at him. “I was,” he said quietly.

“Forget it,” John Quincy answered. “You’re going ashore with me.”

“Really? What is your authority?”

“No authority whatever,” said the boy grimly. “I’m taking you, that’s all.”

===Chinese===
那高大的身影顺着舷梯一路登上最高一层甲板。约翰·昆西跟了上去。他看见那件绿大衣闪进了一间特等舱房。他仍旧跟着，推开了舱门。穿大衣的人正背对着他，猛然间旋过身来。

「啊，詹尼森先生，」约翰·昆西喊道。「您是在打算搭这条船走吗？」

詹尼森盯了他一瞬。「我原是有这打算。」他平静地说。

「打消它吧，」约翰·昆西答道。「您得跟我上岸去。」

「当真？您凭什么权限？」

「什么权限也没有，」小伙子冷冷地说。「我就是要带走您，仅此而已。」

===Original===
Jennison smiled, but there was a gleam of hate behind it. And in John Quincy’s heart, usually so gentle and civilized, there was hate too as he faced this man. He thought of Dan Winterslip, dead on his cot. He thought of Jennison walking down the gangplank with them that morning they landed, Jennison putting his arm about poor Barbara when she faltered under the blow. He thought of the shots fired at him from the bush, of the red-haired man battering him in that red room. Well, he must fight again. No way out of it. The siren of the *President Tyler* sounded a sharp warning.

“You get out of here,” said Jennison through his teeth. “I’ll go with you to the gangplank﻿—”

He stopped, as the disadvantages of that plan came home to him. His right hand went swiftly to his pocket. Inspired, John Quincy seized a filled water bottle and hurled it at the man’s head. Jennison dodged; the bottle crashed through one of the windows. The clatter of glass rang through the night, but no one appeared. John Quincy saw Jennison leap toward him, something gleaming in his hand. Stepping aside, he threw himself on the man’s back and forced him to his knees. He seized the wrist of Jennison’s right hand, which held the automatic, in a firm grip. They kept that posture for a moment, and then Jennison began slowly to rise to his feet. The hand that held the pistol began to tear away. John Quincy shut his teeth and sought to maintain his grip. But he was up against a more powerful antagonist than the red-haired sailor, he was outclassed, and the realization of it crept over him with a sickening force.

Jennison was on his feet now, the right hand nearly free. Another moment﻿—what then, John Quincy wondered? This man had no intention of letting him go ashore; he had changed that plan the moment he put it into words. A muffled shot, and later in the night when the ship was well out on the Pacific﻿—John Quincy thought of Boston, his mother. He thought of Carlota waiting his return. He summoned his strength for one last desperate effort to renew his grip.

===Chinese===
詹尼森笑了，可笑容背后闪着一丝仇恨。而约翰·昆西的心——平常那样温和斯文的一颗心——面对此人时，也盛满了恨。他想起死在小床上的丹·温特斯利普。想起他们登陆那天早晨，詹尼森同他们一道走下跳板；想起芭芭拉被这打击打得脚下发飘时，詹尼森伸手搂住她的样子。他想起树丛里朝他射来的枪弹，想起那间红房间里红头发汉子对他的一顿毒打。好吧，还得再打一场。躲不掉的。泰勒总统号的汽笛发出一声尖利的警告。

「你给我出去，」詹尼森咬着牙说。「我随你到跳板那儿去——」

他住了口，那方案的种种不利已在他心里了然。他的右手倏地伸向口袋。约翰·昆西灵机一动，抓起一只灌满水的水瓶，朝那人头上掷去。詹尼森一闪身；水瓶砸穿了一扇舷窗。玻璃的哗啦声划破夜空，却没有一个人露面。约翰·昆西看见詹尼森朝他扑来，手里有什么东西寒光一闪。他侧身让过，整个人扑上那人的后背，把他压得跪了下去。他死死攥住詹尼森握着自动手枪的右腕。两人这样僵持了片刻，随后詹尼森开始缓缓站起身来。握枪的那只手开始挣脱。约翰·昆西咬紧牙关，拼命想守住这一攥。可他面对的是比那红头发水手强悍得多的对手，他相形见绌，这认知带着叫人发晕的力量，一寸寸爬上他的心头。

詹尼森这会儿已经站直了，右手眼看就要挣脱。再过一刻——下一步呢？约翰·昆西暗想。这个人根本没打算放他上岸；话一出口，那方案就已在他心里作废。一声闷响的枪声，然后夜里晚些时候，等船远远开到太平洋上——约翰·昆西想到了波士顿，想到他的母亲。想到正等他归来的卡洛塔。他鼓起全身的力气，要作最后一搏，把那一攥重新扣死。

===Original===
A serene, ivory-colored face appeared suddenly at the broken window. An arm with a weapon was extended through the jagged opening.

“Relinquish the firearms, Mr. Jennison,” commanded Charlie Chan, “or I am forced to make fatal insertion in vital organ belonging to you.”

Jennison’s pistol dropped to the floor, and John Quincy staggered back against the berth. At that instant the door opened and Hallet, followed by the detective, Spencer, came in.

===Chinese===
一张安详的象牙色面孔，蓦地在破窗前现身。一条持枪的手臂从锯齿状的破洞里探了进来。

「詹尼森先生，请撤手放开火器，」陈查理喝令道，「不然在下便被迫对属于您体内的要害器官施行致命之插入了。」

詹尼森的手枪当啷落地，约翰·昆西踉跄着退到铺位边。就在这一瞬，门开了，哈利特警长走了进来，身后跟着侦探斯宾塞。

===Original===
“Hello, Winterslip, what are you doing here?” the captain said. He thrust a paper into one of the pockets of the green ulster. “Come along, Jennison,” he said. “We want you.”

Limply John Quincy followed them from the stateroom. Outside they were joined by Chan. At the top of the gangplank Hallet paused. “We’ll wait a minute for Hepworth,” he said.

John Quincy put his hand on Chan’s shoulder. “Charlie, how can I ever thank you? You saved my life.”

Chan bowed. “My own pleasure is not to be worded. I have saved a life here and there, but never before one that had beginning in cultured city of Boston. Always a happy item on the golden scroll of memory.”

Hepworth came up. “It’s all right,” he said. “The captain has agreed to delay our sailing one hour. I’ll go to the station with you.”

===Chinese===
「喂，温特斯利普，你在这儿干什么？」警长说。他把一纸文书塞进绿大衣的一只口袋。「走吧，詹尼森。」他说。「我们要你。」

约翰·昆西软绵绵地跟着他们走出特等舱。门外，陈查理同他们会合。在跳板上端，哈利特停了停。「我们等赫普沃思一下。」他说。

约翰·昆西把手搭在陈查理肩上。「查理，我可怎么谢你才好？你救了我的命。」

陈查理躬身一礼。「在下自身的欢愉，非言语所能状述。在下在此处彼处也曾救过几条性命，但从未救过一条发端于教养深厚之波士顿城的。永远是金色记忆卷轴上欢欣的一条。」

赫普沃思赶了上来。「没问题的。」他说。「船长已同意推迟一小时开船。我随你们去警察局。」

===Original===
On the way down the gangplank, Chan turned to John Quincy. “Speaking heartily for myself, I congratulate your bravery. It is clear you leaped upon this Jennison with vigorous and triumphant mood of heart. But he would have pushed you down. He would have conquered. And why? The answer is, such powerful wrists.”

“A great surf-boarder, eh?” John Quincy said.

Chan looked at him keenly. “You are no person’s fool. Ten years ago this Harry Jennison are champion swimmer in all Hawaii. I extract that news from ancient sporting pages of Honolulu journal. But he have not been in the water much here lately. Pursuing the truth further, not since the night he killed Dan Winterslip.”

===Chinese===
下跳板的路上，陈查理转向约翰·昆西。「在下衷心为自身发言，庆贺您的英勇。显而易见，您怀着旺盛而得胜的心境扑上了这位詹尼森。但他本会将您压倒。他本将得胜。为何？答案是：如此强劲的手腕。」

「了不得的冲浪好手，嗯？」约翰·昆西说。

陈查理锐利地看了看他。「您绝非任何人的愚人。十年前，这位哈里·詹尼森曾是全夏威夷之游泳冠军。在下自檀香山报刊陈旧的体育版面中摘取出这一消息。但近来他在此间甚少入水。再进一步追究真相——自他杀死丹·温特斯利普的那一夜起，便不曾下过水。」

## XXII The Light Streams Through / 二十二 光明透进来

===Original===
They moved on through the pier-shed to the street, where Hepworth, Jennison and the three policemen got into Hallet’s car. The captain turned to John Quincy.

“You coming, Mr. Winterslip?” he inquired.

“I’ve got my own car,” the boy explained. “I’ll follow you in that.”

The roadster was not performing at its best, and he reached the station house a good five minutes after the policemen. He noted Dan Winterslip’s big limousine parked in the street outside.

===Chinese===
他们穿过码头的棚屋走到街上，赫普沃思（Hepworth）、詹尼森和三名警察上了哈利特的车。警长转向约翰·昆西。

「您来吗，温特斯利普先生？」他问道。

「我自己有车，」小伙子解释道，「我开它跟在你们后面。」

那辆双座小轿车跑得不大给力，他到警察局时已比警察们足足晚了五分钟。他注意到丹·温特斯利普那辆大轿车停在门外街上。

===Original===
In Hallet’s room he found the captain and Chan closeted with a third man. It took a second glance at the latter to identify him as Mr. Saladine, for the little man of the lost teeth now appeared a great deal younger than John Quincy had thought him.

“Ah, Mr. Winterslip,” remarked Hallet. He turned to Saladine. “Say, Larry, you’ve got me into a heap of trouble with this boy. He accused me of trying to shield you. I wish you’d loosen up for him.”

Saladine smiled. “Why, I don’t mind. My job out here is about finished. Of course, Mr. Winterslip will keep what I tell him under his hat?”

“Naturally,” replied John Quincy. He noticed that the man spoke with no trace of a lisp. “I perceive you’ve found your teeth,” he added.

===Chinese===
在哈利特的房间里，他见警长和陈查理正与第三个人密谈。对后者，他得看上第二眼才认出是萨拉迪尼先生，因为这位掉了牙的小个子如今看上去比约翰·昆西印象中年轻了许多。

「啊，温特斯利普先生。」哈利特说。他转向萨拉迪尼。「喂，拉里（Larry），你害我在这小伙子跟前惹了一大堆麻烦。他指控我企图包庇你。劳驾，你对他松松口，和盘托出吧。」

萨拉迪尼微微一笑。「哎呀，我不介意。我在这里的差事差不多收尾了。当然，我告诉温特斯利普先生的话，他会守口如瓶的吧？」

「那是自然。」约翰·昆西答道。他注意到此人说话已不带半点咬舌音。「我看，您的牙找回来了。」他补了一句。

===Original===
“Oh, yes—I found them in my trunk, where I put them the day I arrived at Waikiki,” answered Saladine. “When my teeth were knocked out twenty years ago in a football game, I was brokenhearted, but the loss has been a great help to me in my work. A man hunting his bridge work in the water is a figure of ridicule and mirth. No one ever thinks of connecting him with serious affairs. He can prowl about a beach to his heart’s content. Mr. Winterslip, I am a special agent of the Treasury Department sent out here to break up the opium ring. My name, of course, is not Saladine.”

“Oh,” said John Quincy, “I understand at last.”

“I’m glad you do,” remarked Hallet. “I don’t know whether you’re familiar with the way our opium smugglers work. The dope is brought in from the Orient on tramp steamers—the *Mary S. Allison*, for example. When they arrive off Waikiki they knock together a few small rafts and load ’em with tins of the stuff. A fleet of little boats, supposedly out there for the fishing, pick up these rafts and bring the dope ashore. It’s taken downtown and hidden on ships bound for ’Frisco—usually those that ply only between here and the mainland, because they’re not so closely watched at the other end. But it just happened that the quartermaster of the *President Tyler* is one of their go-betweens. We searched his cabin this evening and found it packed with the stuff.”

“The quartermaster of the *President Tyler*,” repeated John Quincy. “That’s Dick Kaohla’s friend.”

===Chinese===
「哦，找回来了——在我的衣箱里找到的，抵达怀基基那天我把它们搁在了那儿。」萨拉迪尼答道。「二十年前我踢橄榄球让人打掉了牙，当时痛不欲生，可这桩损失对我的工作帮了大忙。一个在水里摸索自己假牙的男人，是幅招人嘲笑逗乐的活漫画。谁也不会想到把他跟正经大事扯上关系。他尽可以在海滩上转悠个够。温特斯利普先生，我是财政部派来此地捣毁鸦片走私集团的缉私密探。我的名字，自然不叫萨拉迪尼。」

「哦，」约翰·昆西说，「我终于明白了。」

「你能明白我很高兴。」哈利特说。「不知你熟不熟悉我们这儿鸦片贩子的门道。毒品由不定期货轮从东方运来——比如『玛丽·S·艾利森号』（*Mary S. Allison*）。船抵怀基基外海后，他们草草扎起几只小木筏，把一罐罐货装上去。一支小船队——名义上是出海捕鱼的——捞起这些木筏，把毒品送上岸。货再运进市中心，藏进开往旧金山的船里——通常是只往返于本地和大陆之间的那些船，因为它们在那一头受的盘查不那么严。可偏偏泰勒总统号的舵手就是他们的中间人之一。今晚我们搜了他的舱房，发现里面塞满了那玩意儿。」

「泰勒总统号的舵手，」约翰·昆西重复道。「那是迪克·考赫拉的朋友。」

===Original===
“Yeah—I’m coming to Dick. He’s been in charge of the pickup fleet here. He was out on that business the night of the murder. Saladine saw him and told me all about it in that note, which was my reason for letting the boy go.”

“I owe you an apology,” John Quincy said.

“Oh, that’s all right.” Hallet was in great good humor. “Larry here has got some of the higher-ups, too. For instance, he’s discovered that Jennison is the lawyer for the ring, defending any of them who are caught and brought before the commissioner. The fact has no bearing on Dan Winterslip’s murder—unless Winterslip knew about it, and that was one of the reasons he didn’t want Jennison to marry his girl.”

===Chinese===
「对——我这就说到迪克。他一直负责本地收货的船队。凶案当晚，他正是外出干这桩买卖去了。萨拉迪尼瞧见了他，把原委都写在了那张条子上，我这才放了那小子。」

「我欠您一个道歉。」约翰·昆西说。

「哦，那不算什么。」哈利特心情极好。「这位拉里还挖出了几个上层人物。比方说，他查出詹尼森是这走私集团的律师，专门给落网后被解到预审法官面前的人辩护。这桩事实与丹·温特斯利普凶案并无干系——除非温特斯利普本人知情，而那也许正是他不肯让詹尼森娶他女儿的缘由之一。」

===Original===
Saladine stood up. “I’ll turn the quartermaster over to you,” he said. “In view of this other charge, you can of course have Jennison too. That’s all for me. I’ll go along.”

“See you tomorrow, Larry,” Hallet answered. Saladine went out, and the captain turned to John Quincy. “Well, my boy, this is our big night. I don’t know what you were doing in Jennison’s cabin, but if you’d picked him for the murderer, I’ll say you’re good.”

“That’s just what I’d done,” John Quincy told him. “By the way, have you seen my aunt? She’s got hold of a rather interesting bit of information—”

“I’ve seen her,” Hallet said. “She’s with the prosecutor now, telling it to him. By the way, Greene’s waiting for us. Come along.”

===Chinese===
萨拉迪尼站起身。「我把那舵手移交给你们，」他说。「既然添了这另一项罪名，詹尼森自然也一并归你。我的事到此为止。我先走一步。」

「明天见，拉里。」哈利特应道。萨拉迪尼出去了，警长转向约翰·昆西。「好啊小伙子，今晚是我们的重大之夜。我不知道你在詹尼森的舱里干了什么，不过你要是早认准了他就是凶手，我得说你真有两下子。」

「我正是那么认准的。」约翰·昆西告诉他。「对了，您见过我姑婆了吗？她摸到了一条颇为有意思的消息——」

「见过了。」哈利特说。「她这会儿正陪着检察官，把事情讲给他听。对了，格林正等着我们呢。走吧。」

===Original===
They went into the prosecutor’s office. Greene was alert and eager, a stenographer was at his elbow, and Miss Minerva sat near his desk.

“Hello, Mr. Winterslip,” he said. “What do you think of our police force now? Pretty good, eh, pretty good. Sit down, won’t you?” He glanced through some papers on his desk while John Quincy, Hallet and Chan found chairs. “I don’t mind telling you, this thing has knocked me all in a heap. Harry Jennison and I are old friends; I had lunch with him at the club only yesterday. I’m going to proceed a little differently than I would with an ordinary criminal.”

John Quincy half rose from his chair. “Don’t get excited,” Greene smiled. “Jennison will get all that’s coming to him, friendship or no friendship. What I mean is that if I can save the territory the expense of a long trial by dragging a confession out of him at once, I intend to do it. He’s coming in here in a moment, and I propose to reveal my whole hand to him, from start to finish. That may seem foolish, but it isn’t. For I hold aces, all aces, and he’ll know it as quickly as anyone.”

===Chinese===
他们走进检察官的办公室。格林精神抖擞、跃跃欲试，一名速记员随侍在侧，米纳瓦小姐坐在他办公桌近旁。

「你好啊，温特斯利普先生。」他说。「现在你觉得我们的警察队伍怎么样？挺棒吧，嗯，挺棒。请坐，好吗？」他匆匆翻了翻桌上的几份文件，约翰·昆西、哈利特和陈查理各自落座。「不瞒你说，这事把我惊得七荤八素。哈里·詹尼森和我是老朋友；就在昨天，我还跟他在俱乐部共进午餐呢。我打算稍微换个路子来办，不像对寻常罪犯那样。」

约翰·昆西从椅子里半站起身。「别激动。」格林微笑道。「任凭交情不交情，詹尼森该得的都会得到。我的意思是，倘若能当场撬出他的供词、替本准州省下一场漫长审判的开销，我打算就这么办。他一会儿就要进来，我准备把我的底牌从头到尾全摊给他看。这兴许显得蠢，其实不然。因为我手里攥着的是王牌，张张皆是王牌，他很快就会跟任何人一样看明白。」

===Original===
The door opened. Spencer ushered Jennison into the room, and then withdrew. The accused man stood there, proud, haughty, defiant, a viking of the tropics, a blond giant at bay but unafraid.

“Hello, Jennison,” Greene said. “I’m mighty sorry about this—”

“You ought to be,” Jennison replied. “You’re making an awful fool of yourself. What is this damned nonsense, anyhow—”

“Sit down,” said the prosecutor sharply. He indicated a chair on the opposite side of the desk. He had already turned the shade on his desk lamp so the light would shine full in the face of anyone sitting there. “That lamp bother you, Harry?” he asked.

“Why should it?” Jennison demanded.

===Chinese===
门开了。斯宾塞把詹尼森领进屋来，随即退出。被控告的人站在那里，傲然、骄横、寸步不让，一个热带的维京人，一头陷入重围却毫无惧色的金发巨汉。

「你好，詹尼森。」格林说。「这事我非常遗憾——」

「你早该遗憾了。」詹尼森答道。「你正在把自己变成天大的傻瓜。这究竟是什么鬼名堂——」

「坐下。」检察官厉声道。他指了指桌子对面的一把椅子。他早已拧好台灯的灯罩，好让灯光正面照在任何坐到那儿的人脸上。「这灯碍你的事吗，哈里？」他问。

「凭什么碍事？」詹尼森反问。

===Original===
“Good,” smiled Greene. “I believe Captain Hallet served you with a warrant on the boat. Have you looked at it, by any chance?”

“I have.”

The prosecutor leaned across the desk. “Murder, Jennison!”

Jennison’s expression did not change. “Damned nonsense, as I told you. Why should I murder anyone?”

“Ah, the motive,” Greene replied. “You’re quite right, we should begin with that. Do you wish to be represented here by counsel?”

Jennison shook his head. “I guess I’m lawyer enough to puncture this silly business,” he replied.

===Chinese===
「很好。」格林微笑。「我想哈利特警长已在船上把逮捕证送达你手了。你可曾碰巧看上一眼？」

「看过。」

检察官俯身压过桌面。「谋杀，詹尼森！」

詹尼森面不改色。「一派胡言，我说过了。我为什么要杀人？」

「啊，动机。」格林应道。「你说得对，我们正该从此说起。你希望请律师到场代辩吗？」

詹尼森摇了摇头。「谅我这点律师本事，还足够戳穿这场闹剧。」他答道。

===Original===
“Very well.” Greene turned to his stenographer. “Get this.” The man nodded, and the prosecutor addressed Miss Minerva. “Miss Winterslip, we’ll start with you.”

Miss Minerva leaned forward. “Mr. Dan Winterslip’s house on the beach has, as I told you, been offered for sale by his daughter. After dinner this evening a gentleman came to look at it—a prominent lawyer named Hailey. As we went over the house, Mr. Hailey mentioned that he had met Dan Winterslip on the street a week before his death, and that my cousin had spoken to him about coming in shortly to draw up a new will. He did not say what the provisions of the will were to be, nor did he ever carry out his intention.”

“Ah yes,” said Greene. “But Mr. Jennison here was your cousin’s lawyer?”

“He was.”

===Chinese===
「很好。」格林转向速记员。「记下来。」那人点头，检察官随即对米纳瓦小姐开口。「温特斯利普小姐，我们从您开始。」

米纳瓦小姐向前欠了欠身。「正如我方才所言，丹·温特斯利普先生的海滨宅邸已由他女儿挂牌出售。今晚晚饭后，有位先生来看房——一位知名律师，名叫黑利（Hailey）。我们看房的时候，黑利先生提起，他在丹·温特斯利普遇害前一周曾在街上遇见过他，我这位堂弟还说过，要不了多久就要去找他立一份新遗嘱。他没有说明遗嘱条款将是什么内容，后来也始终未把此意付诸实行。」

「啊，是的。」格林说。「可这边的詹尼森先生，是你堂弟的律师？」

「正是。」

===Original===
“If he wanted to draw a new will, he wouldn’t ordinarily have gone to a stranger for that purpose.”

“Not ordinarily. Unless he had some good reason.”

“Precisely. Unless, for instance, the will had some connection with Harry Jennison.”

===Chinese===
「他要立新遗嘱的话，按常理不会为此去找个生人。」

「按常理不会。除非他有什么充分的理由。」

「正是。譬如说，除非那份遗嘱与哈里·詹尼森有某种牵连。」

===Original===
“I object,” Jennison cried. “This is mere conjecture.”

“So it is,” Greene answered. “But we’re not in court. We can conjecture if we like. Suppose, Miss Winterslip, the will was concerned with Jennison in some way. What do you imagine the connection to have been?”

“I don’t have to imagine,” replied Miss Minerva. “I know.”

“Ah, that’s good. You know. Go on.”

===Chinese===
「我抗议。」詹尼森嚷道。「这纯属臆测。」

「的确如此。」格林答道。「可我们现在不在法庭上。我们尽可以随意臆测。温特斯利普小姐，不妨假设那份遗嘱同詹尼森有某种关系。您设想那会是怎样的牵连？」

「我用不着设想。」米纳瓦小姐答道。「我知道。」

「啊，那太好了。您知道。请讲。」

===Original===
“Before I came down here tonight, I had a talk with my niece. She admitted that her father knew she and Jennison were in love, and that he had bitterly opposed the match. He had even gone so far as to say he would disinherit her if she went through with it.”

“Then the new will Dan Winterslip intended to make would probably have been to the effect that in the event his daughter married Jennison, she was not to inherit a penny of his money?”

“There isn’t any doubt of it,” said Miss Minerva firmly.

“You asked for a motive, Jennison,” Greene said. “That’s motive enough for me. Everybody knows you’re money mad. You wanted to marry Winterslip’s daughter, the richest girl in the Islands. He said you couldn’t have her—not with the money too. But you’re not the sort to make a penniless marriage. You were determined to get both Barbara Winterslip and her father’s property. Only one person stood in your way—Dan Winterslip. And that’s how you happened to be on his lanai that Monday night—”

===Chinese===
「今晚动身来这儿之前，我同我侄女谈了一次。她承认，她父亲早知道她与詹尼森相恋，而且曾激烈反对这门亲事。他甚至把话说到那个地步：她若一意孤行，就要剥夺她的继承权。」

「那么，丹·温特斯利普打算立的新遗嘱，其内容多半便是：倘若女儿嫁给詹尼森，他的财产她一个子儿也别想继承？」

「这一点毫无疑问。」米纳瓦小姐斩钉截铁地说。

「你要的是动机，詹尼森。」格林说。「这动机在我看来绰绰有余。谁都知道你财迷心窍。你想娶温特斯利普的女儿，全群岛最有钱的姑娘。他说你休想得到她——更别想连钱一并到手。可你不是那种肯结一门身无分文婚事的人。你打定主意，芭芭拉·温特斯利普和她父亲的财产要双双到手。挡你道的只有一个人——丹·温特斯利普。于是那个星期一夜里，你就出现在他的露台上了——」

===Original===
“Wait a minute,” Jennison protested. “I wasn’t on his lanai. I was on board the *President Tyler*, and everybody knows that ship didn’t land its passengers until nine the following morning—”

“I’m coming to that,” Greene told him. “Just now—by the way, what time is it?”

Jennison took from his pocket a watch on the end of a slender chain. “It’s a quarter past nine.”

“Ah, yes. Is that the watch you usually carry?”

“It is.”

“Ever wear a wrist watch?”

===Chinese===
「慢着。」詹尼森抗辩。「我根本不在他的露台上。我在泰勒总统号船上，而且谁都知道，那条船第二天上午九点才让乘客上岸——」

「我正要说到那一节。」格林告诉他。「眼下先不谈——对了，现在几点了？」

詹尼森从口袋里掏出一块用细表链拴着的怀表。「九点一刻。」

「啊，好。这是你平常用的表？」

「是。」

「戴过手表吗？」

===Original===
Jennison hesitated. “Occasionally.”

“Only occasionally.” The prosecutor rose and came round his desk. “Let me see your left wrist, please.”

Jennison held out his arm. It was tanned a deep brown, but on the wrist was etched in white the outline of a watch and its encircling strap.

Greene smiled. “Yes, you have worn a wrist watch—and you’ve worn it pretty constantly, from the look of things.” He took a small object from his pocket and held it in front of Jennison. “This watch, perhaps?” Jennison regarded it stonily. “Ever see it before?” Greene asked. “No? Well, suppose we try it on, anyhow.” He put the watch in position and fastened it. “I can’t help noting, Harry,” he continued, “that it fits rather neatly over that white outline on your wrist. And the prong of the buckle falls naturally into the most worn of the holes on the strap.”

“What of that?” asked Jennison.

===Chinese===
詹尼森迟疑了一下。「偶尔。」

「只是偶尔。」检察官起身绕过办公桌。「请让我看看你的左手腕。」

詹尼森伸出手臂。手臂晒成了深棕色，腕上却烙着一圈白色的印子，勾出一块手表及其环绕表带的轮廓。

格林微微一笑。「不错，你是戴过手表——而且从样子看，还戴得相当勤。」他从口袋里取出一件小东西，举到詹尼森面前。「也许，是这一块？」詹尼森面如铁石地盯着它。「以前见过吗？」格林问。「没有？好吧，不管怎样，咱们试戴一下。」他把表扣到位置上，系好。「哈里，我不由得注意到，」他接着说，「它同你腕上那圈白印扣得严丝合缝。而且表扣的针尖不偏不倚，正落进表带上磨损最厉害的那个孔眼。」

「那又怎样？」詹尼森问。

===Original===
“Oh, coincidence, probably. You have abnormally large wrists, however. Surfboarding, swimming, eh? But that’s something else I’ll speak of later.” He turned to Miss Minerva. “Will you please come over here, Miss Winterslip.”

She came, and as she reached his side, the prosecutor suddenly bent over and switched off the light on his desk. Save for a faint glimmer through a transom, the room was in darkness. Miss Minerva was conscious of dim huddled figures, a circle of white faces, a tense silence. The prosecutor was lifting something slowly toward her startled eyes. A watch, worn on a human wrist—a watch with an illuminated dial on which the figure two was almost obliterated.

“Look at that and tell me,” came the prosecutor’s voice. “You have seen it before?”

“I have,” she answered firmly.

“Where?”

“In the dark in Dan Winterslip’s living-room just after midnight the thirtieth of June.”

===Chinese===
「哦，多半是巧合。不过你的手腕粗得出奇。冲浪、游泳练的，嗯？这且不说，回头我再谈。」他转向米纳瓦小姐。「可否请您到这边来，温特斯利普小姐。」

她走了过去，刚到他身边，检察官突然俯下身去，啪地关掉了桌上的灯。除了气窗透进的一丝微光，房间陷入黑暗。米纳瓦小姐只觉出影影绰绰的一团团人影，一圈苍白的脸，一片紧绷的寂静。检察官正把什么东西缓缓举向她惊愕的双眼。一块表，戴在一只人的手腕上——一块带夜光表盘的表，盘面上的数字二几乎磨灭不见了。

「看着它，告诉我。」传来检察官的声音。「您以前见过它吗？」

「见过。」她坚定地回答。

「在哪里？」

「六月三十日午夜刚过，在丹·温特斯利普家客厅的黑暗里。」

===Original===
Greene flashed on the light. “Thank you, Miss Winterslip.” He retired behind his desk and pressed a button. “You identify it by some distinguishing mark, I presume?”

“I do. The numeral two, which is pretty well obscured.”

Spencer appeared at the door. “Send the Spaniard in,” Greene ordered. “That is all for the present, Miss Winterslip.”

===Chinese===
格林啪地打开灯。「谢谢您，温特斯利普小姐。」他退回办公桌后，按了一个按钮。「我猜，您是凭某个特别的印记认出它的？」

「正是。凭那个数字二，它已经相当模糊了。」

斯宾塞出现在门口。「把那西班牙人带进来。」格林吩咐道。「暂时就问到这儿，温特斯利普小姐。」

===Original===
Cabrera entered, and his eyes were frightened as they looked at Jennison. At a nod from the prosecutor, Chan removed the wrist watch and handed it to the Spaniard.

“You know that watch, José?” Greene asked.

“I—I—yes,” answered the boy.

“Don’t be afraid,” Greene urged. “Nobody’s going to hurt you. I want you to repeat the story you told me this afternoon. You have no regular job. You’re a sort of confidential errand boy for Mr. Jennison here.”

“I was.”

===Chinese===
卡布雷拉走进来，眼睛望向詹尼森时满是惶恐。检察官一点头，陈查理便取下那块手表，递给西班牙人。

「你认得这块表吧，何塞？」格林问。

「我——我——认得。」小伙子答道。

「别怕。」格林鼓励道。「没人会伤害你。我要你把今天下午对我讲的事情再讲一遍。你没有正经职业。你算是这边这位詹尼森先生的心腹跑腿。」

「曾经是。」

===Original===
“Yes—that’s all over now. You can speak out. On the morning of Wednesday, July second, you were in Mr. Jennison’s office. He gave you this wrist watch and told you to take it out and get it repaired. Something was the matter with it. It wasn’t running. You took it to a big jewelry store. What happened?”

“The man said it is very badly hurt. To fix it would cost more than a new watch. I go back and tell Mr. Jennison. He laugh and say it is mine as a gift.”

“Precisely.” Greene referred to a paper on his desk. “Late in the afternoon of Thursday, July third, you sold the watch. To whom?”

“To Lau Ho, Chinese jeweler in Maunakea Street. On Saturday evening maybe six o’clock Mr. Jennison telephone my home, much excited. Must have watch again, and will pay any price. I speed to Lau Ho’s store. Watch is sold once more, now to unknown Japanese. Late at night I see Mr. Jennison and he curse me with anger. Get the watch, he says. I have been hunting, but I could not find it.”

===Chinese===
「不错——那都过去了。你尽管放胆讲。七月二日星期三上午，你在詹尼森先生的办公室。他把这块手表交给你，吩咐你拿出去修一修。它出了毛病。走不了了。你把它送到一家大珠宝店。后来怎么样？」

「那人说，它坏得很厉害很厉害。修好它，比买一块新表花的钱还要多。我回去告诉詹尼森先生。他笑笑，说它就归我，算是一件礼物。」

「一点不错。」格林翻看了一下桌上的一页纸。「七月三日星期四傍晚，你把表卖了。卖给了谁？」

「卖给了刘何，莫纳克亚街的中国珠宝商。星期六傍晚大概六点钟，詹尼森先生打电话到我家，激动得很。表一定要拿回来，出多少价钱都行。我飞快赶到刘何的铺子。表又卖掉了，这回是卖给一个不认识的日本人。深夜我去见詹尼森先生，他怒气冲冲，把我臭骂一顿。把表弄回来，他说。我一直到处找，可就是找不着。」

===Original===
Greene turned to Jennison. “You were a little careless with that watch, Harry. But no doubt you figured you were pretty safe—you had your alibi. Then, too, when Hallet detailed the clues to you on Winterslip’s lanai the morning after the crime, he forgot to mention that someone had seen the watch. It was one of those happy accidents that are all we have to count on in this work. By Saturday night you realized your danger—just how you discovered it I don’t know—”

“I do,” John Quincy interrupted.

“What! What’s that?” said Greene.

“On Saturday afternoon,” John Quincy told him, “I played golf with Mr. Jennison. On our way back to town, we talked over the clues in this case, and I happened to mention the wrist watch. I can see now it was the first he had heard of it. He was to dine with us at the beach, but he asked to be put down at his office to sign a few letters. I waited below. It must have been then that he called up this young man in an effort to locate the watch.”

“What the devil are you talking about?” demanded Jennison.

===Chinese===
格林转向詹尼森。「那块表，你处置得有点大意了，哈里。不过你一定盘算过自己相当保险——你有不在场证明。再说，案发次日早晨，哈利特在温特斯利普家的露台上向你细数线索时，漏了提曾有人见过这块表。这类走运的意外，正是我们干这一行惟一能指望的东西。到星期六夜里，你意识到了自己的危险——你究竟是怎么发觉的，我不得而知——」

「我知道。」约翰·昆西插话。

「什么！你说什么？」格林道。

「星期六下午，」约翰·昆西告诉他，「我同詹尼森先生打了一场高尔夫。回城路上，我们聊起这案子的种种线索，我碰巧提起了那块手表。现在我才明白，那是他头一回听说这表。他本要到海滨同我们共进晚餐，却要求在他办公室门口下车，说要去签几封信。我在楼下等他。想必正是那时候，他给这位年轻人打了电话，想把表的下落找回来。」

「你到底在胡扯什么？」詹尼森喝问。

===Original===
“Great stuff,” said Greene enthusiastically. “That finishes the watch, Jennison. I’m surprised you wore it, but you probably knew that it would be vital to you to keep track of the time, and you figured, rightly, that it would not be immediately affected by the salt water—”

Again Greene pressed a button on his desk. Spencer appeared at once. “Take this Spaniard,” the prosecutor directed, “and bring in Hepworth and the quartermaster.” He turned again to Jennison. “I’ll show you what I’m talking about in just a minute. On the night of June thirtieth you were a passenger on the *President Tyler*, which was lying by until dawn out near the channel entrance?”

“I was.”

“No passengers were landed from that ship until the following morning?”

“That’s a matter of record.”

“Very well.” The second officer of the *President Tyler* came in, followed by a big hulking sailorman John Quincy recognized as the quartermaster of that vessel. He was interested to note a ring on the man’s right hand, and his mind went back to that encounter in the San Francisco attic.

===Chinese===
「漂亮。」格林兴冲冲地说。「手表这一环就此坐实，詹尼森。你还一直戴着它，这令我意外；不过你大概明白，随时掌握时间对你性命攸关，而且你算得不差——海水一时半会儿泡不坏它——」

格林又按了桌上的按钮。斯宾塞应声而至。「把这西班牙人带下去，」检察官吩咐，「把赫普沃思和那舵手带上来。」他重又转向詹尼森。「我马上就让你明白我在说什么。六月三十日夜里，你是泰勒总统号上的乘客，那条船当时在航道口外海抛锚停候到天亮，对吗？」

「是。」

「那条船直到次日早晨才让乘客上岸，没错吧？」

「白纸黑字，有案可查。」

「很好。」泰勒总统号的二副走了进来，身后跟着一条彪形大汉般的水手，约翰·昆西认出他正是该船的舵手。他饶有兴味地注意到此人右手上有枚戒指，思绪顿时飞回旧金山阁楼上的那场遭遇。

===Original===
“Mr. Hepworth,” the prosecutor began, “on the night of June thirtieth your ship reached this port too late to dock. You anchored off Waikiki. On such an occasion, who is on deck—say, from midnight on?”

“The second officer,” Hepworth told him. “In this case, myself. Also the quartermaster.”

“The accommodation ladder is let down the night before?”

“Usually, yes. It was let down that night.”

“Who is stationed near it?”

“The quartermaster.”

===Chinese===
「赫普沃思先生，」检察官开口道，「六月三十日夜里，你们的船抵港太晚，靠不了码头。你们在怀基基外海抛了锚。逢到这种情形，甲板上是谁当值——比方说，从午夜往后？」

「二副。」赫普沃思答道。「这一次，就是我本人。还有舵手。」

「舷梯是头天晚上就放下去的？」

「通常是这样。那天夜里是放下去了。」

「谁守在舷梯旁边？」

「舵手。」

===Original===
“Ah, yes. You were in charge then on the night of June thirtieth. Did you notice anything unusual on that occasion?”

Hepworth nodded. “I did. The quartermaster appeared to be under the influence of liquor. At three o’clock I found him dozing near the accommodation ladder. I roused him. When I came back from checking up the anchor bearings before turning in at dawn—about four-thirty—he was dead to the world. I put him in his cabin, and the following morning I of course reported him.”

“You noticed nothing else out of the ordinary?”

“Nothing, sir,” Hepworth replied.

===Chinese===
「啊，好。那么六月三十日夜里当班的就是你了。你可注意到那晚有什么异常？」

赫普沃思点了点头。「注意到了。舵手像是喝多了酒。三点钟，我发现他在舷梯旁打盹。我把他叫醒了。黎明前——大约四点半——我核对完锚位准备回舱就寝，再经过那里时，他已经睡得不省人事。我把他弄回舱里，第二天早晨当然把他上报了。」

「你没再注意到别的反常之处？」

「没有了，先生。」赫普沃思答道。

===Original===
“Thank you very much. Now, you—” Greene turned to the quartermaster. “You were drunk on duty the night of June thirtieth. Where did you get the booze?” The man hesitated. “Before you say anything, let me give you a bit of advice. The truth, my man. You’re in pretty bad already. I’m not making any promises, but if you talk straight here it may help you in that other matter. If you lie, it will go that much harder with you.”

“I ain’t going to lie,” promised the quartermaster.

“All right. Where did you get your liquor?”

The man nodded toward Jennison. “He gave it to me.”

“He did, eh? Tell me all about it.”

===Chinese===
「多谢了。现在，你——」格林转向舵手。「六月三十日夜里，你当班时喝醉了。酒是从哪儿弄来的？」那人迟疑起来。「在你开口之前，容我奉劝一句。说实话，老弟。你现在的处境已经够糟。我不作任何许诺，不过你在这儿把话讲直了，在那桩案子上兴许对你有好处。你要是撒谎，你的苦头就有的吃了。」

「我不打算撒谎。」舵手保证道。

「好。你的酒是哪儿来的？」

那人朝詹尼森一扬下巴。「他给的。」

「他给的，嗯？原原本本讲来。」

===Original===
“I met him on deck just after midnight—we was still moving. I knew him before—him and me—”

“In the opium game, both of you. I understand that. You met him on deck—”

“I did, and he says, you’re on watch tonight, eh, and I says I am. So he slips me a little bottle an’ says, this will help you pass the time. I ain’t a drinking man, so help me I ain’t, an’ I took just a nip, but there was something in that whisky, I’ll swear to it. My head was all funny like, an’ the next I knew I was waked up in my cabin with the bad news I was wanted above.”

“What became of that bottle?”

“I dropped it overboard on my way to see the captain. I didn’t want nobody to find it.”

===Chinese===
「刚过午夜，我在甲板上碰见他——那会儿船还走着呢。我以前就认得他——他和我——」

「都在鸦片买卖里，你们俩。这我明白。你在甲板上碰见他——」

「碰见了。他说，你今儿夜里当班，是吧？我说，是。他就塞给我一小瓶，说，这个能帮你熬时间。我这个人不贪杯，老天作证我真不贪，就抿了一小口，可那威士忌里准掺了东西，我可以对天发誓。我脑袋晕晕乎乎直发怪，再一回神，我已在自个儿舱里被人叫醒，得了个坏消息，说上头要找我。」

「那瓶子后来哪儿去了？」

「去见船长的路上，我把它扔海里了。我不想叫任何人捡着。」

===Original===
“Did you see anything the night of June thirtieth? Anything peculiar?”

“I seen plenty, sir—but it was that drink. Nothing you would want to hear about.”

“All right.” The prosecutor turned to Jennison. “Well, Harry—you drugged him, didn’t you? Why? Because you were going ashore, eh? Because you knew he’d be on duty at that ladder when you returned, and you didn’t want him to see you. So you dropped something into that whiskey—”

“Guess work,” cut in Jennison, still unruffled. “I used to have some respect for you as a lawyer, but it’s all gone now. If this is the best you can offer—”

===Chinese===
「六月三十日夜里，你看见什么没有？有什么蹊跷的？」

「瞧见的多着呢，长官——可全是那杯酒闹的。没什么您想听的。」

「好吧。」检察官转向詹尼森。「怎么样，哈里——你给他下了药，不是吗？为什么？因为你要上岸，嗯？因为你知道回来时他还会守在那道舷梯边，你不想让他瞧见。于是你往那威士忌里放了点东西——」

「凭空瞎猜。」詹尼森插进来，依旧不动声色。「往日我对你这个律师还有几分敬意，如今荡然无存。你要拿得出手的就这些——」

===Original===
“But it isn’t,” said Greene pleasantly. Again he pushed the button. “I’ve something much better, Harry, if you’ll only wait.” He turned to Hepworth. “There’s a steward on your ship named Bowker,” he began, and John Quincy thought that Jennison stiffened. “How has he been behaving lately?”

“Well, he got pretty drunk in Hong Kong,” Hepworth answered. “But that, of course, was the money.”

“What money?”

“It’s this way. The last time we sailed out of Honolulu harbor for the Orient, over two weeks ago, I was in the purser’s office. It was just as we were passing Diamond Head. Bowker came in, and he had a big fat envelope that he wanted to deposit in the purser’s safe. He said it contained a lot of money. The purser wouldn’t be responsible for it without seeing it, so Bowker slit the envelope—and there were ten one hundred dollar bills. The purser made another package of it and put it in the safe. He told me Bowker took out a couple of the bills when we reached Hong Kong.”

===Chinese===
「可不止这些。」格林和颜悦色地说。他又按了按钮。「我还有好得多的东西，哈里，只要你肯等一等。」他转向赫普沃思。「你们船上有位乘务员叫鲍克。」他开了腔，约翰·昆西觉得詹尼森的身子似乎僵了一僵。「他近来表现如何？」

「唔，他在香港喝得烂醉。」赫普沃思答道。「不过那自然是因为钱的缘故。」

「什么钱？」

「是这么回事。两个多星期前，我们最后一次驶出檀香山港开往东方，我当时在事务长办公室。船正过钻石头山那会儿，鲍克进来了，手里攥着一只厚厚实实的大信封，要存进事务长的保险柜。他说里面装着一大笔钱。事务长不见内容不肯担这个责，鲍克便撕开信封——里面是十张一百块的票子。事务长另行打包封好，放进了保险柜。他告诉我，船到香港时，鲍克取走了其中两张。」

===Original===
“Where would a man like Bowker get all that money?”

“I can’t imagine. He said he’d put over a business deal in Honolulu but—well, we knew Bowker.”

The door opened. Evidently Spencer guessed who was wanted this time, for he pushed Bowker into the room. The steward of the *President Tyler* was bedraggled and bleary.

“Hello, Bowker,” said the prosecutor. “Sober now, aren’t you?”

“I’ll tell the world I am,” replied Bowker. “They’ve walked me to San Francisco and back. Can—can I sit down?”

===Chinese===
「鲍克这么个人，上哪儿弄来这么一大笔钱？」

「想不出。他说他在檀香山做成了一笔买卖，可是——唉，鲍克这人，我们还不知道么。」

门开了。显然这回斯宾塞猜到了要提审谁，他把鲍克推了进来。泰勒总统号的这位乘务员一副狼狈相，睡眼惺忪。

「你好，鲍克。」检察官说。「这会儿醒过酒来了吧？」

「这话我要昭告天下——醒得透透的了。」鲍克答道。「他们押着我跑了一趟旧金山来回。我——我能坐下吗？」

===Original===
“Of course,” Greene smiled. “This afternoon, while you were still drunk, you told a story to Willie Chan, out at Okamoto’s auto stand on Kalakaua Avenue. Later on, early this evening, you repeated it to Captain Hallet and me. I’ll have to ask you to go over it again.”

Bowker glanced toward Jennison, then quickly looked away. “Always ready to oblige,” he answered.

“You’re a steward on the *President Tyler*,” Greene continued. “On your last trip over here from the mainland Mr. Jennison occupied one of your rooms—number 97. He was alone in it, I believe?”

“All alone. He paid extra for the privilege, I hear. Always traveled that way.”

“Room 97 was on the main deck, not far from the accommodation ladder?”

“Yes, that’s right.”

“Tell us what happened after you anchored off Waikiki the night of June thirtieth.”

===Chinese===
「当然。」格林微笑道。「今天下午，你还醉着的时候，在卡拉卡瓦大道上冈本（Okamoto）的汽车候客站那儿，把一件事讲给了威利·陈听。随后，今晚天刚黑那阵，你又对哈利特警长和我讲了一遍。现在只好请你再复述一回。」

鲍克朝詹尼森瞥了一眼，随即迅速移开目光。「随时乐意效劳。」他答道。

「你是泰勒总统号上的乘务员。」格林接着说。「上一次你从大陆驶来这儿时，詹尼森先生住了你分管的一间舱——97 号。他是独住，我没记错吧？」

「独个儿住。听说为这待遇他还多掏了钱。他出门向来是这个规矩。」

「97 号舱在主甲板上，离舷梯不远？」

「对，没错。」

「讲讲六月三十日夜里，你们在怀基基外海抛锚之后发生了什么。」

===Original===
Bowker adjusted his gold-rimmed glasses with the gesture of a man about to make an after-dinner speech. “Well, I was up pretty late that night. Mr. Winterslip here had loaned me some books—there was one I was particularly interested in. I wanted to finish it so I could give it to him to take ashore in the morning. It was nearly two o’clock when I finally got through it, and I was feeling stuffy, so I went on deck for a breath of air.”

“You stopped not far from the accommodation ladder?”

“Yes sir, I did.”

“Did you notice the quartermaster?”

“Yes—he was sound asleep in a deck chair. I went over and leaned on the rail, the ladder was just beneath me. I’d been standing there a few minutes when suddenly somebody came up out of the water and put his hands on the lowest rung. I drew back quickly and stood in a shadow.

===Chinese===
鲍克扶了扶金丝眼镜，那架势像一位即将发表餐后演说的先生。「呃，那天夜里我熬得挺晚。这位温特斯利普先生借了几本书给我——其中一本我特别上心。我想赶紧读完，好天亮时还给他带上岸。等终于读完，已快两点，我觉得屋里发闷，便上甲板去透口气。」

「你停在离舷梯不远的地方？」

「是的，先生，是停那儿了。」

「你注意到舵手了吗？」

「注意到了——他在一张甲板躺椅上睡得死沉。我走过去倚在栏杆上，舷梯就在我脚下。我在那儿站了几分钟，忽然有个人从水里冒上来，一把攀住了最底下一级梯蹬。我赶紧缩回来，站进了暗影里。

===Original===
“Well, pretty soon this man comes creeping up the ladder to the deck. He was barefooted, and all in black—black pants and shirt. I watched him. He went over and bent above the quartermaster, then started toward me down the deck. He was walking on tiptoe, but even then I didn’t get wise to the fact anything was wrong.

“I stepped out of the shadow. ‘Fine night for a swim, Mr. Jennison,’ I said. And I saw at once that I’d made a social error. He gave one jump in my direction and his hands closed on my throat. I thought my time had come.”

“He was wet, wasn’t he?” Greene asked.

“Dripping. He left a trail of water on the deck.”

“Did you notice a watch on his wrist?”

===Chinese===
「过了不大一会儿，那人便顺着舷梯爬上了甲板。他打着赤脚，通身黑衣——黑裤子、黑衬衫。我盯着他瞧。他走过去，在舵手身上方俯下身去，又顺着甲板朝我这边走来。他踮着脚尖走路，可就连那时，我也没醒悟出有什么不对。

「我从暗影里跨出来。『夜色不错，适合游泳啊，詹尼森先生。』我说。我当即看出，自己犯了个社交上的失礼。他一个箭步朝我扑来，双手掐住了我的喉咙。我以为我的大限到了。」

「他浑身湿漉漉的，对吧？」格林问。

「滴着水。他在甲板上淌下一道水痕。」

「你注意到他手腕上有表吗？」

===Original===
“Yes, but you can bet I didn’t make any study of it. I had other things to think about just then. I managed to sort of ooze out of his grip, and I told him to cut it out or I’d yell. ‘Look here,’ he says, ‘you and I can talk business, I guess. Come into my cabin.’

“But I wasn’t wanting any tête-à-tête with him in any cabin. I said I’d see him in the morning, and after I’d promised to say nothing to anybody, he let me go. I went to bed, pretty much puzzled.

===Chinese===
「注意到了，可你尽管放心，我没工夫对它细加端详。那会儿，我有别的更要紧的事得琢磨。我好歹从他的钳制里像泥鳅似的慢慢挣脱出来，警告他撒手，不然我就喊人。『你听着，』他说，『我想，你和我可以谈笔买卖。到我舱里来。』

「可我不打算跟他到什么舱里去单独密谈。我说早晨再见，等我保证对谁也不吭一声，他才放我走。我上床睡了，心里满是疑惑。

===Original===
“The next morning, when I went into his cabin, there he was all fresh and rosy and smiling. If I’d had so much as a whiff of booze the night before, I’d have thought I never saw what I did.
