I went in there thinking I might get a hundred dollars out of the affair, but the minute he spoke I began to smell important money. He said no one must know about his swim the night before. How much did I want? Well, I held my breath and said ten thousand dollars. And I nearly dropped dead when he answered I could have it.”

Bowker turned to John Quincy. “I don’t know what you’ll think of me. I don’t know what Tim would think. I’m not a crook by nature. But I was fed up and choking over that steward job. I wanted a little newspaper of my own, and up to that minute I couldn’t see myself getting it. And you must remember that I didn’t know then what was in the air—murder. Later, when I did find out, I was scared to breathe. I didn’t know what they could do to me.” He turned to Greene. “That’s all fixed,” he said.

“I’ve promised you immunity,” the prosecutor answered. “I’ll keep my word. Go on—you agreed to accept the ten thousand?”

“I did. I went to his office at twelve. One of the conditions was that I could stay on the *President Tyler* until she got back to San Francisco, and after that I was never to show my face out this way again. It suited me. Mr. Jennison introduced me to this Cabrera, who was to chaperon me the rest of that day. I’ll say he did. When I went aboard the ship, he handed me a thousand dollars in an envelope.

===Chinese===
「第二天早晨，我进他舱里去，他竟满面清新红润，笑吟吟的。头天夜里我哪怕沾过一口酒，都会疑心自己压根没见过亲眼所见的一切。我进舱时盘算着，这事兴许能捞一百块，可他一开口，我就嗅出这是一笔大钱。他说，前一晚他游泳的事绝不能有第二个人知道。我要多少？唔，我屏住呼吸，开价一万美元。他回答说我可以拿——我差点当场倒地死过去。」

鲍克转向约翰·昆西。「我不知道你会怎么看我。我也不知道蒂姆（Tim）会怎么想。我天生不是个无赖。可乘务员那份差事，早让我腻烦透顶、憋闷欲死。我想拥有一份自己的小报，而在那一刻之前，我看不出自己有半点指望。而且你务必记得，我当时并不知道风里酝酿着什么——是谋杀。后来知道了，我吓得连大气都不敢出。我不晓得他们能把我怎么样。」他转向格林。「手续都办妥了。」他说。

「我答应过豁免你的罪责。」检察官答道。「我说话算话。往下讲——那笔一万美元，你答应收下了？」

「收下了。十二点，我去了他办公室。条件之一是，我可以留在泰勒总统号上，等船回旧金山，此后再也不许在这一带露面。这正合我意。詹尼森先生把我介绍给这位卡布雷拉，由他当天余下的时间贴身陪着、看着我。要说尽责，他可真是尽责。我上船时，他递给我一只信封，里头是一千美元。

===Original===
“When I came back this time, I was to spend the day with Cabrera and get the other nine grand when I sailed. This morning when we tied up I saw the Spaniard on the dock, but by the time I’d landed he had disappeared. I met this Willie Chan and we had a large day. This fusel oil they sell out here loosened my tongue, but I’m not sorry. Of course, the rosy dream has faded, and it’s my flat feet on the deck from now to the end of time. But the shore isn’t so much any more, with all the barrooms under cover, and this sea life keeps a man out in the open air. As I say, I’m not sorry I talked. I can look any man in the eye again and tell him to go to—” He glanced at Miss Minerva. “Madam, I will not name the precise locality.”

===Chinese===
「这一回再来，我本该同卡布雷拉消磨一天，等船启航时领那剩下的九千块。今天早晨船靠码头，我瞧见那西班牙人站在码头上，可等我下了船，他已没影了。我遇见了这位威利·陈，我们痛痛快快地过了一天。此地卖的这种杂醇油劣酒松开了我的舌头，可我并不后悔。当然，玫瑰色的美梦散了，从今往后直到世界末日，我这双平脚板就钉死在甲板上了。不过岸上如今也没什么可留恋的，酒馆一概转入地下偷偷摸摸，而这种海上生活好歹让一个人整天待在露天地里。就像我说的，说出了口，我不后悔。我又能正眼直视任何一个人，叫他滚到——」他朝米纳瓦小姐瞥了一眼。「夫人，那确切的去处我就不明说了。」

===Original===
Greene stood. “Well, Jennison, there’s my case. I’ve tipped it all off to you, but I wanted you to see for yourself how airtight it is. There are two courses open to you—you can let this go to trial with a plea of not guilty. A long humiliating ordeal for you. Or you can confess here and now and throw yourself on the mercy of the court. If you’re the sensible man I think you are, that’s what you’ll do.”

===Chinese===
格林站起身。「好了，詹尼森，我的案子全在这儿了。底细我都摊给了你，但我要让你亲眼看看，它是何等滴水不漏。摆在你面前的路有两条——你可以宣称无罪，让案子交付审判。那对你是一场漫长而屈辱的煎熬。你也可以在此刻此地坦白招供，听凭法庭发落。你要是我心目中那个明白人，你就该选后者。」

===Original===
Jennison did not answer, did not even look at the prosecutor. “It was a very neat idea,” Greene went on. “I’ll grant you that. Only one thing puzzles me—did it come as the inspiration of the moment, or did you plan it all out in advance? You’ve been over to the mainland rather often of late—were you waiting your chance? Anyhow, it came, didn’t it—it came at last. And for a swimmer like you, child’s play. You didn’t need that ladder when you left the vessel—perhaps you went overboard while the *President Tyler* was still moving. A quick silent dive, a little way under water in case anyone was watching from the deck, and then a long but easy swim ashore. And there you were, on the beach at Waikiki. Not far away Dan Winterslip was asleep on his lanai, with not so much as a locked door between you. Dan Winterslip, who stood between you and what you wanted. A little struggle—a quick thrust of your knife. Come on, Jennison, don’t be a fool. It’s the best way out for you now. A full confession.”

===Chinese===
詹尼森不答话，连看也不看检察官一眼。「这一手设计得确实干净利落。」格林继续说。「这一点我承认。只有一件事让我纳闷——它是一时灵光乍现，还是你事先筹划周全？近来你往大陆跑得相当勤——你是在候着你的机会吧？反正机会来了，不是吗——它终于来了。对你这样一位泳将，不过是儿戏。你离船时根本用不着那道舷梯——兴许泰勒总统号还在行驶时，你就下了水。一次迅疾无声的潜入，先在水底潜上一小段，以防甲板上有人张望，然后一段漫长却从容的游程，直到上岸。于是你到了，站在怀基基的海滩上。不远处，丹·温特斯利普正在露台上酣睡，你们之间连一扇上锁的门都没有。丹·温特斯利普，那个横在你和你的欲求之间的人。一阵小小的搏斗——一刀飞快地捅进去。来吧，詹尼森，别犯傻。这是你如今最好的出路。一份完完整整的供词。」

===Original===
Jennison leaped to his feet, his eyes flashing. “I’ll see you in hell first!” he cried.

“Very well—if you feel that way about it—” Greene turned his back upon him and began a low-toned conversation with Hallet. Jennison and Charlie Chan were together on one side of the desk. Chan took out a pencil and accidentally dropped it on the floor. He stooped to pick it up.

John Quincy saw that the butt of a pistol carried in Chan’s hip pocket protruded from under his coat. He saw Jennison spring forward and snatch the gun. With a cry John Quincy moved nearer, but Greene seized his arm and held him. Charlie Chan seemed unaccountably oblivious to what was going on.

Jennison put the muzzle of the pistol to his forehead and pulled the trigger. A sharp click—and that was all. The pistol fell from his hand.

===Chinese===
詹尼森一跃而起，双眼喷火。「想让我招？先到地狱里候着去吧！」他吼道。

「很好——你既然是这个态度——」格林转过身去背对着他，开始同哈利特低声交谈。詹尼森和陈查理一同站在书桌一侧。陈查理掏出一支铅笔，一个失手把它掉在了地上。他弯腰去拾。

约翰·昆西看见，陈查理后腰裤袋里揣着的那支手枪，枪柄从上衣下露了出来。他看见詹尼森一个箭步蹿上去，把枪夺在手里。约翰·昆西喊了一声抢上前去，格林却一把攥住他的胳膊，按住了他。陈查理仿佛毫无来由地浑然不觉眼前发生的一切。

詹尼森把枪口顶上自己的额头，扣动了扳机。咔的一声脆响——仅此而已。手枪从他手里跌落。

===Original===
“That’s it!” cried Greene triumphantly. “That’s my confession, and not a word spoken. I’ve witnesses, Jennison—they all saw you—you couldn’t stand the disgrace—a man in your position—you tried to kill yourself. With an empty gun.” He went over and patted Chan on the shoulder. “A great idea, Charlie,” he said. “Chan thought of it,” he added to Jennison. “The Oriental mind, Harry. Rather subtle, isn’t it?”

But Jennison had dropped back into his chair and buried his face in his hands.

“I’m sorry,” said Greene gently. “But we’ve got you. Maybe you’ll talk now.”

Jennison looked up slowly. The defiance was gone from his face; it was lined and old.

“Maybe I will,” he said hoarsely.

===Chinese===
「这就成了！」格林得意地喊道。「这份供词就是我的了——他一字未吐。我有证人，詹尼森——他们全看见了——你受不了这奇耻大辱——你这样身份的人——你试图自杀。用一支没装子弹的枪。」他走过去，拍拍陈查理的肩膀。「妙极了的主意，陈查理。」他说。「这是陈查理想出来的。」他向詹尼森补了一句。「东方人的头脑，哈里。相当微妙，是不是？」

可詹尼森已颓然跌回椅中，双手掩住了脸。

「很抱歉。」格林温和地说。「可我们已经拿住你了。现在，你也许肯开口了吧。」

詹尼森缓缓抬起头。脸上的挑衅神色已荡然无存；满面沟壑纵横，一瞬间苍老下去。

「也许肯。」他嗓音嘶哑地说。

## XXIII Moonlight at the Crossroads / 二十三 十字路口的月光

===Original===
They filed out, leaving Jennison with Greene and the stenographer. In the anteroom Chan approached John Quincy.

“You go home decked in the shining garments of success,” he said. “One thought are tantalizing me. At simultaneous moment you arrive at same conclusion we do. To reach there you must have leaped across considerable cavity.”

John Quincy laughed. “I’ll say I did. It came to me tonight. First, someone mentioned a golf professional with big wrists who drove a long ball. I had a quick flash of Jennison on the links here, and his terrific drives. Big wrists, they told me, meant that a man was proficient in the water. Then someone else﻿—a young woman﻿—spoke of a champion swimmer who left a ship off Waikiki. That was the first time the idea of such a thing had occurred to me. I was pretty warm then, and I felt Bowker was the man who could verify my suspicion. When I rushed aboard the *President Tyler* to find him, I saw Jennison about to sail, and that confirmed my theory. I went after him.”

===Chinese===
他们鱼贯而出，把詹尼森留给了格林先生和速记员。在前厅里，陈查理走近约翰·昆西。

「您这就回家去，周身披着成功那闪亮的衣袍，」他说。「只是有一个念头仍在百般撩拨着在下。您于同一时刻抵达了与我等相同的结论。要抵达彼处，您想必凌空跃过了一道相当可观的深壑。」

约翰·昆西笑了。「可不是嘛。这念头是今晚找上我的。起先，有人说起一位手腕粗大的高尔夫职业球员，开球开得极远。我脑中霎时闪过詹尼森在这边球场上的身影，还有他那雷霆万钧的长打。人家告诉我，手腕粗大，意味着一个人水性出众。接着又有别人——一位年轻小姐——说到一位游泳冠军，当年在怀基基外海弃船而去。这样的念头，我还是头一回生出。那会儿我已经猜得相当烫手了，而且我觉得，鲍克是能证实我疑心的人。等我冲上泰勒总统号去找他，却见詹尼森正要启航，这一下印证了我的推断。我便追了上去。」

===Original===
“A brave performance,” commented Chan.

“But as you can see, Charlie, I didn’t have an iota of real evidence. Just guesswork. You were the one who furnished the proof.”

“Proof are essential in this business,” Chan replied.

“I’m tantalized too, Charlie. I remember you in the library. You were on the track long before I was. How come?”

Chan grinned. “Seated at our ease in All American restaurant that first night, you will recall I spoke of Chinese people as sensitives, like camera film. A look, a laugh, a gesture, something go click. Bowker enters and hovering above, says with alcoholic accent; ‘I’m my own mashter, ain’t I?’ In my mind, the click. He is not own master. I follow to dock, behold when Spaniard present envelope. But for days I am fogged. I can only learn Cabrera and Jennison are very close. Clues continue to burst in our countenance. The occasion remains suspensive. At the library I read of Jennison the fine swimmer. After that, the watch, and triumph.”

===Chinese===
「一场英勇的演出。」陈查理评道。

「可是你也看见了，查理，我手上连一丁点儿真凭实据都没有。光凭瞎猜。拿出证据来的是你。」

「干这一行当，证明乃是必不可少之事物。」陈查理答道。

「我心里也给吊得痒痒的，查理。我记得你在图书室里的那副样子。你盯上这条线索，比我早了好久。怎么做到的？」

陈查理咧嘴一笑。「头一夜，你我安坐全美餐馆，您当还记得，我说过中国人是天生的感应体，好比照相的底片。一个眼神，一声笑，一个手势，什么东西便咔嗒一响。鲍克进得门来，居高临下地凑着，带着酒气说道：『我是我自个儿的主儿，嗯哼？』在下心里，咔嗒一声。他并非自个儿的主儿。我尾随至码头，亲见西班牙人呈递信封。但接连数日，在下如堕雾中。只能查明卡布雷拉与詹尼森过从甚密。线索接连不断地在我们脸面上炸开。局面始终悬而未决。到了图书室，在下读到詹尼森乃游泳健将。此后，便是那块手表，以及凯旋。」

===Original===
Miss Minerva moved on toward the door. “May I have great honor to accompany you to car?” asked Chan.

Outside, John Quincy directed the chauffeur to return alone to Waikiki with the limousine. “You’re riding out with me,” he told his aunt. “I want to talk with you.”

She turned to Charlie Chan. “I congratulate you. You’ve got brains, and they count.”

He bowed low. “From you that compliment glows rosy red. At this moment of parting, my heart droops. My final wish﻿—the snowy chilling days of winter and the scorching windless days of summer﻿—may they all be the springtime for you.”

“You’re very kind,” she said softly.

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐朝门口走去。「可否赐在下偌大荣幸，伴送您至车前？」陈查理问。

到了外面，约翰·昆西吩咐司机独自驾着大型轿车返回怀基基。「您搭我的车回去，」他对姑婆说。「我想同您谈谈。」

她转向陈查理。「我恭喜你。你有头脑，而头脑这东西是顶用的。」

他深深一躬。「这一句夸奖，出自您口中，便烧得玫瑰般红。值此离别之际，我的心不禁低垂。在下最后的心愿——愿冬日雪寒的天、夏日灼热无风的天，于您统统化作春天。」

「您太客气了。」她轻声说。

===Original===
John Quincy took his hand. “It’s been great fun knowing you, Charlie,” he remarked.

“You will go again to the mainland,” Chan said. “The angry ocean rolling between us. Still I shall carry the memory of your friendship like a flower in my heart.” John Quincy climbed into the car. “And the parting may not be eternal,” Chan added cheerfully. “The joy of travel may yet be mine. I shall look forward to the day when I may call upon you in your home and shake a healthy hand.”

John Quincy started the car and slipping away, they left Charlie Chan standing like a great Buddha on the curb.

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西握住他的手。「认识你真是件大乐事，查理。」他说。

「您将再度前往大陆，」陈查理说。「怒海滚滚，横亘于你我之间。在下仍将把您友谊的记忆，如一朵花般揣在心头。」约翰·昆西跨进汽车。「况且这离别未必便是永诀，」陈查理欣然补上一句。「旅行之乐，兴许也终将落在下一份。在下期盼着那一天——登门造访贵宅，握一握您健旺的手。」

约翰·昆西发动汽车，车子悄悄滑走，把陈查理留在路边，伫立如一尊大佛。

===Original===
“Poor Barbara,” said Miss Minerva presently. “I dread to face her with this news. But then, it’s not altogether news at that. She told me she’d been conscious of something wrong between her and Jennison ever since they landed. She didn’t think he killed her father, but she believed he was involved in it somehow. She is planning to settle with Brade tomorrow and leave the next day, probably forever. I’ve persuaded her to come to Boston for a long visit. You’ll see her there.”

John Quincy shook his head. “No, I shan’t. But thanks for reminding me. I must go to the cable office at once.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的芭芭拉，」米纳瓦小姐过了一会儿说。「我实在怕把这消息摆到她面前。不过话说回来，这也算不上全然的新闻。她告诉过我，自从同詹尼森一起上岸以来，她就隐约觉出两人之间有什么不对劲。她不信他杀了自己的父亲，却相信他多少牵连在内。她打算明天同布雷德把账了结，后天就走，多半是永远离开了。我已经劝动她去波士顿长住一阵。你在那儿会见到她的。」

约翰·昆西摇头。「不，见不着了。不过多谢提醒。我得马上就去电报局。」

===Original===
When he emerged from the office and again entered the car, he was smiling happily.

“In San Francisco,” he explained, “Roger accused me of being a Puritan survival. He ran over a little list of adventures he said had never happened to me. Well, most of them have happened now, and I cabled to tell him so. I also said I’d take that job with him.”

Miss Minerva frowned. “Think it over carefully,” she warned. “San Francisco isn’t Boston. The cultural standard is, I fancy, much lower. You’ll be lonely there﻿—”

“Oh, no, I shan’t. Someone will be there with me. At least, I hope she will.”

===Chinese===
等他从电报局出来、重新坐进汽车时，他笑得一脸幸福。

「在旧金山那会儿，」他解释道，「罗杰指摘我是清教徒的孑遗。他还扳着指头数过一小串历险，说这些事我一件也不曾经历。喏，如今这串里的大半都经历过了，我打了电报如此告知他。还说了，他给我出的那份差事，我应下了。」

米纳瓦小姐皱起眉头。「仔仔细细想清楚，」她告诫道。「旧金山可不是波士顿。那里的文化水准，依我看，要低得多。你会孤单的——」

「哦，不会的。有人会陪着我。至少，我希望她肯。」

===Original===
“Agatha?”

“No, not Agatha. The cultural standard was too low for her. She’s broken our engagement.”

“Barbara, then?”

“Not Barbara, either.”

“But I have sometimes thought﻿—”

“You thought Barbara sent Jennison packing because of me. Jennison thought so too﻿—it’s all clear now. That was why he tried to frighten me into leaving Honolulu, and set his opium running friends on me when I wouldn’t go. But Barbara is not in love with me. We understand now why she broke her engagement.”

“Neither Agatha nor Barbara,” repeated Miss Minerva. “Then who﻿—”

“You haven’t met her yet, but that happy privilege will be yours before you sleep. The sweetest girl in the Islands﻿—or in the world. The daughter of Jim Egan, whom you have been heard to refer to as a glorified beachcomber.”

===Chinese===
「阿加莎？」

「不，不是阿加莎。那地方的文化水准，在她眼里是太低了。她已经同我解除婚约了。」

「那么，是芭芭拉？」

「也不是芭芭拉。」

「可我有时候还想过——」

「您原以为芭芭拉是为了我才把詹尼森打发走的。詹尼森自己也这么想——如今全清楚了。所以他才想吓得我离开檀香山，见我不肯走，又放他那伙走私鸦片的朋友来对付我。可芭芭拉并不爱我。她为什么解除婚约，如今我们都明白了。」

「不是阿加莎，也不是芭芭拉，」米纳瓦小姐重复道。「那么是谁——」

「您还没见过她，不过不等您睡下，这份幸福的光荣就会落到您头上。全群岛最甜的姑娘——也是全世界最甜的。吉姆·伊根的女儿——就是那位先生，您在背后说过，他是个『摆阔的海滩游民』。」

===Original===
Again Miss Minerva frowned. “It’s a great risk, John Quincy. She hasn’t our background﻿—”

“No, and that’s a pleasant change. She’s the niece of your old friend﻿—you knew that?”

“I did,” answered Miss Minerva softly.

“Your dear friend of the ’eighties. What was it you said to me? If your chance ever comes﻿—”

“I hope you will be very happy,” his aunt said. “When you write it to your mother, be sure and mention Captain Cope of the British Admiralty. Poor Grace! That will be all she’ll have to cling to﻿—after the wreck.”

“What wreck?”

“The wreck of all her hopes for you.”

“Nonsense. Mother will understand. She knows I’m a roaming Winterslip, and when we roam, we roam.”

===Chinese===
米纳瓦小姐又皱起眉头。「这可是天大的冒险，约翰·昆西。她可没有咱们这样的家世——」

「没有，而这正是个可喜的变化。她是您那位老朋友的侄女——这件事您知道吗？」

「知道。」米纳瓦小姐轻声答道。

「您那位八十年代的亲爱的朋友。您当时是怎么对我说的？倘若您的机会有朝一日来了——」

「愿你们非常幸福，」姑婆说。「给你母亲写信的时候，千万记得提一提英国海军部的科佩船长。可怜的格蕾丝！沉船之后，她能抱住不放的，也就只剩这个了。」

「什么沉船？」

「她对你抱的一切指望，尽数沉没的那艘船。」

「胡说。母亲会明白的。她知道我是个流浪的温特斯利普——我们温特斯利普家的人说流浪，就当真流浪。」

===Original===
They found Madame Maynard seated in her living-room with a few of her more elderly guests. From the beach came the sound of youthful revelry.

“Well, my boy,” the old woman cried, “it appears you couldn’t stay away from your policemen friends one single evening, after all. I give you up.”

John Quincy laughed. “I’m pau now. By the way, Carlota Egan﻿—is she﻿—”

“They’re all out there somewhere,” the hostess said. “They came in for a bit of supper﻿—by the way, there are sandwiches in the dining-room and﻿—”

“Not just now,” said John Quincy. “Thank you so much. I’ll see you again, of course﻿—”

===Chinese===
他们看见梅纳德夫人坐在起居室里，陪着她几位较为年长的客人。海滩那边传来年轻人狂欢的声音。

「哎呀，我的孩子，」老太太喊道，「看来你到底连一个晚上也离不开你那些警察朋友。我算拿你没办法了。」

约翰·昆西笑了。「我这下帕乌（完事）了。对了，卡洛塔·伊根——她是不是——」

「他们全在外头什么地方，」女主人说。「他们进来吃了点夜宵——对了，餐室里有三明治，还有——」

「这会儿先不吃，」约翰·昆西说。「多谢您了。我改日再来看您，一定——」

===Original===
He dashed out on the sand. A group of young people under the hau tree informed him that Carlota Egan was on the farthest float. Alone? Well, no﻿—that naval lieutenant﻿—

He was, he reflected as he hurried on toward the water, a bit fed up with the navy. That was hardly the attitude he should have taken, considering all the navy had done for him. But it was human. And John Quincy was human at last.

For an instant he stood at the water’s edge. His bathing suit was in the dressing-room, but he never gave it a thought. He kicked off his shoes, tossed aside his coat, and plunged into the breakers. The blood of the wandering Winterslips was racing through his veins; hot blood that tropical waters had ever been powerless to cool.

===Chinese===
他一阵风似的冲上沙滩。哈乌树下一群年轻人告诉他，卡洛塔·伊根在最远的那座浮台上。独自一人？呃，不是——还有那位海军中尉——

他一面朝水边匆匆走去，一面暗想，自己这会儿对海军实在有点腻烦了。想想海军待他的种种好处，这实在不该是他该有的态度。可这是人之常情。而约翰·昆西，终于也成了个凡人。

有一瞬，他伫立在水边。游泳衣还在更衣室里，可他压根儿没想起来。他踢掉鞋子，甩开外衣，一头扎进碎浪之中。流浪的温特斯利普家族的血，在他血管里奔涌；那是热带海水从来不曾冷却得了的热血。

===Original===
Sure enough, Carlota Egan and Lieutenant Booth were together on the float. John Quincy climbed up beside them.

“Well, I’m back,” he announced.

“I’ll tell the world you’re back,” said the lieutenant. “And all wet, too.”

They sat there. Across a thousand miles of warm water the trade winds came to fan their cheeks. Just above the horizon hung the Southern Cross; the Island lights trembled along the shore; the yellow eye on Diamond Head was winking. A gorgeous setting. Only one thing was wrong with it. It seemed rather crowded.

===Chinese===
果不其然，卡洛塔·伊根和布思中尉正一起待在浮台上。约翰·昆西爬上去，坐在他们旁边。

「瞧，我回来了。」他宣布。

「全世界都得知道你回来了，」中尉说。「还浑身湿透。」

他们坐在那里。信风越过一千英里温暖的海水赶来，轻拂他们的脸颊。南十字星悬在天上，紧贴着地平线；沿着一整条海岸，岛上的灯火摇曳明灭；钻石头山上那只黄色的眼睛正眨个不停。一派瑰丽的布景。只有一样不好。这儿似乎太挤了些。

===Original===
John Quincy had an inspiration. “Just as I hit the water,” he remarked, “I thought I heard you say something about my dive. Didn’t you like it?”

“It was rotten,” replied the lieutenant amiably.

“You offered to show me what was wrong with it, I believe?”

“Sure. If you want me to.”

“By all means,” said John Quincy. “Learn one thing every day. That’s my motto.”

===Chinese===
约翰·昆西灵机一动。「方才我入水那会儿，」他说，「我好像听见你说起我的跳水来着。是不是觉得不入眼？」

「糟透了。」中尉和颜悦色地答道。

「我记得你还主动提出，要指点指点我毛病出在哪儿？」

「当然。只要你想学。」

「务必请教，」约翰·昆西说。「每天学会一样东西。这是我的座右铭。」

===Original===
Lieutenant Booth went to the end of the springboard. “In the first place, always keep your ankles close together﻿—like this.”

“I’ve got you,” answered John Quincy.

“And hold your arms tight against your ears.”

“The tighter the better, as far as I’m concerned.”

“Then double up like a jackknife,” continued the instructor. He doubled up like a jackknife and rose into the air.

===Chinese===
布思中尉走到跳板尽头。「第一，两脚踝永远要并拢——像这样。」

「懂了。」约翰·昆西答道。

「还有，两条胳膊要紧紧贴住耳朵。」

「贴得越紧越好，在我这边来说。」

「然后像折刀一样对折起身，」教官继续说道。他像折刀一样对折起身，腾入空中。

===Original===
At the same instant John Quincy seized the girl’s hands. “Listen to me. I can’t wait another second. I want to tell you that I love you﻿—”

“You’re mad,” she cried.

“Mad about you. Ever since that day on the ferry﻿—”

“But your people?”

“What about my people? It’s just you and I﻿—we’ll live in San Francisco﻿—that is, if you love me﻿—”

“Well, I﻿—”

“In heaven’s name, be quick. That human submarine is floating around here under us. You love me, don’t you? You’ll marry me?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
就在同一瞬间，约翰·昆西一把抓住姑娘的双手。「听我说。我一秒钟也等不下去了。我要告诉你，我爱你——」

「你疯了。」她喊道。

「为你而疯。打渡船上那一天起——」

「可你的家人呢？」

「我的家人怎么了？只有你和我——我们去旧金山过日子——也就是说，如果你爱我的话——」

「呃，我——」

「看在老天的份上，快一点。那条人形潜艇就在咱们脚底下转悠呢。你爱我，是不是？你肯嫁给我？」

「肯。」

===Original===
He took her in his arms and kissed her. Only the wandering Winterslips could kiss like that. The stay-at-homes had always secretly begrudged them the accomplishment.

The girl broke away at last, breathless. “Johnnie!” she cried.

A sputter beside them, and Lieutenant Booth climbed on to the float, moist and panting. “Wha’s that?” he gurgled.

“She was speaking to me,” cried John Quincy triumphantly.

===Chinese===
他把她搂进怀里，吻了她。唯有流浪的温特斯利普家的人，才能这样接吻。留守故土的那些人，从来都暗暗嫉妒他们这门本事。

姑娘终于挣脱出来，气喘吁吁。「约翰尼！」她喊道。

旁边哗啦一阵水响，布思中尉爬上浮台，浑身水淋淋，直喘粗气。「什——什么？」他咕噜道。

「她在跟我说话呢。」约翰·昆西得意洋洋地喊道。
